# Decision log

The canonical record of **why** the specs are what they are — the reasoning and the **rejected alternatives** behind decisions that have closed. It is the guard against silently relitigating a settled decision.

**Consult it before acting.** Before proposing a change, reopening a question, or picking an approach, check here first: the path you're considering may already be a decided-and-recorded one — or an explicitly rejected one. If it is, follow the record (or make the case to revisit it); don't quietly re-decide.

**When a seam closes, record the verdict in both places.** Capture the *what* + *why* + rejected alternatives **here** (the canonical cross-ticket index) **and** in the GitHub issue's `## Decision` section (the per-ticket record), keeping the two in sync; put the full reasoning in the relevant [`docs/`](.) companion (spec, schema, runbook) when the decision touches a documented area. This file is the durable, cross-ticket index of settled decisions — it does not replace the issue tracker (still the system of record for a single ticket's status), it sits above it.

See the [`design-decision-forks`](../.claude/rules/design-decision-forks.md) rule for how a fork is surfaced and driven to resolution *before* its verdict lands here.

## How to add an entry

Oldest first, one entry per closed decision — so **a new entry is appended to the end of the file**, which is where writing one naturally puts it. Keep it terse; the companion or issue carries the depth.

This order was reversed on 2026-09-05 (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2236). It had been newest-first, and the six most recent entries had drifted out of order because appending is the natural way to add one and appending was the one position that convention forbade. Rather than police the conflict with a check, the convention was changed to match the action — and the check now enforces *that*.

```markdown
## YYYY-MM-DD — <short decision title> (<#issue or area>)

**Decision.** <what was decided, in a sentence or two>

**Why.** <the reasoning that settled it>

**Rejected.** <the alternatives considered, and why each lost>

**Companion.** <link to the docs/ companion or GitHub issue holding the full reasoning>
```

---

## 2026-07-06 — Conditions & treatments are NOT published as pages (product direction)

**Decision.** The `clinical_conditions` and `clinical_services` (treatment) collections have their **front-end views removed** — no public entry page/profile. Conditions/treatments serve as association + aboutness signals feeding Areas of Expertise and provider/location profiles, not as standalone (encyclopedic/MedlinePlus-style) pages.

**Why.** Product direction stated on the 2021 Geonetric findings-call: the Statamic build moves the major browseable sections into their own primary-nav nodes (Areas of Expertise first, then Locations), and the AoE node becomes *the* topic anchor — so standalone condition/treatment pages are redundant and not worth maintaining. Directly makes UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1207 moot (its premise was condition/treatment pages + templates).

**Rejected.** Keep condition/treatment profiles as published pages (redundant with the AoE node; encyclopedic content not worth maintaining).

**Companion.** Surfaced via the UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1237 Geonetric triage. Affects UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1052/UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1061 (subtype discriminators/relabel), #1280, UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1324, and closed UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1207 and UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1174 (referral-info block on AoE/condition/treatment pages) as moot.

## 2026-07-06 — Site search = one unified result list + page-type filters + per-type cards (product direction)

**Decision.** Site search (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#815/UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#56) renders a **single unified result list** with **page-type filters**, each result a **card formatted per type** (providers / locations / pages / posts) — not siloed per-collection result sets. Engine/relevance/indexing remain UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#815's open backend decisions.

**Why.** Product direction (2021 findings-call notes); also validated by 2023 user testing (the "false floor" — a siloed search showing "0 results" for pages while ignoring provider hits — is exactly what a unified list prevents). Establishes the results-URL contract (`/search?q=`, since renamed from `/search/results?q=`) that dependent tickets (e.g. the former UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1207) consume.

**Rejected.** Per-collection siloed result pages (the false-floor failure mode observed in 2023 testing).

**Companion.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#815 (engine) + UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#56 (UI). Surfaced via the Geonetric triage (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1237/#1282).

## 2026-07-06 — Areas of Expertise align to ABMS ↔ NUCC/NPI taxonomy (post-launch direction)

**Decision.** After the AoE virtual-subsite (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1053) launches, align the Areas-of-Expertise set to the **ABMS specialties/subspecialties**, which align to the **NUCC/NPI taxonomy codes** already used for provider clinical titles (`clinical_specializations`) — so providers can be auto-associated to AoE nodes via their specialty codes.

**Why.** Product direction (2021 findings-call notes); composes with #1202 (automate NPPES → primary `clinical_specializations` code) and UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1220 (the shipped NUCC hierarchy). A durable spine (ABMS↔NUCC↔NPI) lets provider↔AoE association be derived rather than hand-curated.

**Rejected.** Hand-curate provider↔AoE associations indefinitely (doesn't scale; drifts).

**Companion.** Post-launch enhancement to UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1053; composes with #1202/UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1220/UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1271. The "Areas of Expertise → Specialties" collection relabel + `clinical_expertise` → `clinical_specialties` handle rename this direction motivates is tracked in **#1391** (deferred until this lands — see the 2026-07-08 entry). Surfaced via the Geonetric triage.

## 2026-07-06 — Clinical-trial block condition/treatment link: closed as moot (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1207)

**Decision.** Close UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1207 as not planned. Its every acceptance criterion is premised on `clinical_conditions` / `clinical_services` (treatment) **public pages and templates**, which are being removed (no public entry page/profile) — so there is no page for the block to appear on and no template to consolidate.

**Why.** The ticket (FD20-328) exists solely to make the clinical-trial block context-aware *on condition/treatment pages* and to dedupe *those templates'* markup; with both gone, the premise is void.

**Rejected.** Rescope now to a surviving surface (no confirmed requirement/surface — a context-aware trials link on an AoE page or a trials listing would be a new, differently-scoped ticket). Keep open (permanently-broken premise).

**Companion.** Issue UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1207 (closed not planned). If a context-aware trials deep-link is later wanted on a surviving surface, file fresh — the mechanics are settled (`title` → `/search?q=`), only the render surface changed. Composes with the C/T-not-published-as-pages decision.

## 2026-07-06 — Provider clinical-title → multi-select specialties: close as superseded (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1271)

**Decision.** Close UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1271 as superseded. The provider clinical-title field is *already* the multi-value NUCC `clinical_specializations` taxonomy (a `terms_search_index` with no `max_items`), values are *already* NUCC terms (nothing to rewrite), and UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1061 concerns the ontology-collection *labels* (kept as "Areas of Expertise" — see the 2026-07-08 entry), not this field — so it's not a blocker.

**Why.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1271 (FD20-2672) was authored against a WordPress single free-text clinical title the Statamic build already replaced; its "convert to multi-select / rewrite titles" deliverables are already true or moot. The provider card already carries a comma-separated list of specialization term IDs and the `provider_title` filter axis *is* `clinical_specializations`.

**Rejected.** Keep UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1271 owning the filter wiring (overlaps UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#64). Treat as gated by UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1061 (wrong-axis false dependency).

**Companion.** Issue UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1271 (closed). Residual: filter-aggregation build → UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#64; the "permit >1 specialization per provider" content-policy question → a small #1202 follow-up (parked there as "defer unless secondary specialties displayed").

## 2026-07-06 — Org-wide holiday closures: central source in the FaD Settings global (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1327)

**Decision.** Put the central closure source in the `uams_fad_settings` global as a new "Clinical Locations Closures" section holding a `holiday`-shaped replicator, fed to `SchemaLocationHours` as a second `special_hours` binding. Scope = broadcast-by-default + per-location opt-out (mirror the shipped "Clinical Locations Alert" modification pattern). Precedence = per calendar date at merge time — a location's own `special_hours` covering a date suppresses the central entry for that date.

**Why.** The repo already ships this exact broadcast-with-per-location-override mechanism (the FaD-settings alert) and the closure data shape (the `holiday` set); reusing both is the lightest build (no new collection/route/Stache), reuses the existing tag via a second binding, and is trivially reversible. Merge-time de-dup is mandatory since the tag emits every spec and schema.org has no intrinsic precedence.

**Rejected.** Dedicated `location_closures` collection (heaviest; recommended second option only if editors manage dozens/yr with publish/expiry workflow — ties to UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1328). Taxonomy (can't hold dated ranges + hours rules).

**Companion.** Issue UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1327 (`afk`); follow-up of UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1294. Residual: merge in the tag vs the schema-template partial (call site in the content submodule).

## 2026-07-06 — Clinical Resource video: ratify the merged posts-segment caption model (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1090)

**Decision.** With UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1086 merged, `clinical_resources/video.yaml` was aligned to the posts video-segment model (`video` replicator `max_sets:1` → `uamsweb_video_segment_*` importing `uamsweb_video_caption` + `uamsweb_post_video_segment` + creators), which already answers all three of the issue's "decisions": always-full segment, `digital_source_type_video` (IPTC dropped), no short `caption`. Ratify; flip `afk`.

**Why.** The substance is already implemented and test-locked (`ClinicalResourceVideoSegmentTest`); re-deciding relitigates merged code.

**Rejected.** Re-align to the Bard set model (`embed_mode`/short `caption`/`align` — reopens 3 decisions, breaks tests). Build the show render inside this issue (the show template is an empty stub — the render is a slice of the unbuilt collection front-end).

**Companion.** Issue UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1090; residual = the show-page render (a mechanical port of `posts/video.antlers.html`) tracked with the clinical_resources front-end.

## 2026-07-06 — Guard clinical_* mounts: reject any mount resolving to a pages tree root (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1324)

**Decision.** Option C — a `CollectionSaving` listener (beside `PreventDeletingMounts`) that hard-blocks the save when `collection->mount()` resolves to its site's `pages` tree root, plus a feature test; not keyed to the `clinical_` prefix or an allowlist.

**Why.** Only `clinical_expertise` mounts today, so A/B/C guard the identical surface now and diverge only on future mounts. C is self-maintaining (zero drift), least code (drops the handle gate that only removes protection), and false-positive-free (`Tree::root()` self-nullifies where no root). No collection should ever mount to a site-home root, so reaching beyond the clinical family is a feature.

**Rejected.** `clinical_` prefix (rename drops the guard). Config allowlist (forgettable curation gap). Both add code that only narrows coverage.

**Companion.** Issue UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1324; blocker UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1054 closed. Residual: CP error copy, nonexistent-id handling, multi-site root resolution (specified, not forks).

## 2026-07-06 — Press Ganey integration: hybrid cache-warm; reclassified action-flavor (#1290)

**Decision.** Architecture = the hybrid cache-warm pattern (cache-read at render + scheduled out-of-band warm), the proven PubMed (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#865) shape — zero request-time HTTP, cold cache degrades to "no rating," ≥30 suppression at warm/emit, `AggregateRating` fed from cache. The architecture fork is decided, so the issue flips `decision-fork → action-flavor` (stays `hitl`): the residue is provisioning Press Ganey credentials + confirming API tier/cost/terms/threshold.

**Why.** A carry-over of a working WP feature, not a greenfield question; the repo already rejected fetch-on-render for PubMed (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#865) because a cold cache blocks a public profile on third-party latency — the exact risk this issue names.

**Rejected.** Live fetch-on-render (rejected for PubMed in UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#865). Scheduled import into entry fields (only if ratings must be queried/sorted; Stache churn) — escalate later only if that need emerges.

**Companion.** Issue #1290 (research spike → action-flavor). Display half = #1289 (`afk`, blocked by this). Locations genuinely greenfield; NPI mapping unverified.

## 2026-07-06 — Press Ganey display: both surfaces, one suppression-aware block, #1289 owns JSON-LD (#1289)

**Decision.** Render the rating on both `people` and `clinical_locations` via a single shared, suppression-aware partial surfaced as a `:when`-guarded page-builder block (below-threshold/missing → renders nothing); #1289 emits the `AggregateRating` JSON-LD via a new merger. Build data-gated on #1290 (`blocked_by` edge added).

**Why.** The person template already uses the `:when`-guarded `uamsweb_block` idiom, so suppression + empty state come for free; the location template is a thin shell, so "also on locations" is a small second include. The `AggregateRating` emission is display-of-sourced-rating (not sourcing) → belongs to #1289; folding it into #1290 risks a UI-only ship.

**Rejected.** Provider-only now (re-opens the location question the title raised). Both surfaces UI-only, JSON-LD deferred (splits one rating across two owners; schema-never-lands risk).

**Companion.** Issue #1289; blocked_by #1290. Residual: metric granularity, copy/threshold wording, whether location-level data exists (else the location surface sits at its empty state).

## 2026-07-06 — Scheduled closures / modified hours: ratify the shipped per-location model (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1294)

**Decision.** Go — adopt the already-shipped native per-location model: the `special_hours` replicator (`holiday` + `temporary_change`) on `clinical_locations` + `SchemaLocationHours` emitting `specialOpeningHoursSpecification` (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#967 / PR UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1122), with whole-location closure on the `Closing` tab and banners on the `Alert` tab. Defer the two genuinely-open dimensions to follow-ups.

**Why.** The hard modeling + schema emission are built, tested, and in production; reopening "how to model it" relitigates a shipped decision. Only scope (org-wide broadcast) and staleness/visible-notice carry real un-built trade-offs — and those are deferred, not folded into the spike.

**Rejected.** Build a central holiday-broadcast source + fan-out now (→ deferred to UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1327). Pull auto-expiry + visitor notice into this ticket (→ deferred to UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1328). Reopen the grid-vs-free-text / holiday-vs-temporary modeling (relitigates tested code).

**Companion.** Issue UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1294 (spike, closed completed). Follow-ups: UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1327 (broadcast, `hitl`/decision-fork — central-source shape + precedence open), UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1328 (expiry + visitor notice, `afk`).

## 2026-07-06 — Provider-profile section order: clinical-first, uniform (#1280)

**Decision.** Front-load clinically actionable content (clinical bio → conditions → treatments → expertise → locations), push academic/research bios lower; one uniform order for all providers. Record here + file the front-end build follow-up.

**Why.** The render surface is greenfield (`clinical_providers/show` is a passthrough; `people/show` renders only header + `bio` + professional background), so the order is set at the cheapest moment — and #1231 already settled the deciding axis (prospective-patient-first, uniform, per-type overrides rejected), with #1236's jump-links neutral to reorder.

**Rejected.** Legacy WP order (buries actionable content — the status quo the spike challenges). Wholesale deferral to a content-owner (a research spike would close with no decision). Per-type ordering (rejected by #1231; faculty served by `people/show`).

**Companion.** Issue #1280 (research spike); residual (ratings/podcast unmodeled, fine-grained sequence) folded into the build follow-up.

## 2026-07-06 — Related providers: bidirectional sync via stillat/relationships (#1281)

**Decision.** Import an `entries` field (collection `people`, `create: false`, `exclude_self`, e.g. `related_providers`) onto `person.yaml`, rendered as a section that hides when empty; register a self-referential **`Relate::manyToMany('entry:people.related_providers','entry:people.related_providers')`** in `StillatRelationshipsServiceProvider::boot()`; run `relate:fill` to backfill. True stored bidirectional symmetry.

**Why.** `stillat/relationships` `^2.4` is already a dependency with a centralizing provider (precedent: certifications↔credentials `oneToOne`) and `relate:list`/`relate:fill` helpers — so stored symmetry comes from adding one mapping, not a dependency, and the addon owns the sync + delete-cleanup a hand-rolled `EntrySaved` listener would get wrong (loops, orphan back-refs, drift on a file backend). The field itself already exists as the `uamsweb_common_fad.providers` fieldset.

**Rejected.** One-way stored + reciprocal *read* (originally recommended — needs a reverse query every render, asymmetric YAML; moot once a vetted sync addon is installed). One-way independent (asymmetric; misses care-team reciprocity). Hand-rolled write-back listener (the risk the addon eliminates).

**Companion.** Issue #1281; user override of the read-time recommendation. Residual: confirm self-referential `manyToMany`, field handle/label (default "Care team"), eligibility scope (default active/clinical).

## 2026-07-06 — Related research articles: dedicated clinical_research collection, PubMed-computed (#1285)

**Decision.** Model each scholarly article as a PMID-keyed `clinical_research` entry whose bibliographic fields are *computed* from `PubMedEsummaryService`; associate to `clinical_expertise` + `clinical_locations` via curated `type: entries` relations; render a section that hides when empty; add a `MedicalScholarlyArticleMerger` extending `PmidMerger` to emit `MedicalScholarlyArticle`. Scope = articles, not trials.

**Why.** The NCBI E-utilities integration is already live in-repo (`PubMedEsummaryService` cached/warmed, `research_publication_select` on `person.yaml`, `Pmid` rule), so "internal vs external feed" is pre-resolved — metadata is PubMed-derived. A dedicated collection is the only shape that serves "same research on multiple subsections" without re-entering PMIDs, keeps no-drift authoritative metadata, matches the FaD relation norm, and gets empty-state free. `SchemaType::MedicalScholarlyArticle` already exists; only the merger is new.

**Rejected.** Embed PMID on each expertise/location entry (re-enter PMIDs per subsection — fights reuse). 6th `clinical_resources` type (conflates scholarship with marketing content).

**Companion.** Issue #1285; genuine in-codebase fork (feed operational — not action-flavor). Trials (`MedicalTrial`/ClinicalTrials.gov) = a separate future action-flavor ticket.

## 2026-07-06 — Curated grouping taxonomy: per-collection, reuse people_groups + add location_groups (#1288)

**Decision.** Reuse `people_groups` for `people`; add a new curated (`create: false`) `location_groups` for `clinical_locations`; add the *additive* group filter to the FaD Provider + Location blocks (no term selected → existing association behavior unchanged). Defer `clinical_resources` to a follow-up.

**Why.** `people_groups` is already a curated grouping taxonomy the People block queries via `:taxonomy:people_groups=""` (empty → unfiltered) — the exact "curation option, default unchanged" behavior the AC demand. Statamic can't scope terms per collection, so a shared taxonomy mixes provider/location/resource groups into one picker, and its cross-type win isn't the single-collection use case ("physical therapists" = providers).

**Rejected.** One shared `curated_groups` across all collections (mixed picker; term bleed). Fresh dedicated handle for every collection (near-duplicate of `people_groups`; kept only as the `people`-side fallback if `people_groups` is semantically loaded).

**Companion.** Issue #1288; residual: `people_groups` semantics unverified (submodule absent — reuse contingent), AND/OR composition, CP labeling; `clinical_resources` deferred.

## 2026-07-06 — NPI/NUCC provider specialty: automate NPPES → primary code (#1202)

**Decision.** The NUCC model is *not* rebuilt — it is already shipped as the hierarchical `clinical_specializations` taxonomy, with the **primary** NPPES code on `people.clinical_specializations` and JSON-LD emission live. Resolve the one open fork (ingestion depth) by building a re-runnable command that reads each person's stored `npi` and writes the single primary code; no blueprint/schema change.

**Why.** No automated NPPES/NUCC ingestion exists anywhere today (the "ingest" is a human copying the primary code from `npiregistry.cms.hhs.gov`). Automating the existing single-primary workflow matches the shipped model + FD20-452 occupation-title derivation exactly, is additive/reversible, reuses the `npi` join key on every person, and kills the drift the blueprint already warns about.

**Rejected.** Keep manual entry (known toil + drift). Multi-code capture with a primary flag (a real content-model expansion the shipped single-primary design assumes against — YAGNI; defer unless secondary specialties must be *displayed*).

**Companion.** Issue #1202; residual = automation source (public NPPES vs internal Epic feed — default public NPPES); shares the `clinical_specializations` spine with FD20-452 / UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#943.

## 2026-07-06 — patient_types age bands: nest as child terms (#1213)

**Decision.** Add the finer age bands as **child terms** under the existing coarse `patient_types` terms (via the taxonomy's existing `parent` field), adopting the issue's Kyruus age-band scheme as the starting point pending a content sign-off. Additive, reversible; coarse-only providers stay valid.

**Why.** The taxonomy is already hierarchical and the person field already multi-select, so children give both coarse rollup and fine filtering from one tree with no data loss — the only option that honors the on-disk blueprint, keeps day-one results correct, and needs no forced migration (the issue's own pinned default).

**Rejected.** Flat coexist / new siblings (discards the hierarchy; redundant overlapping facets). Replace the coarse terms (orphans every existing tag; correctness depends on an unverifiable manual re-tag).

**Companion.** Issue #1213; residual = exact band boundaries (content sign-off), net-new `patient_type` facet in `_block_fad_filter.antlers.html` (own slice), e2e blocked on provider archive (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#64 / UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#919).

## 2026-07-06 — Clinical-titles NUCC restructure: already satisfied, closed (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1220)

**Decision.** Close UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1220 as already-satisfied. All four "Decisions to resolve" are embodied in the shipped `clinical_specializations` term blueprint (single self-referential `parent` for classifications+specializations; NUCC `code`/`code_name`/`code_definition`/`code_definition_source` + CMS/Schema.org/O*Net/ISCO crosswalks; `extension_query` flow for no-code terms; `code` as a stored `identifier` distinct from `slug`). UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#943 already owns the import-readiness audit.

**Why.** The modeling questions are pre-blueprint Jira scaffolding since answered in code, and a live sibling (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#943 → `wordpress-importer#336`) already tracks the mapping — keeping UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1220 open duplicates it.

**Rejected.** Narrow to the multi-citation sub-question (single textarea very likely sufficient — NUCC lists one citation per definition; folded into UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#943 as a note). Retarget at content data-mapping (overlaps `wordpress-importer#336`).

**Companion.** Issue UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1220 (closed completed); residual (repeatable-citation question) folded into UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#943.

## 2026-07-06 — Recognition-list display: per-placement `display_style` (#1276)

**Decision.** Add a per-placement `display_style` select (profile section / page block), mirroring the shipped `topic_display_style` idiom; unset falls through to the current default. No data-model change. Ship a minimal first cut (default + one variant), defer the full variant catalog.

**Why.** The issue's motive — recognitions deserve different weight in different contexts — is inherently placement-driven, which a term-fixed style can't express. Per-placement reuses an established pattern, needs no model change (the per-person replicator + `AugmentedRecognitionListTerm::award_name` already supply associations + labels), and satisfies the "unset renders the default" AC for free.

**Rejected.** Fixed per-term style (one look everywhere; can't express contextual weight). Hybrid term-default + override (override-precedence complexity, hardest to unwind).

**Companion.** Issue #1276; residual = variant catalog (ship one first), and the unverified "locations" placement (no location blueprint wires recognitions today — confirm before building it).

## 2026-07-06 — Outbound-redirect GA: client-side beacon interstitial, flagged-only (#1247)

**Decision.** A minimal client-side interstitial that fires GA via the existing GTM / `dataLayer` (`sendBeacon` / `eventCallback`, then `location.replace`), scoped to flagged redirects via a new `track_outbound` toggle in the #1105 store; internal / UTM redirects stay a bare server redirect.

**Why.** Redirects are a server-side HTTP hop with no page for GTM to run on, so nothing fires on external hops today. A server-side Measurement Protocol call loses consent state + `client_id`; a visible interstitial adds friction on every hop. A near-instant client interstitial reuses GTM, is consent-aware, and is reliable with `sendBeacon`.

**Rejected.** Visible "leaving UAMS" interstitial (friction). Server-side Measurement Protocol (no consent/`client_id`; duplicates GA config).

**Companion.** Issue #1247; builds on #1105 (`track_outbound` toggle) + UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1110.

## 2026-07-06 — clinical_resources landing: curated tiles layered above the list (#1268)

**Decision.** A categorized-tile landing = curated, editor-ordered tiles layered above the plain filterable list, homed on the UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1069 `pages` landing entry (no dedicated template), tiles linking to UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#576's filtered-list URLs. Go.

**Why.** The facets are first-class relation fields + a subject-matter taxonomy + blueprint-type, and UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#576 already filters them, so tiles are a curated cover layer, not a new engine. Fully dynamic tiles are noisy on a young archive; a plain list discards a cheap UX win.

**Rejected.** Fully dynamic tiles (noisy, no editorial control). Plain list only (discards the win).

**Companion.** Issue #1268 (research spike); prereqs UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#576 + UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1069 unmerged.

## 2026-07-06 — OPAL resources: closed as not planned (content, not development) (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1270)

**Decision.** Close UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1270 as *not planned*. Publishing OPAL as structured entries is a content-authoring problem, not a development one — `clinical_resources`'s `document` blueprint (from UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#799) already pairs a structured searchable body with an optional PDF, so no new modeling is needed; the remaining work is editorial.

**Why.** The content model already supports OPAL; the remaining work (authoring `document` entries from the PDFs) the dev tracker doesn't own.

**Rejected.** Dedicated OPAL collection / new discriminator (the `document` blueprint already covers it).

**Companion.** Issue UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1270 (closed not planned).

## 2026-07-06 — Baptist Health signal: reuse brand/organization attribution, no new field (#1273)

**Decision.** Reuse the existing `brand_organization` attribution — no new field. Create a Baptist Health third-party brand-org entry in `organizations` and attribute its locations, identical to the Arkansas Children's precedent. Defer the GMB CSV export-filter consumer to a follow-up gated on product confirming the exclusion is wanted.

**Why.** The codebase already models "belongs to a separate org" as a `brand_organization` entry (the AC precedent), so a Baptist signal needs no new attribute. A boolean duplicates that + drifts from AC; generic export machinery is a superset and speculative (YAGNI).

**Rejected.** Dedicated boolean flag (duplicative, drifts from AC). Generic owning-org export machinery now (speculative).

**Companion.** Issue #1273 (research spike, borderline); export-filter build deferred + product-gated.

## 2026-07-06 — Accepted insurance: curated plan taxonomy interim, feed follow-up (#1234)

**Decision.** A curated insurance-plan taxonomy referenced from `people` (graceful-degrade section + mandatory "verify with your plan" disclaimer) as the shippable interim; file a follow-up to name a credentialing / payer-feed owner (Option C supersedes later). Build gated on a named data maintainer.

**Why.** No insurance model exists, so every option is net-new and the decision hinges on maintenance realism; free-text has the worst staleness (patient-misleading), a feed is real-world-accurate but needs an unprovisioned external system; a curated taxonomy ships controlled terms + disclaimer now.

**Rejected.** Per-provider free-text (no vocabulary, worst drift). External feed as the immediate build (right long-term; unprovisioned — filed as the superseding follow-up).

**Companion.** Issue #1234; DO NOT build until a named maintainer exists; feed-owner follow-up to file.

## 2026-07-06 — ER resource-nav: dual-code icon + visible label (#1235)

**Decision.** Dual-code the ER resource-nav entry: an unambiguous emergency glyph + a short visible label ("ER" / "Emergency"), collapsing the redundant aria-label + sr-only to one visible name; keep the visible label on ER even where other icon items stay icon-only.

**Why.** Icon-only burdens glyph recognition under stress (the named failure); plain text loses the affordance and crowds the icon row. Dual-coding is the a11y + wayfinding gold standard.

**Rejected.** Icon-only. Plain text.

**Companion.** Issue #1235 (research spike); relates to #1224 mobile resource-nav.

## 2026-07-06 — Provider jump-links heading: "On this page", visible h2 (#1236)

**Decision.** "On this page" as the greenfield provider-profile jump-links heading, a visible `<h2>` beneath the profile `<h1>`; sr-only fallback.

**Why.** Greenfield, so the deciding axis is the document outline; "On this page" is the established in-page-nav convention AT users recognize and stays neutral as sections reorder (#1231). "Jump to" is less self-describing; provider-specific risks a noisy outline.

**Rejected.** "Jump to". Provider-specific phrasing.

**Companion.** Issue #1236 (research spike).

## 2026-07-06 — Location scheduling: top schedule action in the #1222 action bar (#1243)

**Decision.** Surface online scheduling as the top schedule action in the #1222 action bar (no duplicate component), gated by `mychart_scheduling_query` + the site-wide MyChart toggle; the MyChart widget stays a mid-page section the action deep-links to.

**Why.** #1222 already commits location profiles to a data-driven action bar with a schedule action, so this is cheapest + least drift-prone. A banner adds clutter + an empty-strip failure when off; a mid-page section under-serves above-the-fold visibility.

**Rejected.** Promoted banner. Promoted mid-page section.

**Companion.** Issue #1243 (research spike); sequenced behind UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#64 / #1222 + #1231.

## 2026-07-06 — A-Z browse: dedicated `#` bucket for numeric-leading terms (#1229)

**Decision.** A dedicated `#` bucket in the greenfield A-Z browse, positioned after Z, raw-string within-bucket sort; all numeric-leading condition / treatment terms land there.

**Why.** Standard glossary convention (predictable home), keeps alphabetic buckets clean, avoids fragile numeric parsing of "3D". Grouping under the first alphabetic char hides "3D…" under "D" and interleaves digits.

**Rejected.** Group under the first alphabetic character.

**Companion.** Issue #1229; feeds the UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#62 ontology-list build.

## 2026-07-06 — Archive search: server-backed `/search` + AoE-only browse (#1230)

**Decision.** Build a server-backed `/search` route + results view over the already-provisioned `local` index + light A-Z browse on the surviving AoE archive (AoE-only). Boundary: UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1053 owns index wiring + mounts; #1230 owns query route + results + AoE browse.

**Update (2026-07-14).** Shipped and rescoped — the search surface is the `/search` page (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1504) and AoE faceted browse is UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1491, so #1230 is narrowed to the residual A-Z / letter-jump browse question.

**Why.** The index is already defined (only the route / view are missing), uses `alternate_name` transformers, and beats client-side at hundreds of terms; UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1053 unmounts conditions / treatments, so only AoE survives to browse.

**Rejected.** Client-side A-Z filter (degrades at scale). No-go / fold into UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1053.

**Companion.** Issue #1230 (research spike); bounded vs epic UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1053.

## 2026-07-06 — Location profile: portal content pinned to the bottom, uniform (#1231)

**Decision.** Pin Portal content to the bottom of the greenfield `clinical_locations` profile, uniform for all locations; jump-links follow section order.

**Why.** Portal serves existing patients; the top is for prospective patients (settled premise). No template renders sections yet, so this is a build-time placement, cheapest greenfield; uniformity keeps jump-link order deterministic.

**Rejected.** Leave in place / middle (contradicts premise). Per-type overrides (unproven).

**Companion.** Issue #1231.

## 2026-07-06 — Reusable FaD CTA sections: `reusable_content` collection + relationship block (#1232)

**Decision.** Model reusable FaD content CTA sections as a CTA blueprint on / beside the existing `reusable_content` collection, attached via an `entries`-relationship block in a configurable page_builder slot. Distinct from #1222's data-driven action bar.

**Why.** A reusable-collection + entries-relationship + inline-render seam already ships, so almost no new machinery; explicit per-page opt-in + editor-controlled placement. Curated-per-page CTAs favor explicit selection over taxonomy auto-inclusion or a single global.

**Rejected.** Taxonomy auto-inclusion (opaque placement; surprise renders). Global set (single instance).

**Companion.** Issue #1232 (research spike); reuses the `reusable_content` seam.

## 2026-07-06 — Niche appointment logic: enumerated `appointment_mode`, seeded from gates (#1219)

**Decision.** Model niche non-physician appointment logic as an enumerated `appointment_mode` field on `people` (`direct_booking` / `referral_required` / `workflow_referred` / `follow_up_only` / `not_bookable`), seeded from the existing query-gate cascade; each mode → a documented CTA contract the external FaD consumer renders. Keep providers discoverable.

**Why.** The gate cascade already discriminates modes but can't distinctly express "follow-up-only" and forces consumers to re-derive intent; an enum seeded from the gates gives a legible label + CTA contract with no second drifting source.

**Rejected.** Ride existing fields (can't express follow-up-only). Suppress in search (hides legitimate providers).

**Companion.** Issue #1219 (research spike); CTA render is external (uamshealth.com).

## 2026-07-06 — FaD action bar: fixed data-driven, scoped to provider+location profiles (#1222)

**Decision.** Build a fixed, data-driven FaD action bar (schedule / call / directions from entry fields) scoped to `clinical_providers` + `clinical_locations` profiles; archives keep the existing generic editor `action_bar` block. Sequence behind UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#64 / UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#919.

**Why.** No hand-rolled action rows exist (drift premise inverted) and a configurable block already covers archives, so a fixed component everywhere is unwarranted; but profiles carry structured data that should drive the bar from the template.

**Rejected.** Editor block everywhere (profiles re-type structured data). No-go.

**Companion.** Issue #1222 (research spike); blocked by UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#64 / UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#919.

## 2026-07-06 — Mobile menu: curated resource-nav subset below primary nav (#1224)

**Decision.** Add a curated subset of resource-nav text links to the mobile menu, in a distinct group below the primary list, deduped against the icon bar (which already shows ER / MyChart on mobile).

**Why.** High-value icon links already persist on mobile, so omit leaves no gap and a full port duplicates them; only a few text links are task-critical. Bottom placement preserves primary-nav dominance; matches the framework-free mobile pattern.

**Rejected.** Full set (crowds / duplicates). Omit (text links unavailable on mobile).

**Companion.** Issue #1224 (research spike).

## 2026-07-06 — FaD archives geo: region-label filter now, distance deferred (#1226)

**Decision.** Region-label filter on the FaD locations archive now (wire the scaffolded Region `<select>` to `regions`); defer true distance-from-visitor to a follow-up after lat/long coverage + a geolocation strategy.

**Why.** Region data + filter UI already exist (near-zero cost, no visitor-location plumbing); distance is weakest today (lat/long not universal, providers have none, provider archive gated off); ZIP needs an absent centroid table.

**Rejected.** ZIP-based distance sort. Lat/long distance-from-visitor (incomplete coordinates).

**Companion.** Issue #1226 (research spike); provider-archive geo blocked on UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#64 / UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#919.

## 2026-07-06 — Referral-likelihood: extend `people.referral_required` (#1228)

**Decision.** Extend the existing unused `people.referral_required` boolean into a referral-likelihood signal (enum / scale) mapped to appointment-block copy / CTA variants with an unset → default fallback; build the missing (unported) appointment-block render surface here.

**Why.** Referral likelihood is a provider / specialty property and the field already encodes that intent, so extending it avoids a parallel owner; only this can vary both copy and CTA at provider granularity. Location = wrong axis (copy-only); ontology = no field + plumbing.

**Rejected.** On `clinical_locations` (wrong axis). Ontology / service layer (no field).

**Companion.** Issue #1228; new-vs-returning wording needs Marketing sign-off (action); render layer must be built.

## 2026-07-06 — Location directions: single smart maps link (#1199)

**Decision.** Render "get directions" as a single smart link from the location `map` GPS: `geo:` (Android native chooser) / `maps.apple.com` (iOS) / `google.com/maps/dir` universal fallback; hide when coords are null.

**Why.** A web page can't enumerate installed apps; emitting one platform-appropriate URL honors the OS default at one tap. A named-app dropdown can't detect installs either and duplicates the Android chooser.

**Rejected.** Explicit Google / Apple / Waze chooser.

**Companion.** Issue #1199.

## 2026-07-06 — Per-site people variants: presentation-only slicing (#1200)

**Decision.** Express per-site people display variants as presentation-only template slicing over the one central entry (select existing UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#802 role field-groups per audience), not stored per-site state. Phased: audience → field-group map → partials → per-site mount.

**Why.** `people` content is already non-localizable across 254 sites and the role slices already exist as gated field groups, so a variant is a view, not data — preserving single-source-of-truth with no routing change.

**Rejected.** Per-site stored overrides (drift + 254-site edit surface). Separate / display-config profiles (duplication).

**Companion.** Issue #1200 (research spike).

## 2026-07-06 — Provider search: `specialist_query` flag replaces `primary_care_query` (#1201)

**Decision.** Replace `primary_care_query` with a `specialist_query` `true_false`; provider-search radio: Specialists = set, Primary Care = explicitly false, No Preference = all; null = No-Preference-only (null-guard). Migrate `specialist_query = NOT primary_care_query`; update eligibility + renderers.

**Why.** Specialist is the meaningful curated designation and the split is a complete binary; a specialist-centric flag centers the field on what the search is about; the null-guard prevents unanswered entries defaulting into a bucket.

**Rejected.** Invert `primary_care_query` at read time (keeps the off-center field; lossy at null). Separate specialty taxonomy (no added fidelity; needs NPI curation + backfill).

**Companion.** Issue #1201; FaD filter block still gated on UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#919 / UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#64. Marketing label / default sign-off is a residual action.

## 2026-07-06 — Virtual/mobile providers: no-address `clinical_locations` subtype (#1208)

**Decision.** Model virtual / mobile / in-home providers as one no-fixed-address `clinical_locations` subtype differentiated by a `location_types` term (or practice-setting radio); gate the already-conditional address `if`s on it. Phased.

**Why.** All three cases are one shape (a practice setting with no patient-facing address); address is already conditional (facilities / children skip it), so it's one more `if`, and the entity stays a real location the people link + search consume unchanged.

**Rejected.** Provider attribute on people (strands the shared place; breaks location search). Separate concept (dual location sources everywhere).

**Companion.** Issue #1208 (research spike); touches the same location-classification surface as #1196.

## 2026-07-06 — Future-open locations: mirror the Closing tab (#1210)

**Decision.** Model future-open + pre-open messaging by mirroring the Closing tab: an `opening_query` toggle + `opening_date` (empty = already open) + a minimal acceptance radio + free `opening_info` copy. Standalone; default-unchanged.

**Why.** The Closing tab already ships this pattern in reverse, so cloning it is the lowest-risk, editor-familiar path. Reusing #1212's alert-window blocks on an unbuilt, non-appointment-aware mechanism; a full acceptance enum over-models a rare state.

**Rejected.** Open-state + acceptance enum with templated copy. Reuse #1212 alert-window (unbuilt; overloads Alert).

**Companion.** Issue #1210.

## 2026-07-06 — News ordering: enum importance + global sticky window + slot cap (#1157)

**Decision.** Model post importance as a small enum (Normal / Featured / Breaking) + a global sticky window + a fixed sticky-slot cap; teach `NewsSyndicationQueryService` to order by them before `date desc`. Post-MVP.

**Why.** The existing `sticky_query` boolean (authored but ignored by the service today) shows editorial appetite is "front-page-worthy?", not a numeric rank; a global window stays deterministic and avoids stale per-post dates; a cap prevents an all-sticky front page.

**Rejected.** Numeric importance + per-post sticky-until date (over-serves; dates go stale).

**Companion.** Issue #1157; feeds UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#76.

## 2026-07-06 — News springboard: fixed blueprint + query-exclusion dedup (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1158)

**Decision.** A dedicated springboard blueprint (sections grid of category + count) whose template owns a surfaced-ID accumulator and reuses UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#76's renderer, extending `NewsSyndicationQueryService` with an `exclude` param. Dedup page-wide, earlier-section-wins.

**Why.** Cross-section dedup needs ordered shared state that independent page-builder / Bard blocks can't carry; a fixed template owns the accumulator deterministically while reusing the proven UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#76 renderer.

**Rejected.** Free page-builder / Bard section set (can't carry cross-section dedup state — the issue's core promise).

**Companion.** Issue UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1158; extends the same service as #1157.

## 2026-07-06 — Magazine: taxonomy hierarchy over `posts`, newsletter deferred (#1159)

**Decision.** Model publication → release → section as a taxonomy hierarchy over the existing `posts` collection (publication = category, release = new subcategory layer, section = tags) + landing / archive views; defer newsletter / email to a scoped follow-up. XL, post-MVP.

**Why.** Flat `post_categories` / `post_tags` already map onto publication / section, so only the release subcategory layer is new — far cheaper than a parallel collection, which would duplicate the post substrate and fight the sibling news tickets. Newsletter is a separable distribution concern.

**Rejected.** Taxonomy + in-scope newsletter (scope-couples model + email). Dedicated release collection (duplicates substrate, XL).

**Companion.** Issue #1159.

## 2026-07-06 — Clinic-category provider filter: new back-end taxonomy on `clinical_locations` (#1196)

**Decision.** Derive the FMC / clinic-category provider filter from a location classification — add a new **back-end location-category taxonomy** to `clinical_locations` (terms like `Family Medical Center`, `Neighborhood Clinic`, …), not surfaced on the front end, plus a computed scope walking `people.clinical_locations → location.<category>` so page-builder blocks can filter providers by category. Not a `people` flag; not the existing display `location_types`.

**Why.** Clinic-category membership is a property of the location, derivable for its providers; a flag stores a derivable fact and drifts, a broader affiliation field duplicates the existing link. A dedicated back-end taxonomy keeps one source of truth, generalizes to any clinic category, and avoids conflating a back-end filter classification with front-end location typing.

**Rejected.** `true_false` flag on people (redundant, drifts). Location-affiliation relationship (duplicates the link). Reusing the existing `location_types` taxonomy (conflates display typing with a back-end filter classification).

**Companion.** Issue #1196 (research spike).

## 2026-07-06 — Find-a-Doc read API: custom controllers mirroring `provider_meta` (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1043)

**Decision.** Build a versioned, read-only custom API (controllers + resources) mirroring the WP `provider_meta` payload shape, with UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#919 provider-eligibility and #803 PII exclusion enforced inline and its own caching / rate-limiting.

**Why.** The shipped native app (`edu.uams.uamsnow`) is an uncontrolled consumer of `provider_meta`; only mirroring that shape avoids an uncoordinated app rewrite. Custom controllers also give the tightest inline eligibility / PII control.

**Rejected.** Statamic Content API — native shape ≠ contract. GraphQL — shape mismatch + schema upkeep.

**Companion.** Issue UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1043 `## Decision`.

## 2026-07-06 — Podcast schema: `PodcastEpisode`+`PodcastSeries` layered over `AudioObject` (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1097)

**Superseded (2026-07-17, UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1611, above).** The `PodcastEpisode` / `PodcastSeries` schema *layering* stands, but the **`podcast_series` taxonomy is retired** — podcast is now a `subtype` of `audio_series`, and the podcast gates read the series term's subtype (via a computed `is_podcast_episode`) rather than a dedicated `podcast_series` field.

**Decision.** Layer `PodcastEpisode` / `PodcastSeries` over the existing `AudioObject` node in the shared audio blueprint (additive). New `podcast_series` taxonomy; podcast feed out of scope; required = series + episode number + one media segment.

**Why.** UAMS Health Talk is serialized — it needs a distinct series node + `partOfSeries`, which `AudioObject`-only can't express and replacing `AudioObject` would discard. Layering reuses existing segments / media at modest cost.

**Rejected.** AudioObject-only (no episode/series, no feed). Replace AudioObject (discards the audio node).

**Companion.** Issue UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1097; blocker UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1096 shipped.

## 2026-07-06 — Redirect intake: CP staff utility, decomposed, sequenced after UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1110 (#1105)

**Decision.** Build a CP-based, staff-created redirect utility on the existing `redirects` store, structured so a public form / approval workflow can bolt on later. Manual creation; collision / validity required. Decompose into children: (1) CP utility + model, (2) collision / validity, (3) approval workflow, (4) optional public intake. Blocked by UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1110.

**Why.** All approaches write the same store; the choice is the trust boundary. Staff already authenticate to the CP and the write path exists — the smallest safe increment. Public auto-create is an open-redirect vector; the hybrid flow needs an approval model that doesn't exist yet.

**Rejected.** Front-end / public intake form (unsafe alone). Hybrid form → CP approval (premature).

**Companion.** Issue #1105.

## 2026-07-06 — Shared redirects on multisite: read-time merge in the tag (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1110)

**Decision.** Fix `find_redirect_rule` to merge `in(site)` with `in(default)` (per-site wins, default fills the rest), replacing the raw per-site `get()` that returns `[]` on origin-null non-default sites.

**Why.** Preserves the settled one-shared-editable-list model with zero content churn and subsumes the UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1088 workaround. Making `redirects` localizable abandons the shared model; the `value()`-cascade + origin config pays a ~100-site migration + drift risk.

**Rejected.** Cascade + ~100-site origin config. Make `redirects` localizable.

**Companion.** Issue UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1110. Land before #1105 / #1247.

## 2026-07-06 — Remove Peak SEO: migrate robots / sitemap in a blocker ticket first (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1117)

**Decision.** Split a blocker ticket that builds an app-owned `/robots.txt` + `/sitemap.xml` (maintained sitemap addon; bespoke robots view), land it, then reduce UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1117 to a clean `composer remove` + delete the no-op 404 hook + drop the orphaned `seo.yaml` imports.

**Why.** The body's "404 hook is the only blocker" premise is wrong (that hook is a no-op); the real blocker is live, test-guarded SEO routes with no replacement. Accepting the loss is an SEO regression; one combined ticket fuses unlike concerns. Prefer a maintained sitemap addon over bespoke (multisite / XML edge cases).

**Rejected.** Expand this ticket (mixed concerns). Accept losing robots / sitemap (SEO regression).

**Companion.** Issue UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1117; new robots / sitemap blocker migration ticket.

## 2026-07-06 — Outbound RSS feed: `posts`-only, per-site, RSS 2.0 at `/feed.xml` (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1042)

**Decision.** Build the outbound syndication feed as: the `posts` collection only; one feed **per site** at **`/feed.xml`**, only on sites where `posts` is enabled (driven off the collection's own `sites` list); **RSS 2.0**; standard item mapping (`title`←title, `link`←permalink, `pubDate`←publish date, `author`←author, `description`←excerpt). The 10-item, excerpt-only *output policy* was already pinned by UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#185/UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#186 (in `docs/specs/syndication-feed-output-policy.md`) and is not part of this decision.

**Why.** `posts` is the only syndication-shaped collection (no `news` collection exists). WordPress served a per-site `/feed/`; per-site `/feed.xml` mirrors that with zero hand-maintained config. RSS 2.0 is what the WP-legacy path and the UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#76 news-block consumers expect.

**Rejected.** One global feed — collapses the per-site news scoping WordPress had and UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#76 expects. Atom, or both formats — no consumer for a second format. Feeding additional collections — no syndication-shaped consumer today.

**Companion.** Issue UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1042 `## Decision`; output policy in `docs/specs/syndication-feed-output-policy.md` (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#185/UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#186).

## 2026-07-06 — Label every `hitl` issue by flavor: `decision-fork` vs `action-flavor` (hitl triage)

**Decision.** Stand up this decision log and split the `hitl` label's two flavors into explicit GitHub labels: `decision-fork` (a genuine design decision a human must make — a real choice between alternatives with trade-offs) and `action-flavor` (blocked only on a real-world action — deploy, access/credentials, upstream release, content authoring — with no fork to decide). A `hitl` issue that is neither — a mechanical audit with a conventional default already implied by the ticket — is a candidate to flip to `afk` (`buildable with no decision-forks`).

**Why.** The [`design-decision-forks`](../.claude/rules/design-decision-forks.md) rule already frames `hitl` as exactly these two flavors, but nothing on the issue distinguished them, so every `hitl` ticket read as if it needed a decision. Labeling the flavor makes the real decision-forks legible, lets action-only tickets be scheduled as real-world work instead of waiting on a nonexistent fork, and surfaces the mislabeled audits that should just be built.

**Rejected.** Keeping the single `hitl` label and inferring flavor per-ticket each time — loses the at-a-glance signal and invites repeated re-triage. Recording decisions only on the tracker with no `DECISIONS.md` — the issue records a ticket's status but not a durable cross-ticket "why we chose X over Y" index.

**Companion.** This session's `hitl` triage; the [`design-decision-forks`](../.claude/rules/design-decision-forks.md) and `writing-issues` rules.

## 2026-07-07 — AoE content-page `parent_page`: remove the decorative field (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1371)

**Decision.** Remove the `parent_page` reference field from the `clinical_expertise` `basic_page` / `landing_page` blueprints and delete the now-orphaned `ValidContentPageParent` picker scope. The structure tree (`content/trees/collections/health/clinical_expertise.yaml`) remains the sole source of the AoE parent hierarchy.

**Why.** No code reads `parent_page` or syncs it to the tree — the tree is already authoritative and enforced on both save paths (`ValidateClinicalExpertiseTree` / `ValidateClinicalExpertiseEntry`), so the field is decorative: it can silently drift from the real parent and misleads editors into thinking they've set the hierarchy when they haven't. `clinical_expertise` has no entries yet, so there is no data to migrate and re-adding later is trivial — the reversible, simpler move.

**Rejected.** *Reconcile field ↔ tree via a sync listener* — builds machinery to keep a field consistent that nothing reads; earns its keep only if a downstream consumer needs the parent as queryable entry data (the sole realistic candidate, the AoE importer, does not target this collection yet). *Keep as-is / defer* — leaves the misleading picker in place.

**Companion.** Issue UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1371 `## Decision`; introduced under AoE epic UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1053 by UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1055 / UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1056.

## 2026-07-07 — Dropped-but-revisitable blueprint UI: preserve as hidden fields (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#800)

**Decision.** In the `clinical_expertise` (Area of Expertise) blueprint normalization, UI dropped from the editing form but flagged as *possibly revisited* — the page-header block (style selector + graphic / marketing-landing-page / hero option groups) and the page-level "use intro as the short description" toggle — is **preserved as hidden fields**, reusing the canonical `uamsweb_header_selector_landing.*` / `uamsweb_common.true_false` definitions so imported data keeps a home. The WP-derived "selected excerpt" cache is **computed, not stored** (the importer backfills `excerpt`).

**Why.** The importer still carries this data; dropping the fields outright would discard it and force re-deriving the WP → Statamic mapping if the block returns. Reusing the canonical fieldset definitions keeps the hidden data shape identical to the live header, so a future un-hide is a visibility flip, not a remodel.

**Rejected.** Drop the fields entirely (discards importable data; re-derivation cost on any revisit). Store a "selected excerpt" field (redundant with a computable value).

**Companion.** Issue UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#800 `## Decision`; mapping table on [`wordpress-importer#17`](https://github.com/UAMS-Web/wordpress-importer/issues/17). Delivered in UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1380 (+ content [`uams-statamic-content#27`](https://github.com/UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-content/pull/27)).

## 2026-07-07 — SCSS `@import`→`@use` migration is blocked on Bootstrap 4 (#623)

**Decision.** Defer #623 (marked blocked-by UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1115). No `@use`/`@forward` migration shipped; the deprecation-visibility half of the ticket was already in place.

**Why.** The theme sits on vendored Bootstrap 4 — `@import`-based, global-scope "override-before-import" theming with no `@use … with()` equivalent. 704 Bootstrap-token references across ~60 first-party partials read those tokens from global scope, so there is no Bootstrap-independent first-party island to migrate; a partial `@use` migration yields a mixed root that still trips the `import` deprecation. `vite.config.js` already sets `silenceDeprecations:['import']` + `quietDeps:true`, and the Sass built-in-function deprecations were already migrated. The `@use` migration is gated on a Bootstrap 4→5 upgrade (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1115).

**Rejected.** Ship a token deprecation-visibility housekeeping slice (near-zero value; the suppression already exists). Partial first-party `@use` now (high regression risk over 704 refs; still trips the deprecation).

**Companion.** Issue #623 `## Decision`; blocked-by UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1115. Surfaced batching Wave 5.

## 2026-07-07 — Keep the `block-library` CSS layer: it styles live Bard sets (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#935)

**Decision.** NO-GO on dropping the `block-library` Vite input. It styles `wp-block-file` / `wp-block-file__button` / `wp-block-embed`, which the native `_file` / `_audio` / `_video` Bard sets emit — a blanket removal would visually regress those blocks. Extracting the live rules + dropping the ~90% dead remainder is filed as UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1341.

**Why.** The ticket's "zero `wp-block-*`" premise only grepped `content/`; extending the sweep to `resources/views/` finds three native Bard sets that emit those classes, and `block-library` defines 10 / 7 / 32 rule-refs for them respectively. The rest of the file (social-links / cover / navigation / gallery / …) is genuinely dead, but must be surgically separated, not dropped wholesale.

**Rejected.** Drop the whole `block-library` input (regresses File/Audio/Video blocks). Do nothing / re-evaluate blindly later (the live paths are now documented so it isn't).

**Companion.** Issue UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#935 `## Decision` (closed); partial-extraction follow-up UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1341. Folded into Wave 5.

## 2026-07-07 — Do not reuse the booted app across tests; re-baseline the cap (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1121)

**Decision.** Do NOT reuse a booted application across test methods. Re-baseline the importer per-file cap with the framework-boot floor characterized (~0.8s/method, measured), and guard the once-per-process `statamic:stache:warm` (the real ~45s cost) with `TestBootstrapCostTest`.

**Why.** The per-method boot is ~0.8s — ~3% of a slow importer file's runtime (e.g. Formidable ~18min / 36 tests); the other ~97% is real `statamic:import:wordpress` execution (~30-40s/case), a wordpress-importer optimization out of scope. So reuse cannot bring the importer dir under the cap, and reusing one app across the stateful import tests — which mutate extensive global Stache/content/config — would forfeit Laravel's per-method isolation. The ticket's own fallback ("if reuse is unsafe, document why and re-baseline") applies.

**Rejected.** Reuse the app across methods (marginal ~0.8s/method gain; large state-leakage blast radius across the whole suite; still over the cap).

**Companion.** Issue UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1121 `## Decision`; reasoning in the `tests/CreatesApplication.php` docblock. Shipped in Wave 5/12.

## 2026-07-07 — FaD taxonomy term import = match-only + flag, no metadata port (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#942–UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#952)

**Decision.** For all 10 FaD taxonomy **term** blueprints normalized in UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#942–UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#952 (`patient_types`, `clinical_specializations`, `affiliations_hospital`, `education_organizations`, `residency_years`, `certifications`, `certification_organizations`, `membership_organizations`, `education_types`, `recognition_lists`), the WordPress importer **matches** each WP term to an existing Statamic term by title/slug, attaches the reference, and on no match **flags** the term for editorial seeding — it does **not** create terms, does **not** write term metadata, and **never imports WP term IDs**. No blueprint field changes were needed: every WP data-bearing field already has a faithful target (verified against the plugin ACF JSON), the four orphan `certificate_primary_specialty_*` fields on UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#947 are **dropped** (no target, no consumer), and the plural-directory rename these depend on is already on `main` (`dfde3e9ad`, UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#882).

**Why.** Extends the settled UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#801 attached-taxonomy policy (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#951/UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#953/UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#954/UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#955/UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#956) to the provider-attached taxonomies. Term metadata is curator-owned; auto-minting bare or partially-populated terms would corrupt controlled vocabularies (and label-only sources like `recognition`/`educationtype` cannot supply the required editorial fields at all). Match-only makes every "relax `required` so a bare term validates" question moot — the importer never saves a term — so the blueprints stay unchanged.

**Rejected.** *Match-or-create + metadata port* (auto-creates bare/partial terms; would force relaxing `required` on `recognition_lists`/`education_types`). *Adding the four `certificate_primary_specialty_*` fields* (speculative surface nothing reads). *Renaming the singular blueprint filenames* (immaterial to resolution; kept per the UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#953/UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#954 precedent).

**Companion.** Hand-off mapping tables on UAMS-Web/wordpress-importer#335–#343, #345; blueprint work + regression test in UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1340. Composes with the UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#801 policy and the UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#882 dir-rename sweep.

## 2026-07-08 — Drop UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1060 from Wave 72: the `searchable` toggle was normalized out, and there's no search surface to gate (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1060)

**Decision.** Pull UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1060 out of Wave 72 and defer it — re-scoped to "add a per-entry search toggle + honoring scope **when the interactive search surface is built**," gated on that (unfiled) FD20-3307/3308 surface work. Wave 72 ships the 5 presentation/chrome tickets (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1057, UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1058, UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1059, UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#572, UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#573). The editorial need (SEO-index parent entries but exclude from interactive returns, WP FD20-3254) stays captured on UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1060, not closed.

**Why.** All three of UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1060's premises are stale against `origin/main`: (1) the `searchable` toggle it wants to mirror was renamed `searchable`→`searchable_query` (`81b2a441c`) then **deliberately dropped** in the clinical_condition normalization (`2dae94665`), a removal locked by `ClinicalConditionBlueprintTest.php:113`; (2) the `alternate_name` vs `alternate_names` index bug does not exist — the live blueprint handle is singular `alternate_name` and `config/statamic/search.php` already indexes it; (3) there is no `/search/results` / AoE-archive surface in the app to gate. Building the toggle+gate now yields an orphan field with no consumer and re-opens a settled normalization removal.

**Rejected.** *Build UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1060 as written* — mirrors a field that no longer exists, "fixes" a non-existent index bug, and gates a surface that isn't built. *Close UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1060 not-planned* — the editorial need is real; closing loses it. *Auto-file the interactive-search-surface epic (FD20-3307/3308) now* — a large speculative ticket beyond this wave's scope; left as a named residual to file when that work is scheduled.

**Companion.** Issue UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1060 `## Decision`; the clinical_condition normalization (`2dae94665`, UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#804/UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1052). Wave 72 (`wave/aoe-presentation`).

## 2026-07-08 — AoE subsite chrome: extend the UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#52/UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#53 subsection system, not a parallel resolver (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1057)

**Decision.** Deliver the Area-of-Expertise header subbrand + global-nav replacement by **extending the existing marker-driven subsection system** (`App\Support\Navigation\SubsectionResolver`, `App\Tags\Subsection`, shipped by UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#52/UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#53) to treat any `clinical_expertise`-blueprint entry as an automatic subsection root (blueprint rule **or** the existing `subsection` marker). AoE then inherits the already-wired subbrand and subsection-scoped primary nav; UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1057 reduces to three AoE-specific deltas: a small typed nearest/top-expertise computed (consumed by UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1058 nav + UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1059 CTA), the routed-section nav links, and the breadcrumb section-crumb.

**Why.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1057 was authored before UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#52/UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#53 existed, so its "hard-disabled `$subsection_query`" premise is stale — the subbrand and the "replace global with subsection-scoped" primary nav already ship generically and are exercised site-wide. Building UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1057 literally would stand up a *second* "what's my subsection root?" resolver that drifts from the first. One resolver, one chrome path, is the clean long-term shape.

**Rejected.** *New parallel AoE chrome system (literal UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1057)* — duplicates the subsection resolver + subbrand/nav path; two systems drift. *Marker-only (manual)* — makes "an AoE is its own subsite" a per-entry editor toggle and weakens content-page auto-inheritance; the subsite identity should be automatic from the blueprint.

**Cost accepted.** Extending the shared resolver changes chrome semantics **site-wide**, so the change carries a regression test proving non-AoE pages are unaffected.

**Companion.** Issue UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1057 `## Decision`; the UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#52/UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#53 subsection system (`SubsectionResolver`, `Subsection` tag). Wave 72 (`wave/aoe-presentation`).

## 2026-07-08 — Provider/location SEO title: constant "UAMS Health" brand, patient specialization title (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1329)

**Decision.** The templated `<title>` for `people`/`clinical_locations` is a computed `document_title` = `Name | Primary Specialty | City, ST | UAMS Health`, empty segments dropped. Brand is the constant "UAMS Health"; "Primary Specialty" is the primary `clinical_specializations` term's `title_specialization_patient` (or `code_name` when `title_specialization_query` is true); "City, ST" is the primary location's city + its state normalized to the USPS two-letter code. The stored `title` field is untouched.

**Why.** Find-a-Doc provider/location profiles are a UAMS Health product regardless of which multisite section the entry lives in, so a per-site brand would mislabel default-site providers; the AC examples use "UAMS Health" verbatim. `title_specialization_patient` is the field literally named the patient-facing specialization title. The Address fieldtype stores the full state name under the canonical `state_province_region` key, so the code must normalize it (via `App\Enums\State`, as `SchemaPlace` does).

**Rejected.** Sourcing the brand from the entry's site name (would yield "University of Arkansas for Medical Sciences" for default-site providers); using the occupation noun (`title_occupation`) for the specialty (a one-line switch if the content team prefers it); overwriting the stored `title` (breadcrumbs / CP / `<h1>` / disambiguator depend on it); emitting the full state name (an adversarial-review-caught bug — the spec is "City, ST").

**Companion.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1329; `app/Services/Seo/DocumentTitle.php`.

## 2026-07-08 — Unique page titles: proportionate per-collection disambiguation, not a save-time engine (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1330)

**Decision.** Keep provider/location `<title>`s unique within a collection/site inside the same computed `document_title` service (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1329): an index-scoped same-title scan gates the work and a deterministic " (2)", " (3)" ordinal is appended only to entries whose *composed* title actually collides, leaving titles that already differ (by specialty or city) unchanged. Scoped to the two formula collections (`people`, `clinical_locations`); bare-title collections keep rendering `title`.

**Why.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1329 already makes provider/location titles highly specific, so the realistic collision is exactly the AC's fixture (same name + specialty + city). Because the name is the first segment, only entries with the same stored title can share a composed title, so the existing `TitleDisambiguator` exact-title index scan is a cheap, correct gate — no new persisted state and no per-save cost.

**Rejected.** A full save-time cross-collection uniqueness engine (materialize the computed title into a stored field on `EntrySaved`, generalize `TitleDisambiguator` to scan all six templated collections, append " (n)") — over-built for a collision UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1329 makes rare, adding persisted duplication and a cross-collection query on every save. Also rejected: deferring UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1330 to its own ticket (the AC is satisfiable proportionately now).

**Companion.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1329, UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1330; `app/Services/Seo/DocumentTitle.php`.

## 2026-07-08 — Taxonomy term archives: app-repo view only; Statamic 6 auto-derives the route (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1145)

**Decision.** Deliver the `post_categories` / `post_tags` term-archive pages (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1145) and their descriptive titles (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1171/UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1172) as an app-repo change only — `post_categories.show` / `post_tags.show` templates plus shared `taxonomies/*` partials — folded into the titles/footer/forms PR. No content-submodule change.

**Why.** Statamic 6 auto-derives the term route (`LocalizedTerm::route()` → `/{taxonomy}/{slug}`); a term URL 404s solely because `toResponse()` checks `view()->exists($this->template())`. The taxonomy `route:` config lives in the content submodule, but it is unnecessary — creating the view is what makes the auto-route render, keeping the whole change in one app PR.

**Rejected.** Adding a `route:` key to `content/taxonomies/post_categories.yaml` (a cross-repo content-submodule change + pointer bump, splitting the delivery); an app-repo custom Laravel route + controller (non-idiomatic, duplicates Statamic's term routing). Also: deferring wave 106 until UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1145 shipped separately — unnecessary once the route proved app-repo-deliverable.

**Companion.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1145, UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1171, UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1172; `resources/views/post_categories/`, `resources/views/taxonomies/`.

## 2026-07-08 — Provider-eligibility CP-selection scope deferred to UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#919 (split from UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1361 → UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1405)

**Decision.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1361 ships its five `people` ↔ ontology **stored-sync** mappings (Family 1) now; its second AC — restricting the "Related Clinical Providers" pickers to clinical-provider-eligible `people` at the **CP selection surface** — is split to **UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1405**, blocked by **UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#919**. UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1361 closes on the sync.

**Why.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1361 specified the scope as "following the `searchable:is` pattern established for UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#919," but UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#919 is still open — that queryable/indexed eligible-flag pattern is UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#919's deliverable. `clinical_provider_profile_eligible_query` is a **computed** flag (not stored/indexed), and a Statamic entries `query_scopes` class filters the Stache query, so it cannot efficiently constrain on it. The sync foundation (what UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1063/UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1064/UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1067 consume) is complete and independent of the guard; the only residual is that an editor can still pick an ineligible person until UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#919 + UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1405 land.

**Rejected.** *In-memory filter now* — a Scope that materializes eligible IDs via `ClinicalProviderProfileEligibility::qualifies()` across all `people`, then `whereIn`: correct but O(n) per picker-open, no server-side pagination, and throwaway once UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#919's index exists. *Pull UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#919 into this wave* — does it right but broadens a foundation wave with a separate output/import ticket.

**Companion.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1361 (sync, shipped) → UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1405 (CP-selection scope, blocked by UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#919); UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#919 (output/import eligibility + the searchable pattern). Part of the UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1360 stillat cluster above.

## 2026-07-08 — Ontology M2M sync: `stillat/relationships` stored bidirectional sync supersedes UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1062's computed-inverse (Fork D) (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1062 → UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1360)

**Decision.** The three medical-ontology M2M edges (Specialty↔Service, Specialty↔Condition, Condition↔Service) are kept in sync via **stored bidirectional `stillat/relationships` mappings** — the UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1360 epic (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1361 people↔ontology, UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1362 cross-ontology "related", UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1363 schema-semantic) — **not** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1062's single-authored + computed-inverse model. UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1062 is **closed as superseded**; its built service/registrar/test (parked commit `9f04e1add` on branch `worktree-aoe-epic`, recoverable from reflog) is discarded. Both sides of each edge stay authored `entries` fields; the addon writes the inverse on save and owns delete-cleanup.

**Why.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1062 (2026-06-24, Fork D) and the UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1360 cluster (2026-07-07) are two mechanisms for the *same* problem — double-authored, unsynced ontology edges that silently drift — and they collide on the **identical field pairs** (e.g. `clinical_expertise.conditions` ↔ `clinical_conditions.clinical_expertise`): a field cannot be both a computed read-only value and a stored addon-synced field. The stillat direction is newer, covers a **superset** (13+ pairs across 3 families + people vs. UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1062's 3 edges), builds on the already-recorded 2026-07-06 `stillat/relationships` decision (#1281) plus the installed `^2.4` addon with a live certifications↔credentials precedent, and buys true two-way authoring and addon-owned delete-cleanup (loops, orphan back-refs) the computed model can't offer. Its costs — a one-time `relate:fill` backfill, cold-cache warming (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#937), drift if an event is missed — are the addon's known, owned surface, not new hand-rolled machinery.

**Rejected.** *UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1062 computed-inverse (Fork D)* — zero duplication and no drift by construction, but covers strictly fewer edges, collides head-on with the direction already in motion, and exposes a computed value (not a real stored field) to consumers, import, and export. *Hybrid (stillat for the stored pairs + keep only UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1062's derived generic Condition↔Service rollup)* — the generic "union of the semantic subtype fields" edge is a genuine derived concept, but downstream (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1063 transitive, UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1067 JSON-LD) can union those fields at read time, so no stored/computed field is warranted now.
