Fork D in the D9 mapping doc is superseded accordingly.

**Companion.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1360 (epic) + UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1361/UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1362/UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1363 (per-family mappings); #1281 and the 2026-07-06 "related providers via `stillat/relationships`" entry below; Fork D/E/G in [`docs/ontology-update/aoe-d9-relationship-ontology-mapping.md`](ontology-update/aoe-d9-relationship-ontology-mapping.md) (now marked superseded). UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1062's dependents UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1063/UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1064 read the same logical edges from the stored fields.

## 2026-07-08 — Keep "Areas of Expertise" labels; defer the `clinical_expertise` → `clinical_specialties` reframe post-launch (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1061 → #1391)

**Decision.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1061 **keeps** the established "Clinical Areas of Expertise" display vocabulary rather than relabeling the ontology collections — so it has **no code change** (its relabel is already delivered by UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#799's "Clinical `<X>`" fieldset rebrand, and its subtype tweaks are already satisfied by the normalization wave UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#805/UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1083/UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1380). The family prefix is **"Clinical"** (not the D9 mapping doc's "Medical"); the Schema.org `MedicalSpecialty` field labels stay "Medical Specialties"; the `clinical_specializations` taxonomy (NUCC Health Care Provider Taxonomy + in-house extensions) stays "Clinical Specializations". The full "Areas of Expertise → Specialties" reframe — collection handle `clinical_expertise` → `clinical_specialties`, blueprint file rename, display relabel, reference sweep, content migration, importer coordination — is **deferred to post-launch** and tracked in **#1391**, gated on the ABMS-alignment direction below.

**Why.** "Areas of Expertise" / "Clinical Areas of Expertise" is the established, tested (`FadFieldsetClinicalRebrandTest`), pervasive term across the shared FaD fieldset, the FaD settings global, the validation listeners, the front-end blocks, and this log. The D9 mapping doc's "Medical Specialties" lock was superseded by UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#799's "Clinical `<X>`" rebrand. The "Specialties" reframe is a non-urgent post-launch ABMS direction; executing it now is disproportionate cosmetic churn plus a large importer-coupled handle sweep (Fork C). A handle-only rename would make the handle disagree with the retained "Areas of Expertise" label — worse than today's consistent pairing.

**Rejected.** *Relabel to "Medical Specialties" (D9 as-written)* — splits the `clinical_*` collection family, collides nominally with the Schema.org "Medical Specialties" field label, and the project already went "Clinical" via UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#799. *Relabel to "Clinical Specialties" now (editor-facing or full sweep)* — pervasive churn against the established/tested term for a non-urgent reframe. *Handle-only rename now* — creates a handle/display mismatch.

**Companion.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1061 (relabel decision, no code change) → #1391 (deferred full reframe, post-launch); UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#799 (Clinical rebrand); UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#805/UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1083/UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1380 (subtype consolidation); Fork C in [`docs/ontology-update/aoe-d9-relationship-ontology-mapping.md`](ontology-update/aoe-d9-relationship-ontology-mapping.md). Composes with the 2026-07-06 ABMS↔NUCC post-launch direction below.

## 2026-07-08 — Per-entry sitemap exclusion via a dedicated toggle; taxonomy-term sitemap deferred (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1345 / UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1346)

**Decision.** Per-entry sitemap exclusion (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1345) is a new `include_in_xml_sitemap` toggle (default = included) on the `pages` blueprints that feeds the existing Wave-86 `meta_include_in_xml_sitemap` **computed** — never a stored blueprint field under that mapped handle. The computed AND-combines the editor toggle with the automatic exclusion of `private` / `password_protected` pages; the security gate always wins, so an editor cannot force a non-public URL into the sitemap. Taxonomy term URLs (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1346) are **deferred** (blocked by UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1145): no routable taxonomy term page exists yet. Extending the sitemap — and this toggle — to the other front-end-facing collections is tracked as follow-ups UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1402 (`posts` / `people` / `clinical_expertise`) and UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1403 (`knowledge_bases`); the sitemap stays `pages`-only until those land, per the Wave-86 scope.

**Why.** Sitemapamic reads inclusion as `$entry->get($handle) ?? $entry->getComputed($handle)`, so a stored field under the mapped handle `meta_include_in_xml_sitemap` would **shadow** the Wave-86 computed and let an editor advertise a `private` / password-protected URL (or, with a stored default, disable the gate wholesale). A distinct editor handle feeding the computed keeps the gate authoritative. UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1346 cannot meet its own "term `<loc>`s resolve, not 404s" criterion while no taxonomy is routable, and building term-listing pages is out of its scope.

**Rejected.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1345 — a stored `meta_include_in_xml_sitemap` field (shadows the computed, leaks non-public URLs); wiring the legacy `robots_index_meta` (noindex) field to drive sitemap exclusion (it is inert — rendered nowhere — and making it honest means also building per-entry `noindex` output, out of scope; its misleading "XML sitemaps" label is corrected instead). UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1346 — enable `includeTaxonomies` now (emits 404ing `<loc>`s, fails its own AC); a synthetic-fixture guard-only test (no real taxonomy to protect yet). Expanding the sitemap to all front-end collections inside UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1345 (reverses the Wave-86 pages-only scope in an under-tested rush; deliberately tracked as UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1402 / UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1403 instead).

**Companion.** Issues UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1345, UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1346 `## Decision`; follow-ups UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1402 / UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1403. Extends the Wave-86 pages-only sitemap scope — built in UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1326 (PR UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1348), recorded in `config/sitemapamic.php`; the Peak-SEO-removal decision it grew out of is the `UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1117` entry below.

## 2026-07-08 — Bootstrap-4 runtime JS removed; SCSS kept, popper.js silenced (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1342/UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1343)

**Decision.** Remove the deprecated *runtime* JS off the `uams_2020` theme — port `app.js`/`headertrays.js` and reimplement the smartmenus desktop nav, hero carousel, and gallery modal as framework-free vanilla ES modules (`resources/js/uams_2020/source/*.js`, jsdom-tested), then drop `jquery`, `smartmenus`, `smartmenus-bootstrap-4`, and the `bootstrap.bundle.js` import. The modules toggle the **same state classes Bootstrap/smartmenus toggled** (`.dropdown-menu.show`, `.carousel-item.active`, `.modal.show`), so the theme's **Bootstrap 4 SCSS stays untouched** and the UI is visually unchanged. The transitive `popper.js@1` deprecation is silenced with an npm `overrides` alias to the non-deprecated `@popperjs/core@2` (nothing loads it once the bundle is gone). The one remaining `bootstrap@4.6.2` install-time deprecation warning is **accepted, documented tech-debt**: it is the theme's whole design system, its removal is a separately-justified effort deferred per UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1115, and npm has no per-package deprecation suppression short of hiding *all* warnings — which the recurring dependency-update chore needs to keep seeing.

**Why.** The UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1115 spike found the blocker to dropping jQuery was **smartmenus, not Bootstrap** (smartmenus is jQuery-only, no BS5 port), and Popper already positions nothing. Replacing the smartmenus nav with a vanilla disclosure menu (mirroring the shipped UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#53 mobile nav) drops the entire jQuery stack **without touching Bootstrap's grid/SCSS** (566 call-sites, ~60 files) — the incremental JS decouple, not the disproportionate framework migration.

**Rejected.** *Bootstrap 4→5 or Tailwind rebuild* — deferred per UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1115 (huge SCSS/visual-regression surface, and BS5 would force the nav rewrite anyway). *Silence the `bootstrap@4` warning via `.npmrc loglevel=error`* — hides every other/future deprecation, defeating the dependency-hygiene chore; documenting the single accepted warning is the honest floor. *Keep `smartmenus-bootstrap-4` for its dropdown CSS* — its expanded-mode caret rules were vendored into `supporting/_desktop-nav.scss` so the package leaves entirely.

**Companion.** Issues UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1343 (runtime migration) + UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1342 (warning silencing); research spike UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1115. Extends the #623/UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1115 Bootstrap-4-deferral thread.

## 2026-07-08 — Custom fieldtype CP Vue: build UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#16 / UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#193, defer #191 / #486 / #198 to Statamic upstream

**Decision.** Of five custom-fieldtype Control-Panel-Vue tickets, build the two the installed **Statamic v6.24.1** extension surface cleanly supports — **UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#16** (Link → Files-asset option; config-only `container: files` on the shared `uamsweb_common_link.link_url` fieldset, since core already ships the `asset` mode) and **UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#193** (a custom `custom_date` component enforcing the Boundaries & Limits config, gated to fields that actually configure a boundary) — and **defer #191, #486, #198 to a Statamic core feature** (each relabeled `upstream` + `hitl`, carrying a `statamic/ideas` filing task). UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#193's config-select population spun off as follow-up UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1393.

**Why.** v6 ships a **closed** CP extension surface — base `FieldtypeMixin` + UI primitives only, **no** concrete core fieldtype components. So UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#16 is already native (a blueprint edit); UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#193 composes cleanly from the exported `DatePicker` + a `reka-ui` per-date predicate + a pure, unit-tested date engine. But #191 (core Assets is single-container end-to-end — an array `container` **throws `AssetContainerNotFoundException`** on CP render), #486 (the core `Uploader` exposes no `accept`/drag-drop seam and isn't wrappable without the unbuildable full fork the v6 upgrade already removed), and #198 (feasible **only** by re-implementing Grid's entire component tree — a permanent per-upgrade re-diff tax for a CP editor-convenience payoff) are each better served by a native core feature than a fragile in-app fork.

**Rejected.** *Build all five in-app* — #191/#486 are unbuildable cleanly on v6; #198's fork is disproportionate. *DOM-hack / full Assets re-fork for #486* — depends on core-internal DOM/timing, breaks on upgrade. *Unconditional `component()` override on `Date`* — would route **every** date field (entry publish dates, the fieldtype's own date config sub-fields) through the custom picker, a site-wide blast radius; gated to bounded fields instead.

**Companion.** Issues UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#16 / UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#193 (`## Decision`) + #191 / #486 / #198 (`## Decision update` — deferred to upstream, each blocked by a standalone `statamic/ideas` request: UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1396 / UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1397 / UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1398) + follow-ups UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1393 / UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1394, and the PR closing UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#16 / UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#193.

## 2026-07-08 — Structured taxonomies: use native structured collections, no CP relabel (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#252)

**Decision.** The editorial-need audit finds native **structured collections** (`knowledge_bases` = knowledge base → topic → article, the AoE `clinical_expertise` tree, and their siblings) already satisfy editors' need for a hierarchical, "taxonomy"-style authoring UI — so the thin "Structured Taxonomies" CP-nav relabel is **not built**, and UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#252 closes resolved-by-native. Use structured collections wherever hierarchy is needed. **Revisit if/when Statamic ships native hierarchical taxonomies:** at that point, convert the collections that exist purely for their hierarchy back to hierarchical taxonomies.

**Why.** Hierarchy is already delivered natively by structured collections, with CP create-flows blueprint-relabeled via `patches/statamic-*-create-label-blueprint.patch`; a parallel "taxonomy" framing is redundant chrome over the same collections and adds a maintained nav surface for no confirmed editorial gain. Native structured collections already read to editors as the hierarchy UI.

**Rejected.** *Revive/rebase the parked `structured-taxonomies` branch* (`76335e3`) — ~1yr stale, pre-Statamic-v6, built on a fragile "extend every core CP controller" pattern (settled 2026-06-23; branch is reference-only). *Build the thin CP-nav relabel now* — no confirmed need, and Statamic upstream may ship native hierarchical taxonomies, which would moot even the relabel.

**Companion.** Issue UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#252 (`## Decision` 2026-06-23 + `## Audit finding` 2026-07-08). The `post_categories` flat→structured *data* migration stays carved out to its own future issue, gated on a front-end-surfacing audit.

## 2026-07-08 — Release cadence: cut at each iteration close, plus on any breaking change (release process)

**Decision.** Cut a release at every iteration close (a step of [`iteration-end`](../.claude/skills/iteration-end/SKILL.md)), and additionally the moment any breaking change merges. Releases are retrospective `--prerelease` tags on `main`; **minor** bump if the iteration shipped feature/structural work, **patch** if refinement-only.

**Why.** Trunk-based merges leave "when to tag" undefined, and an undefined cadence decays — the repo sat 15 months / ~250 PRs untagged before this backfill. Anchoring to the iteration boundary matches how work is already batched and makes the cut an owned checklist item; breaking changes get their own release so the breaking-change callout isn't buried among unrelated work.

**Rejected.** *Per-PR / per-merge* — ~250 PRs would mean ~250 releases, pure noise. *Milestone-driven only* — no rhythm, which is exactly what produced the 15-month gap. *Time-based (weekly)* — arbitrary against the existing iteration structure.

**Companion.** [`.claude/rules/cutting-releases.md`](../.claude/rules/cutting-releases.md); how-to in the [`writing-release-notes`](../.claude/skills/writing-release-notes/SKILL.md) skill.

## 2026-07-08 — Versioning: 0.x milestone scheme, releases retroactively backfilled (release process)

**Decision.** Stay in 0.x until Launch (`1.0.0` = Launch); every release is a `--prerelease`. `0.x.0` (minor) marks a feature/structural milestone, `0.x.y` (patch) a refinement. A `U+26A0` flag annotated a change that *would* be a major bump at 1.0+ (framework major, breaking content-model change, structural extraction); the marker is words as of the 2026-09-05 entry below. The v0.8.8–v0.26.0 gap was backfilled retroactively as 19 milestone releases, and all pre-existing releases restyled to one house format.

**Why.** This is an app (`uams-web/uams-statamic`, no `version` field, no external consumers), so tags are milestone/changelog markers, not a library API contract. SemVer permits breaking changes in 0.x without a major bump, so pre-launch breaking work (Statamic 6, Laravel 13, the collection renames) stays a minor with a breaking-change heads-up rather than forcing a premature 1.0.

**Rejected.** *Jump to 1.0 on the first framework major* — premature; the product isn't launched. *Skip retroactive releases* — leaves a 15-month changelog hole. *Bury breaking changes as ordinary minors with no marker* — hides the migration impact a reader needs.

**Companion.** [`writing-release-notes`](../.claude/skills/writing-release-notes/SKILL.md) skill; cadence in [`.claude/rules/cutting-releases.md`](../.claude/rules/cutting-releases.md).

## 2026-07-09 — The provider D9 JSON-LD (`knowsAbout` + special-interest `Role`) emits on the `people` `[Person]` node, not the `clinical_providers` `IndividualPhysician` node (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1067)

**Decision.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1067's provider-side emission (`knowsAbout` over the `implied_services`/`implied_conditions` transitive sets, and the special-interest `Role` node) is wired on the **`people`** collection's `[Person]` schema node (`430ada55…`, rendered at `/people/{slug}`), reading the computed sets **directly** off the `people` entry — **not** on the `clinical_providers` `[Person, IndividualPhysician]` node (`1129c2ec…`). This resolves the "confirm the `clinical_providers`-vs-`people` identity before wiring the provider hop" wrinkle the D9 mapping flagged.

**Why.** `clinical_providers` is a sparse 77-line Workday/Epic **sync record** (`title`, a `person`→`people` reference, `npi`, `epic_ser_id`) with **no `expertise`/`implied_*` data of its own and no `route:`** — so its node never renders on a public URL and its JSON-LD could never reach a search engine (the entire point of UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1067). `people` is routable, renders the profile, natively carries `expertise` + the UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1063 `implied_*` computeds, and is already the canonical provider identity everywhere else (the FaD `providers` picker targets `collections: [people]`; UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1063 registered the computeds on `people`). Decisively, **`people` is the strategic future of the provider identity: #803 retires the legacy `providers`/`clinical_providers` collection in favor of `people`** — so emitting on `clinical_providers` would build on a collection being eliminated. `knowsAbout` is valid on `Person`, so the bare `@type` is sufficient.

**Rejected.** *Emit on the `clinical_providers` `IndividualPhysician` node via a `person`→`people` `secondary`-mode hop* — richer `@type`, but emits into a void (non-routable) and builds on the collection #803 eliminates. *Emit on both nodes* — maximum coverage, most machinery, and only the `people` half renders today; pointless while `clinical_providers` is being retired. *Retype the `people` node to `[Person, IndividualPhysician]` wholesale* — wrong, since not every `people` entry is a physician (a conditional `IndividualPhysician`-when-`expertise`-present enrichment is a separate typing decision, not this wiring slice).

**Companion.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1067 issue `## Decision`; `content/collections/schema_objects/default/people-collection-person-entry-as-content-person.md` (the emitting node). Aligned with #803 (retire legacy providers → `people`). Residual: if `clinical_providers` is ever routed before #803 lands, a later slice can add the `IndividualPhysician` node on top (the two nodes are not mutually exclusive, dedupe by `@id`); the `knowsAbout`-loose-fit vocab judgment is recorded in the UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1067 PR body regardless.

## 2026-07-09 — UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1065 ships the special-interest filter fields + scope and the aboutness fields; the live render (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#61-64/UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#919) and the JSON-LD emission (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1067) stay with their owners (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1065)

**Decision.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1065's two deliverables land as **authoring/infrastructure only**, deferring the render and the schema emission to the tickets that own them:
- **D1 (FaD filter).** Both FaD blocks gain **two typed filter fields** — `special_interest_clinical_conditions` + `special_interest_clinical_services` (reusing the UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1064 defs, relabeled "Medical Conditions"/"Medical Services") — plus one `OntologyOverlapSpecialInterests` query scope that OR-matches a profile whose conditions field OR services field overlaps the block's selection (grouped so the OR can't leak into an outer conjunction). The **live render wiring** (dropping the scope into the card markup) is **deferred to UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#61/UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#62/UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#63/UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#64/UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#919**, which own the currently-placeholder render partials. UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1065 AC #2 (wire the render partials) is retired to them.
- **D2 (aboutness).** Content-page blueprints (`basic_page`, `landing_page`) gain **entries-typed** `about_clinical_entries` / `mentions_clinical_entries` / `main_entity_of_page_clinical_entry` fields (a new `uamsweb_clinical_aboutness` fieldset) targeting `clinical_conditions` + `clinical_services`, following the `knows_about_entries` precedent. The **`MedicalWebPage.about`/`mainEntity` JSON-LD emission** is **owned by UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1067**. UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1065 AC #7 (verify the emission) is retired to UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1067.

**Why.** Verified against the code: the providers render partial hard-gates its entire listing behind `{{ $fad_providers_implemented = false }}` (pending the UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#64 card body + UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#919 AJAX-filter contract), and the locations partial delegates to a title-only placeholder body that does not even wire the *existing* `expertise` scope (its docblock assigns the live card+filter to UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#61/UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#62/UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#63). Wiring a special-interest scope into that dead code would be untestable throwaway those tickets rewrite. Symmetrically, UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1067 is the dedicated JSON-LD slice — its AC already emits `MedicalWebPage.about`/`mainEntity` and its Out-of-scope already lists UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1065's aboutness fields as "consumed, not built." So UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1065 ships exactly the infrastructure both consume; render and emission stay single-sourced with their owners. This is the same **data/infrastructure now, render/emission with the owner** seam as the UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1064-data / UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1067-schema decision below.

**Rejected.** *UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1065 wires the placeholder render partials now* (its literal AC #2) — produces untestable dead code UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#61-64/UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#919 must rip out, and would half-wire a block whose card/AJAX contract isn't defined yet. *UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1065 also builds the live render* — absorbs UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#61-64/UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#919's card + AJAX contracts, ballooning the ticket and colliding with five open tickets. *UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1065 emits the `about`/`mainEntity` JSON-LD itself* (its literal AC #7) — duplicates UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1067 AC #5; UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1067 must re-shape it as a `Role`-wrapped, `@id`-deduped `@graph` node, contradicting the UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1064/UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1067 precedent. *One combined both-collections filter picker on the block* — re-introduces the single `special_interests` field the UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1064 split retired; the two typed pickers reuse the split defs and give the clean relabel.

**Companion.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1065 issue body (`## Decision`, ACs #2/#7 retired with pointers); `app/Scopes/OntologyOverlapSpecialInterests.php`, `resources/fieldsets/uamsweb_block_fad_{providers,locations}.yaml`, `resources/fieldsets/uamsweb_clinical_aboutness.yaml`, `resources/blueprints/collections/pages/{basic_page,landing_page}.yaml`. A consume-note for the scope is posted to the render-owning block tickets UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#63 (Locations) / UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#64 (Providers); UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1067 already owns the emission. Residual: the filter has no end-to-end render proof until those tickets build the live blocks (scope proven in isolation), and a location special-interest *condition* has no valid schema predicate (the UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1067 residual on the UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1064 decision).

## 2026-07-09 — Special Interest is two typed fields (Conditions / Services), not one mixed picker (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1064)

**Decision.** The `special_interests` field is split into two typed `entries` fields authored on both Provider and Location: `special_interest_clinical_conditions` (→ `clinical_conditions`) and `special_interest_clinical_services` (→ `clinical_services`). Each holds only its own collection's ids. The save guard (`ValidateSpecialInterestsScope`) checks the **union** of both against the specialty candidate set; the implied-set override union (`ImpliedOntologyComputedValues::explicitOverride`) and the computed inverse (`SpecialInterestInverseComputedValues`) read the **field matching the collection being resolved** (keyed off the C/T entry's own collection). This supersedes the single-field-targeting-both-collections shape UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1064 originally built.

**Why.** schema.org, the WordPress source model, and UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1065's aboutness relabel all treat Conditions and Services as distinct: a Provider's special-interest condition emits Physician `knowsAbout` while a Location's special-interest service emits `availableService` (and a Location condition has no valid predicate at all), so the distinction is re-derived by every consumer if it isn't captured at authoring. Two typed pickers capture it once, give editors two clearly-labelled fields (and a clean "Medical Conditions"/"Medical Services" relabel in UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1065), and let each computed read a pre-typed list rather than bucket a mixed bag. Decided pre-merge (PR UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1432 still draft), so no data migration.

**Rejected.** *One `entries` field targeting both `clinical_conditions` + `clinical_services`* (the originally-built shape) — leaner, and every consumer *except the display label* handles the mixed bag, but it muddies the relabel and pushes the condition/service split into each consumer. *Split, but Location = Services only* (structurally bar a Location special-interest condition, which cannot emit schema) — rejected: a Location condition is still valid for the FaD filter and the computed inverse, so both profiles get both fields symmetrically; the location-condition schema gap stays a known UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1067 residual.

**Companion.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1064 issue body (`## Decision`), fieldset `resources/fieldsets/uamsweb_common_fad.yaml`, `app/Listeners/ValidateSpecialInterestsScope.php` (`OVERRIDE_FIELDS`), `app/Services/ComputedFields/Ontology/ImpliedOntologyComputedValues.php` + `SpecialInterestInverseComputedValues.php` (`OVERRIDE_FIELD_BY_COLLECTION`). Composes with the 2026-07-09 UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1064-data/UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1067-schema boundary below.

## 2026-07-09 — Migrate `post_categories` from a flat taxonomy to a structured collection (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1433 / UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#252)

**Decision.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#252's carved-out `post_categories` front-end-surfacing audit resolves to **migrate + surface**: `post_categories` moves from a flat Statamic taxonomy to a **native structured collection** (the `knowledge_bases` / `clinical_expertise` pattern) preserving the WordPress category tree, with hierarchical archive URLs. Sub-decisions (fork review): **hierarchical URLs**; a **literal `category/{parent_uri}/{slug}` route prefix** (the `people` idiom, no mount); **hard replace** reusing the `post_categories` handle; the **`KbEntryCreator` tree import**; **repoint + reuse** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1145's flat archive (not rebuild); bare `/category` gets a **browse-all index** (not an accepted 404). Owned by keystone UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1433, with UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1434 (archive rework), UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1435 (`/category` index), UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#220 (Branch B nav), UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1144 (news-block re-point), importer `UAMS-Web/wordpress-importer#13`.

**Why.** The production source is pervasively hierarchical (`<wp:category_parent>` non-empty 648×, multi-level); a flat taxonomy discards that structure on import. Hierarchy is delivered natively here by structured collections (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#252), with a proven importer precedent (`KbEntryCreator`) and a proven hierarchical-URL route (`clinical_expertise`). Pre-Launch the content is test-import-purged, so a hard replace is free now and costly after Launch.

**Rejected.** *Keep `post_categories` flat* — discards the 648-edge tree the source carries and the front end surfaces. *Flat category URLs* — re-discard the structure at the URL layer and reintroduce cross-parent slug collisions. *Mount the `/category` prefix to a `pages` entry* (the `clinical_expertise` idiom) — its editable-landing / per-site-prefix benefits don't justify localizing a Categories page across ~28 sites; the literal route prefix (`people` idiom) is config-driven via one YAML value. *App-config-key prefix* — not a native Statamic route seam. *Parallel collection coexisting with the taxonomy* — hedges a live-data risk that doesn't exist pre-Launch and forces a second rename. *Rebuild the archive from scratch* — throws away UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1145's tested presentation and risks the shared `taxonomies/*` partials `post_tags` still uses.

**Companion.** Issue UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#252 (`## Decision update` 2026-07-09) + keystone UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1433 (`## Decision`); downstream UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1434 / UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1435 / UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#220 / UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1144 and `UAMS-Web/wordpress-importer#13`.

## 2026-07-09 — Orphaned-ancestor handling is one universal mechanism, not per-collection wiring (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1429)

**Decision.** A single, collection-agnostic mechanism prevents a **published** entry from being reachable as an orphan page when it has an **unpublished ancestor**, across **all routable + hierarchical** collections (present and future), with a per-collection opt-out flag. It generalizes the `knowledge_bases`-only handling (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#217 redirect, UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1077 CP banner): a global `Statamic\Events\ResponseCreated` listener issues the 302 (nearest published ancestor → collection mount root → site root), and a `Statamic\Events\EntryBlueprintFound` listener injects the CP orphan banner into descendant blueprints — both gated by one predicate (non-empty `route` **and** a structure tree). `knowledge_bases` migrates onto it (behavior preserved); `clinical_expertise`, `clinical_locations`, and `pages` are covered automatically; non-routable structured collections (`facilities`, `organizations`, `schema_objects`) and flat collections are excluded by the predicate.

**Why.** An orphaned published page is never desirable, so universal coverage is the correct default — future collections are covered *structurally* rather than by a "remember to wire it" reminder, and four bespoke copies of one behavior (KB + the three affected collections) collapse to one, with no drift. Statamic v6's global `ResponseCreated` / `EntryBlueprintFound` hooks make the universal form feasible without per-collection wiring.

**Rejected.** *Per-collection wiring + a Claude rule* (wire each collection via a shared core, add the banner to each blueprint, and add a `.claude/rules` rule so future collections get wired) — explicit and lower-risk per step, but repeats the wiring and leans on the rule being followed every time, a weaker guarantee than automatic coverage; the rule was consequently **not** created. *A separate `pages` ticket blocked by the clinical-collection work* — folded into the universal mechanism instead. *Hybrid (universal redirect, per-collection banner)* — the banner universalizes cleanly via `EntryBlueprintFound`, so the split added complexity for little gain. *Hard-404 for broken chains* — already rejected on UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#217 (redirect preserves SEO/UX).

**Companion.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1429 `## Decision` (per-ticket record + build spec). Generalizes UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#217 / UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1077 — `app/Services/KnowledgeBase/KnowledgeBaseComputedFieldsService.php` (ancestor walk to reuse), `app/Fieldtypes/KbOrphanBanner.php` (banner to generalize).

## 2026-07-09 — UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1064 delivers the Special-Interest data model; the JSON-LD emission belongs to UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1067 (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1064, UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1067)

**Decision.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1064 ("lean `special_interests` fields") delivers only the **data model** — the `special_interests` field on Provider + Location, the specialty-scoped `EntrySaving` save guard (`ValidateSpecialInterestsScope`), and the computed inverse on the C/T collections. The **schema.org emission** (provider `special_interests` → Physician `knowsAbout`; location `special_interests` → `availableService`) is delivered by **UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1067** ("Wire JSON-LD for the D9 transitive, Special-Interest, and `about`/`mainEntity` relationships"), which already specifies it as a **`Role`-wrapped node** with `@type` gating and lists building the `special_interests` fields/inverse as *out of its* scope. UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1064's original schema ACs — which duplicated UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1067 — are retired to UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1067.

**Why.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1067 is the dedicated JSON-LD ticket and owns the whole D9 emission holistically (transitive `knowsAbout`/`availableService`, Special-Interest, `relevantSpecialty`, `about`/`mainEntity`) as Role-wrapped nodes. Emitting the SI legs from UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1064 as a simpler direct mapping would duplicate work UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1067 must re-shape, and would force UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1064 to make the location-`@type` → `MedicalClinic`/`Hospital` modeling decision (the location node is currently `MedicalOrganization`+`LocalBusiness`, on which `availableService` is invalid — verified via `Spatie\SchemaOrg` reflection: `MedicalClinic`/`Hospital` support it, `MedicalOrganization` does not) that UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1067 owns. Clean seam: **UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1064 = data (single-authored field + derived inverse); UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1067 = schema (render the derived model).** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1067 is blocked-by UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1064.

**Rejected.** *Emit the SI schema from UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1064* (its literal original ACs) — duplicates UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1067, forces a premature location-`@type` decision, and ships a direct mapping UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1067 would re-wrap as a `Role` node. Surfaced during the UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1064 build when the schema wiring was found to live in `content/` `schema_objects` property templates and to overlap UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1067's explicit scope.

**Companion.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1064 issue body reconciled (schema AC → UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1067, built ACs ticked, boundary Decision added); UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1067 unchanged (already owns it). The location `@type` gating (via `location_types`: the `hospital` term vs the clinic terms) is a UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1067 modeling step.

## 2026-07-09 — Provider/Location ↔ Condition/Service edges are computed/derived, not stored `stillat` sync (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1063, UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1064)

**Decision.** The Provider (`people`) and Location (`clinical_locations`) relationships to Conditions/Services are **read-time computed**, not stored bidirectional `stillat/relationships` sync:
- **UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1063** — a Provider/Location's *implied* services & conditions are computed by flat `expertise` overlap (`implied_services` / `implied_conditions` via `ImpliedOntologyComputedValues` + `ImpliedOntologyCollectionComputedsRegistrar`, Blink-memoized, published-only, empty-specialty → empty set). The direct `clinical_providers` / `clinical_locations` fields on the C/T blueprints are **soft-deprecated** (moved to a `deprecated_soon` tab, relabeled "(Deprecated — Inferred Transitively)", **data preserved** — not prohibited-cleared), so they no longer drive default rendering.
- **UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1064** — the Special-Interest *inverse* (the Providers/Locations that flagged a C/T as a special interest) is likewise a **computed** `SpecialInterestInverseComputedsRegistrar`, authored only on the Provider/Location `special_interests` side; the C/T side is derived. The **bidirectional-sync idea is dropped.**

**Why.** This whole edge is *derived from a single authored anchor* — the shared `expertise` specialties (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1063) or the one-sided `special_interests` field (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1064) — so there is no genuine second authoring side to keep in sync. Computed gives zero drift and zero duplication by construction, with no `relate:fill` backfill and no legacy-data reconciliation. That differs deliberately from the Specialty-anchored M2M ontology edges (2026-07-08 entry below), which are genuinely double-authored and so use stored `stillat` sync — a different edge shape, not a contradiction.

**Rejected.** *Stored `stillat` `manyToMany` for the SI inverse (reuse the deprecated C/T fields, or add reciprocal `special_interest_*` fields)* — matches the other ontology edges but invents a second authoring side for a one-sided fact, un-deprecates fields UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1063 just deprecated, and forces reconciling preserved legacy data. *Prohibited-clear deprecation of the direct C/T fields (the sibling "Legacy Import — Migrate, Then Clear" pattern)* — these fields are inference-*superseded*, not re-classified, so their data is preserved for reference/audit rather than force-migrated (user-chosen: soft-preserve). *Descendant-walk overlap (walk the specialty tree for implied sets)* — flat overlap (Fork H) is sufficient and cheaper.

**Companion.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1063 (`app/Services/ComputedFields/Ontology/ImpliedOntologyComputedValues.php`, `…/Registration/ImpliedOntologyCollectionComputedsRegistrar.php`) shipped on `wave/aoe-transitive`; UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1064 `## Decision` (SI-inverse = computed, to build). Supersedes the 2026-07-08 note on UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1064 that reconsidered stored-sync. Diverges by design from the 2026-07-08 ontology-M2M stored-sync entry below (different edge shape — single- vs double-authored). Forks F/G/H in [`docs/ontology-update/aoe-d9-relationship-ontology-mapping.md`](ontology-update/aoe-d9-relationship-ontology-mapping.md).

## 2026-07-10 — Sitemap the `post_categories` / `post_tags` term URLs via the global-taxonomies path, now their archives are public (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1346)

**Decision.** The taxonomy-term sitemap deferred on 2026-07-08 (below) is now delivered: `config/sitemapamic.php` `globals.taxonomies` emits `post_categories` and `post_tags` term URLs — the two taxonomies whose public, routable `{handle}/show` term-archive views shipped with UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1145/UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1410 (auto-routed by Statamic 6 at `/post-categories/{slug}` and `/post-tags/{slug}`). `defaults.pages.includeTaxonomies` stays `false`. A present-vs-absent test (`tests/Feature/Seo/SitemapTaxonomyTermsTest.php`) covers an enabled taxonomy emitting real term `<loc>`s and a disabled one (`subject_matter`) producing no loader at all.

**Why.** The 2026-07-08 deferral's gate ("no routable taxonomy term page exists yet") is lifted — UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1410 created the archive views, and Statamic auto-routes the terms, so their `<loc>`s resolve to real published pages, satisfying the ticket's own "not 404s" criterion. The **global-taxonomies** path is the right mechanism, not `includeTaxonomies`: that flag only emits terms of a taxonomy *attached to a sitemapped collection*, and the sole sitemapped collection (`pages`) has none attached — the term taxonomies live on `posts`, which is out of the pages-only entry scope. Sitemapamic self-guards each global taxonomy on `view()->exists('{handle}/show')`, so a non-routable taxonomy emits nothing even if listed.

**Rejected.** *Enable `includeTaxonomies` on `pages`* — `pages` has no attached taxonomies, so it emits nothing; the term taxonomies are on `posts`. *List every taxonomy in `globals.taxonomies` and lean on the view guard* — self-guarded but misleading; only the two with public archives are listed, to keep intent explicit. *Defer `post_categories` to its UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1433 collection migration* — would gut UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1346's stated goal (it is gated on UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1145 = the `post_categories` archive); the flat-taxonomy archive is the live, routable page today. **Caveat:** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1433 migrates `post_categories` from this flat taxonomy to a structured collection with hierarchical `/category/...` URLs; its archive rework (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1434) owns moving `post_categories` off the global-taxonomies path when that lands — this config is the correct interim state. Supersedes the UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1346 clause of the 2026-07-08 entry below.

**Companion.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1346 issue `## Decision`; `config/sitemapamic.php` `globals.taxonomies`; `tests/Feature/Seo/SitemapTaxonomyTermsTest.php`. Builds on UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1326 (the app sitemap) and UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1145/UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1410 (the public term archives); hands the `post_categories` follow-through to UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1433/UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1434.

## 2026-07-10 — Special Interest emits as a `Role`-wrapped node, subtracted from the plain transitive set (not duplicated) (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1067 slice 5)

**Decision.** The provider `special_interest_*` fields (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1064) emit as a `Role` node (`roleName: "Special Interest"`, bare `@type: Role`) feeding Physician `knowsAbout`, and the location's as a `Role` feeding `availableService`. The plain-transitive legs are **subtracted** from: slices 3/4 now read new overlap-**minus**-override computeds (`transitive_services` / `transitive_conditions`) instead of the override-**unioned** `implied_*`, so a Condition/Service that is both overlap-implied and a hand-picked special interest is emitted **only** inside the `Role` — never also in the bulk list. **Rejected:** the additive wrapper (keep `implied_*` on the plain legs and *also* emit the `Role`), which double-lists special-interest items.

**Why.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1064 frames Special Interest as the retained *explicit emphasis* that survives D9's severance of the direct C/T↔Provider/Location edges — a distinct signal, not part of the bulk transitive competence. Listing an item once, in the category carrying the stronger signal (the `Role`), is the truer graph and the most faithful reading of AC #4 ("distinguishable from the plain transitive edges"). Cost is bounded: the overlap set was already computed internally, so `transitive_*` is a registrar addition, not new logic.

**Mechanics that shaped the build.** The `Role` node is emitted via `{{ schema_template_node id="…" }}` from the provider/location node against the subject entity; its own `knowsAbout`/`availableService` reads the raw `special_interest_*` fields. An empty special interest would otherwise emit a bare `{@type: Role, roleName}` — the raw relationship fields are **not** in the property Antlers context (`{{ if special_interest_* }}` can't see them), so a scalar `has_special_interest` computed was added purely to gate the wrapper. Location `special_interest` **conditions** stay unemitted (no Condition→Location predicate — the standing UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1067 residual); the location `Role` feeds `availableService` from services only.

**Companion.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1067 issue `## Decision`; `app/Services/ComputedFields/Ontology/ImpliedOntologyComputedValues.php` (+ `…/Registration/ImpliedOntologyCollectionComputedsRegistrar.php`); content nodes `special-interest-role-provider-knowsabout.md`, `special-interest-role-location-availableservice.md`, and the rewired `people`/`location` business nodes. Builds on UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1063 (`implied_*`) and UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1064 (the `special_interest_*` fields).

## 2026-07-11 — Find-a-Doc locations block ships the card directory now; live faceting lands with UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1242 (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#63)

**Decision.** Build the Find-a-Doc Locations page-builder block as a live `clinical_locations` card directory — reusing the shared `clinical_locations/_card` (extended with a 3:4 `_no-image-available` fallback and a compact address block) via a new shared `clinical_locations/_list_body` (empty state + card grid + pagination) that both the block and the UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#574 collection index render. The region / location-type filter UI is populated from the `location_types` / `regions` taxonomies but gated behind `$fad_locations_filter_implemented = false`, exactly as the providers block (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#64) gates its body: the live reader-facing faceting engine is the shared AJAX facet contract (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1242), built on the provider block first and sequenced after this wave. Each card carries a robust `data-id` facet anchor; the region / location-type facet `data-*` are UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1242's (it verifies the augmented term shapes against real content).

**Why.** The FaD directory faceting is fixed as ONE shared custom `fetch()` → `@alpinejs/morph` engine reused across all directories, with a duplicate interim facet layer explicitly rejected (the UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1242 decision above). UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1242 is sequenced *after* UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#63 (Group ZZ, blocked on the T→U chain), so UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#63 cannot consume an engine that lands later — it ships the presentation layer (the card directory, genuine value over the prior `{{ title }}` stub) now, gated for the behavior layer, mirroring how the provider block ships gated card markup awaiting UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#919/UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1242. The region/location-type card `data-*` are deferred because the augmented shapes of `regions` (a `max_items:1` terms field) and `location_types` (a custom `terms_search_index`) can't be verified against real content — both collections are empty today — and a term-loop over an un-augmented value silently falls through to the entry slug, so shipping it unverified is worse than deferring it to the ticket that can verify it.

**Rejected.** *UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#63 builds its own location faceting engine now* — duplicates the shared engine UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1242 supersedes (the interim-facet layer UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1242 rejects); JS is hard to test hermetically. *Card listing only, defer all filter markup* — drops the filter contract this option keeps. *Emit the region/location-type card `data-*` now* — unverifiable augmented shapes with 0 live entries; the term-loop falls through to the entry slug.

**Companion.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#63 issue `## Decision`; `resources/views/clinical_locations/{_card,_list_body,_listing}.antlers.html`, `resources/views/page_builder/_uamsweb_block_fad_locations.antlers.html`, `resources/views/page_builder/components/{_block_fad_filter,_block_fad_ontology_list}.antlers.html`. Downstream: UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1242 reuses `_list_body` + the card `data-id` + the populated filter selects. Sibling UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#64 (provider card).

## 2026-07-11 — Marketing-landing nav suppression: chrome-on default + opt-in *hide* toggles, not the literal strip-by-default (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1173)

**Decision.** Build the per-page navigation control the Jira ticket wanted, but with the polarity inverted (Option B): two `true_false` fields on the marketing-landing settings (`uamsweb_header_selector_landing` → the `header_marketing_landing_page` group) that let an editor **opt in to hiding** the primary-nav and resource-nav regions, **both defaulting off** so every page renders exactly as it does today. The conditional lives in the global `resources/views/layout/header/_header.antlers.html`, gated to fire only when the entry's `title_options == 'marketing-landing-page'` — no other page type is touched. A test covers the toggle combinations (each region appears/disappears per its control) and the default (full chrome with no editor action).

**Why.** The ticket's premise was false — nothing strips the nav today; the global header renders both navs unconditionally on every page — so there is no "restore" to build, only a net-new suppression capability, and its default polarity is a free choice. Inverting the polarity delivers the same editorial control the Jira author actually wanted (distraction-free campaign layout) while changing **no existing page** and giving the **safe failure mode**: an editor who forgets the toggle ships full chrome (the status quo), never a campaign page stripped of navigation. Build cost is low — two conditionals wrapping existing header includes, scoped to the marketing-landing surface via the entry's `title_options` (which rides the entry cascade and is readable in the global header partial). The capability is cheap and safe enough that offering it default-off is a lower-regret hedge than closing and relitigating later.

**Rejected.** *Option A — strip by default + opt-in restore toggles* (the literal legacy wording): matches Jira fidelity but silently removes nav from every existing marketing-landing page on deploy and has an unsafe failure mode (forget the toggle → campaign page with no nav); a bad trade for wording that was itself based on a misread of what the template does. *Decline & close*: correct only if no campaign page would ever want reduced chrome — but given B's near-zero cost/risk, building it default-off beats closing and reopening. *A second physical landing layout file*: rejected as pinned — the single `layout.antlers.html` stays single; the toggle is a scoped conditional, not a layout fork.

**Residual (folded into the build, not this decision).** "Resource nav" is coarse — hiding it drops MyChart + utility links + search together; finer-grained control is a separate follow-up. The `#site-nav-primary` skip-link must be suppressed alongside a hidden primary nav (a11y). Breadcrumbs stay unless a third toggle is later requested. No stakeholder has *confirmed* demand for distraction-free chrome; B only makes offering it cheap/safe, it doesn't prove usage.

**Companion.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1173 issue `## Decision` (label flipped `hitl`/`decision-fork` → `afk` in the same change); driven per the [`design-decision-forks`](../.claude/rules/design-decision-forks.md) rule. Unrelated to the in-`<main>` marketing-landing hero-title style (`_marketing-landing-page.antlers.html`, whose instruction copy is #1241). Lands as a clean `afk` build under Group HH / a follow-up wave.

---

## 2026-07-11 — Retired WordPress condition/treatment URLs 301-redirect to their assigned Area of Expertise (#1441)

**Decision.** At launch, retired WordPress condition/treatment URLs 301-redirect to the **Area-of-Expertise entry the condition/treatment is assigned to** — the single `/conditions/{slug}` and `/treatments/{slug}` pages to their primary AoE, and the `/conditions` / `/treatments` archives to the `/expertise` index. The per-slug C/T→AoE map is **derived from the existing C/T→expertise ontology** (not hand-built), with a defined tie-break when several AoEs share a condition. The UTM scheme is ported verbatim (FD20-3267); the redirect engine is out of scope (`wordpress-importer#133`).

**Why.** The ticket's goal is preserving SEO / inbound-link equity now that C/T are data with no pages (D9). Redirecting to the topically-matched AoE keeps per-condition relevance and inbound-link value, and because the mapping is derivable from the ontology already in the content, it is not a manual maintenance burden.

**Rejected.** *`/expertise` index for everything* — one target, no mapping, but coarse and dilutes the SEO signal onto a generic hub. *Site-search results* (`/search?q=`) — always resolves (the endpoint now exists per UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#815/UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#56) but reads as a soft-404 to search engines and forfeits most equity. *`410 Gone`* — cleanest de-index but forfeits ALL inbound equity and breaks existing links, contradicting the SEO-preservation goal.

**Companion.** #1441 issue `## Decision`; the C/T→expertise ontology (`app/Scopes/OntologyOverlap*` / the expertise relationship), `resources/views/clinical_expertise/index.antlers.html`. **Build-order:** blocked_by `wordpress-importer#133` (the redirect mechanism) — the target is decided; the engine to apply it lands with UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#133.

## 2026-07-11 — FaD directory facet layer: native PHP facet counts + Alpine morph, no package or hosted engine (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1242, UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#570, UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#63)

**Decision.** Build the Find-a-Doc directory faceting (providers/locations/expertise/resources) with **zero new packages and no hosted search engine**, per UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1242's custom-AJAX-facet-over-query-scopes plan. Facet **filtering** = the existing `app/Scopes/OntologyOverlap*` query scopes via the collection tag `query_scope`. Facet **counts** (FacetWP's signature feature) = computed in PHP with `Illuminate\Support\Collection::countBy()`/`groupBy()` over the scoped result set, recomputed per filter state (exclude-self for a live distribution). **AJAX refresh** = `fetch()` → `@alpinejs/morph` (already installed, `^3.14.8`) — no new dependency. **Name search** = the UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#815 `local` search driver. A-Z alpha, sort, pagination, result count, reset, and shareable URL state are custom Alpine + native query.

**Why.** No Statamic addon delivers live facet counts over flat-file (Stache) entries on an Alpine front end without a hosted engine; the two that do (croox Meilisearch-extendable, Rad Pack Typesense) reopen UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#815. But facet counts are just a `countBy`/`groupBy` aggregation, trivially cheap at directory scale (thousands of entries), so an engine's real value (typo tolerance / relevance at large scale) is marginal here. Scout is Eloquent-only (entries are flat-file); Reach Livewire Filters drags Livewire onto an Alpine-only front end and ships no counts.

**Optional sugar (no UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#815 impact, adopt only if wanted).** `Tom Select` (Apache-2.0) for accessible searchable multi-select on long facet lists (e.g. specialties); `daun/statamic-loupe` (MIT, local SQLite) as a drop-in typo-tolerant upgrade to the `local` name-search driver — no server. `@imacrayon/alpine-ajax` is thin declarative sugar over morph (optional).

**Rejected / escape hatch.** `laravel/scout` + `spatie/laravel-query-builder` (Eloquent-only, N/A to flat-file); `reach/statamic-livewire-filters` (Livewire conflict, no counts, no A-Z); Algolia InstantSearch + Meilisearch/Typesense adapters (need a hosted engine, alien widget model). **Escape hatch:** if the corpus grows ~10x, per-option counting becomes a measured bottleneck, or engine-grade typo tolerance is needed for the directories, adopt **`croox/statamic-meilisearch-extendable`** (the one addon surfacing `facetDistribution` to Antlers) via Statamic's OWN Meilisearch driver — scoped to the FaD indexes only, NOT sitewide search, consciously reopening UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#815 for the directories.

**Companion.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1242 (facet contract), UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#570 (provider directory), UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#63 (locations), UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#61 (resources); `app/Scopes/OntologyOverlap*`, `@alpinejs/morph` (installed), `config/statamic/search.php`. Composes with UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#815 (local site search, unchanged).

## 2026-07-11 — Provider directory is a clinical-provider-eligible slice of the `people` index, at full WP facet parity (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#570)

**Decision.** Build the Find-a-Doc provider directory (the equivalent of the legacy WP `provider` CPT archive at `/provider/`) as a **specific index of the `people` collection, filtered to entries eligible for a clinical-provider profile** — consolidating with the people index (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#568), NOT a separate `clinical_providers` index. Mount the eligible-people slice to a "Find a Doctor" landing (the `clinical_locations`/`clinical_expertise` mount+route pattern). Reuse the shared provider card (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#64), eligibility filter (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#919), and AJAX facet contract (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1242) the gated-off block already depends on — no duplicated facet layer. Target **full WP facet parity**: A-Z alpha, name search, Area of Expertise, special-interest conditions, treatments, location, region, gender, language, patient_types, primary_care; the provider card (headshot + `_no-image-available_3-4` fallback, name + clinical title, excerpt, "Full Profile" CTA, related locations); result count, sort, pagination, 911 disclaimer, empty state; and the NRC/DocScores **ratings** display.

**Why.** `clinical_providers` has no route/mount and is a sparse Workday/Epic sync record being retired (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1067/#803); `people` is the canonical routable provider identity, and the FaD block's `providers` picker already targets it — so the directory is the provider-eligible slice of the people index. Reusing the UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#64/UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#919/UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1242 trio avoids duplicating a facet layer UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1242 supersedes. Full parity because the WP filters (gender/language/patient-type/primary-care) are real patient wayfinding, not chrome.

**Rejected.** *A separate directory over `clinical_providers`* (non-routable, retired). *A page carrying the curated `fad-providers` block* (that's a hand-picked subset, not the archive). *A server-side-first interim facet layer* (duplicates UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1242). *Reduced MVP facet parity* (user chose full parity).

**Residual / dependencies.** The NRC/DocScores **ratings** display needs an external ratings API (cf. #1290 Press Ganey) — spin off as a follow-up, not a blocker for the rest. The directory is downstream of the UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#64/UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#919/UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1242 trio (reuses them, lands when they do). Sibling archives location/expertise/clinical-resource (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#572/UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#574/UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#576) are being coverage-checked against the WP plugin.

**Companion.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#570 issue `## Decision`; consolidates with UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#568; `content/collections/people.yaml` (mount the eligible slice), `resources/fieldsets/uamsweb_block_fad_providers.yaml` (facet fields), `app/Scopes/OntologyOverlapExpertise.php` + `OntologyOverlapSpecialInterests.php` + UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#919 eligibility. WP source: `UAMSWP-Find-a-Doc/templates/archive-physicians.php`.

## 2026-07-11 — KB article ratings: atomic app-owned counter store, not entry writes (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#286)

**Decision.** Store like/dislike tallies in an app-owned store (a DB table keyed by entry id, or an atomic cache counter) mutated via the explicit AJAX endpoint; expose `net = likes − dislikes` (default 0) to the topic sort via a computed value. Not on the entry YAML. Share one generic engagement-counter design with UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#287 (views).

**Why.** Entries live in a git submodule, so writing a counter onto the entry means per-vote YAML writes → Stache re-index, git churn, save contention, and lost-update races — unacceptable for a vote button. A sideband store is concurrency-safe, touches no content, and the sort reads it via a computed value.

**Rejected.** *Write counts onto the entry via `Entry::save()`* — simplest + native sort field but races on the YAML write and invalidates Stache/static cache per vote. *Hybrid* (atomic store denormalized back onto the entry) — native sort field but eventual-consistency lag + sync machinery not worth it.

**Companion.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#286 issue `## Decision`; UI UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#281 + topics block UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#269 (both closed) consume the count. Shares the engagement-counter store with UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#287.

## 2026-07-11 — KB view counts: sideband store surfaced through the existing `view_count` computed (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#287)

**Decision.** Hold the article view count in an app-owned DB table keyed by entry id, incremented via a throttled endpoint (per-session throttle + bot filtering, required), and surface it through the `article` blueprint's EXISTING `view_count` computed field. Not on the entry YAML. Same engagement-counter store as UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#286.

**Why.** The blueprint already declares `view_count` as `visibility: computed`, so a sideband store surfaced through the computed provider matches the declared shape and avoids all content writes; the required throttle + bot filter already caps write rate, so a throttled DB upsert per view suffices without a cache-flush pipeline.

**Rejected.** *Write the count onto the entry YAML* — conflicts with the `computed` declaration and churns the git submodule per view. *Atomic cache/Redis counter + periodic flush* — cheapest per-write but a flush job + cache dependency, over-engineered for KB view volume (add later behind the same computed field if it spikes).

**Companion.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#287 issue `## Decision`; `resources/blueprints/collections/knowledge_bases/article.yaml` (`view_count` computed), `app/Entries/KnowledgeBaseEntry.php` (add the provider). Shares the store with UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#286; most-viewed block/sort UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#269 (closed).

## 2026-07-11 — Provider profile reuses extracted `people/show` components over the linked `person` (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#571)

**Decision.** Structure `clinical_providers/show.antlers.html` by resolving the provider's `person` relationship and rendering the profile head (portrait/name/credentials/bio) via shared partials factored out of `people/show`, layering the FaD-only sections (specialties/conditions/treatments/locations/scheduling CTA) on top. Reuse the `_no-image-available_3-4` portrait fallback; schema.org `Physician`/`Person` is already wired in `clinical_providers.yaml`.

**Why.** `provider.yaml`'s `person` relationship makes a provider a thin FaD wrapper (Workday/NPI/Epic IDs) whose profile content lives on the linked `people` entry — which already has a 228-line `people/show` + components. Reusing extracted partials is DRY, matches the data model, and eases the eventual #803 merge of `clinical_providers` into `people`.

**Rejected.** *Bespoke standalone template* — duplicates the portrait/name/credentials/bio markup `people/show` already implements. *Delegate wholesale to `people/show`* — the FaD-specific sections + health-site context don't belong there and it couples two collections with different blueprints/sites.

**Companion.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#571 issue `## Decision`; `resources/views/clinical_providers/show.antlers.html` (4-line stub), `resources/views/people/show.antlers.html` + `people/components/`, `resources/blueprints/collections/clinical_providers/provider.yaml`. Cost: extract partials without regressing `people/show`. Sibling UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#570 (provider index) pending WP-plugin archive investigation.

## 2026-07-11 — `knowledge_bases` adopts the universal orphaned-ancestor outcomes (retiring KB's bespoke path) (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1455)

**Decision.** Fold `knowledge_bases` onto the universal `RedirectOrphanedDescendants` + `AppendOrphanBanner` resolver with **no** per-collection branching, retiring KB's bespoke pieces (`{{ %redirect }}`, `kb_orphaned_redirect_url`, `kb_orphan_banner`, `KbOrphanBanner` fieldtype + Vue). The sitemap-inclusion gate (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1403) now sources its orphaned signal from the universal `OrphanedAncestorResolver::resolve()` instead of the retired KB computed.

**The anticipated "mount root" delta does not occur.** The decision originally accepted one delta (an unpublished KB root redirecting to a "KB mount root" instead of the site root). Verification against the code disproves its premise: `knowledge_bases` carries **no collection-level `mount`** (it uses per-entry mounting via the `mount_parent` field + the `{mount_prefix}` route token, resolved live in `App\Entries\KnowledgeBaseEntry`), so `Collection::mount()` is null and `OrphanedAncestorResolver::mountRootUrl()` returns null. An unpublished KB root therefore falls through to the **site root** — byte-for-byte the retired KB policy.

**KB adopts the universal nearest-live-ancestor policy; two narrow deltas follow, both accepted.** The retired KB policy sent *every* orphaned descendant of a published KB root to the **KB root URL**; the universal policy sends it to the **nearest live ancestor** above the highest unpublished break. These coincide for the common shapes — an unpublished KB root (→ site root) and an unpublished topic that is a *direct child* of the root (nearest live ancestor = the root → KB root URL) — so those outcomes are unchanged. **(1)** They diverge only when an unpublished topic sits **below a published subtopic** (a depth ≥ 3 hierarchy): the redirect then lands on that intermediate published subtopic's URL rather than the KB root — a more precise target. **(2)** Separately, because the orphaned redirect moves from a render-time template `%redirect` (which aborted first) to a post-render `ResponseCreated` listener (which only rewrites a 2xx), an **empty** topic (no published descendant article) that is *also* orphaned by an unpublished topic now hits the retained UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#216 empty-topic `%redirect` first → **site root**, where the old code reached the orphaned redirect → published KB root URL. Both deltas still redirect the visitor away; **no orphan is left reachable.** Covered by `KnowledgeBasesUniversalFoldTest`.

**Why.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1429 exists to make this *one* mechanism; a per-collection policy hook would reintroduce exactly the branching it set out to eliminate, into the code meant to be unified. The two deltas above are the accepted consequences of adopting the universal nearest-live-ancestor rule — which is the whole point of UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1429 — and both are more-precise, redirect-away outcomes.

**Rejected.** *Extend the universal resolver with a per-collection policy hook* reproducing KB's root-specific outcomes byte-for-byte — undercuts UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1429's single-mechanism goal and adds ongoing per-collection policy maintenance to the unified resolver, to preserve outcomes (KB-root-for-every-break; empty-topic-to-KB-root) that are no better than the universal ones.

**Companion.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1455 issue `## Decision`; `app/Services/OrphanedAncestors/OrphanedAncestorResolver.php`, `app/Listeners/RedirectOrphanedDescendants.php`, `app/Listeners/AppendOrphanBanner.php`, `config/orphaned_ancestors.php`, `app/Services/ComputedFields/Registration/KnowledgeBaseCollectionComputedsRegistrar.php` (sitemap gate re-point); retires `app/Fieldtypes/KbOrphanBanner.php` + KB computed/blueprint/template pieces. Builds on UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1429.

## 2026-07-11 — Map legacy WordPress image credits into the asset `creator` field on import (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1350)

**Decision.** Add an importer mapping of `_media_credit` (with EXIF `image_meta.credit` as fallback) → the Statamic asset `creator` field, in the importer asset-metadata seam (`MetadataSetter.php`). The display path (`creator` → photo-credit span) is unchanged.

**Why.** This is a one-time migration; photo credits that lived only in WP meta/EXIF can carry attribution/licensing obligations that can't be cheaply recovered post-import. The mapping is a small `MetadataSetter` addition; EXIF is fallback-only (behind the explicit `_media_credit`), so noise risk is low, and the display already guards on `creator` presence (a blank credit is a harmless no-op).

**Rejected.** *Intentional drop* (structured `creator` as sole source of truth) — zero importer work and a clean structured-only slate, but any credit that lived solely in `_media_credit`/EXIF is silently lost on migration, unrecoverable without a re-import.

**Companion.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1350 issue `## Decision`; `vendor/uams-web/wordpress-importer/src/Services/WordPressImport/Assets/MetadataSetter.php`, `docs/assets-metadata.md`.

## 2026-07-11 — Areas of Expertise FaD block: list-only cards, no facets (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#62)

**Decision.** Complete the net-new `_uamsweb_block_fad_expertise` block as list-only cards (featured image + title + short description → the AoE page), with no reader facets — matching the current `_block_fad_filter` config (all four expertise facet switches resolve to `false`). Ship a real visible block (clinical_expertise is a live render surface via Wave 71).

**Why.** clinical_expertise renders live, so a visible block beats a stub. Region-filtering a specialty list (specialties span regions) is marginal value, and expertise is already pinned to no facets — a facet would only add UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1242 coupling.

**Rejected.** *Filterable grid with a Region facet wired to UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1242* — richer but marginal value for a specialty list and couples UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#62 to UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1242's contract (add later if product asks). *Guarded parity stub* (`$fad_expertise_implemented = false`) — family-consistent with the stubbed providers block but ships nothing visible despite the live surface.

**Companion.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#62 issue `## Decision`; `resources/views/page_builder/_uamsweb_block_fad_expertise.antlers.html`, `components/_block_fad_filter.antlers.html`. UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1242 owns the AJAX facet mechanism; sibling anchors UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#63/UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#64 afk, parallel UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#61.

## 2026-07-11 — Clinical Resources FaD block: uniform card + the shared UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1242 AJAX facet (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#61)

**Decision.** Complete the `_uamsweb_block_fad_clinical_resources` block with one uniform card partial for all 5 `clinical_resources` subtypes (title/description/link) and server-side facet-option population, reusing the shared AJAX facet/`data-*` mechanism from the providers block (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1242, afk). **Rejected.** *Subtype-aware cards* (per-subtype affordances — audio duration, video thumbnail, document download, article read) — richest UX honoring the split but a card partial per subtype (runner-up, a later enhancement). *Self-contained server-side query-param filtering* — ships standalone but diverges from the siblings' client-side facet UX and full-reloads on every change.
