## 2026-07-16 — `news` site architecture: standard shell + the already-built post model; newsroom home + department hub share one parameterized magazine core (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1609)

**Decision.** The `news` site — the central newsroom (`subject: major_entity`; the source every Departmental News Hub reads from) — runs the **standard Statamic shell**, not a bespoke newsroom shell, retiring the WordPress "The Olympian" / `uams-2016` child theme. Grounding against the live blueprints showed the `posts` model **already absorbs** most of the WordPress newsroom surplus, so the spike is largely an accounting. Per-fork verdicts:
- **Home page & section pages** — the newsroom home is the **`category` / all / no-zones configuration of a shared, parameterized news-magazine core**: a sticky-first **lead** → N **sections** with **page-wide, earlier-section-wins accumulator dedup** (extending `NewsSyndicationQueryService` with an `exclude` / `not-id` param) → a bottom **CTA**, parameterized by **axis** (`category` | blueprint-`type`), a **polymorphic `scope`** (all | one or a set of `organizations` entries | a `post_categories` entry | a blueprint-`type` | an `audio_series` / `video_series` taxonomy term), and **zones** (none | department hero/people/Action-Path). The newsroom's non-home **section pages are a heterogeneous mix, keyed on whatever the page is actually about** — `scope=org` for pages whose category maps to `organizations` via the decided `category→organizations` crosswalk (`UAMS-Web/wordpress-importer#438`), with "University News" keying on a **union of college `organizations` entries** (no single shared-parent org); `scope=a post_categories entry` for topical sections ("Patient Stories"); `scope=the news_release blueprint` for the News Releases archive; and `scope=an audio_series term` for a branded show — "Here's to Your Health" is an `audio_series` term (not a `post_categories`-promoted publication), with the same series-term scoping wanted for `video_series`. Org-keyed section pages reuse the hub's org-filter machinery (post→`organizations` UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1598 + the crosswalk, generalized to accept a **set** of orgs) minus the department zones; the **Departmental News Hub** (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1602, on department *sites*) is that same `scope=org` path *with* department zones. One core serves the home, the hubs, and every section page; which key each section page takes (+ routing) is UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1600's to finalize. **UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1158 is redefined as the shared core** (its recorded 2026-07-06 accumulator-dedup + `exclude`-param decision *is* the core's dedup, generalized by axis/scope/zones); **UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1602 is re-scoped to consume the core** (adopting the accumulator dedup in place of its narrower lead↔type offset; re-estimate); the **newsroom-home** build (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1612, `scope=all`) is **not** coupled to the org infrastructure, though the org-keyed section pages (in UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1600's scope) do depend on post→`organizations` UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1598 + the crosswalk.
- **Primary category** (cards / breadcrumbs) — the **first selected `post_categories` entry wins** (the field is order-preserving); no new field. **New importer work required** (`UAMS-Web/wordpress-importer#441`): the importer takes WXR document order today with no Yoast handling — it must read `_yoast_wpseo_primary_category` and reorder categories primary-first (the meta is present in the news export, so it is feasible). Yoast's runtime primary-term machinery is not ported.
- **External integrations** — **preserve** the `calendar.uams.edu` events embed (a separate external system, not migrated) and the YouTube playlist (via YouTube-Lyte, UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#224); **drop** the "UAMS In the News" `newsdataservice.com` media-mentions scroller; **drop** the internal `[events_list]` "Academic & Continuing Education Events" zone (the eventpost plugin — no events collection exists; the external calendar embed covers upcoming events; an internal events model is a separate track if ever wanted).
- **Site settings / boilerplate** — **already built**: the per-post `boilerplate` term, else the site default `uams_multisite_settings.boilerplate` (the field lives in the `post_settings` tab but the data path is the flat `boilerplate` key), honored by the post templates and `PostsReadingMetricsService`. WordPress's "Exclude Categories" listing filter is **dropped** as obsolete under per-site `posts` (`propagate: false`).
- **`convert` post status** — the news export carries ~673 `wp:status=convert` ("To Be Converted") items; **import them as drafts** (the importer's current fall-through + warning) — non-destructive, preserved for later human triage. Blesses the current behavior explicitly rather than by omission.

**Already built (no action), verified against the blueprints.** Boilerplate (above); `media_contact` (→ `media_contact_groups`) + `media_contact_query`; the auto-generated `news_release_pdf` (publish-gated by UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#921); `post_categories` as a structured collection (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1433); related posts + CTA; subject matter (`about` / `mentions`); the aspect-ratio featured-image fields + Glide (which absorb the theme's 16:9 `news-full/half/third/quarter` crops — the odd 16:10 `uams_news` crop has no built equivalent); **single-post detail pages** (the theme's `content.php` / `content-post.php` + `sidebar.php` — cover image + caption + `_media_credit` + coauthor byline + per-post boilerplate + the sidebar news-release-PDF button + media-contact widget) map to the built `posts` templates + `news_release_pdf` / `media_contact`. The `sticky_query` **field** exists, but its **importance-ordering engine (#1157) is unbuilt/OPEN** — the lead uses the `sticky_query` boolean + date-desc until #1157 ships. Page-wide cross-section dedup is **new** work (the shared core), not an existing `NewsSyndicationQueryService` capability.

**Delegated.** The news REST API (`fullcontent` / `post-meta-fields` custom `register_rest_field`s) → #1044; the newsroom **section pages** (per-page polymorphic scope key: org-set / `post_categories` / `news_release` blueprint / `audio_series` term) + the bespoke layouts (generic; the `news-release` year→month→day archive; the `heres-to-your-health` show landing) → UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1600 (routing) + UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1434 (archive rework), with one **blocking gap** — neither `post_categories` nor the `audio_series`/`video_series` term blueprints carry a featured-image field, and the here's-to-your-health hero (WP `cat_image` term image) needs one; magazine publications → #1159 (whose cross-section-dedup reference now points at the shared core). AP-style dates are a template date-format convention (trivial).

**Why.** The multisite already models the newsroom as a `posts`-carrying site on the standard shell, and the `posts` blueprints carry the content model — re-porting the theme would duplicate shipped work. The newsroom home and the hub are the same magazine layout *except* for axis/scope/zones **and** dedup strength — the category axis (a post belongs to many categories) needs page-wide earlier-section-wins accumulator dedup, which the hub's type axis (a post is one type) did not; one parameterized core that owns the *stronger* accumulator dedup serves both without duplication and keeps the org-free newsroom home off the hub's org-infra blocker chain. First-selected-primary is deterministic and zero-field. The calendar and media-mentions are external services; only the embed is in question.

**Rejected.** A bespoke newsroom shell (standard shell pinned). **Closing UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1158 as a duplicate of the hub cluster** — an adversarial review refuted the "same engine" equivalence: UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1158's cross-section, category-axis, earlier-section-wins accumulator dedup + `exclude` param does **not** reduce to UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1602's lead↔type *offset* dedup (a post in two categories duplicates across category sections under offset dedup); closing it would drop that capability into an unowned gap and couple the org-free newsroom home to UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1602's UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1598/UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1599/UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1556/UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1557 org blockers. Keeping UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1158 an independent standalone springboard (forgoes the DRY core UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1602 also wants). A dedicated `primary_category` field (heavier; only if display-primary must differ from category order). Keeping the "In the News" scroller (paid external, low value). A per-site listing-exclusion field (obsolete under per-site `posts`). A news-only settings global (boilerplate already on `uams_multisite_settings`). Skipping `convert` items (would discard content flagged mid-conversion).

**Deferred / residual.** Whether the shared core's lead can express WP's mixed-size featured grid; re-estimating UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1602 after it adopts the core; the featured-image (`cat_image`) gap on the category/series term blueprints (→ UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1600 / UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1434); the news REST API consumer disposition (→ #1044); the 16:10 `uams_news` crop equivalent (likely none needed).

**Companion.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1609 issue `## Decision`. Follow-ups — **UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1158 redefined = the shared magazine core**; **UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1612** newsroom-home build (consumes the core, `category`/all/none, blocked by UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1158, not org-coupled); **`UAMS-Web/wordpress-importer#441`** (Yoast primary → category order); **UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1602 re-scoped** to consume the core (blocked by UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1158; re-estimate); comments on #1044 (news REST fields) and UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1600 (`cat_image` gap + the polymorphic section-page scope). Verified against `resources/blueprints/collections/posts/*`, `resources/fieldsets/uamsweb_post_*`, `resources/blueprints/globals/uams_multisite_settings.yaml`, `resources/views/posts/*`, `app/Services/Posts/PostsReadingMetricsService.php`, `NewsSyndicationQueryService.php`, `vendor/uams-web/wordpress-importer/src/.../PostEntryCreator.php`, the series taxonomy blueprints, and the `news-uams` theme — across three adversarial-review passes that corrected the UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1158 equivalence error, the #1157 / importer overstatements, and the section-page scope model.

## 2026-07-16 — Inherent parallel-suite cost: warm + boot dominate; shared-seed the warm (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1537 → UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1581 / UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1582)

**Decision.** Attribution of the inherent `Services --parallel --processes=12` cost (drift-validated, 0.3% boot-bench drift): `stache-warm` 50% + `app-boot` 45% = 95%; per-test churn 1.4%, test-body 3.3%. Fix warm with a shared seed — `test:seed-stache` warms once and snapshots the cache; each paratest worker copies the snapshot into its own per-worker cache dir and skips the forced warm (`tests/CreatesApplication.php`), wired into `ci.yml` + `scripts/local-ci.mjs`. Shipped as UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1581 (`Services` p12 507s → 345s, −32%, all tests passing, isolation preserved). Boot deferred to an isolation-preserving spike (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1582).

**Why.** warm balloons from 21s solo to ~168s under 12-way disk contention (the largest bucket) yet is trivially removable — the pre-run warm just writes the wrong cache dir (the default `data/`, not the per-worker `data/<TEST_TOKEN>/`), so seeding the per-worker dir each worker actually reads is a copy, not a rebuild, with no per-test behavior change and no isolation change (each worker seeds only its own dir → UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1136 preserved). warm is a fixed startup burst, so the seed is a ~32% win on the fast per-probe cycle developers run most; boot is per-test and dominates the less-frequent full-suite wall. Resolves the "attribute first, then decompose" deferral recorded in the UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1573 entry below.

**Rejected.** (a) Churn reduction — the UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1537 plan's other candidate lever; churn is only 1.4%, not worth a change. (b) Reusing the booted app to cut boot — remains rejected per UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1121 (forfeits Laravel per-method isolation, blast radius = whole suite); UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1121's "boot is ~3%" was scoped to the import tests, but the feature-suite 45% does not change the isolation objection, so UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1582 pursues isolation-preserving boot reductions rather than reopening UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1121. (c) A lazy first-worker-builds-the-seed-under-a-lock scheme — rejected as more complex and deadlock-prone than a one-shot pre-suite build step mirroring the existing `route:cache` precedent.

## 2026-07-16 — Departmental News Hub template: enriched news-surface, department-filtered central news via a post→org crosswalk (reverses UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1160) (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1555)

**Decision.** The hub is the department site's **enriched news-surface** — the same concept/designation as the WordPress "Posts page" (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#179 = base rung; hub = enriched rung; complementary, not merged), opted in via a **guarded per-site designation** on the `uams_multisite_settings` global (one-per-site by construction; not a bare page toggle, not native `mount`). Its feed is **department-filtered central `news` posts + optional local posts**, filtered by a new **post→`organizations`** association (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1598) populated by a `wordpress-importer` **`category`→`organizations` crosswalk** (`UAMS-Web/wordpress-importer#438`; categories only — newsroom posts carry no tags; partial by design, with an unmapped audit). `organizations` is the **universal department key** across all three zones (schema `schema_subject`, people `job_title` UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1557, news post→org), all resolved via **UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1556**'s site→org bridge — so the news zone now depends on UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1556 too. Presentation is a **news-magazine front page**: a mixed sticky-first **lead** → per-**blueprint** type sections (next-N **offset by the lead's per-type count** to de-dupe; the existing unconsumed `sticky_query` reused) → a **bottom CTA** to the **org-filtered archive** (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1599 — one org×type×source-parameterized `posts/_listing`). Build **held for the full three-zone hub** (Fork B).

**Why.** By convention the newsroom is centralized on `news` and departments don't author local posts, so UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1160's `source="current"` feed is empty for real departments; department relevance needs a post→org link, and the WXR shows newsroom posts are `category`-tagged only, so the link is crosswalk-derived. Keying every zone on `organizations` makes UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1556 the single hub linchpin. By-blueprint zoning + chronological + sticky is the only automatic, curation-free model that scales to ~200 department hubs.

**Reversal.** Supersedes two UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1160 conclusions: (1) "Spoke Feed already shipped via `source="current"`, the lean MVP" and (2) "no new post→org association/taxonomy needed." Both reversed; the news zone is a real slice gated on UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1556 + the crosswalk. UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1160's other conclusions (department = site; opt-in; schema via `schema_subject`; people via `job_title`) stand.

**Rejected.** Per-page toggle designation (no one-per-site guard — the UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#179 drift); native `mount` of `posts` (one localized entry, too rigid at ~200 sites); full-paginated-archive as the hub body (firehose vs. durable node); whole-newsroom unfiltered CTA; per-type local "More" links; tags in the crosswalk (none exist).

**Deferred.** Archive **location** (news-site `?org=` view vs. department-site page) + the college-vs-department **granularity** question → the newsroom-per-college-page IA spike (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1600); the short-section hide **threshold** value → design finalization; child-unit roll-up remains deferred (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1160).

**Companion.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1555 issue `## Decision`; follow-ups UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1598 (post→org field) / `UAMS-Web/wordpress-importer#438` (crosswalk) / UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1599 (archive view) / UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1600 (newsroom-IA spike) / UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1602 (hub-template build); prerequisites UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1556 (now also unblocks the news zone) / UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1557. Verified against `resources/blueprints/collections/posts/*` (`sticky_query` on all four; no post→org field), `resources/views/posts/_listing.antlers.html`, `resources/blueprints/globals/uams_multisite_settings.yaml`, `resources/sites.yaml` (single-domain path multisite), the UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#76 `NewsSyndicationQueryService`, and the news WXR (`category`/`post_format` only, no `post_tag`).

**Build notes (2026-07-18, the UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1557 / UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1599 / UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1612 / UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1602 group).** Four choices the build settled, including the two values this entry deferred:

1. **Short-section hide threshold = 1** (the deferred value). A hub type-section renders when it has **any** post after offset dedup and is dropped when it has none — so a thin department shows exactly the sections with content, down to a lead-only or empty hub. It stays configurable (`section_threshold`) rather than hardcoded, but 1 is the shipped default: a higher floor would silently swallow a department's only video or podcast, which is the opposite of the "shows whatever has content" degrade this entry specifies.
2. **The two dedup mechanisms are implemented separately, on purpose.** The newsroom home (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1612, `axis=category`) uses a **cross-section accumulator** (a post in the lead or an earlier section is excluded by id from every later section) because a post can sit in several categories, so only exact-id exclusion is sound. The hub (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1602, `axis=type`) uses **offset** (each type section skips that type's count in the lead), which is sound only because a post has exactly one blueprint and the lead and each type query share one `(sticky, date-desc)` order over the same org-filtered set. Conflating them was the error UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1609's review caught; the code keeps them in distinct methods with the reasoning in their docblocks.
3. **Derived-vs-curated for the fuller people roster (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1557's open question): leadership derives, the fuller roster derives only where the data earns it.** The required leadership links (`academic_leadership_role` / `clinical_leadership_role` / `institutional_leadership_role`) are the reliable spine and are what the hub renders today. Whether the *optional* member/faculty links (notably `faculty_role_title.department`) are populated enough to derive a fuller roster is a **data** question, not a design one, so it ships with a measurement rather than a guess: `php artisan people:audit-department-role-links` reports per-role-set link completeness, and the fuller roster is derived where a set clears ~80% linked and left curated below that. The org-role → role-set mapping itself is `config/department_hub.php`, so extending the zone to another org tier is config, not code.
4. **UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#179's `posts_page_query` is retired outright — there was nothing to migrate.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1602 shipped the guarded `department_hub_page` designation and the shared surface, and deferred the toggle's removal to UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1661 on the assumption that live pages carrying it would break. That assumption was **wrong and unverified**: `posts_page_query` appears in **zero** of the 728 `content/` entries, so the retirement is a code-only change (fieldset + two blueprint imports + two template blocks + its test) with no content step and no importer sequencing. UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1661 does it; the WordPress `page_for_posts` → designation mapping remains `UAMS-Web/wordpress-importer#452` (the vendored importer sets neither field today). The retirement also **drops the per-page `posts_per_page` editor control** rather than porting it: the listing size is centralized in `posts/_listing` (default 10) so pagination stays consistent across ~255 sites instead of drifting per page. Separately, the newsroom YouTube **playlist** renders as a lazy `iframe` because the UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#224 lite-video facade explicitly does not support playlists yet.

## 2026-07-16 — Dependent-publish tether: opt-in directional field, render-suppress (never mutate stored status) (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1551 / UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1593)

**Decision.** Tie a dependent entry's reachability to one or more source entries via a **deliberate, opt-in directional field** (a `publish_depends_on`-style `entries` field on the dependent), and when all tethered sources go dark **suppress the dependent at render** — redirect + listing-exclusion + a CP banner — modeled on the orphaned-ancestor mechanism (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#443 / UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1429 / UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1455). The dependent's stored `published` / `status_and_visibility` is **never written**. Default **AND** across sources (down only when the last source leaves), per-field **OR** available. Terms are **out** (no native published state; entry → entry only). Spike UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1551 → build UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1593.

**Why.** A real, recurring incident: a provider leaves, their `people` profile is set inactive, and `clinical_resources` that exist only because of them stay silently published until an urgent escalation. Because the tie is *declared*, suppression is the contracted behavior, not a false positive. Render-suppress (vs mutate-stored) keeps every safety property the status model is built on: no split-brain with [`SyncEntryPublishedStatus`](../app/Listeners/SyncEntryPublishedStatus.php) (nothing is written on the dependent, so nothing to clobber), the source is re-read each request so **re-publishing it restores the dependent automatically** — dissolving the re-publish A/B fork rather than answering it — and the on-read trigger has no cascade fan-out and no UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1406 re-entrancy surface. A directional opt-in field encodes source → dependent by construction, so the pinned one-way constraint holds without a per-edge source declaration.

**Rejected.** *Advisory-only* (surface a warning/report, leave the flip to the editor) — under-delivers a feature whose whole point is a declared tether, leaving the recurring incident unfixed. *Automatic mutate-stored* (flip the dependent's stored status via an `EntrySaved` cascade) — the only option that can clobber an independent editor edit and that forces the re-publish A/B decision; cascade fan-out + UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1406 re-entrancy; fights the never-split-brain discipline. *Reuse the symmetric `stillat` edges as the fate-tie* — conflates topical relatedness with a fate-tie (false cascades) and risks status leaking back up a symmetric edge. *No-go* — the harm is real and recurring.

**Companion.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1551 `## Decision` (the spike); UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1593 (the build). Precedents: render-suppression [`app/Listeners/RedirectOrphanedDescendants.php`](../app/Listeners/RedirectOrphanedDescendants.php) + [`app/Listeners/AppendOrphanBanner.php`](../app/Listeners/AppendOrphanBanner.php) (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1429 / UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1455); status model [`app/Listeners/SyncEntryPublishedStatus.php`](../app/Listeners/SyncEntryPublishedStatus.php); re-entrancy bound avoided [`app/Support/Relationships/BoundedRelationshipProcessor.php`](../app/Support/Relationships/BoundedRelationshipProcessor.php) (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1406). Interacts with the #803 `clinical_providers` → `people` merge (relocates the source signal; not a hard block).

## 2026-07-16 — Create the recurring dep-update chore just-in-time, not as a pre-created backlog (`iteration-end` / `iteration-pack`)

**Decision.** Stop pre-creating the `Update dependencies (Iteration N)` chore for every future iteration. Create it **just-in-time — one open ticket at a time, for the current/upcoming iteration**: [`iteration-end`](../.claude/skills/iteration-end/SKILL.md) creates Iteration N+1's ticket (Step 4a) when it closes Iteration N, immediately before it posts that iteration's calibration comment on it; [`iteration-pack`](../.claude/skills/iteration-pack/SKILL.md) carries a create-if-missing safety net for the *active* iteration only. The shared creation recipe (issue body + `afk`/assignee + board fields Priority=Chore, Size=XS, Estimate=1, Phase=`0: Foundation`, Iteration) lives once in the [`update-dependencies-ticket`](../.claude/skills/update-dependencies-ticket/SKILL.md) skill. The 47 pre-created future tickets (Iterations 9–55, UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#715–UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#795) were closed as not-planned.

**Why.** Pre-creating ~50 tickets made the tracker read as far more outstanding work than truly existed, and each ticket's hard-coded date range went stale the moment the iteration calendar shifted (e.g. UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#716 "Iteration 10" still carried a September range after the calendar moved it to August). `iteration-end` is the natural roll-forward point — it already had to comment on Iteration N+1's ticket (the calibration comment), so it now creates that ticket first. This keeps exactly one open Update-deps ticket ahead, always for the iteration about to be worked, with live dates.

**Rejected.** (a) *Keep pre-creating the backlog* — the bloat and date-staleness are the whole problem. (b) *Make `iteration-pack` the primary creator* — packing runs often across many future iters and its job is scheduling, not creation; as primary it would risk regrowing the backlog, so it holds only an active-iteration safety net. (c) *Close 9–55 but leave the skills unchanged* — would regrow the gap with no owner for the next ticket.

**Companion.** [`.claude/skills/iteration-end/SKILL.md`](../.claude/skills/iteration-end/SKILL.md) Step 4, [`.claude/skills/update-dependencies-ticket/SKILL.md`](../.claude/skills/update-dependencies-ticket/SKILL.md) "Creating the ticket".

## 2026-07-16 — Test-suite route-count tax: route:cache the CI test jobs (testing env), not an upstream `mitydigital/sitemapamic` change (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1573 / UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1537)

**Decision.** The ~2× per-test regression bisected to UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1348 is `mitydigital/sitemapamic`'s 255 per-site `sitemap.xml` routes making Laravel's URL generation scan a bloated *uncached* route table. Fix: build the route cache as `APP_ENV=testing` before the Pest jobs in `ci.yml` + `scripts/local-ci.mjs` (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1569). Spun out of UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1537 as UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1573; UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1537 keeps the inherent-cost work.

**Why.** Skipping `bootRoutes` is necessary and sufficient — the addon's whole `boot()` is ~1.2s across 376 boots, so it is a registration side-effect, not boot time. Production already `route:cache`s on deploy, so only the uncached test env was affected. Building as `testing` keeps env-conditional routes (the `local`-only dev preview route) correct. Validated green in a clean local-ci worktree (5,407 parallel + 330 serial, 0 failures); heavy save/render subset 338s → 202s.

**Rejected.** (a) An upstream sitemapamic change to register one host-dispatched route instead of 255 — unnecessary since prod caches, so it would only help uncached dev/test; a low-priority nicety, not a blocker. (b) Decompose UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1537 into an epic + four children now — deferred: the three inherent-cost contributors (×12 cold Stache warm, `StacheIsolatedTestCase` churn, per-test hydration) are not yet attributed, so pre-creating fix-issues would guess a cost split we have not measured. Attribute first, then decompose. (c) Treat it as environmental — rejected: the ~2.36× Windows platform penalty is separate and stays with UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1537; this contributor is real code/config that travels via git.

**Companion.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1573 (the fix), UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1569 (the PR), UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1537 (the parent inherent-cost investigation).

## 2026-07-17 — Podcast is a `subtype` of `audio_series`, not a parallel taxonomy; gate server-side via a computed `is_podcast_episode` (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1611)

**Decision.** Retire the `podcast_series` taxonomy and fold it into `audio_series` as a multi-select **`subtype`** (`podcast`, `radio`, extensible; a series may hold more than one). The `podcast_series` field is removed from both audio blueprints; `series` (→ `audio_series`) is the sole series association. Podcast-ness is the single fact "the entry's selected `series` term declares `subtype: podcast`", resolved by one helper (`App\Support\AudioSeries::entrySeriesHasSubtype`, a raw-slug read + `Term::find`) and surfaced two ways: (1) **server-side** — the schema subsystem injects a computed `is_podcast_episode` boolean into the Antlers context, and every podcast-only schema property (`audio`, `episodeNumber`, `associatedMedia`, `partOfSeason`, `partOfSeries`) gates on `{{ if is_podcast_episode }}`; the PHP `PodcastEpisode` co-typing resolver reuses the same helper. (2) **CP field visibility** — the six episode fields gate on `series 'not empty'`, and the folded `description` gates on the native `subtype: 'contains podcast'`. The `uams-health-talk` term migrates into `audio_series` with `subtype: [podcast]`; the existing radio show `heres-to-your-health` gains `subtype: [radio]`. A term query scope `audio_series_subtype_*` lists series by subtype. The `PodcastSeries` / `PodcastSeason` JSON-LD is preserved for a podcast-subtype series — same `@type`, `name`, `description`, `episodeNumber`, and `partOfSeries` structure — and emits nothing for a non-podcast one; the only change is the graph-internal `@id`, which base64-encodes the term URL and now reflects the migrated term's `/audio-series/…` path (unavoidable once the term moves taxonomies, and the graph stays self-consistent).

**Why.** A podcast *is* a specialization of an audio series; two parallel taxonomies where one specializes the other let a recording attach to both and is a modelling smell. A multi-select subtype captures podcast/radio/future kinds (and multiples) without a new taxonomy each time. The gate must be evaluable where terms-field augmentation does **not** hydrate (the schema subsystem's synthetic in-memory fixtures), so a PHP-computed `is_podcast_episode` (via `Term::find`, the same independent term resolution the schema tag already uses) is hermetic and keeps one source of truth; a live term lookup scattered across templates and a stored/denormalized signal were the two rejected shapes the ticket named. Statamic field `if:` cannot reactively traverse to a related term's field, so the CP episode-field visibility keys on series-presence rather than the term's subtype — output correctness is unaffected because the authoritative podcast determination is subtype-driven server-side.

**Rejected.** *Keep `podcast_series` as a parallel taxonomy* (the superseded UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1097 model) — the ambiguity + per-kind-taxonomy growth this fixes. *Live `Term::find` gate scattered across each schema template* — duplicates the lookup, no single source of truth. *A stored/synced `is_podcast` field on the entry* — denormalized, drifts when the series subtype changes without re-saving. *A CP reactive custom condition + `provideToScript` subtype map* for the episode fields — correct but adds JS/composer/build surface beyond the ticket's two named options, for entries that do not exist yet (0 audio entries store `series`); revisit if radio audio series with episodes materialize (follow-up). *Relax or conditionally-gate `boilerplate`/`genre` required-ness for podcasts* — coupling required-ness to a custom JS condition risks server-side validation gaps; kept unchanged (the migrated podcast term is valid on disk; completing its boilerplate/genre is an editorial follow-up).

**Companion.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1611 issue `## Decision`; supersedes the 2026-07-06 `Podcast schema` entry below (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1097). Content companion PR (taxonomy + schema_objects) + app pointer bump. Importer follow-up filed in `UAMS-Web/wordpress-importer` to map podcast content onto `audio_series` + `subtype`.

## 2026-07-17 — No migration 404s: a deterministic redirect map (WXR + importer), split build vs. validate (#1640 / UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1641)

**Decision.** Guarantee zero migration 404s with a **deterministic redirect map** derived entirely from data already in hand — the WXR, via the importer — split into two tickets. **UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1641 (`afk`)** builds the map generator: entry/asset URL-change 301s from the importer's stored `original_wordpress_url` key (on entries via the creators, on assets via `AssetDownloader`), WordPress structural URLs *synthesized* from WXR fields (date/author/pagination/feed/attachment-page/`?p=`), and a **nearest-surviving-ancestor → 410** rule for not-migrated permalinks. **#1640 (`hitl`)** runs that generator against the final migration (#696) and validates completeness at launch (crawl the old-URL inventory → 0 unhandled 404s, with Search Console + access logs as a backstop). Pre-existing WordPress redirects need no new work: the importer's `RedirectRuleGlobalCreator` already merges the Safe Redirect Manager `redirect_rule` table (~5,700 rules) into the shared `redirects` store.

**Why.** The redirect map's only *sources* are the WXR and the importer; everything else is either *synthesized* (the structural URLs, all derivable from WXR fields) or a *proof* (the ground-truth crawl, which checks completeness rather than feeding the map). That makes the bulk deterministic and `afk`-buildable now against a test import — so it leaves launch scope entirely — while only the authoritative run plus the completeness validation genuinely need the live production site. Splitting keeps the launch ticket thin and lets the generator be built, tested, and waved during development.

**Rejected.** (a) One `hitl` launch ticket for the whole thing — buries the large deterministic core behind the launch and a human sign-off. (b) Sourcing the map from a live-site crawl or Search Console as the *primary* input — those are validation/backstop, not sources; the WXR already enumerates the permalinks. (c) Carrying redirects configured *outside* Safe Redirect Manager (server `.htaccess`/nginx, CDN/edge) — presumed none, not carried, by decision (the team manages redirects in-WordPress via Safe Redirect Manager, ~5,700 rules). (d) Blanket-410, or 301-everything-to-the-homepage, for not-migrated content — worse for SEO and UX than nearest-surviving-ancestor → 410.

**Companion.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1641 (`afk`, the generator — project 1) and #1640 (`hitl`, launch validation — project 4, `blocked_by UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1641` + #696). Dedup collapses handled by #798 / UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1639. Redirects store UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1355 / UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1356; importer `RedirectRuleGlobalCreator` + `AssetDownloader`; stored lever `original_wordpress_url`.

## 2026-07-17 — `provider_spotlight`: reconcile the analog to the moved `staging` model (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1428)

**Decision.** `staging` had moved substantially since the 2026-07-10 as-built basis (PR #803), so UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1428 is not the expected near-no-op. Applied on the Statamic analog: the introduction is always generated (editor `intro` field removed) with reworked prose (lowercased clinical title, short-name second sentence, possessive pronoun); the auto-excerpt prose reworked and the `title_short` / `excerpt` inputs hidden so both are provider-derived; the six seeded Q&A questions refreshed; the featured provider's ontology (locations, areas of expertise, conditions, treatments) mirrored onto the spotlight relationships on save (an overwrite) alongside the existing `clinical_providers` union, with the derived relationship inputs hidden; the CTA appointment phone formatted (national display + `tel:`-safe href, raw fallback for non-standard numbers). The mirror listener is renamed `MirrorSpotlightFeaturedProviderRelationships`.

**Why.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1426 pinned `staging` as the model of record; UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1428 applies whatever delta it carries. Deriving the content from the featured provider keeps the spotlight from drifting from the provider profile. Hiding the excerpt/short-title inputs (rather than only falling back when blank) matches the shipped "always regenerate" behavior. Title/slug derivation and the JSON-LD provider node are separable, individually scoped behaviors — deferred to named follow-ups; imported spotlights already carry `title`/`slug`, so deferring their derivation idles nothing.

**Rejected.** Treating UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1428 as a no-op (staging had moved). Keeping the editor `intro` / `excerpt` overridable (diverges from the always-derived shipped model). Bundling the save-time title/slug derivation and the schema `about` / `mainEntity` node into this PR (separable; the slug freeze-at-publish semantics and the schema uplift each warrant their own ticket).

**Companion.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1428 `## Decision`; builds on the UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1426 model-of-record decision below. Follow-ups: title/slug derivation, schema `about`/`mainEntity` node (both Statamic); importer mapping `UAMS-Web/wordpress-importer#419`. Interacts with UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1378 (spotlight = derived-summary exception).

## 2026-07-17 — `people` profiles: one `default` entry, N site-specific lenses (clinical `/provider` first; not a collection, not a redirect) (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#571)

**Decision.** A person is **one `people` entry on the `default` site** (no localizations across the ~250 sites) — the single source of truth for the data. Every public appearance is a **site-specific _lens_** on that one entry: *(route, template under `resources/views/people/<lens>/`, field selection, gating association)*. The **clinical provider lens** is the first: an eligible `default` entry rendered through a dedicated **`people/provider/show`** at **`/provider/{slug}`** (singular, WordPress-parity `uamshealth.com/provider/…`), gated by the UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#919 public-provider output filter. Other lenses (academic, academic-leadership, institutional, institutional-leadership, researcher, general) render the same entry differently for different audiences, gated by the **org-to-people bridge** (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1556/UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1557). Lenses **coexist, each its own canonical page** — different content, so **no `/people`→`/provider` redirect**. The legacy `clinical_providers`/`provider` *collections* are **to be retired** into `people` (#803) — `clinical_providers` is empty and unrouted but still wired as the active provider hub (reverse-reference computeds, `stillat` relationships), so retiring it is a real refactor, not a completed drop; the target end-state is all data on `people`.

Structure: a **cross-site substrate** (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1635) resolves the `default` entry and renders it through a lens template on a site with no localized copy — its first task is pinning the `default`↔`health`↔`uamshealth.com` mapping; if the clinical profile is same-site, cross-site work collapses to the department lenses only. A **general lens mechanism** (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1636) treats a lens — *(template, gate, route)* — as configuration over UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1635 + the org bridge, and supplies the non-clinical lenses. The FaD **directory** (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#570, shipped at the `/providers` mount via `people/index`) is re-homed under `people/provider/` to quarantine its eligibility filter + physician facets to the clinical lens. Per-provider `/{section}` subpages + subbrand chrome remain #1475 (Post-MVP).

**Why.** `people` already carries the fields + the built eligibility layer (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#919/UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1405) and the FaD REST projection, and the person's data is intentionally single (`default`-only) — so multiple audiences are served by multiple *renders* of one entry, never by duplicating the human or forking the data. Lenses differ only by template + gate, so UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#571's clinical route+template is the reusable prototype UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1636 generalizes. Because lenses are different content, each is self-canonical and a redirect would be wrong — it would erase the person's other identities (a clinician is also a faculty member). Namespacing views under `people/<lens>/` structurally quarantines lens-specific chrome (the FaD facets cannot bleed into another lens's index).

**Rejected.** (a) A `provider`/`clinical_providers` **collection** for provider profiles — retired; duplicates the human + data (the DRY violation this removes). (b) Reusing the base `people/show` for `/provider/{slug}` — the clinical lens shows different fields, arranged differently; it wants its own template. (c) **Localizing the entry per site** — contradicts the single-`default`-source model; the render is cross-site instead. (d) `/people/{slug}` canonical with `/provider/{slug}` a `rel=canonical` alias, or a `/people`→`/provider` redirect — treats them as duplicate content / erases the coexisting non-clinical identity; they are distinct pages. (e) A conditional collection route to `/provider/{slug}` — eligibility is a computed, non-indexed flag unavailable at URI generation, and it would not produce the directory. (f) Keeping the FaD facets in the generic `people/index` slot — reads as the default people index and bleeds into every lens; hence the `people/provider/` re-home.

**Companion.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#571 (clinical lens build) `## Decision`; generalization UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1636; cross-site substrate UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1635; directory UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#570 (shipped); section subpages #1475 (Post-MVP); sunset #803; org bridge UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1556/UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1557. Eligibility: UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#919, UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1405, `ClinicalProviderProfileEligibility`, `config/clinical_provider_profile.php`. FaD REST: `ProviderIndexController`/`ProviderShowController`. Naming: WP `uamshealth.com/provider/{slug}`.

## 2026-07-17 — `provider_spotlight`: model the Statamic analog on the `staging` shipped feature, not the deployed/production state (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1426)

**Decision.** The shipped Provider Spotlight on the **`staging`** branch of `UAMS-Web/UAMSWP-Find-a-Doc` (plugin, PR #803) + `UAMS-Web/uams-2020` (theme, PR #562) is the **authoritative model** for the Statamic `provider_spotlight` analog — treated as as-good-as-done. This closes the UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1426 spike, whose sole purpose was to confirm whether the *deployed* plugin had drifted from the 2026-07-10 as-built spec: with `staging` declared the model of record, there is nothing left to research. The reconciliation folds into **UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1428** — a plain diff of the shipped UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1425 blueprint/schema against current `staging`, applying any delta (a no-op only if `staging` has not moved since 2026-07-10) — and the importer go-ahead for `UAMS-Web/wordpress-importer#419` is given (map source → the analog's handles once UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1428 finalizes them).

**Why.** The feature has shipped to `staging` on both repos and is stable enough to model against; the spike existed only because a `staging`-based plugin PR left staging→production drift possible, and that uncertainty is now resolved by fiat — build to `staging`. Waiting for production would idle the Statamic + importer work behind an external release train for no modeling benefit.

**Rejected.** (a) Chasing the *deployed/production* WordPress state to confirm drift before building (the original spike premise) — moot once `staging` is the model of record. (b) Keeping UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1426 open as a research spike — nothing left to research; it closes with this decision as its deliverable. (c) Deferring the analog until the feature reaches production — idles dependent Statamic + importer work with no upside.

**Companion.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1426 issue `## Decision` (closed) → UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1428 (reconcile to `staging`) → `UAMS-Web/wordpress-importer#419` (importer mapping, unblocked). Analog: UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1425 (closed). As-built spec 2026-07-10; plugin PR `UAMS-Web/UAMSWP-Find-a-Doc#803`, theme PR `UAMS-Web/uams-2020#562`.

## 2026-07-18 — Newsroom per-college pages + news→org granularity: department-site archive, degrade gracefully, widen the org-key to college/institute (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1600)

**Decision.** (1) The org-filtered newsroom archive lives on the **department site** and cross-queries the `news` site (org implicit from the site via the UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1556 bridge), not a `?org=` view on the newsroom. (2) News→org granularity **degrades gracefully**: the importer crosswalk (`UAMS-Web/wordpress-importer#438`) maps categories→orgs at each category's native tier, and a department with no department-level category degrades to news-only (an already-accepted invariant), optionally a labeled college roll-up. (3) The newsroom's own per-college pages map onto the **already-shipped native `post_categories` archive** (`/category/{parent_uri}/{slug}`, UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1433/UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1434); the hub org-archive is a separate org-keyed surface. (4) The per-post `related_organizations` (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1598) and per-site `department_organization` (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1556) org-key **includes `college` and `institute`**, not only department/division/service-line.

**Why.** WordPress has no per-college template — the pages are native category archives, and the category taxonomy is the *only* org signal on a post; the tree spans college/institute/department tiers and ~4,000 posts are tagged only at college/institute, so a department-only org-key could neither store nor filter them (breaking "University News", a union of college orgs). The department-site archive reuses the existing cross-site query + UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1556 bridge (less new code, no public `?org=` surface to defend). Mandating finer categories cannot fix 9,771 historical posts.

**Rejected.** A `news`-site `?org=` view (needs a new allow-listed public param surface; newsroom chrome on a department archive; splits the hub across two domains). Mandating finer newsroom categories (huge editorial cost; no fix for history). A department-only org-key (leaves the 4,000+ college/institute posts impossible to store or filter).

**Companion.** Issue UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1600 `## Decision`; the org-key widening ships in the UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1598 / UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1556 wave; downstream hub layer UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1557 / UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1599 / UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1602 / UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1612; importer crosswalk `UAMS-Web/wordpress-importer#438`.

## 2026-07-23 — Footer `tax_id` is display-only; schema `taxID` stays sourced from the Organization's `ein` (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1351)

**Decision.** The three new `uams_multisite_settings` fields (`tax_id`, `display_tax_id`, `footer_action_link`) are **footer-display** fields, and live in the **Global Footer** tab. `tax_id` deliberately does **not** feed schema.org `taxID` — the issue's acceptance-criteria bullet requiring it is struck as incorrect. An organization's `taxID` continues to come from the `ein` field on its `schema_objects` entry via `EinMerger`.

**Why.** The entity named in a giving footer is the **University of Arkansas Foundation, Inc.** (tax id `71-6056774`) — the 501(c)(3) that actually receives the gifts — which is a **different legal entity** from UAMS itself (EIN `71-6046242`). The UAMS Organization node already emits its own correct `taxID` from `ein`, together with a rich `identifier` `PropertyValue` (EIN/FEIN/TIN alternate names, Wikidata + CTDL `propertyID`s). Sourcing that node from this global would assert the Foundation's tax id as the university's own — factually wrong structured data — while replacing a richer identifier with a bare scalar. The bullet was written believing `taxID` was unwired; it was already wired.

**Rejected.** *Global becomes canonical for schema* (point the Organization template at `get_global var="tax_id"` and clear `ein`) — emits the wrong entity's tax id, degrades the `identifier` node, and drags a `content/`-submodule change into a footer ticket. *Emit from `ein`, fall back to the global* — a fallback path with no consumer (no per-site Organization node exists today) carrying the same wrong-entity risk. *Place the fields beside `founder`/`founding_date` in `organization_information`* (the issue's stated placement) — that placement was justified solely by the schema premise, which is void; they are footer-display fields.

**Data correction.** The WordPress `uams-2020` advancement footer published `71-6056674`, which resolves to Boston Mountain Fire Department; it was a typo, corrected upstream to `71-6056774` (University of Arkansas Foundation, Inc.). Do not migrate the old value.

**Companion.** Issue UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1351 `## Decision`.

## 2026-07-24 — `researcher` lens gates person-level (research identity); `general` lens ships opt-in (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1667)

**Decision.** The `researcher` `people` lens (the UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1636 lens-table remainder) gates PERSON-LEVEL on a public research identity — a UAMS TRI research profile, a scholarly author identifier (ORCID / ResearcherID / Scopus / Google Scholar / PubMed), or declared research interests (`config/researcher_profile.php` + `ResearcherProfileEligibility`) — registered on every site with the person-level gate as the filter, not an org-roster gate. The `general` (ungated `people/show`) lens is registered but ships OPT-IN: an explicit empty `sites: []` registers a lens active on no site, so the ungated base profile does not render on all ~255 sites at once. The academic and institutional lenses stop sharing one `people/_lens_body`; each renders a curated `people/<lens>/profile` body over a shared `people/components/identity_header`.

**Why.** The org-to-people bridge (`config/department_hub.php`) has no research `organization_role`, and researcher profiles are scattered across many department/college sites rather than centralized on the `research` site — so an org-roster gate could not express "is a researcher" and a single-site scope would miss most of them. The person-level signal is real and populated on `people`, and mirrors the shipped clinical eligibility gate, so the researcher lens fits the registry with the same shape. An ungated lens on `['*']` would render every person on all ~255 sites; "a site that wants the plain profile" (the issue's framing) is opt-in, so `sites: []` = registered-but-dormant is the honest default. The identical shared body was an MVP; the point of a lens is a per-audience presentation.

**Rejected.** *Extend the org-role map with a research role* — no organization carries it, so it would gate nothing; speculative. *Scope `researcher` to the `research` site only* (mirroring clinical on `health`) — researchers are scattered across sites, so it would hide most of them. *General lens on `['*']`* — renders every person ungated on every site (over-exposure plus ~255 duplicate URLs). *General lens on `default` at `people/{slug}`* — shadows the native people route, a bigger change out of scope for UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1667. *Keep the shared `_lens_body`* — the identical body defeats the purpose of an audience lens.

**Companion.** Issue UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1667 `## Decision`; builds on the 2026-07-17 provider-lens decision (one `default` entry, N site-specific lenses) and the UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1635 / UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1636 substrate.

## 2026-07-25 — No-location appointment CTA: offer the main line rather than a dead `#locations` anchor (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1695)

**Decision.** Split `!location_valid && referral_required && !accepting_patients` out of the appointment CTA's catch-all and give it the main appointment line, matching the three other no-location branches. WordPress has no branch for this combination: it falls into the catch-all, which emits the "contacting the clinic directly" fragment linked to the on-page `#locations` anchor — and with no published location that section never renders, so WordPress ships a link to an anchor that is not on the page. The **copy is unchanged** (the referral sentence still drops, exactly as the catch-all does); only the dead link is replaced. With `show_portal` implying `location_valid`, this was the only combination reaching the catch-all without a location, so the catch-all's behavior for location-carrying cases is untouched.

**Why.** A dead in-page link on a patient-facing appointment block is a worse defect than a one-branch divergence from the port. WordPress's own three no-location branches already carry an explicit comment justifying the main line in exactly this state — with no location there would otherwise be no way to make an appointment at all — so the fallback follows the source's stated reasoning rather than inventing a new policy. The combination is also close to unreachable from the CP (`referral_required` is only asked when `accepting_patients_query` is true), so the blast radius of the divergence is minimal while the fix removes a real broken link.

**Rejected.** Reproducing the dead link for strict parity — knowingly shipping a broken link to patients. Making the `direct` fragment fall back when `location_count` is zero — pushes a branch-specific decision into a fragment four other branches share, where "contacting the clinic directly" would still be the wrong phrase for a provider with no clinic. Patching inside the catch-all — would have re-decided the location-carrying cases the catch-all legitimately owns.

**Companion.** Issue UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1695 `## Decision`; the branch table and its per-branch reasoning in `resources/views/people/provider/_appointment_cta.antlers.html`. #1228 still owns any revision to the copy itself.

## 2026-07-25 — Foot-of-page CTA repeat: a computed section list, not a restatement at the call site (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1695)

**Decision.** Gate the clinical provider lens's second appointment CTA on a `provider_profile_body_rendered` computed backed by `ProviderProfileBodyResolver`, which enumerates the ten sections between the two CTAs in one place. Relation-backed sections are checked for a **published** related entry (not stored ids), and replicator-backed sections ignore sets flagged `enabled: false`, so the resolver reports what the template actually renders. The residual coupling — the *list* of sections — is guarded by a test that counts the section-producing partial calls between the two CTA include sites and fails when one is added or removed.

**Why.** WordPress gates its second instance on an intervening section having rendered; ours rendered it unconditionally, so a provider with an appointment CTA and nothing else got the same block twice, back to back. Reproducing that gate in Antlers means restating ten section conditions at an eleventh site, which drifts the moment a section is added or re-gated. The bias is deliberately toward rendering: over-reporting reproduces the pre-existing duplicate in a narrow case, while under-reporting would *drop* the foot-of-page CTA from a real profile — a regression against a patient trying to book.

**Rejected.** A hand-maintained boolean expression at the call site — the duplication that drifts. Asserting a per-section resolver↔template render coupling the tests do not actually perform — the honest gap (no test compares the two on a given edge value) is named in the resolver's docblock instead, after it bit once on a whitespace-only podcast guest name.

**Companion.** Issue UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1695 `## Decision`; `app/Services/ComputedFields/ProviderPeople/ProviderProfileBodyResolver.php`.

## 2026-07-25 — `?provider=` archive filter: unknown slugs browse all, known-and-empty shows empty (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1692)

**Decision.** Filter the `clinical_resources` archive by `?provider={slug}` through a query scope that resolves the person and constrains positively to the ids they relate. An **absent, blank, unknown, unpublished, private, or password-protected** slug leaves the archive **unfiltered**; a **known, publicly-renderable** provider who relates nothing yields an **empty** list. The slug lookup lives in one place (`ClinicalResourceProviderFilter`) shared by the scope and the tag that names the provider in the on-page notice. The archive root for the lens's "more" CTA is read from the collection's **mount**, never hard-coded.

**Why.** The asymmetry is the point: an unknown slug is a *malformed question* (a stale or mistyped link), and degrading to browse-all matches how `clinical_resource_type` already treats an absent type; a known provider with no resources is a *real, empty answer* and must say so. Gating on the full publicly-renderable check rather than publication alone closes a leak — a private provider would otherwise be named in the notice and their related resources listed, exposing through a query parameter what the lens route deliberately withholds. One shared lookup prevents the scope and the notice from disagreeing about whether the archive is filtered and to whom, which is the failure class the UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1695 review caught between a resolver and the template gate it mirrored.

**Rejected.** Rendering an empty archive for an unknown slug — punishes a stale link with a dead end. Gating on `published` only — the privacy leak above. Accepting `clinical_providers` slugs — that blueprint carries no `clinical_resources` field, so it resolved a real person and then filtered to nothing. Hard-coding `/health/resources` — a moved landing page would silently point every profile at a 404. Reading the notice's provider name from the entry `title` — documented as control-panel-only and stored surname-first, so patients would have read a back-office sort key.

**Companion.** Issue UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1692 `## Decision`; `app/Support/People/ClinicalResourceProviderFilter.php`, `app/Scopes/ClinicalResourcesForProvider.php`.

## 2026-07-25 — Disable the Statamic Stache warming lock in the test environment (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1689)

**Decision.** `statamic.stache.lock.enabled` reads `STATAMIC_STACHE_LOCK_ENABLED` and `phpunit.xml` sets it false, so `Statamic\Http\Middleware\StacheLock` short-circuits and the Stache lock factory uses a `NullLockStore`. Production keeps the lock — the variable defaults to `true`. This is the sibling of the `NullCacher` line already in `phpunit.xml` for the static-cache lock, and it retires that setting's status as the *only* 503 guard the suite needed.

**Why.** The Stache lock is a Symfony `FlockStore` over `storage/statamic/stache-locks` (`Statamic\Stache\ServiceProvider::createFileLockStore()`) — one directory that, unlike the Laravel cache (`config/cache.php` tokenizes it by `TEST_TOKEN`), is **not** per worker. Two things take it with a blocking `acquire(true)` and hold it for their duration: `Stache::warm()`, which UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1537 measured at ~168s per worker under parallel contention, and `Duplicates::find()`. Meanwhile every front-end HTTP request in every *other* worker waits `statamic.stache.lock.timeout` (30s) on the same flock and then returns **503**. That is the captured mechanism behind `Error404PageTest`'s end-to-end flake (`Expected response status code [404] but received 503`), and behind the `GET /` 503 that `ExampleTest` gated to `serial` while recording its race as unpinned. The lock's purpose — stopping concurrent requests from duplicating one warm — does not apply in tests, where every worker has its own cache store, so the warm it would deduplicate is one the *other* workers do not want anyway.

**Scope of the window.** It is narrower on the canonical CI paths than a naive reading suggests, and this is why the flake was intermittent rather than constant: `ci.yml` and `scripts/local-ci.mjs` build a Stache snapshot with `test:seed-stache` first, and `tests/CreatesApplication.php` then has each worker *copy* that snapshot into its own cache instead of warming (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1537). A warm is the **fallback** when no snapshot exists — which is exactly the state of an ad-hoc `vendor/bin/pest --parallel` run, where this was reproduced on the first attempt. The window never closes entirely, though: a worker with no usable snapshot still warms, and `Duplicates::find()` takes the same flock regardless.

**Rejected.** *Gate the failing test to `serial`* — the house remedy for the sibling flakes (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1443, UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1300, UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1310) and the cheapest change, but it treats one symptom: any future test making a real HTTP request in the parallel pass inherits the same 503, and the serial tail is already the suite's cost problem (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1537). *Tokenize the lock directory per worker* — correct in principle, but the path is hard-coded in `createFileLockStore()`, so it needs a patch to buy a lock tests do not need. *Raise the 30s timeout* — makes the flake rarer and the suite slower, and still fails whenever a warm runs long.

**Accepted cost.** Concurrent warms are no longer serialized. The flock genuinely did serialize them across workers — that is not a cost the lock was failing to pay — so on a run with no snapshot, several workers can now warm at once and saturate the disk in the way UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1537 documents. That is accepted because the serialization was never free either: a worker waiting on the flock is a worker not running tests, and a worker *serving a request* while waiting is a 503. It also barely applies on the canonical paths, where the snapshot means workers copy rather than warm. A future change that gives workers a shared Stache cache has to revisit this.

**Companion.** Issue UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1689 `## Root cause` + `## Decision`. Sibling flakes UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1443 / UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1300 / UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1310; parallel-suite cost UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1537; per-worker tokenization UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1136.

## 2026-07-25 — Gate `entries` relations on `| pluck:'id' | count`, as an idiom rather than a modifier or a patch (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1693)

**Decision.** An Antlers section gated on an `entries` relation counts it as `{{ rel | pluck:'id' | count }}` — never a bare `{{ rel | count }}`, and never `{{ rel | length }}`, `| to_array | count`, or `| safe_count`, all of which have the same defect — with a short comment at each site saying why. `terms` relations keep the bare `| count`. A relation already resolved by `normalize_list` (the `creator_role_row` → `creator` → `person` chain), by `clinical_provider_eligible`, or by a computed provider also keeps the bare `| count` / `| length`, documented as such. No new modifier, and no `composer` patch against `statamic/cms`.

**Why.** `Statamic\Query\StatusQueryBuilder` applies its published-only filter **only in `get()`**; `count()` and every other terminal forward through `__call` to the wrapped builder unfiltered. So `CoreModifiers::count()`, which calls `$value->count()`, counts the **stored ids**, while the `{{ rel }}` loop beside it resolves through `get()` and emits only the published entries — a heading over an empty list. `CoreModifiers::length()` opens with the identical query-builder branch, which is why the rule is about the defect rather than about one modifier's name; the first pass of this audit swept `| count` alone and left four `| length` gates over the clinical-resource, clinical-location, and knowledge-base relations still counting stored ids. `CoreModifiers::pluck()` calls `->get()` first, which is exactly the resolution the count needs, and behaves identically on the plain-array fixtures the render tests use. Terms carry no publication status, so a `terms` relation resolves to what it stores and the extra resolution would buy nothing.

**Rejected.** *A named modifier (`resolved_count` or similar)* — the `| pluck:'id' | count` idiom already shipped across the clinical provider lens in UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1684, so a second spelling would mean two idioms for one gate and a migration with no behavioral gain. *A `cweagans/composer-patches` entry fixing `StatusQueryBuilder::count()`* (we carry three such patches, so the machinery exists) — correcting `count()` would change the arithmetic of every stored-id count in the framework, including ones that legitimately count what is stored rather than what renders; a one-modifier template idiom is the smaller blast radius. *Leaving the bare `| count` and gating on the loop's own emptiness* — Antlers has no post-hoc "did that loop emit anything" hook, so the heading is already written by then.

**Accepted cost.** The idiom does not read as "count what will render", so it depends on the comment at each site; and it stays a workaround until the upstream inconsistency is resolved.

**Superseded** on 2026-10-25 by UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2422: `statamic/cms` v6.35 carries the upstream fix, and every gate is now a bare `| count`.

**Companion.** Issue UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1693 `## Decision`; the upstream report is tracked separately. Surfaced by UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1684; the trap's first half (a bare `{{ if rel }}` being truthy) is UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#571.

## 2026-07-25 — The provider-lens podcast section ports the RadioMD embed for MVP (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1683)

**Decision.** The UAMS Health Talk podcast section on the clinical provider lens ships as a **verbatim port of the WordPress RadioMD embed** — the third-party `easyXDM.js` widget filtered by the provider's guest name (`podcast_guest_name_uams_health_talk`) — gated on that field being set. Rendering the episodes from our own first-party `audio` content is split to #1685. The port carries mandatory CSP work: `radiomd.com` is absent from both `script-src` and `frame-src` in `config/security-headers.php`, and CSP ships report-only, so a missed allowance would pass silently now and break at enforcement.

**Why.** Migration parity is the MVP bar, and the embed is what the live site does today: it reflects whatever RadioMD holds — including episodes that never migrated — and needs no provider-to-episode association modeled first. That association is the real blocker for the first-party version: WordPress filters by a **display-name string**, not a relationship, and matching on it is fragile across middle initials, credentials, and inconsistent guest-name entry. Modeling the relationship, deciding which end owns it, and populating it (importer crosswalk vs. editorial backfill) is product work, not a port, so it should not gate the section appearing at all.

**Rejected.** *Query our own `audio` content now* (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1097 / UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1611 modeled the episodes) — better long-term and the direction of the migration, but it depends on the unmodeled provider-to-episode join, so it would either block the section or ship a lossy list. *Defer the section entirely* — leaves a visible parity gap on a patient-facing profile for no gain, since the embed is a known quantity. Both are revisited by #1685, which also retires the CSP allowances this decision adds.

**Accepted cost.** A third-party script and frame on a patient-facing page, and a region that cannot be asserted without the network. Bounded by the explicit CSP allowances and by #1685.

**Companion.** Issue UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1683 `## Decision`; follow-up #1685 (blocked by UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1683). Surfaced by the WordPress-parity audit of the UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#571 clinical provider lens.

## 2026-07-27 — Find-a-Doc settings global: root at the `default` origin, not a per-reader site-walk (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1698)

**Decision.** Fix the "`uams_fad_settings` is localized on `health` only, so `inDefaultSite()` is null in every non-`health` context" bug at the **data layer**: relocate the global's origin to the `default` site (`sites: {default: null, health: default}`, values moved `content/globals/health/…` → `content/globals/default/…`), matching how `seo` and `uams_multisite_settings` are already shaped. Then **delete the shared reader `App\Support\Statamic\FindADocSettings`** (extracted in UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1702 solely to centralize the site-walk) and read the global **inline** — `GlobalSet::findByHandle('uams_fad_settings')?->inDefaultSite()?->get($handle)` — at each site, matching the app's one and only global-read convention (`seo` in `RobotsController`, the logo global, redirects, `get-global`; no other global has a wrapper class). UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1698's "fold `SchemaLocationContactPoints::globalPhone()` onto the shared reader" is reinterpreted as an inline read.

**Why.** Every Find-a-Doc settings field is `localizable: false` (the blueprint declares none per-site localizable) — it is one shared config that was stored on the wrong site, not per-site content. Statamic's `GlobalSet::in($locale)` hard-returns null for any site not in the global's `sites:` map ([`vendor/statamic/cms/src/Globals/GlobalSet.php`](../vendor/statamic/cms/src/Globals/GlobalSet.php)), and the front-end Cascade hydrates a global only via `in($currentSite)` — so the walk was a workaround for a data shape that mismatched the intent. Rooting at `default` makes the naive, obvious `inDefaultSite()` the *correct* code, which kills the standing failure mode the ticket was trying to contain: the next reader written from memory reintroducing the bug. Because the fields are non-localizable and only the `health` site renders Find-a-Doc collections, `health` renders byte-identically (inherits from the `default` origin) and no other site is affected.

**Why this differs from the 2026-07-06 redirects verdict (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1110)**, which *rejected* the origin-cascade for `redirects` in favor of a read-time merge: `redirects` is **per-site editable content** (the shared-editable-list model) whose origin-config fix was a **~100-site migration + drift risk**. Find-a-Doc settings are **non-localizable shared config** rendered on **one site** (`health`), so the fix is a single inheritance entry with no per-site values to reconcile — the migration cost and drift risk that sank the origin-cascade for redirects do not exist here.

**Rejected.** (a) *Root at the `health` origin, `default` inherits from it* (`sites: {health: null, default: health}`) — same runtime result with a smaller content diff, but inverts the origin tree (`default` as a leaf inheriting from `health`) against every other global's convention, surprising the next maintainer for no benefit. (b) *UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1698 as written — DRY the site-walk into one `FindADocSettings` reader (code-only, no data fix)* — reduces copies but leaves the trap armed: a reader that bypasses the wrapper and writes `inDefaultSite()` still returns null. (c) *Keep `FindADocSettings` after the data fix, reframed as a typed accessor* — considered and rejected: once the data is fixed, its `raw()` is byte-identical to the inline read every other global already uses, its founding rationale (the walk) is gone, and it would be the app's lone per-global wrapper; the thin DRY win (a handle const + a one-line coercion across ~4 readers) does not outweigh matching the single inline convention. Re-extract a reader only if Find-a-Doc settings PHP readers proliferate well beyond today's ~4.

**Companion.** Issue UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1698 `## Decision`. Content-companion PR (`content/globals/uams_fad_settings.yaml` + moved values) + app pointer bump. App code: delete `App\Support\Statamic\FindADocSettings`; inline `inDefaultSite()` reads in `AppointmentContextResolver`, `ProviderProfileBodyResolver`, and `SchemaLocationContactPoints::globalPhone()` (drop the walk); `SystemLocationClosures` is already inline (now correct post-fix). Preserve the "unset means enabled" toggle semantics — `provider_mainline` (`=== null || truthy(...)`) and `health_talk_query` (`!== false`) must still resolve null/unset to *enabled* (the UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1694 trap); keep each call site's existing guard, change only how the value is fetched. The two resolvers already own `stringValue()` / `truthy()` helpers, so the deleted reader's `string()` coercion was redundant there. Reader extracted in UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1702; original bug UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1694; prior fold UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1695 → UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1702.

## 2026-07-28 — Rector enforcement: an advisory per-change rule now, a narrowed gate later (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1720)

**Decision.** Keep Rector **advisory** for now, but give it a *home* in the flow where changes are authored: a `## Rector on changed paths (project rule)` section in [`CLAUDE.md`](../CLAUDE.md) telling every PHP change to run `vendor/bin/rector --dry-run` **scoped to the changed paths under `app/`**, alongside the `vendor/bin/pint --dirty` the flow already requires. Enforcement is **staged behind three chained tickets** rather than switched on: split `rector.php` into a gating profile and sweep-only rules (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1720), re-zero the gating profile's `app/` backlog (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1721), then add a `rector` CI job between `pint` and `larastan`, mirrored into [`scripts/local-ci.mjs`](../scripts/local-ci.mjs) (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1722). Pint remains the formatter of record and the sole owner of unused-import removal.

**Why.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1005 landed Rector config-only and deferred the gate on "a clean baseline is the precondition for enforcing it"; UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1006 produced that baseline set-by-set and closed 2026-06-23 on the criterion "`composer test:refactor` reports no pending changes." It did not hold — five weeks later the dry-run reported **124 files** across roughly 201 rule applications, with nothing in the pipeline to notice. Sweep-then-forget is therefore the empirical failure mode, and the correction belongs where code is written: Pint gets run on every change precisely because `CLAUDE.md` says so, and Rector appeared in that file zero times. A gate still cannot simply be switched on, for two independent reasons. It needs a zero baseline (124 ≠ 0). And it converts every Rector suggestion from ignorable to mandatory, which a subset of the currently-enabled rules cannot bear: `ReadOnlyClassRector` / `ReadOnlyPropertyRector` (20 files — `readonly` promotion is a semantic change interacting with framework mutation-after-construction, cloning, and mocking), `LocallyCalledStaticMethodToNonStaticRector` (3 files — unsound at the `app/`-only path scope, since it cannot see static callers in `tests/`, Antlers templates, or `vendor/uams-web/*`), and the docblock-removal rules (conflict with the PHPDoc-coverage convention). Cost is not the obstacle: 112s repo-wide against roughly 2s scoped to a single file.

**Rejected.** (a) *Gate the current config as-is* — impossible at a 124-file baseline, and it would make the three hazardous rule classes above mandatory, where a wrong `readonly` promotion or static demotion is a runtime defect the suite may not catch. (b) *Leave Rector fully manual* — this is what the measured 124-file drift rejects; tooling with no home in the per-change flow decays, and the periodic big-bang sweep it forces is itself harmful (the UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1031 `app/`-wide sweep left PR UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1073 validated against stale code and motivated the [`sync-pr-branch`](../.claude/rules/sync-pr-branch.md) rule). (c) *Put the rule beside the Pint rule it pairs with* — that rule sits inside the `<laravel-boost-guidelines>` block, which `boost:install` regenerates, so the edit would be silently reverted; it goes in the hand-maintained project-rules section instead. (d) *Say "run it on your changed files" without qualification* — a path argument on the command line **overrides** `withPaths()` (verified: `vendor/bin/rector --dry-run tests/Pest.php` analyzes and reports a rewrite of that file), so an unscoped instruction would apply an `app/`-tuned rule set to `tests/`, `config/`, and `routes/`.

**Companion.** [`CLAUDE.md`](../CLAUDE.md) `## Rector on changed paths (project rule)` (PR UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1723). Staged enforcement: UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1720 → UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1721 → UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1722. Prior art: UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1005 (config-only setup that deferred the gate), UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1006 (the set-by-set sweep whose baseline drifted), UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1131 (why Pint owns unused-import removal). Residual recorded on UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1722: `withComposerBased(laravel:, phpunit:)` activates rule sets from *installed* package versions, so a dependency bump can red the gate on files nobody touched — pin the sets explicitly, or accept that the recurring `Update dependencies (Iteration N)` ticket occasionally carries a small Rector sweep.

## 2026-07-29 — The recurring dep-update chore is a pair of tickets, one per repo (`iteration-end` / `wordpress-importer`)
