**Decision.** The just-in-time `Update dependencies (Iteration N)` chore now creates **two** tickets per iteration, not one: the existing host-app ticket in `UAMS-Web/uams-statamic` plus a Composer-only ticket in `UAMS-Web/wordpress-importer` covering the importer package's own `require` / `require-dev`. Both carry the **identical title** — the repo disambiguates them on the board, and the shared prefix is what keeps [`iteration-pack`](../.claude/skills/iteration-pack/SKILL.md)'s `title.startswith("Update dependencies")` skip rule covering both without a second pattern. [`iteration-end`](../.claude/skills/iteration-end/SKILL.md) Step 4a runs the idempotency check and the creation recipe **per repo** (both judged independently), and the pack safety net does the same for the active iteration. Board fields are identical (`Chore` / `XS` / Estimate `1` / `0: Foundation` / `Todo`), so the pair costs `joshdaugherty` 2 Chore est-days per iteration instead of 1. The calibration comment still goes on the **host-app** ticket only.

**Why.** The host app requires the importer as `dev-main`, so the app's sweep resolves the *package* and never the package's own constraints — those move only when someone bumps them in that repo, and nothing in the cadence owned that. They drifted: `UAMS-Web/wordpress-importer#476` records `pestphp/pest` sitting a major behind the app after UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1726 took the app to Pest 5 / PHPUnit 13, which matters because the package's `tests/Feature` band is executed by the **host app's** runner — a toolchain divergence there is invisible to the package's own CI and only ever surfaces in a host-app run. The importer's issues already live on project 1 (so they schedule, estimate, and report with no new machinery), which makes a paired ticket the cheapest place to put that recurring check.

**Rejected.** (a) *Fold the importer bumps into the host-app ticket* — one ticket spanning two repos means two branches, two PRs, and two CI gates under a single `Closes #N`, and it hides the importer sweep's genuinely different shape (no npm, no exact pins, caret ranges kept because it is a library, host-parity check on the shared toolchain). (b) *Keep handling importer bumps ad hoc, as one-off tickets like #476* — that is the observed failure mode: #476 exists precisely because nothing recurring caught the drift. (c) *Give the importer ticket a distinct title* (e.g. naming the package) — reads better in isolation, but it breaks the packer's single-prefix skip rule and the shared idempotency query for no real gain, since the board already renders the repo. (d) *Have `iteration-pack` create the importer ticket* — same reasoning that made it only a safety net for the app ticket in the 2026-07-16 entry below.

**Companion.** [`.claude/skills/iteration-end/SKILL.md`](../.claude/skills/iteration-end/SKILL.md) Step 4a, [`.claude/skills/update-dependencies-ticket/SKILL.md`](../.claude/skills/update-dependencies-ticket/SKILL.md) ("Creating the ticket" + "The importer sweep"), [`.claude/skills/iteration-pack/SKILL.md`](../.claude/skills/iteration-pack/SKILL.md) (safety net). Extends the 2026-07-16 just-in-time entry below. First importer ticket: `UAMS-Web/wordpress-importer#478` (Iteration 9). Prior art for the drift it catches: `UAMS-Web/wordpress-importer#476`.

## 2026-07-30 — The importer's script-stripping invariant is asserted host-side, and stops at node attributes (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1742)

**Decision.** Assert the Tiptap allowlist in **`uams-statamic`**, not in `wordpress-importer`, with both a parser-level test and an end-to-end WXR-import test. Record on `App\Services\BardContentSanitizer` that it is a YAML-safety helper and **not** an XSS control. Additionally: the allowlist covers node *types* and *marks* only — never node *attributes* — so the importer's custom `set` node is a **separate trust boundary**, pinned as a labelled known gap rather than fixed here.

**Why.** The regression vector UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1742 names is a dependency bump, and `ueberdosis/tiptap-php` is required by neither repo directly — it arrives via `statamic/cms`. The two repos are pinned to different builds: `wordpress-importer` locks `statamic/cms` v6.10.0 → tiptap 2.1.0 (floating `^6.0`), while the app pins v6.26.0 → tiptap 2.1.1 (exact). Only host CI gates the build that actually parses production content, so a green run in the importer would assert the allowlist holds on a version no UAMS content ever sees. On the attribute gap: `Tiptap\Core\Schema::apply()` filters marks and node types and nothing else, and `Set::parseHTML()` copies `data-set-attr-*` values verbatim — verified by running the parser, and reachable from WordPress post content, with `_pull_quote.antlers.html` rendering `{{ quote }}` raw. UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1742's premise that "there is no bug to fix here" was therefore wrong.

**Rejected.** *Tests in `wordpress-importer` only* — the parser is framework-free (bare PHPUnit, 0.4s) so it would run in that package's CI, but against tiptap 2.1.0; it cannot gate the host pin. *Tests in both repos* — defensible defense-in-depth, but duplicates ~180 lines across two repos that drift independently; deferred to `UAMS-Web/wordpress-importer#492` along with the constraint-drift question. *Escaping the set values at the Antlers sink* — `quote` is a Bard field, so CP-authored HTML there is legitimate; escaping breaks real content and leaves the trust boundary in the wrong place. *Fixing the set passthrough in this ticket* — the fix is importer-side and needs a regression test proving shortcode-derived sets still import, a larger blast radius than a test-only ticket should carry.

**Companion.** Issue UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1742 `## Decision`; PR UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1792. Follow-ups: `UAMS-Web/wordpress-importer#494` (the set-attribute passthrough, with repro and candidate fixes), `UAMS-Web/wordpress-importer#492` (package-side test + `statamic/cms` constraint drift), `UAMS-Web/wordpress-importer#491` (the `WpAutoP` docblock companion).

## 2026-07-30 — Campaign email: author + gate in-app, keep Sendy sending, lock the templates (#1777)

**Decision.** Build a first-party HTML-email layer that owns **authoring and governance** only, and leave sending where it works. Five forks settled together: **(1) Send layer** — keep Sendy, behind a `CampaignDispatcher` seam. **(2) Subscriber system of record** — Sendy only; the app persists list *metadata* (Sendy list id, provenance, consent basis, owning site, complaint-rate health) and **never an email address**. **(3) Template lock** — a closed set of approved templates, one blueprint each, curated replicator sets per template, HTML compiled from partials and never stored, and **no raw-HTML block anywhere**. **(4) Tracking** — aggregate-only by default (campaign-scoped tokens, not per-recipient); per-recipient requires recorded justification and is hard-blocked on patient-facing templates. **(5) One-off sends** — an institutional from-address via SES running the identical gate, replacing the ungoverned Stripo → Outlook path.

**Why.** The broken layer is authoring and governance, not sending. Stripo is a free-form canvas that structurally cannot enforce a rule because it knows nothing about UAMS — not which of the 254 sites is sending, not that site's parent-entity lockup or postal address, not whether a list is patient-derived. So every brand and compliance rule is currently a convention in someone's head. Sendy meanwhile already holds the compliance- and reputation-critical plumbing (suppression, bounce and complaint handling, SES), and its API accepts exactly what a compiler produces. Keeping subscribers out of the app gives one PII custodian and no addresses in git. The closed template set is what converts brand and accessibility from review to guarantee — and as a side effect removes any need for MJML or a Node dependency, since six hand-written email-safe partials beat a general-purpose compiler for a closed set. Aggregate-only tracking is deliberately stricter than the current floor: the June 2024 vacatur in *AHA v. Becerra* narrowed OCR's theory only as to IP address plus unauthenticated public webpage visits, which does not address a per-recipient pixel tying a named individual to a clinical topic.

**Rejected.** *Send layer:* SES-direct in-app (XL, takes on deliverability and domain-reputation risk for no gain in the layer that is actually broken); export-only, where a human pastes compiled HTML into Sendy (the gate becomes bypassable by construction). *Subscribers:* an in-app database (a second PII custodian to secure and audit); subscribers as flat-file Statamic entries, which is the Postmaster addon's default (puts patient and donor addresses in a version-controlled repository — disqualifying for a health system). *Template lock:* a permissioned custom-HTML block (the escape hatch becomes the default path and voids every guarantee); free-form blocks with lint warnings (reproduces Stripo's problem — nothing is enforced). *Tracking:* per-recipient by default (real HIPAA exposure on health content); no tracking at all (leaves "did this work?" unanswerable). *One-off sends:* send-as-the-user via Microsoft Graph (app registration, admin consent, and per-user OAuth for a marginal gain); reply-to-only splitting; leaving one-offs in Outlook, which keeps the largest compliance gap open — CAN-SPAM applies regardless of client, and a Sendy unsubscribe is not honored by an Outlook send.

**Residual.** The rule *values* (brand palette and lockup rules, approved sender domains, list-provenance and campaign-purpose taxonomies, § 1557 artifact, BAA coverage of the send path) are organizational facts a build cannot derive — obtained under #1778, which may amend the tracking decision if compliance overrules it. Sendy exposes **no statistics API**, so in-CP campaign reporting is unavailable; that is the strongest future argument for revisiting the send-layer fork. Migrating existing Stripo templates is a re-authoring exercise, not an import.

**Companion.** Epic #1777 `## Decision` (with the full guardrail catalog); sub-issues #1778 – #1783. Supersedes the newsletter deferral in the #1159 entry below.

## 2026-07-30 — `composer validate` gates the chain as its own job, second (not folded, not first) (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1735)

**Decision.** `composer validate --no-check-publish --no-check-all` runs as its **own** job — `composer-validate` in [`.github/workflows/ci.yml`](../.github/workflows/ci.yml), `validate` in the [`scripts/local-ci.mjs`](../scripts/local-ci.mjs) mirror — placed **second** in the cost-ordered chain: `isolation → validate → encoding → spell → js → build → pint → larastan → phpunit → browser`. Second rather than first because the ordering contract is measured, not assumed: `composer validate` runs ~1.5s against the isolation guard's ~0.8s on the same machine, so shortest → longest puts it after. The flags scope the check to an application rather than a published package; measured, they do **not** change pass/fail (bare `composer validate` also exits 0 clean and 2 on a stale lock — warnings fail only under `--strict`), they keep the output to the signal by dropping advice against this repo's deliberate exact-version pins and absent `license`. `--strict` is deliberately not adopted: it exits 1 on a clean checkout even with both flags, because "No license specified" is a *general* warning `--no-check-publish` does not suppress.

**Why.** A `composer.json` edit that leaves `composer.lock`'s content hash stale breaks nothing but warns on every `composer install` on every machine, forever — and it shipped twice in one session on two different keys (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1726 the root `name`, UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1728 an `extra.patches` entry), the second time by an author who had fixed the first an hour earlier. That is the mechanical class the [`adversarial-review`](../.claude/rules/adversarial-review.md) rule says to hand to tooling. Its own job keeps the mirror **1:1** — one `ci.yml` job ↔ one local-ci runner ↔ one `--jobs` key — which is the property a hand-maintained mirror lives on, and it leaves the isolation guard's documented "dependency-free, PHP only" single purpose intact. It also matches the shape the repo already uses: `isolation`, `encoding`, and `spell` are each one guard, one job. Placement anywhere ahead of `encoding` captures the entire saving, because `encoding` is the first job that runs `composer install` and everything past it costs more.

**Rejected.** *Fold the check into `test-isolation-guard`* — saves the one extra runner hop (GitHub bills per job, rounded up, so ~1 minute of the ~100 a full green chain costs), and the failure-path saving is identical since either way it fails in the first or second link; rejected because it makes a job named for test isolation also own dependency hygiene, contradicts that job's stated dependency-free property, and costs the 1:1 parity mapping — ~1% of a green run's budget is the deliberate price of a mirror that stays legible and independently addressable (`--jobs validate`). *Front of the chain* — the issue's default framing, but it is measurably not the cheapest link, and preempting the 0.8s isolation guard buys nothing. *`.githooks/pre-commit`* — that hook is deliberately a narrow CI-parity gate that runs no tooling; widening it into a general linter contradicts its stated purpose. *Rely on the per-change rule instead of a gate* — the two incidents hit different keys, so a patches-specific or rename-specific instruction catches one and misses the other.

**Companion.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1735 issue `## Decision`. Extends the 2026-07-15 cost-ordered-chain decision below (unchanged in kind — one more link, same contract). Parity between the two files is now asserted by [`scripts/local-ci.chain.test.mjs`](../scripts/local-ci.chain.test.mjs), which flattens `ci.yml`'s `needs:` graph into the order it encodes and holds that against the script's `CHAIN`. Related gate-coverage gaps from the same session: UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1727 (browser suite), UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1731 (mutation testing).

## 2026-07-30 — Link health: build first-party in `uams-statamic`, not an addon and not a SaaS (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1258 / UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1262 / #225)

**Decision.** The site gets a **first-party link-health checker**, built **inside `uams-statamic`** under `App\Services\LinkHealth\` — not bought as a Statamic addon, not deferred to SiteImprove/Monsido, and not split into a separate `uams-web/*` package. It checks the HTTP status of every link occurrence in content; classifies each as **external**, **first-party-but-not-this-CMS** (another UAMS property), **internal to the current site**, or a Statamic reference (`statamic://entry::`, bare `entry::`/`asset::`/`term::`, and bare-UUID `entries`-fieldtype relations); resolves internal targets in-process rather than over HTTP; reports a link that resolves only via redirect so it can be repointed to its final URL; validates `#fragment` anchors; diffs each run against the previous one to surface **newly** broken links; supports dismissal of an individual occurrence **attributed to the accepting user**; and surfaces results in a **single cross-site CP utility** plus a per-site dashboard widget. Egress goes through the existing `App\Support\Http\OutboundUrlGuard`. The pure core (extraction, classification, result model) stays free of CP and HTTP coupling so a later extraction into a package remains mechanical, but no extraction is planned or committed to.

**Why.** Two research spikes (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1258 Policy 9, UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1262 Policy 13) both ask whether link rot is going unmanaged and whether platform tooling is warranted; both were open with no verdict. The generic half of the answer is commodity — `martinoak/statamic-link-checker` (MIT, Statamic 6) already does extraction, HEAD→GET probing, anchors, run history, an ignore list, a widget, and scheduling. What it cannot do is the half that makes this a *UAMS* tool, and each gap was verified against its source rather than its marketing: it drives a **bare Guzzle client with no SSRF guard**, following up to five redirects into private address space from editor-supplied URLs — precisely the surface UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#925, UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#927, and UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1013 were filed to close, mitigated only by a config denylist where this app uses an allowlist with per-hop DNS re-pinning; it scans **entries only**, missing globals and terms, which `App\Support\References\EntryReferenceRepointer` already traverses; it never sees a dangling `entries`-fieldtype relation, because that fieldtype stores **bare UUIDs** and its extractor only recognizes a `^(entry|asset|term)::` prefix (this repo has 107 `entries` fields); its `LinkResolver::isExternal()` compares only against the origin site's own host, so each of 255 sites treats the other 254 and `uamshealth.com` as third-party, where `App\Rules\FirstPartyUrl` + `config/first_party.php` already model ownership correctly; and its `IgnoreStore` is a flat JSON array of URLs in `storage/` with no user, timestamp, or occurrence granularity, so accepting one instance suppresses that URL everywhere.

In-core rather than a package because **every collaborator the feature is built on lives under `App\`** — `OutboundUrlGuard`, `FirstPartyUrl`, `EntryReferenceRepointer`, and `MigrationRedirectGenerator`'s routable-URL index — so a package would have to duplicate or extract them. That cost is observed, not projected: `uams-web/wordpress-importer` already carries a **parallel copy of `OutboundUrlGuard`**, an entire wave (PR UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1753) existed only to move a version constraint from `^0.6` to `^0.7` so a fix became reachable, and UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1725 is open to relocate tests that landed in the wrong repo. A package also buys no Control Panel affordance the app lacks: `Statamic::pushCpRoutes()` is already called from five app providers, two with `Nav::extend()`, and widgets register in `config/statamic/cp.php`.

**Rejected.** (a) *Adopt `martinoak/statamic-link-checker`* — the unguarded egress is a security regression this repo has already paid three times to close, and four of the thirteen requirements (first-party classification, `entries`-relation integrity, unverifiable-host handling, attributed occurrence-level dismissal) are absent; its MIT license means the good parts (the three-pass HEAD/GET/anchor pool and the `FoundLink` state model) can be lifted without taking the dependency. (b) *`jonassiewertsen/oh-dear`* — abandoned, Statamic 3–5 only, requires the Oh Dear SaaS, and crawls only publicly-reachable pages, so `inside.uams.edu` and unpublished states are invisible. (c) *`arturrossbach/linkwise`* — $49 per site is roughly $12.5k across 255 sites, and it covers none of anchors, redirect chains, asset existence, unverifiable hosts, attributed dismissal, or a cross-site roll-up. (d) *Leave it to SiteImprove/Monsido* — this is the 2026-06-23 #225 position, **superseded here**; that decision scoped #225 down to in-CP phrase/pattern search and parked link-checking with the external SaaS, which leaves Policy 9's "regularly checking those links" dependent on a tool with no view of draft content, no per-entry editing affordance, and no knowledge of Statamic references. (e) *A separate `uams-web/statamic-link-health` package* — inverts the dependency direction for zero present benefit; extraction stays available later if an external consumer ever appears. (f) *Extract at Launch by default* — commits to a repo split on a schedule rather than on evidence.

**Companion.** Epic #1762 and its slices. Superseded position: #225 (2026-06-23 decision, `## Decision` amended). Spikes resolved: UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1258, UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1262. Substrate: [`app/Support/Http/OutboundUrlGuard.php`](https://github.com/UAMS-Web/uams-statamic/blob/main/app/Support/Http/OutboundUrlGuard.php), [`app/Rules/FirstPartyUrl.php`](https://github.com/UAMS-Web/uams-statamic/blob/main/app/Rules/FirstPartyUrl.php), [`config/first_party.php`](https://github.com/UAMS-Web/uams-statamic/blob/main/config/first_party.php), [`app/Support/References/EntryReferenceRepointer.php`](https://github.com/UAMS-Web/uams-statamic/blob/main/app/Support/References/EntryReferenceRepointer.php). Evaluated alternative: [`martinoak/statamic-link-checker`](https://github.com/martinoak/statamic-link-checker).

## 2026-07-30 — Stache-isolated tests get their own Stache cache store (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1747)

**Decision.** `Tests\Support\StacheIsolatedTestCase` and `Tests\Support\SchemaFixtureStacheTestCase` now point `Stache::cacheStore()` at a per-test in-memory `array` store via [`Tests\Concerns\IsolatesStacheCache`](../tests/Concerns/IsolatesStacheCache.php), so a test that re-roots the Stache store *directories* also re-roots the *index* built from them. Isolation is a property of the base case, not of any individual test — no per-test opt-in, no allow-list.

**Why.** Statamic's `PreventsSavingStacheItemsToDisk` re-roots what the Stache reads **from disk**; it leaves the cache store those directories index into pointing at the worker's shared `file` cache — the same store the per-process Stache seed populates. That leaked in both directions. Erasing: the `Stache::clear()` on entry and exit of every isolated test forgets the paths, keys, and index entries of every handle `Collection::handles()` resolves at that moment, so the seeded real-`content/` index was destroyed repeatedly (70+ forgets measured in one band) and every later test in the worker re-traversed `content/` to rebuild it. Substituting: any entry query inside an isolated test builds a real collection handle's index **from the throwaway root** and persists it to the shared store, and teardown only clears what `Collection::handles()` still resolves — by then the fixture handles, not the real ones. An empty fixture tree writes an empty list, which is falsy in `Store::paths()` and self-heals on the next read; **one** fixture entry does not, and the next test reads a one-entry collection as authoritative. That asymmetry is why the failure was intermittent and silent: the content guards report the missing entries as an absent-submodule skip, so the band exits `0` having run fewer tests (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1740's measurement of the same shape: `paths=1`, `uri_index=1`, `blueprint {article: 1}`).

**Rejected.** (a) *Clear more aggressively at teardown* — teardown cannot enumerate the handles the test touched (that is exactly what `Collection::handles()` no longer knows by then), and it leaves the erase direction unfixed: the seeded index would still be destroyed on every isolated `setUp`. (b) *Give each test its own file-backed Stache cache directory* — buys nothing over an in-memory store that dies with the application, and pays per-test directory create/delete on Windows, the precise hazard UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1696 already had to guard in this base class. (c) *Fix the leaking tests one at a time* — the band only exposes the leak when a fixture handle happens to collide with a real one, so the observable set is a moving target; the defect is in the isolation contract, not in the tests that trip it. (d) *Patch `PreventsSavingStacheItemsToDisk` upstream* — worth reporting alongside UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1699, but the base classes that declare this project's isolation contract are ours, and a `composer` patch against a test-only trait would buy nothing a subclass does not.

**Companion.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1747 (`## Decision`); [`tests/Concerns/IsolatesStacheCache.php`](../tests/Concerns/IsolatesStacheCache.php) carries the mechanism, [`tests/Feature/StacheIsolationCacheStoreTest.php`](../tests/Feature/StacheIsolationCacheStoreTest.php) pins both directions. Unblocks UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1738 → UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1744 (the skip-shaped false green). Supersedes UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1740 as the explanation for the `knowledge_bases` starvation; UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1740's snapshot-copy defect is real and stays fixed.

## 2026-07-31 — Structural migration redirects: pattern rules for the unbounded shapes, a middleware for `?p=` (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1657)

**Decision.** Synthesize the structural WordPress URLs UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1641 deferred as four separable things rather than one uniform row-per-URL map. **(1)** The *finite* shapes — `?p=`/`?page_id=` ugly permalinks, attachment pages, date archives (`/YYYY`, `/YYYY/MM`), author archives — are planned as one literal `redirect` row each, deduped and folded through the existing collision-safe append. **(2)** The *unbounded* shapes — every nested feed (`/<anything>/feed/`) and all pagination (`/<archive>/page/<N>/`) — are planned as a single **PCRE pattern rule** each (`use_regex: true`), because no finite row set covers them; `FindRedirectRule` runs its exact-source pass first, so a pattern can only ever match a request that matched nothing else. **(3)** An archive with no Statamic equivalent targets the **`posts` collection's mount** when one exists, and otherwise falls through to UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1641's nearest-surviving-ancestor → `410` resolver — never the home page. **(4)** `?p=`/`?page_id=` is served by a new `RedirectWordPressQueryPermalinks` **middleware** on the `statamic.web` group, not by the 404 template, and both spellings are normalized onto the single stored `/?p=<id>` source. The author archive slug is derived in the app with `Str::slug()` from the importer's `wordpress_author_login`.

**Why.** The four shapes differ in the only dimension that matters — whether they enumerate. Rows work where the set is finite and give an editor something to retarget; they cannot express `/news/page/2..N` or a comment feed per post, where one pattern does. `?p=` is separable for a harder reason: `/?p=123` never 404s, because its *path* is the site root, so it renders the home page with a `200` and the 404 template that serves every other redirect never runs — and `FindRedirectRule` matches `request()->path()`, which carries no query string either. Only a middleware in front of the response can intercept it. Targeting the collection mount keeps every destination a URL that provably resolves, and inheriting UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1641's resolver for the rest keeps one rule for "no survivor" instead of two. The slug derivation lands here because WXR exports no `user_nicename` anywhere in the corpus — `dc:creator` carries the `user_login` (`Ashley`) while the archive URL uses the slug (`ashley`) — so `UAMS-Web/wordpress-importer#446` deliberately stamped the login and left `sanitize_title()` to the consumer that knows its own routing.

**Rejected.** (a) *A row per paginated URL, capped at N pages* — an arbitrary cap that is silently wrong above it and bloats the store below it. (b) *Rewriting `/<archive>/page/2` → `/<archive>?page=2` in a middleware* — better UX, but it makes a second serving path the redirects store cannot see or override, for a shape that only ever fires on a 404. (c) *A second stored row per `?page_id=<id>`* — doubles the largest row family to express what one normalization step covers. (d) *Extending `FindRedirectRule` to match `path?query`* — necessary but not sufficient (the request still 200s), and it widens a shipped, security-sensitive matcher for no gain. (e) *301 date and author archives to the site root* — the soft-404 `NotMigratedRedirectResolver` was explicitly built to avoid. (f) *410 every archive outright* — throws away a real destination on the sites that do mount a posts index. (g) *Emitting a per-post `/<post>/feed` row from each entry's original URL* — finite, but one row per entry for the lowest-value shape in the set.

**Companion.** Issue UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1657 `## Decision`; core generator UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1641 and its 2026-07-17 entry above; importer metadata `UAMS-Web/wordpress-importer#446`; parent guarantee #1640. Residual: the store is path-keyed, so a structural source that ~254 WordPress subsites all served (`/2019/05`, `/feed`) resolves to one rule — the same first-wins collision the UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1641 core already has.

## 2026-07-31 — The render breadth is not the JSON-LD compose cost, and the shared-node hoist already ships (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1801)

**Decision.** **No-go** on restructuring the JSON-LD graph walk to reduce its Antlers render count. The breadth is real but cheap, and the one structural reduction worth having — hoisting the shared subject node out of the per-node walk — already ships as [`SchemaJsonLdFragmentCache`](../app/Services/SchemaTemplates/SchemaJsonLdFragmentCache.php) (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#937) and is on by default in production. No implementation ticket is filed against this hypothesis. The one genuine finding — that 19 of the 20 `SchemaJsonLd*` suites measure a regime production never runs in — is spun off instead.

**Why.** Measured on the `DiagnosticLab` location fixture at `content@a79b0686`, instrumenting both `Parse::template()` sites in `SchemaOutputService`. One standalone location node issues **1204 renders** (1205 through the full compose path), close to the 1105 UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1801 quotes. Three findings, in order of what they overturn:

1. **The cost is not distributed across the breadth.** Exclusive per-render time (nesting subtracted via absolute interval containment) has a **median of 1.6ms**, not the ~10ms the issue assumed — mean 41.8ms, max 9316ms. **50% of exclusive render time sits in 5 of the 1204 renders (0.4%); 80% in 25 (2.1%); 95% in 105 (8.7%).** The other 1099 renders together are ~5%. The top five are `founder` (9316ms), `contactPoint` (5693ms), `hasCertification` (4590ms), `hasCredential` (4278ms) and `memberOf` (1898ms) — every one a `schema_thing_from_relationship` traversal whose cost is entry and term resolution *inside* the render, not Antlers parsing. "Cut the render count" was always aimed at the wrong quantity.
2. **97.8% of the renders are one shared node.** Attributed by the `SchemaTemplateNodeBuildStack`, **1177 of 1204 renders (99.9% of exclusive render time) build the location's `parentOrganization` — the shared UAMS organization node** and its `subjectOf` WebSite and founder/media-contact people. The location's own 26 properties cost **27 renders and 57ms (0.1%)**. That subtree is **byte-identical (49,399 bytes) across two different location pages with the fragment cache off**, so its page-invariance is a property of the content, not an artifact of caching.
3. **The hoist already exists, and every prior measurement had it switched off.** `SchemaJsonLdFragmentCache::enabled()` is gated behind `json_ld_cache.enabled`, and **19 of the 20 `SchemaJsonLd*` suites disable that flag in `beforeEach`** to stop payloads leaking through a shared page-output key. So UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1797, UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1800 and UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1801 all measured a cache-off regime. A/B on the second page of a compose pair, same process, after `Cache::flush()` and one cold compose: **cache off — 1206 renders / 113.6s; cache on — 73 renders / 20.6s.** 94% fewer renders, 5.5× faster, and the **emitted JSON-LD is byte-identical in both regimes on both pages**. Both `enabled` and `fragments_enabled` default to `true` and nothing in the repo overrides them, so on shipped defaults the full breadth is paid once per bust rather than once per page — which is what `schema-templates:warm-json-ld-cache` exists to absorb. The deployment environment is not visible from the repo, so that is a statement about the defaults, not a reading of live config.

**Rejected.** (a) *Memoize same-input repeats* — the definitive answer to the issue's question 3 is that they **do** exist, contrary to what UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1800 concluded from a weaker key: keyed on the template plus a full context signature with the mutable emit context excluded, 90 of 1204 renders (7.5%) repeat an earlier render's inputs and **all 90 produced byte-identical output**. But they are all cheap leaves (`{{ official_website }}`, `{{ permalink }}`) worth ~178ms — **0.3% of the compose**. The easy path is open and not worth walking. (b) *Short-circuit property templates that cannot contribute output* — 689 renders (57.2%) do emit nothing, but they cost 3313ms exclusive, **6.6% of render time and 6.2% of the wall**, and deciding emptiness in general means rendering. (c) *Hoist the shared node out of the walk by hand* — this is what the fragment cache does, cross-request, already proven byte-identical; rebuilding it inside the walk would alter production JSON-LD for no gain.

**Residual.** The first compose after a bust still pays the full 1204 renders — UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#937's cold-compose cost is mitigated, not removed. Fragment keys embed the site, so each site pays its own first compose. The page-invariance check covers two `health`-site locations, not every content type. The context signature reduces objects to `class#id` and caps recursion at depth 6, so the 90 repeats are proven by identical *output* over one compose rather than by input purity.

**Companion.** Issue UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1801 `## Decision` and its findings comment; spun-off follow-up on the suite cache regime. Supersedes the residual UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1797 recorded as "reducing it is a genuine production win".

## 2026-07-31 — Rector's version-gated sets stay unpinned; the deps ticket owns the sweep a bump causes (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1722)

**Decision.** Leave `withComposerBased(laravel: true, phpunit: true)` in [`rector.php`](../rector.php) **unpinned**, and accept that a dependency bump can add rule applications on files nobody edited. The recurring `Update dependencies (Iteration N)` ticket owns the resulting sweep: when a bump activates a new version-gated set, that ticket's own PR re-zeros the gate. This closes the residual the 2026-07-28 entry recorded against UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1722.

**Why.** The drift lands where it is already in scope, so it never surprises an unrelated PR. Verified mechanism: `withComposerBased()` resolves through `SetManager::matchBySetGroups()` → `InstalledPackageResolver::resolve()`, which reads **`vendor/composer/installed.json`** — the materialized vendor tree — not `composer.json` constraints and not `composer.lock` directly. The `rector` CI job installs with `composer install … --prefer-dist`, which materializes exactly the committed lock, so **in CI the active rule set is a pure function of `composer.lock`**. A new set can therefore only activate on a commit that changes the lock, and by the [dep-update pair convention](#2026-07-29--the-recurring-dep-update-chore-is-a-pair-of-tickets-one-per-repo-iteration-end--wordpress-importer) that is the deps ticket's own branch — the one branch where a Rector sweep is already expected. `DescriptionPropertyToDescriptionAttributeRector` (6 files) and `FillablePropertyToFillableAttributeRector` (1) in the UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1721 backlog were exactly such rules, and both landed as ordinary mechanical rewrites.

**Rejected.** (a) *Pin the Laravel/PHPUnit sets explicitly* — trades a visible, self-announcing red on the deps PR for an invisible staleness that nothing reports, which is the decay mode UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1720 → UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1721 → UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1722 exists to end, moved one level down; it also duplicates version knowledge `composer.lock` already holds. (b) *Drop `withComposerBased()` and hand-maintain the set list* — same staleness, and it discards the version-awareness that makes the Laravel rules correct for the installed major. (c) *Exempt the version-gated Laravel rules from the gating profile* — those three rules are mechanical and safe to make mandatory; the sweep-only carve-out in `rector.php` is reserved for rules whose suggestion is a judgment call, and diluting that criterion to solve a scheduling problem would blur it.

**Residual (verified).** Locally, `installed.json` reflects the last `composer install`, **not** the checked-out branch's lock, so after switching to a branch whose lock differs, `composer install` must run before `composer test:refactor` is trustworthy. CI is unaffected — every job installs from the lock on a clean checkout.

**Companion.** [`.claude/skills/update-dependencies-ticket/SKILL.md`](../.claude/skills/update-dependencies-ticket/SKILL.md) carries the operational step. Closes the residual on UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1722 recorded in the 2026-07-28 entry.

## 2026-07-31 — Claude Code cloud sessions are scoped to catalog, issue, and docs work; app work stays local (cloud environments)

**Decision.** Configure one cloud environment — **Trusted** network access, no environment variables, and a setup script that installs `gh`, `php8.4-imagick`, and runs `npm ci` — and use cloud sessions for **catalog, issue, and docs work only**. App work (tests, Pint, Larastan, Rector) stays on a local checkout, and [`local-ci`](../.claude/skills/local-ci/SKILL.md) remains the gate. The full operational record is [`docs/runbooks/claude-code-cloud-sessions.md`](runbooks/claude-code-cloud-sessions.md).

**Why.** The scope limit is **structural, not a preference**. `api.github.com` is unreachable for generic HTTP clients in a cloud session, so every Composer `dist` URL fails and the only workable strategy is `preferred-install=source`. That covers 231 of 232 locked packages; `phpstan/phpstan` publishes **dist-only** from 2.1.30 onward — a deliberate upstream fix to its ~7.15 GB repository ([phpstan/phpstan#10921](https://github.com/phpstan/phpstan/issues/10921), [#13617](https://github.com/phpstan/phpstan/issues/13617)) — so it has no source path to take. `add_repo` would resolve the 403, but it refuses **cross-owner-tier adds**: a session holding `uams-web` repos cannot attach `phpstan/phpstan`, and seeding a session with `phpstan/phpstan` instead makes every `UAMS-Web` add cross-tier by the same rule. `composer install --no-dev` therefore is the only install that completes, and it drops all 82 dev packages including Pest, PHPUnit, Pint, and Larastan. A cloud session can boot the app and resolve content (verified: 19 collections) but cannot run a test. Independently, 4 vCPUs against a suite that takes ~20 minutes on 12 workers would make it the wrong home regardless.

**Rejected.** *The stock Default environment* — ships no `gh`, which every issue, PR, and board workflow here drives. *Raising network access to Full or Custom* — `api.github.com` and `codeload.github.com` are already on the Trusted allowlist; the 403 comes from the GitHub proxy, which is independent of the access level, so this widens egress for no gain. *A PAT in the environment variables* — the dialog states values are readable by anyone using the environment and there is no secrets store. *`--prefer-dist`* — every dist URL is an `api.github.com` zipball, so all 230 packages fail and fall back to a git mirror anyway, after a per-package CONNECT timeout: 14m28s producing nothing. *`composer config use-github-api false`* — fails faster, not better. *Hand-seeding a zip into Composer's dist cache* — indistinguishable from routing around a policy denial, and blocked by the auto-mode classifier. *Querying Projects V2 from a cloud session* — GraphQL is pinned to a PR-review allowlist, so board reads are refused outright; UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1825 commits an exclusion snapshot instead.

**Residual.** `gh api --paginate` truncates at page 1 behind the proxy and is silent through a pipe (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1823). Repository attachment is session-scoped, so `add_repo` cannot be pre-baked into a setup script or `SessionStart` hook — it is a per-session step. The `preferred-install=source` strategy is fragile by construction: any package that adopts dist-only publishing later breaks the bootstrap the same way, silently, whenever its next release lands.

**Companion.** [`docs/runbooks/claude-code-cloud-sessions.md`](runbooks/claude-code-cloud-sessions.md). Issues surfaced: UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1823 (pagination), UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1824 (`afk-waves` forest null-prune), UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1825 (project-3/4 exclusion snapshot), UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1826 (`min-release-age`), UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1830 (undeclared `ext-imagick`).

## 2026-07-31 — Assert "completed cleanly" by capturing the throwable, never by inverting `toThrow()` (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1759)

**Decision.** A Pest test meaning "this call did not blow up" asserts `expect(throwableFrom(fn () => …))->toBeNull()`, via the `throwableFrom(callable): ?Throwable` helper in [`tests/Support/Throwables.php`](../tests/Support/Throwables.php), loaded suite-wide from `tests/Pest.php`. `expect(fn () => …)->not->toThrow(…)` is **not** a way to express that and is not to be reintroduced. `toThrow()` keeps both of its legitimate forms: a **concrete class** for a positive throw assertion, and a **non-class string** for Pest's message-substring match (`->toThrow('Modifier [int] not found')`).

**Why.** `->not->toThrow(X)` inverts a `toThrow(X)` that fails its `assertInstanceOf`, so a throwable that is *not* an `X` reads as **success** — `->not->toThrow(Exception::class)` stays green on a `TypeError`, or on the "call to a member function on null" `Error` that a deleted guard produces, which is exactly the regression such a test exists to catch. `Throwable::class` cannot repair it and makes it strictly worse: `Throwable` is an **interface**, so Pest's `class_exists()` check in `Mixins/Expectation.php` returns false and `toThrow()` degrades to `assertStringContainsString('Throwable', $e->getMessage())`, which `OppositeExpectation` then inverts into an unconditional pass — green whether an `Error` is thrown, a `RuntimeException` is thrown, or nothing is thrown at all. Confirmed by running it, not by reading it. The suite held exactly one such site; an audit of every `toThrow()` argument in `tests/` confirmed `Throwable` was the only one for which `class_exists()` is false.

**Rejected.** *`->not->toThrow(Throwable::class)`* — the degradation above; unconditionally green, so it asserts nothing. *Naming both `Exception::class` and `Error::class`* — closes the type hole, but Pest evaluates the closure once per expectation, so any subject carrying a Mockery `->once()` breaks and any side effect runs twice. *A bare call with no assertion at all* — leaves the test with zero assertions, which PHPUnit reports as risky and which is the same false-green class UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1759 exists to remove. *An inline `try`/`catch` at each call site* — what the helper does, restated at thirteen of them.

**Residual.** The helper proves *a* throwable did not escape; it does not prove the subject ran. A test whose code under test is unreachable — an earlier guard returning first — passes either way. `ExcludeSelfScopeTest` was exactly that: fixing the assertion was not enough, and it needed a resolvable edit-context fixture before deleting the guard it names turned it red. Mutation-verify the rewrite; do not assume the assertion fix alone made the test falsifiable.

**Companion.** Issue UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1759 `## Decision`; PR UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1794. The mechanism is documented at length in the [`tests/Support/Throwables.php`](../tests/Support/Throwables.php) file header. Parent: UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1719, which adopted `pestphp/pest-plugin-phpstan` and whose `pest.expectation.redundant` rule surfaced the 99 sites.

## 2026-07-31 — Content guards discriminate on the submodule's presence, and the skip baseline ships at 2 (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1738, UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1744)

**Decision.** The content guards key on a **filesystem fact** — `content/collections/*.yaml` being populated — rather than on a `null` lookup: submodule absent still `markTestSkipped()`, submodule present now **fails**, naming what did not resolve. The discriminator lives in one place ([`tests/Support/ContentSubmodule.php`](../tests/Support/ContentSubmodule.php)) shared by the `RequiresContentSubmodule` trait and `KnowledgeBaseMountResolutionTest`'s top-level guard. On top of it, the skip-baseline gate reinstated from `wave/local-ci-hardening` ships with `parallel: 2` and `serial: 0`, and the `phpunit` job gains `imagick` so the runner can reach that number.

**Why.** A `null` lookup is the *symptom*; "the submodule is absent" is the *condition* the guards name, and conflating them converted every infrastructure failure into a pass — three `KnowledgeBaseMountResolutionTest` tests self-skipped in a fully populated checkout while the band reported `PASS`. The presence check is cheap and unambiguous, and it preserves the bare-worktree / `local-ci --skip-submodule` behavior UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#856 and UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#900 were built for. The baseline of **2** is the two `SchemaThingFromRelationshipResolverTest` cases guarding on a `subject_matter::cellulitis` term the content repo has never contained; it is evidence, not assertion — the UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1786 soak ran ten consecutive parallel bands reporting `2 skipped, 6068 passed (16836 assertions)` after UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1747's fix landed as UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1785.

**Split, not hardened, for data preconditions.** Three `requireContentEntry()` call sites — in `KnowledgeBaseEmptyTopicTest`, `KnowledgeBaseEmptyKnowledgeBaseTest`, and `KnowledgeBaseDirectChildCountTest` — guard an *editorial* fact ("no published topic has a published descendant article"), not a lookup that must resolve. Hardening those would red the band on a content edit and point the reader at a starved Stache worker that does not exist, so they move to a second, explicitly named entry point (`skipWithoutQualifyingContentEntry()` → `ContentSubmodule::skipWithoutQualifyingEntry()`) that still always skips. `requireContentEntry()` now means strictly "this can never legitimately be null".

**Rejected.** *Raising the baseline to absorb the live starvation skips* — [`tests/skip-baseline.json`](../tests/skip-baseline.json) exists to make degradation deliberate and reviewable, so recording a known defect as the expected value would use that file to conceal exactly what it exists to surface; the gate waited for UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1747 instead. *Sequencing the gate after UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1740* — the snapshot-copy defect is real and stays fixed, but instrumenting the guard showed a starved worker seeding with `copy_failures=0` and still resolving 1 of 30 `knowledge_bases` entries, so UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1740 was never the cause. *Capturing a skip reason from the JUnit report* — PHPUnit 13's `JunitXmlLogger` writes a bare `<skipped/>` and paratest's merger does the same, so identity is all the pipeline can prove and any reason capture is dead code. *A Node guard* — `ci.yml`'s `phpunit` job sets up PHP but not Node. *Converting the 68 bare `Requires the content/ submodule` call sites in the same change* — a blind mechanical sweep of guards this session cannot execute (no access to the private content repo) risks reddening every PR; deferred and named rather than silently skipped.

**Residual.** The 68 bare idiom call sites outside the trait (measured across 49 files) keep the old shape, so the false green persists there — tracked separately. The serial band's baseline of 0 is asserted rather than soak-measured (the UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1786 evidence covers the parallel band only); this PR's own gating local-CI run is what proves it. The `subject_matter::cellulitis` pair is a genuine fork (add the term to `content/`, or repoint the tests at a term that exists) and is deliberately left alone here: deciding it moves the baseline off 2.

**Companion.** Issues UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1738 and UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1744 (`## Decision`); [`scripts/check-skip-baseline.php`](../scripts/check-skip-baseline.php) + [`tests/skip-baseline.json`](../tests/skip-baseline.json) carry the gate; blocked on the 2026-07-30 `UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1747` entry below.

## 2026-07-31 — Podcast episodes sync app-side from the public DoctorPodcasting feed, not the WXR importer (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1803)

**Decision.** Source UAMS Health Talk episodes from the **public** vendor feed (`podcastfeed/287`) plus the public per-episode pages (`client/uams/item/<customid>`), ingested by a **scheduled app-side sync** in `uams-statamic` — not by `wordpress-importer`. The sync handles all four transitions (new, changed, departed, returned); a departed episode is **unpublished, never deleted**. The credentialed vendor portal is **evidence already extracted, not a data dependency**. `season_number` is recorded as **unmappable**, not pending. An episode whose speakers cannot all be resolved to `people` entries is **not imported at all** — the sync records each unresolved speaker as a row in a CP-editable mapping and re-checks on the next run, importing the episode once every speaker resolves. `UAMS-Web/wordpress-importer#424` is closed as not-planned; `#500` closes as "source identified, not ours to import"; `#443` is unaffected.

**Why.** The episodes were never WordPress content — `uamshealth.com/podcast/` renders a third-party widget `<iframe>`, which is why no podcast post type or episode metadata appears across all 259 WXR exports and why the migration API (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#358) could never have surfaced them. Both vendor endpoints answer unauthenticated, giving 114 episodes with duration and show notes at 114/114, credits at 114/114, transcripts at 111/114, and a `uamshealth.com/provider/<slug>/` join key on 45 — so there is no vendor-access blocker. The importer is a WXR tool and rewriting it into a general content importer was rejected; more decisively, the chosen posture is *ongoing* reconciliation rather than a one-shot load, because the series publishes roughly monthly and the vendor withdraws an episode when its featured clinician departs (three already are), expressing that only by the item disappearing. Deferring an unmapped episode rather than holding a draft keeps the state machine honest: with no entry until it resolves, an *unpublished* entry can only ever mean "withdrawn from the feed" — no second reason, no stored reason field, and no risk that a feed return republishes a still-unmapped episode.

**Rejected.** *Portal as the inventory source* — its Content Library reports *file* status, showing all three withdrawn episodes as `Created and Sent`, so importing from it would republish content UAMS deliberately took down for departed clinicians. *Capturing publication status as a stored field* — redundant (the feed encodes it by omission) and unsustainable, since refreshing it needs a credentialed login; the numbering reconciliation is documented in the spec instead. *Recording dates and Airtable record ids* — no target field and no join not already available on `<customid>`. *A one-shot import* — correct only on the day it runs. *Name-matching speakers against `people`* — the source's name strings are dirty (`Tyler Rives, M.D`), and the ingestion must not guess. *Importing with empty credits* — ships incomplete episodes silently and leaves #1685's provider list thin with no signal. *Holding unmapped episodes as drafts* — creates a second reason for an entry to be dark and 69 placeholder drafts at launch. *Publishing from a listener on the mapping's save* — the fan-out and re-entrancy surface `BoundedRelationshipProcessor` (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1406) exists to bound, and the same cascade UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1551 rejected. *A config-file crosswalk* (`config/crosswalk.php`) — every new speaker would need a deploy.

**Residual.** 69 of 114 episodes have no provider link, carrying 66 distinct speakers — some physicians whose item page merely omitted it, some non-physician staff who may never warrant a `people` entry — so the mapping backlog sits on the critical path to a populated archive, and a "no entry expected" disposition is required to stop the long tail blocking its episodes permanently. The video ("vodcast") rendition is unmodeled and out of scope here — the feed is audio-only, 10 episodes have video, and the presentation fork is #1804.

**Companion.** [`docs/specs/uams-health-talk-podcast.md`](specs/uams-health-talk-podcast.md) — the full source and rendition record. Issue UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1803 (ingestion + reconciliation), #1804 (video epic: #1805 vendor ask, #1806 contract review, UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1807 presentation decision, #1808 build), #1685 (the first-party provider-lens render this unblocks). Closes `UAMS-Web/wordpress-importer#500` and `#424`.

## 2026-08-03 — The table-cell sanitizer fails closed: a throw there is a stored-XSS fail-open (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1853)

**Decision.** `TableCellSanitizer::sanitize()` never throws. On any `Throwable` it abandons the on-disk definition cache and compiles the definition again in-process (keeping the allowlist, so links still render), and if even that fails it returns `e(strip_tags($html))`.

**Why.** Found by the adversarial review of UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1853 and reproduced twice: Statamic catches a throwing modifier and returns the value it held **before** the modifier ran — `NodeProcessor::runModifier()` rethrows only when `app.debug` is on — and `resources/views/sets/_table.antlers.html` emits that value unescaped, Antlers being raw-by-default here. So in production a throw inside `sanitize()` published the raw cell verbatim; the review rendered `<script>alert(document.cookie)</script>` live through the real modifier. Two triggers reach it, both from the definition cache: HTMLPurifier raises `E_USER_WARNING` when it cannot create its type subdirectory, and `unserialize()` warns on a `.ser` a concurrent writer truncated — and Laravel promotes both to an `ErrorException` in *every* environment, not only under test. The defect predates UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1853 (`ensureDirectoryExists()` could throw the same way), but UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1853 is the slice that touches this path, so it was fixed here rather than deferred. A sanitizer is a security control: the only safe direction to fail is closed.

**Rejected.** *Escape immediately on any failure* — safe, but a transient cache problem would strip every table's links site-wide; the in-process recompile restores full behavior. *Return `''`* — discards the cell's text for no security gain over escaping it. *Fix only the cache triggers* (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1869) — leaves the fail-open intact for any unforeseen throw, which is the property that actually matters.

**Companion.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1853 `## Decision`; PR UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1870. Related residual UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1869.

## 2026-08-03 — HTMLPurifier cache: race-safe *and* per-worker; `SerializerPermissions` withdrawn for want of a repro (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1853)

**Decision.** `TableCellSanitizer` creates its serializer-cache directory race-safely — `File::makeDirectory(…, $force: true)`, then `clearstatcache()` + a native `is_dir()` re-read — **and** the path is scoped to the per-worker `TEST_TOKEN` in a new `config/tables.php`, mirroring `config/cache.php`. Production and serial runs keep one shared, warm cache. A directory that cannot be created now degrades (`Cache.DefinitionImpl => null`, logged once) instead of throwing on every cell. Setting `Cache.SerializerPermissions` to `null` — which would make HTMLPurifier's *own* bare `mkdir()` of the `HTML/` and `URI/` subdirectories race-safe — was implemented and then **withdrawn**; it splits to **UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1869**.

**Why.** The ticket's own rule decided the first half: prefer tokenizing over `$force` when the directory's *contents* are also shared, and they are — HTMLPurifier writes each `.ser` with a non-atomic `file_put_contents()` and reads it back behind nothing but a `file_exists()`. Both halves shipped rather than one because they cover different populations: tokenizing fixes the parallel band, and race-safe creation fixes production, where `TEST_TOKEN` is unset and concurrent PHP-FPM workers still share one path on a cold cache. The verification was run on the **shared** path precisely so the race-safety claim does not lean on the tokenization: 12 failures in 50 concurrent children before, 0 in 100 after. `SerializerPermissions` was withdrawn because the same harness could not reproduce the race it addresses — 0 failures in 40 — the definition compile between the two creations apparently de-synchronizing the cohort; and because `HTMLPurifier::$config` carries a legacy `@type` tag PHPStan does not read, so it could not be pinned by a test without the inline `@var` override `phpstan.neon.dist` forbids. A concurrency fix with no reproduction behind it, and no way to test it, is scope creep rather than defense in depth.

**Rejected.** *`$force: true` alone* — leaves ~12 workers reading and writing each other's `.ser` files. *Tokenize only* — a no-op in production, where the race is equally real. *Tokenize inside `app/`* — `config/testing.php` already records the standing reason to keep `env()` out of runtime code (it returns null once config is cached); `config/cache.php` is the precedent. *Extend `scripts/check-parallel-safe-tests.php`* — its rules are static and scoped to `tests/`; this defect is in `app/`, and a rule broad enough to catch it would flag every legitimate shared storage path.

**Companion.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1853 `## Decision`; PR UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1870; residual filed as UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1869.

## 2026-08-03 — Nonce-gate `script-src` now; `'unsafe-eval'` splits to its own ticket (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1741)

**Decision.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1741 adds `script-src` to `csp.nonce_directives` and removes `'unsafe-inline'`, and stops there. `'unsafe-eval'` and the `@alpinejs/csp` migration split to **UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1859**. The Control Panel question is struck from UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1741 and left to **#667**, which already carries an acceptance criterion for `cp/*` handling at flip time.

**Why.** Both of UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1741's stated blockers were mis-priced, in opposite directions. Blocker 1 **does not exist**: `statamic-peak-tools`' `_button_attributes.antlers.html` is dead code here — no `_button` component exists in `resources/views/components/`, the addon ships none, and the only occurrence of the string `button_attributes` anywhere is inside the addon's own updater script. `resources/views/**` emits **zero** inline event-handler attributes, and both inline `<script>` blocks we control already carry `nonce="{{ csp_nonce }}"`. So there is no view to publish and no sweep to run; the change is the config plus its test, and the existing `it('retains unsafe allowances on script-src')` case simply inverts. Blocker 2 is far cheaper in *code* than UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1741 claimed — that text describes the older CSP build, while `@alpinejs/csp` 3.15.12 ships an AST parser and interpreter covering `Binary`, `Call`, `Member`, `Object`, `Conditional`, `Assignment`, `Unary`, `Update` and `Array` expressions, contains zero `new Function` (standard Alpine has 7), and every component here is already registered via `Alpine.data()`. Its real cost is **verification**: a bundle swap re-tests every interactive surface on a patient-facing site. Splitting is therefore right for a reason opposite to the one UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1741 gave — not because the migration is expensive to write, but because a nearly-free config-only win should not wait on a front-end regression pass. The two allowances are independent: `'unsafe-eval'` cannot execute an inline `<script>` element, so removing `'unsafe-inline'` delivers the whole injected-script defense alone.

**Rejected.** *Ship both in UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1741* — one browser-verification pass instead of two, but it delays the config win behind a swap touching Find-a-Doc filters, the gallery lightbox, lite-video, signature and replicator fields, and the calculators. *Keep `'unsafe-eval'` permanently* — premature to close given the corrected code cost; UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1859 holds it open. *Decide `cp/*` here* — #667 owns it, and deciding it twice invites drift.

**Companion.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1741 `## Decision`; follow-up UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1859; #667 (enforce-flip, owner of the `exclude_paths` call); closed predecessor UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#668. **Residual.** Google Tag Manager propagates its nonce to tags it injects, but a "Custom HTML" tag in the container could still emit an un-nonced inline script — unverifiable from this repo, and the reason UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1741 keeps a browser-verification criterion. Report-Only means it surfaces as a report, not a break, until #667 flips.

## 2026-08-03 — SVG stays; upload sanitization is the control, plus an at-rest scan for what it cannot see (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1739)

**Decision.** The five containers with no `validate:` rules get them, scoped to their actual contents: `logos` → `svg` only, `logos_unrestricted` → `svg,png`, `images` → raster web formats, `files` → a broader allowlist plus a size cap (it is the general-purpose bucket), `form_entry_uploads` → its document and image types. **SVG stays everywhere it is used.** The control is Statamic's existing `svg_sanitization_on_upload`, asserted by a new test, **plus an at-rest scan** over stored SVGs for `<script>`, event-handler attributes and `<foreignObject>`. Serve-time hardening splits to **#1860**. The `logo_image` instructions in `logo_list_item.yaml` are rewritten to say PNG **or** SVG with the pixel minimum scoped to raster.

**Why.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1739 framed "is SVG genuinely needed?" as open and read the field's "must be a PNG" instructions as authoritative. The repo says otherwise from four directions: **UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#72** (closed) shipped SVG rendering in the logo-list block — which is why the item partial branches on `extension == 'svg'` to a plain `<img>`; **UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#50** (closed) added SVG→PNG rasterization; `app/Rules/AssetMinimumDimensions.php` explicitly exempts SVG from pixel minimums; and **#832** is an open work order to upload brand logo SVGs. The instructions are the stale artifact. Sanitization then turns out to already ship — `Statamic\Assets\Uploader::write()` runs every `.svg` through `rhukster/dom-sanitizer` — so keeping SVG costs a test, not a build. The gap actually worth closing is **at rest**: `public/assets` is a separate submodule repository where a file can be committed directly, bypassing the uploader entirely, which is how the 64 SVGs now in `logos` arrived. They are clean today; nothing keeps them so. `logos` is SVG-only because every stored file is SVG, #832's work order is entirely SVG, and UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#50's generated PNGs live outside the container in `public/img/brand-raster`.

**Rejected.** *Drop `image/svg+xml` from `logo_list_item.yaml`* — proposed first on the stale-instructions reading, withdrawn once UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#72 surfaced. *Drop SVG at the container* — not viable; `logos` is 64/64 SVG. *Sanitizer without the at-rest scan* — matches UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1739's original criterion but leaves uncovered exactly the path the current files took. *Serve-time headers now* — correct defense in depth, split to #1860 because it is web-server configuration this repo does not own. *`svg,png,webp`* / *permissive `svg,png,jpg,jpeg,webp`* for the logo containers — WebP is used by no logo in either, so the rule would describe a hypothetical; JPEG has no transparency and renders a white box on colored backgrounds.

**Companion.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1739 `## Decision`; follow-up #1860; UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#72 / UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#50 / #832 / #719. **Correction this decision carries:** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1739's `## Out of scope` claimed an enforcing CSP would blunt script in a directly-navigated SVG. It would not. `public/.htaccess` routes to `index.php` only when `REQUEST_FILENAME !-f`, so an existing file under `public/assets/` is served by the web server and never reaches PHP — `SecurityHeaders` never runs, and the response carries **no CSP at all**. Neither #667 nor UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1741 changes that; #1860 does. **Residual.** The scan proves what is on disk at run time, not between runs; and `rhukster/dom-sanitizer` is a third-party allowlist whose bypasses we inherit.

## 2026-08-03 — The browser suite joins the default gate; the eleven-day gap was memory, not cost (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1727)

**Decision.** `browser` moves into local-CI's default `all` set, staying last in the cost-ordered chain, and its `npm run build` / `npm run cp:build` become conditional on the `build` job not having already run in the same worktree (a `--jobs browser`-only run still builds).

**Why.** The opt-in framing treats this as a cost trade, and it is not: the suite's expense did not cause four CP smokes to sit red on `main` for eleven days — **needing a human to remember** did. The opt-in worked exactly as designed. So every option that keeps a person in the trigger loop reproduces the incident, and default-on is the only one that cannot silently miss. The price is roughly 130s against a gate already dominated by the Pest band — on the order of a tenth of a validation run — and part of that is recoverable, because in a full run the `browser` job currently repeats two builds the `build` job just did. With remote Actions dormant behind `RUN_REMOTE_CI`, this is also the only path on which the CP smokes and the UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1046 axe-core accessibility gate ever execute at all.

**Rejected.** *Path-triggered auto-opt-in* (auto-enable on a diff touching `tests/Browser/`, `tests/Support/CpSmoke.php`, `resources/js/`, or CP-facing code, reusing the changed-file set `local-ci.mjs` already computes for `pint`) — near-free and it would have caught this bug, since UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1623 touched `CpSmoke.php`; rejected because it swaps a list that drifts for a memory that lapsed, and UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1726 is the counterexample — a Pest and plugin upgrade broke the suite through `composer.lock`, which no path list would name. *A static test-lint* on `tests/Support/` return types — catches this bug and nothing else; the suite still never runs. *Accept and document the gap* — defensible if browser smokes were a pre-release check, but with remote CI off there is no pre-release gate for them to belong to, so it means no coverage.

**Companion.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1727 `## Decision`; suite origin UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#852 / UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1009 / UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1046; kill-switch UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#980. Sibling gate decision from the same pass: UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1731. **Residual — now closed, and the figure was wrong.** Measured at the UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1727 build on a correct Pest 5.0.1 install: `vendor/bin/pest tests/Browser` is **336.8s** as shipped (17 passed, 82 assertions — 322.1s for the 14 tests that predate this change, plus ~15s for the three UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1854 guards it adds), not ~130s. Parity basis — worktree at `f30407d39`, `php -d memory_limit=4G` exactly as the runner invokes it, assets freshly built, and **zero** competing `pest`/`phpunit`/`paratest`/`local-ci` processes machine-wide, verified by PowerShell (`Win32_Process`) immediately before the run. The quiet state is load-bearing rather than ceremonial: the identical suite measured **713.3s** earlier the same day beside another session's band, so contention inflated it 2.2x and either number taken alone would have been published as fact. The correction does **not** overturn the decision, because the decision never rested on the cost: it rested on the eleven-day gap being caused by human memory, which 322s changes not at all. It does correct the magnitude: against the parallel band's measured ~806s (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1786) this is a substantial fraction rather than the claimed tenth of a validation run. Deliberately not restated as a single ratio — a full `all` run was not timed end to end here, and replacing one unmeasured ratio with another is the error this correction exists to undo. The build de-duplication shipped alongside means the *added* cost is the suite alone, since `npm run build` + `npm run cp:build` are no longer paid twice in one worktree.

## 2026-08-03 — Gate the mutation *tooling*, not the mutants — 9 seconds (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1731)

**Decision.** A `mutate` job joins the cost-ordered chain in `ci.yml` and `scripts/local-ci.mjs`. It runs `--mutate` scoped to **one small class** and fails on a zero mutation count or a sub-100% score. It does not run mutation testing over the suite, and it does not change the `adversarial-review` rule's per-change, author-side scoping — which is documented in `tests/README.md` alongside it.

**Why.** The incident (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1728) was not a mutant escaping a weak test; it was the tooling silently reporting `0 Mutations for 0 Files created` — a false green. Gating the tooling is a different and much cheaper thing, and it was measured rather than estimated: `vendor/bin/pest tests/Unit/Support/UamsAlert/AlertSiteKeyResolverTest.php --mutate --path=app/Support/UamsAlert --class="App\Support\UamsAlert\AlertSiteKeyResolver" --covered-only` returns `8 Mutations for 1 Files created`, `Score: 100.00%`, in **9 seconds** — matching UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1730's own verification table exactly. That is cheaper than several links already in the chain and asserts precisely the signature that shipped.

**Rejected.** *Leave it author-side and document it* — free, but a tooling regression then surfaces only when someone runs `composer mutate` by hand, which is the identical blind-spot shape UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1727 documents for the browser suite. *A diff-scoped mutation job* — closest to the rule's intent and still the theoretically right answer, but it needs a reliable changed-file-to-class mapping and costs minutes, not seconds; left unbuilt until needed.

**Accepted cost.** The job promotes a coverage driver (PCOV, or Xdebug) from optional to a **required** local-CI toolchain floor, alongside the existing `npm >= 11.10.0` one. A "skip when no driver" fallback is deliberately not adopted — it would make the job a false green, which is the exact failure it exists to catch. The smoke's subject class must carry a comment explaining why it was chosen, so a later refactor draining its mutable surface cannot quietly turn the gate into a no-op.

**Companion.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1731 `## Decision`; the breakage UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1728 and its fix UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1730; the [`pcov-setup`](../.claude/skills/pcov-setup/SKILL.md) skill. Sibling gate decision from the same pass: UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1727. Extends the cost-ordered-chain decisions of 2026-07-30 and 2026-07-15 — one more link, same contract.

## 2026-08-04 — Slowest-class ceiling is 700s, not UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1537's 300s (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1890)

**Decision.** [`scripts/check-slowest-class.php`](../scripts/check-slowest-class.php) commits a **700s** per-class ceiling rather than the 300s UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1537 named. Measuring the band to confirm 300s showed it cannot ship: **ten classes exceed it**, so committing it would red the `phpunit` job on day one.

**Why.** The measurement (n=3, quiet machine, gate parity) reframed the problem. Band walls 1018/1055/939s (mean 1004s) against 10797s of summed class time over 686 classes — an ideal 12-way of **900s**, so the band runs at ~88% scheduling efficiency. The slowest class is **628.5s worst of three**, comfortably under that 900s ideal, which means the band is **work-bound, not straggler-bound**: splitting the slowest class would buy nothing. UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1797 already did the straggler work. 700s guards the invariant that still matters — no class becoming the *binding* constraint — while staying green: below the 900s work-bound so it fires before a class binds, and far enough above the worst observed not to flap (per-class spread is +5.4%/−9.1% around a 596.1s mean; reaching 700s needs +17.4%, about 3× the largest upward excursion measured). Note the per-class spread is wider than the band-level ±5.5%, which does not govern a single class.

**Rejected.** *Keep 300s* — reds the gate immediately on ten classes, and a gate that fires on arrival gets switched off. *Guard `slowest ÷ ideal-N-way`* (fail only when a class actually becomes binding) — the most principled metric and self-calibrating, but it changes the "one absolute ceiling" form UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1890 pinned and needs the worker count at check time, which differs between CI and local. *Ship non-blocking until the cluster is split* — reinstates exactly the silence the guard exists to remove.

**Companion.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1890 carries the full gate record; the `SchemaJsonLd*` cost cluster it surfaced (twelve of 686 classes ≈ 47% of band time) is spun off to its own ticket against UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1537.

## 2026-08-04 — `character_limit` stays a soft CP hint; `max:` is opt-in per field (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1887)

**Decision.** `character_limit` does **not** imply a `max:` validation rule in this codebase. The two stay independent: `character_limit` is the CP-only affordance it already is (it renders `maxlength` on the input), and enforcement is an explicit `validate: ['max:N']` written beside it, field by field. UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1887 enforces the two limits in the shared `uamsweb_common` fieldset — `excerpt` (160) and `title_short` (43) — and records the remaining 111 declarations as deliberate hints, pinned per file by [`tests/Feature/Blueprints/CharacterLimitValidationTest.php`](../tests/Feature/Blueprints/CharacterLimitValidationTest.php) so adding a `character_limit` anywhere forces the enforce-or-hint choice rather than defaulting silently.

**Why.** The blast radius decides it. A scan of `content/` on 2026-08-04 found **1,757 stored values already over their field's limit** across the presentational block and header fields — `caption` 81 of 111, `body` 61 of 76, `heading` 15 of 190. WordPress enforced no limit, so the overflow is inherited rather than authored here. A blanket implication would turn the [`IncompletePublishedEntries`](../app/Widgets/IncompletePublishedEntries.php) widget — the audit surface this exists to feed (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#481) — into a wall of inherited failures, which is the opposite of an actionable list. The two enforced fields are the narrow case where the limit is a real contract rather than a layout hint: `excerpt` is the search-result and meta description, and its exposure is **3 stored values** — the `bio_short` of three published `people` entries (165, 169 and 166 characters), all from UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1883's flatten and named on UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1887. Those three entries now fail blueprint validation, which is the point: they surface in the audit widget rather than being silently compliant. It also means an editor opening one and saving is blocked until the copy is trimmed, which UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1887 accepts — its "the copy stays" is about not editing them *in that ticket*, not about keeping them savable.

`title_short`'s exposure is **0**, but that number is a floor rather than a measurement: `content/` holds no `title_short` value at all, because `posts` — the field's main home — has not been imported yet, and `PostEntryCreator` populates it from the WordPress SEO title with no truncation. The rule's real blast radius arrives with that import, where it will surface over-limit titles in the widget rather than block the import (`Entry::save()` runs no blueprint validation).

**The trap this decision has to survive.** Statamic resolves a fieldset import's `config:` with a shallow `array_merge`, so a `config.validate` **replaces** the referenced field's rules instead of adding to them. Enforcing on the fieldset alone reached 43 resolved fields and was silently dropped from 23 others — `pages`, `posts`, `knowledge_bases`, `clinical_expertise`, `clinical_resources`. The rule is therefore restated at each of the nine overriding import sites, and the guard measures **resolved `Field::rules()`** rather than the YAML, because a hand-picked blueprint dataset is exactly what hid this. A second guard pins every import site that drops an inherited rule; 27 pre-existing drops are recorded there for triage in UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1904.

**Rejected.** (a) *A helper that makes `character_limit` emit `max:` globally* — the cheaper-to-remember option UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1887 floated, and the one the numbers close: 1,757 new failures, and it would also make every block field unsavable in the CP at its current copy. (b) *Enforce only on the `person` blueprint's `bio_*` imports via `config:`* — holds exposure to the same 3 entries, but leaves the site-wide `excerpt` (the field the limit actually means something for) unenforced, and puts the rule somewhere a reader of the fieldset cannot see it. (c) *Enumerate every hint-only declaration individually in the audit* — 111 entries of churn for no more signal than the per-file counts already give. (d) *Trust the fieldset to propagate the rule* — measured false, see the trap above.

**Companion.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1887, and the audit tests named above. Residue: UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1904 (the 27 inherited-rule drops) and UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1905 (the widget audits only 500 of 732 published entries).

## 2026-08-04 — Split the JSON-LD cache switches; the suites run production's fragment regime, bust rotated per test (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1842)

**Decision.** `schema_templates.json_ld_cache.enabled` becomes an explicit **master switch**, and a new `output_enabled` gates the page output cache alone, so `fragments_enabled` is its sibling rather than something the master switch silently swallows. The 19 `SchemaJsonLd*` suites that cleared `enabled` to get page output off now call `useProductionJsonLdFragmentCacheRegime()`: output off, fragments **on**, and the bust generation rotated **per test**. `SchemaJsonLdFragmentCache::enabled()` keeps honoring the master switch — behavior unchanged — but its stated rationale was wrong and is corrected. Shipped defaults are untouched: all three flags default to `true`.
