**Why.** The suites needed page output off for a real reason — `SchemaJsonLdOutputCache::cacheKey()` falls back to `spl_object_hash()` for an entity with no id, PHP reuses those hashes once an object is freed, and these suites build in-memory fixtures — but reached for the master switch to get it, taking the cross-request fragment cache down too. Nothing in a suite mentions fragments, so it was invisible, and those suites are the harness UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1797, UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1800 and UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1801 all profiled through. Three findings settle the design:

1. **The coupling was never load-bearing for bust rotation.** The old docblock justified requiring `enabled` "so bust rotation stays meaningful". `InvalidateSchemaJsonLdCache` reads only the three `invalidate_*` allow-lists and **no cache switch at all**, so the generation rotates on every watched entry, global-set and term save regardless of any flag. There is no other bump path: `BumpSchemaJsonLdCacheWhenAssetSaved` was removed in UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#937 and `AssetSavedDoesNotBustSchemaJsonLdCacheTest` pins that an asset save must *not* bust. The coupling survives only as an operator kill switch, which is what the flag is now documented as.
2. **Per-test rotation is required, and `Cache::flush()` is not an option.** Fragment keys are `(bust generation, site, template id, target)` — nothing in that tuple moves when a test mutates the content a fragment was built from. Saved mutations rotate via the listener above; **in-memory** ones do not, and the suites do exactly that (`SchemaJsonLdLocationIdentifiersContactTest` mutates `uams_fad_settings` through `inDefaultSite()` and restores it without saving). Rotating per test closes that by construction instead of by auditing which test mutates what. A flush would take the per-worker Stache index with it — same store.
3. **The regime is 11.1% *faster*, not a cost.** Same worktree, same base `fb83e1179`, byte-identical `vendor/bin/pest <the 20 classes> --no-tia`, `route:cache` up front and `route:clear` after (exit codes logged), a fresh `test:seed-stache` per band, and **no foreign PHP process at either band's start** — verified by resolving each process's cwd, not by counting. Baseline **2480s**, shipped **2204s**, both `148 passed (531 assertions)`, which is what licenses attributing the delta to the regime. 17 of 20 classes improved; the wins concentrate where a test builds several nodes (`PodcastEpisode` −25.3%, `PostClinicalAssociations` −21.3%, `ClinicalResourceSyndication` −18.8%), because rotation leaves reuse available *within* a test. Three classes are +2.4% to +4.7%, the write overhead with no reuse to pay for it.

**Rejected.** (a) *Share one bust generation across a class* — the suite-time lever UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1842 floated for UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1537. Measured, not assumed: reusing a fragment built in an **earlier compose** shifts template-instance `@id` suffix allocation, so the fixture person page emits `hasOccupation` as a full `Occupation` node at `…-1` (41 full nodes) cold and as a compact reference to `…-0` (37 full nodes) warm. Both graphs are internally consistent — zero dangling references either way, the invariant `SchemaJsonLdPersonEntriesTest` already pins — so this is **not** a production defect, but it makes emitted shape depend on **test order**. Unsafe, not merely declined; the lever is closed. (b) *Decouple `enabled()` from the master switch entirely* — finding 1 says nothing forces the coupling, but an operator clearing "the JSON-LD cache" expects both layers off; keeping it a master switch preserves that and costs the suites nothing now that `output_enabled` exists. (c) *Leave the suites alone and just document the trap* — UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1842's own fallback, and strictly worse: the trap already had a docblock and three tickets walked into it anyway. (d) *Rely on `ttl_seconds => 0` to disable page output* — it does work (`SchemaOutputService` bypasses the cache at TTL ≤ 0, independent of any flag), but it is a side effect rather than a switch, and 19 suites depending on it undocumented is the same class of trap.

**Residual.** `SchemaAntlersParseIsolationTest` deliberately keeps the master switch off and now says why — the shared org node it renders twice is the fragment-cacheable shape, so a fragment hit would satisfy its UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1800 memo guard without proving the memo is gone. Anything measured through *that* suite is still a fragments-off regime. The byte-identity guarantee is **within a generation**, which is what per-test rotation provides; across generations, see rejected (a). The measurement is a serial run of the 20 classes, not the parallel band, so the effect on UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1537's band is inferred from class totals rather than observed. An earlier arm-B run measured 4621s purely because a peer session's Pest run was competing — discarded, and a reminder that the quiet-machine parity step is checked by resolving each process's working directory, not by trusting a count.

**Companion.** Issue UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1842 `## Decision`; PR UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1871; supersedes finding 3 of the 2026-07-31 UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1801 entry, whose "19 of the 20 suites disable that flag" was true of the tree as it stood then and no longer is. `.claude/rules/measurement-parity.md` carries the generalized trap: a suite's own `beforeEach` is part of the harness.

## 2026-08-04 — `@alpinejs/csp` is blocked by Statamic's own form-conditions driver; UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1859 defers (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1859)

**Decision.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1859 is **deferred, not attempted**, and its `## Current state` is corrected. The `@alpinejs/csp` swap is **not** the drop-in the ticket describes: it breaks **every public form**. `'unsafe-eval'` stays until UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1859 is rebuilt around the corrected approach below. UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1741 shipped independently, as its split intended.

**Why.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1859's audit checked `resources/views/**` for global access in expressions and found none — but the breaking expression is not ours. `Statamic\Forms\JsDrivers\Alpine::renderAlpineShowFieldJs()` emits `Statamic.$conditions.showField({…}, $data, '<handle>')` for **every field**, and this app's overridden `fields.antlers.html` wraps every field in `<template x-if="{{ show_field }}">`; both public-form entry points render with `js="alpine"`. The CSP build's interpreter resolves identifiers **only from the Alpine scope stack** — `case "Identifier": if (node.name in scope) {…} throw new Error("Undefined variable: " + node.name)` — and `Statamic` is a `window` global, so every field's `x-if` throws. A second, independent gate would catch it even if the first did not: `checkForDangerousValues()` throws `"Accessing global variables is prohibited in the CSP build"` for any value in its `globalThis` snapshot. The observable failure is a form that renders its heading and Submit button with **no input fields** — the exact symptom `fields.antlers.html`'s own troubleshooting note describes. Everything else in UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1859's audit holds: 0 `new Function` in the CSP build, and this repo's own 134 directive occurrences across 22 files all fall inside the supported node set (no `??`, template literals, arrow functions, optional chaining, or `x-html`).

**The corrected approach**, for whoever picks UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1859 up: the CSP restriction is on **attribute expressions**, not on JS modules — a module may read `window.Statamic` freely. So subclass the driver, register it in `app('statamic.form-js-drivers')`, override `renderAlpineShowFieldJs()` to emit a scope-local call (e.g. `$showField({…}, '<handle>')`) backed by an `Alpine.magic()` that closes over `window.Statamic`, and point the two entry points at it. That leaves `x-data` and all 47 `x-model` bindings untouched. Note `js="alpine::component"` is **not** a shortcut: `Tags::parseJsDriver()` splits on `:`, so an empty scope segment becomes the string `''`, which `renderAlpineXData()` treats as a real scope and nests the data under an empty key.

**Rejected.** *Build it in the group-DP wave* — the code is small, but UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1859's real cost was always the browser regression pass, and that cost went **up**, not down: the estimate rested on an audit that missed the one expression that matters. Gambling a patient-facing form surface on a change only partly verifiable headlessly is the wrong trade for a narrow win. *Close UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1859 as not viable* — premature; the fix above is real, just larger than advertised. *Ship it gated on `tests/Browser` passing* — `ReplicatorPublicFormTest` would catch a total failure but not a subtle conditional-logic regression, so green there would not have licensed the merge.

**Companion.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1859 `## Current state` (corrected in place); UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1741 (shipped, the `'unsafe-inline'` half); #667 (enforce-flip). **Residual.** `'unsafe-eval'` remains in `script-src`. It cannot execute an injected inline `<script>` — UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1741's nonce gate closed that path — so what stays open is the narrower "stage further script via `eval`/`new Function`" case. CSP is still Report-Only, so nothing breaks in production either way until #667 flips.

## 2026-08-04 — `bio_short` stays a `textarea`; the four Bard-shaped entries are flattened (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1883)

**Decision.** Keep `bio_short` on `uamsweb_common.excerpt` (a `textarea`) and repair the four `people` entries that stored it as a Bard/ProseMirror document. Guard the shared SEO partial alongside, so a non-string description or title source is dropped rather than fataling the page.

**Why.** The blueprint's full/short split is deliberate and uniform: all six "full" biographies (`bio`, `bio_institutional`, `bio_clinical`, `bio_academic`, `bio_research`, `bio_creator`) are `bard_simple_lists_links`, and all six "short" ones are `uamsweb_common.excerpt`. Across the whole content set, `bio_short` was the only short field carrying Bard-shaped data — 4 entries of 5 populated, against 6 plain strings spread over the other five short fields — so those four are import residue, not a signal about intent. The field also feeds a `<meta name="description">` capped at 160 characters, where rich text has no meaning. The guard ships with it because a blueprint and its data can drift again, and the failure mode was severe out of all proportion to the cause: `strip_tags()` on an array is fatal, the partial lives in `layout.antlers.html`, so **every** page for an affected entry 500'd — surfaced only because a PR that merely added tests reshuffled which person a query returned first.

**Rejected.** *Make `bio_short` a Bard field* — matches 4 of 5 stored values but contradicts the other five short-bio fields, hands editors a rich-text editor for a plain meta description, and still needs a text derivation before it can be emitted. *Guard the partial only* — leaves the blueprint and its data disagreeing, so the next import writes the same shape back. *Trim the flattened text to the 160-character limit* — three of the four land at 165–169 characters, and shortening editorial bio copy is a content decision, not a bug fix; the text is preserved verbatim and the overflow is noted on the issue.

**Companion.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1883 `## Decision`; `content` PR flattening the four entries. Surfaced by the local-CI run on UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1870.

## 2026-08-04 — Alpine CSP migration: ship the `'unsafe-eval'` removal ahead of the `helpers.js` fix (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1859)

**Decision.** Migrate the front end to `@alpinejs/csp` and drop `script-src 'unsafe-eval'` in the same PR, even though Statamic's public-side `helpers.js` still calls `eval()` on the standard field-conditions path. Route public forms through a new `App\Forms\JsDrivers\AlpineCsp` driver (`js="alpine_csp"`) that emits a `$showField(…)` Alpine magic in place of the stock driver's `Statamic.$conditions.showField(…)`, and encode both the `x-data` payload and the conditions object with `JSON_UNESCAPED_UNICODE`. File the `helpers.js` `eval()` as UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1902 and add a native `blocked_by` edge so #667 cannot flip CSP to enforcing until it is resolved.

**Why.** Two independent gates in the CSP build reject the stock expression: the interpreter resolves identifiers only from the Alpine scope stack (`Undefined variable: Statamic`), and `checkForDangerousValues()` refuses anything in its `globalThis` snapshot. The restriction is on *attribute expressions*, not on JS modules, so a magic closing over `window.Statamic` in module scope satisfies both while keeping the vendor signature and argument order — scoped forms keep working for free. The JSON flags matter because the CSP tokenizer implements only `\n`, `\t`, `\r`, `\` and the quote escape and falls through to `value += char` for the rest, so the parent's `json_encode($value, 0)` turns `René` into `Renu00e9` — silently corrupting the condition comparison and, via `x-data` + `x-model`, the value a user re-submits after a failed validation round-trip. On sequencing: CSP is Report-Only, so the removal breaks nothing today and buys no security today — it changes what gets *reported*. Keeping the allowance would suppress the exact violation report that tells #667 the `helpers.js` `eval()` exists, which inverts step 2 of the rollout plan recorded in `config/security-headers.php` ("tighten until reports are quiet").

**Rejected.** *Patch the `eval()` out of `helpers.js` in the same PR* — the only mechanism is a Composer patch against a **minified** dist bundle (the package ships no source for that file, only a `.d.ts`), so the patch context changes every Statamic release; the repo's three existing `statamic/cms` patches all target readable PHP. Debt bought to prevent a break that cannot occur until a ticket that has not started. *Split — ship the Alpine migration and hold the policy change* — misses the ticket's headline criterion and keeps the `eval()` out of the violation reports, the worst of both. *Rewrite conditions into `custom` conditions at render time so they take the non-`eval` branch* — `prepareParams` splits the parameter string on `:` then `,` and trims, so any condition value containing a colon, a comma, or meaningful whitespace is silently corrupted. *Override the core `alpine` handle instead of adding `alpine_csp`* — implicit, and leaves no way to render a form the stock way.

**Companion.** Issues UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1859 (`## Decision`), UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1902 (the `helpers.js` `eval()`, blocking #667), #667 (the enforce flip), UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1741 (the `'unsafe-inline'` half). Files: `app/Forms/JsDrivers/AlpineCsp.php`, `app/Providers/FormJsDriverServiceProvider.php`, `resources/js/conditions/showFieldMagic.js`, `config/security-headers.php`. `@alpinejs/collapse`, `@alpinejs/focus` and `@alpinejs/persist` are dropped as unimported; `@alpinejs/morph` is kept and aligned because the UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1242 / UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1506 / UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1524 faceting and search decisions above pin their "no new dependency" claim to it being installed.

## 2026-08-05 — Safe Redirect Manager parity lands in the engine, not the importer (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1933 / UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1934)

**Decision.** Close the `FindRedirectRule` parity gaps **in the serving engine**: it learns SRM's trailing-`*` wildcard (matching *and* remainder-append into a `*`-terminated target), compiles the **bare** PCRE sources SRM stores, substitutes regex captures into `target_url`, folds case on matching, canonicalizes trailing slashes on both the request path and the stored source, and matches/forwards query strings. The importer is **not** changed: it keeps writing SRM rules verbatim. Two behaviors deliberately diverge from SRM, both documented in [`RedirectPattern`](../app/Support/Redirects/RedirectPattern.php): capture substitution runs even when the target is an absolute URL, and a substituted result whose stored target named no scheme or authority is held to a site-relative path.

**Why.** The gap is far larger than "wildcards do not work". Measured across every WXR export in `wordpress-importer-exports`, **5,234 of the 5,375 stored SRM rules — 97% — can never fire**: 2,532 wildcard sources the engine has no mode for, 911 regex sources that *error* rather than fail to match, 1,637 literal sources stored with a trailing slash, 97 with mixed case, 57 naming a query. Only 141 are reachable. The regex half was worse than the ticket recorded: SRM supplies its own `@…@` delimiters at match time, so a stored source reads `^\/legacy\/(.+)` and `preg_match()` parses the leading `/` of a source like `/legacy/(.*)` as a delimiter and `(.*)` as modifiers — a compile error, not a non-match. Capture substitution is unreachable without fixing that first, so it is fixed here rather than deferred. Wildcards belong in the engine because [`redirects.yaml`](../resources/blueprints/globals/redirects.yaml) **already documents `*` to Control Panel editors** in two field instructions, so the engine is contractually obliged to support it regardless of what the importer does. The bare-vs-delimited precedence ("use it verbatim if it compiles, else wrap it") is unambiguous rather than heuristic: **zero of the 911 corpus regex sources compile as a delimited pattern**.

**Rejected.** *Translate `*` into an equivalent regex at import time* — the ticket's stated alternative. It fixes the same 2,532 rules while leaving Control-Panel-authored `*` rules broken against a blueprint that promises them, costs a second repo plus a re-import to deploy or undo, and is a correctness hazard: the wildcard base must be `preg_quote`d (a source containing `.`, `+` or `(` silently becomes a different pattern), and SRM's `trailingslashit`-then-prefix compare plus its original-case remainder append have to be re-encoded into the generated pattern — reproducing by construction what eight lines of engine code express directly. Wildcard matching in the engine is pure string work with no escaping surface at all. *Port SRM's `filter_var($target, FILTER_VALIDATE_URL)` guard, which skips capture substitution for an absolute-URL target* — that guard sends **76 of the 508** capture-carrying corpus rules to a literal `https://jobs.uams.edu/$1` in WordPress today. It is a defect, not a contract, and UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1933's acceptance criterion says captures substitute. *Match regex sources against `path?query` as SRM does* — SRM then needs a second guard to avoid re-appending the query it just captured; matching the path and forwarding the query separately reaches the same destination for the 657 corpus patterns carrying an optional `(\?.*)?` group, and **no** corpus pattern requires a query to match. *Reproduce SRM's `strpos($source, '?')` query test for regex sources* — in a PCRE source `?` is a quantifier, and that shape carries three of them; SRM forces the test off for regex rules and so does this. *Use `Request::getQueryString()` for the forwarded query* — it normalizes and **sorts** parameters by key, and a forwarded query has to reach the destination as the visitor sent it.

**Why this does not disturb the 2026-07-31 `UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1657` verdict.** That entry rejected "extending `FindRedirectRule` to match `path?query`" for the **`?p=` ugly-permalink** case, on the grounds that it is necessary but not sufficient there — `/?p=123` never 404s, so the 404 template never runs and only a middleware can intercept it. That reasoning is untouched: `?p=` stays with `RedirectWordPressQueryPermalinks`. The query handling added here serves rules that *do* reach the 404 path. UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1657's ordering guarantee also survives — literal sources are still resolved in a first pass, so a pattern can only ever claim a request nothing literal did.

**A `404` rule renders its message; it does not abort.** Every other targetless code (`403`, `410`, `451`) aborts out of [`errors/_redirects`](../resources/views/errors/_redirects.antlers.html) and reaches the visitor correctly, because Laravel resolves no `errors::<code>` view for them and answers from the exception directly. `404` is the exception in both senses: `errors::404` **does** resolve — to the very page that includes this partial — so `abort(404)` makes `Handler::renderHttpException()` re-render it inside a try/catch, match the same rule, and abort again. The catch then rethrows under `app.debug` or answers from the ORIGINAL exception, so the rule's message is discarded either way. Measured against the running site: **HTTP 500** with debug on, a generic error page with it off, and the message never rendered once. The partial's `404` branch is therefore deliberately inert — the response is already 404 by the time it runs, so the only thing left to do is show the message, which [`errors/_redirect_message`](../resources/views/errors/_redirect_message.antlers.html) does from inside the page body where a message can be seen. Verified against the running site after the change: 404 status, styled page, message rendered, in both debug states.

**Residual, named rather than silent.** `403`, `410` and `451` return the right status but their `message` never reaches the visitor either — `convertExceptionToResponse()` builds a generic page that has no slot for it, and unlike `404` those codes have no page of ours to render it into. That is UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1641's design rather than this change's, and it has its own follow-up. Worth stating alongside it: no test in the suite could have caught the `404` defect. The redirect suites render `errors/_redirects` directly, which proves the partial aborts and stops there; an in-process HTTP test cannot substitute, because a frontend 404 raised under `APP_ENV=testing` resolves through a path where Antlers binds none of the tag's row values — the pair iterates for a matching rule and not at all for none, with every key empty. The defect was found by serving real requests against the running site, and the fix is pinned by asserting the partial's branch behavior in the harness that does bind.

**Companion.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1933 and UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1934 issue `## Decision` sections; [`RedirectPattern`](../app/Support/Redirects/RedirectPattern.php) carries the per-behavior rationale, and [`RedirectServingTest`](../tests/Feature/Redirects/RedirectServingTest.php) pins the chain end to end through the real partial. Parent spike: `UAMS-Web/wordpress-importer#133`.

## 2026-08-05 — Own the public field-conditions evaluator; drop Statamic's `helpers.js` from the front end (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1902)

**Decision.** Remove the `eval()` on the public field-conditions path by **implementing the evaluator ourselves** under `resources/js/conditions/`, calling it from the `$showField` Alpine magic this app already owns, and **dropping the `<script src="/vendor/statamic/frontend/js/helpers.js">` tag from [`layout.antlers.html`](../resources/views/layout.antlers.html) entirely**. No Composer patch on `statamic/cms`, and no vendored copy of upstream's source. A **constants-parity guard test** pins `KEYS`, `OPERATORS` and `ALIASES` against the values in the shipped vendor bundle, so an upstream change to the condition vocabulary reds a test instead of drifting silently. The upstream report is a separate ticket chain, not an acceptance criterion here.

**Why.** The framing that makes this look hard — "how do we edit Statamic's file?" — is already obsolete in this app. `App\Forms\JsDrivers\AlpineCsp` emits `$showField(…)` rather than `Statamic.$conditions.showField(…)`, both public form templates render with `js="alpine_csp"`, and the CSP Alpine build refuses globals in attribute expressions — so the **only** public-page path into the vendor evaluator is one line, [`showFieldMagic.js`](../resources/js/conditions/showFieldMagic.js)'s forwarding call. The call site is ours. And `helpers.js` exports exactly two things, `$conditions` and `$passkeys`; `$passkeys` has **zero** first-party consumers, so 98,735 bytes ship on every public page to serve two conditional fields. Owning ~200 lines of pure logic replaces a 4.5 KB minified class we cannot read, puts the whole conditions vocabulary under the existing framework-free Vitest job, and removes the payload.

**The operand-coercion trap, verified, is why a comparator swap is not a one-liner.** `prepareLhs`/`prepareRhs` hand `eval` **JSON-stringified** operands (`'"Yes"'`), which `eval` then re-parses as source literals. Measured against the real shapes: for a number LHS and a string RHS, `eval` yields `true` where comparing the raw operands yields `false`; routing both through `JSON.parse` restores that case but **throws `SyntaxError`** where `eval` returns `false` for an `undefined` LHS. Our evaluator dissolves this by never taking the round-trip: it keeps native values and compares them directly, which is what `eval` effectively did after parsing.

**Rejected.** *Composer patch on the minified bundle* — the ticket's first option, and the one with the best failure mode (`composer-exit-on-patch-failure: true` fails loudly). It loses on the cost of that failure: the `eval()` sits on a **27,287-character line** of [`helpers.js`](../vendor/statamic/cms/resources/dist-frontend/js/helpers.js) that also carries the Converter, the WebAuthn client and lodash chunks, so the patch is a ~55 KB diff invalidated by any upstream edit anywhere in that line — and a stale one blocks `composer install` for every developer and every CI job until someone hand-diffs minified output. The three existing `statamic/cms` patches all target readable PHP with small hunks; there is no precedent for this shape. It also leaves the 98 KB payload and forces the guard test into the PHP suite, since the Vitest `js` job runs on `npm ci` alone with no Composer tree. *Vendor upstream's source and build our own bundle* — the ticket's second option, whose **stated premise is false**: `@statamic/cms` ships only `.d.ts` types for `components/field-conditions/`, and its `src/*.js` are re-export shims over `__STATAMIC__.core`, a global only the CP bundle defines, so nothing is importable. Real source exists on GitHub (Validator 309 lines + Converter 84 + ParentResolver 37 + Constants 21), so this route means copying ~450 lines into the repo — carrying the patch route's maintenance burden *plus* a silent drift failure mode, plus a licensing question against a repository GitHub reports with no asserted license. Dominated on every axis. *Rewrite conditions into `custom` conditions at render time* — rejected on the ticket already: `prepareParams` splits on `:` then `,` and trims, silently corrupting any value containing a colon, comma, or meaningful whitespace. *Fold the upstream report into UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1902's acceptance criteria* — repeats the mistake group `ED`/UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1924 exists to avoid: an external round-trip nobody here controls would hold this PR's ship hostage.

**Residual, accepted.** The two live conditional fields are both `'equals Yes'`, so unit tests — not production traffic — are the only evidence for `>`/`>=`/`<`/`<=`/`contains_any` and the `'empty'` RHS. Forms are authored in the CP, so an editor can introduce a new condition shape at any time; the parity guard catches *upstream* drift, not our own mis-implementation of an operator we do support. `$passkeys` is unused **today**, and dropping the script tag would need revisiting if front-end WebAuthn is ever adopted. The CP bundle still generates code at runtime — unchanged, out of scope, and still #667's blocker. Bake-window evidence remains #667's to gather.

**Corrected while building it (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1956): the parity guard is a Pest test, not a Vitest one.** The rejected-alternatives paragraph lists "forces the guard test into the PHP suite" as a cost of the *Composer-patch* route, which reads as though the chosen route escapes it. It does not. The constraint is about where the vendor bundle lives, not which route was taken: `vendor/` is untracked and `public/vendor/statamic` is git-ignored, while the CI `js` job is `checkout` + `npm ci` with no Composer step — so a Vitest guard cannot open the file under **any** route. Skipping when the bundle is absent would be worse than useless: the guard would never run in CI while still reporting green. It lives at [`tests/Feature/Forms/FieldConditionsConstantsParityTest.php`](../tests/Feature/Forms/FieldConditionsConstantsParityTest.php); the behavioral tests stay in Vitest, which needs no vendor tree.

**The port fixes four vendor defects rather than reproducing them, each filed for upstream report.** Porting line by line surfaced defects none of our live conditions reach, so each is fixed locally, marked `DELIBERATE DIVERGENCE` in `validator.test.js`, and tracked: `isEmpty` reporting every number and boolean as empty (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1962 — `Object.keys(5)` is `[]`); `contains_any` building an unescaped `RegExp` from authored needles (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1963); **`unless` plus a custom condition double-inverting**, so it behaves like `if` (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1964 — the consequential one, and found by a test going red rather than by reading); and `contains` against an object-valued field throwing `TypeError` (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1965). A fifth candidate — an unknown operator reaching `eval` as a `SyntaxError` — was **not** filed: the Converter can only ever emit an operator from `OPERATORS`, so the throw is unreachable from a blueprint and the guard against it is defensive coding, not a vendor bug.

**What the guard does not cover.** Parity covers the *vocabulary* only. The evaluation *semantics* are a hand port with no automated drift detection, so an upstream change to how a comparison behaves passes every test here. Re-syncing on a `statamic/cms` bump is a standing manual obligation, recorded in the `STAYING IN SYNC WITH UPSTREAM` section of [`validator.js`](../resources/js/conditions/validator.js) along with the four upstream files to diff. `.github/workflows/watch-upstream.yml` is the eventual home for that watch, but it pairs a tracking issue with an upstream issue/PR and UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1929 has not filed one yet.

**Companion.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1902 `## Decision`; the build is UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1956; the upstream chain filed per the [`upstream-patch-lifecycle`](../.claude/skills/upstream-patch-lifecycle/SKILL.md) skill (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1929, UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1930, #1931) plus the four defect reports UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1962–UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1965; predecessors UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1741 and UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1859 below; #667 (the enforce flip this unblocks).

## 2026-08-05 — Tia ships as a guarded local accelerator, never in the gate (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1717)

**Decision.** Adopt Pest 5's Tia engine as an **opt-in local accelerator only**: `composer test:tia` (and `composer test:tia:fresh` to rebuild), gated by [`scripts/check-tia-safe.php`](../scripts/check-tia-safe.php), which refuses the run when any declared submodule has moved or no coverage driver is active. It is **excluded from both gate paths** — [`ci.yml`](../.github/workflows/ci.yml) and [`scripts/local-ci.mjs`](../scripts/local-ci.mjs) — with a `PARITY` comment in each recording why, so it cannot be quietly wired in later.

**The guard covers three shapes, not one.** A content change reaches the tree as a dirty worktree, as HEAD/gitlink drift, or as a **committed pointer bump against which the whole tree is clean** — and Tia is equally blind to all three, because the changed path is the bare directory `content` and every consumer in `Graph::affected()` gates on `is_file()`. The third is the *routine* shape: **25 of the last 300 commits move the `content` gitlink**, `1ba5c02ce` is a commit whose entire diff is that one path, and the [`sync-pr-branch`](../.claude/rules/sync-pr-branch.md) rule requires merging `origin/main` before validating. Inspecting the tree cannot detect it, so the guard records a submodule fingerprint to `storage/app/` and refuses when it no longer matches. A missing record also refuses — absent state is indistinguishable from state wiped while a graph survived. This was caught by adversarial review after a first implementation that only checked the worktree; that version would have passed the common case while Tia replayed stale results, and Tia's `bumpRecordedSha()` would then have baked them in permanently.

**Why.** The gate could not use Tia even if we wanted it, for three independent reasons: `ci.yml` pins `coverage: none` and `local-ci.mjs` passes `-d pcov.enabled=0`, so no driver means zero recorded edges; every Actions job is a fresh runner with a fresh `$HOME`, so `~/.pest/tia/` never survives; and the `content` blind spot below. So the only live question was the local path, where the win is real and measured — 51.4s cold to 6.0s replay on `tests/Unit`. Three of the four objections standing against adoption were retired by measurement rather than argument: `config/` (35 files) and `routes/` (3) **are** in the graph, so the suspected third blind spot does not exist; `--tia` + `--mutate` no longer collides on `pest-plugin-mutate` v5.0.1 (8 mutations, `Score: 100.00%`, exit 0, three ways); and UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1799 had already retracted the "change detection is broken" finding as a comment-only-probe artifact. What survived is the **`content` blind spot, and it is ours, not upstream**: `.gitmodules` sets `ignore = dirty` on `content`, so `git status --porcelain` — Tia's exact detection command — reports nothing when content changes, and `Graph::applyWatchPatternFallback()` drops the path anyway because a submodule is a directory, not a file. **Reproduced end to end**: with one `people_groups` entry added, `--tia` returned `2 passed, 2 replayed` in 3.02s (exit 0) where `--no-tia` on the identical tree returned `1 failed` in 45.28s (exit 1). A guard that refuses is the honest response to a blind spot that reports green.

**Rejected.** *Decline Tia entirely* — defensible and zero-maintenance, but throws away a measured ~8.6× local iteration win over a hazard with a cheap, tested fix. *Adopt unguarded with a documented caveat* — leaves a reproduced false green live for anyone who edits content, and a comment does not fire at 3am. *Remove `ignore = dirty` from `content`* — makes the dirty case visible to the root status but still unusable, because the `is_file()` drop discards the path regardless; and the setting exists to keep a large, churning submodule out of every `git status`. *Enable Tia globally via `pest()->tia()` in `tests/Pest.php`* — would cover the `PEST_TIA` env path the guard misses, but puts Tia-aware code in the gate's own bootstrap, which is the exact coupling this decision exists to prevent.

**Residual, accepted.** The per-worktree cache isolation is an **accident** of `Storage::rawOriginUrl()` doing `is_file()` on `.git` (a file in a linked worktree), not a designed guarantee. And `PEST_TIA=1` in an environment enables Tia for any `pest` invocation, bypassing the composer script the guard hangs off. Both are recorded on UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1717 rather than fixed.

> **Superseded 2026-08-13 (#1992), and the wording above was wrong in both directions.** `projectKey()` returns `slug(basename($projectRoot)).'-'.$hash`, so the folder-name slug is a **second, independent lock** that resolving the remote does not touch — "every worktree collapses onto one key" overstated it. But the correction "only same-*basename* checkouts collapse" understates it, because `slug()` lowercases and collapses runs of non-alphanumerics: `probe_x`, `probe-x`, `PROBE.X` and `probe--x` all produce one key, and punctuation-only names collapse to an empty slug and share a bare hash. The real condition is **same slug**. Both readings are now moot for us — the cache is pinned per checkout, so `projectKey()` is never consulted. See the 2026-08-13 (#1992) entry above.

**Companion.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1717 issue `## Decision`; [`scripts/check-tia-safe.php`](../scripts/check-tia-safe.php) + [`tests/Feature/TiaSafetyGuardTest.php`](../tests/Feature/TiaSafetyGuardTest.php).

## 2026-08-05 — Time-balanced sharding is declined; the per-shard tax outgrows the win (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1718)

**Decision.** **No-go** on Pest 5's time-balanced `--shard` for the CI test gate. `tests/.pest/shards.json` is not committed and no shard step is added. `--update-shards` is retained as a **profiling tool** only — it is how UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1797's straggler was found.

**Why.** Sharding trades more total runner minutes across N machines for less wall-clock on one, which is the same trade the 2026-07-15 sequential cost-gate decision already declined one layer up. Three things settle it here. **It pays nothing today**: with `RUN_REMOTE_CI` off, validation runs through `local-ci` on a single machine, where splitting across machines buys zero. **The measured win was small and shrinking**: N=3 gave 1.25× and N=4 gave 1.30× against a 1306s band, so a fourth machine bought **4%** (1047s → 1007s) while total compute rose to 1.68×. **Fixing the straggler made sharding *less* attractive, not more** — the counter-intuitive result worth recording. Each shard re-pays a fixed setup cost that does not shrink as tests get faster; measured directly here at **82s** (`route:cache` 5s + `test:seed-stache` 75s + warm 2s) for the app-side portion alone, before a remote runner's checkout, `composer install`, and submodule clone. UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1797/UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1820 split the class that made the band straggler-bound, and UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1890 then measured the band as **work-bound at ~88% scheduling efficiency** — so the useful work per shard shrank while the per-shard tax stayed put.

**Rejected.** *Adopt at N=2* — `--shard` + `--parallel` crashed at N=2 (`WorkerCrashedException`, `--phpunit-argv` missing) though it ran at N=3 and N=4; never characterized, and moot given the economics. *Defer the verdict until a full N=2/3/4 re-measure post-#1797* — the re-measure was **not run** (see below), but its own predicted direction, the 82s direct measurement, UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1890's work-bound finding, and the `RUN_REMOTE_CI` fact all point the same way, and a spike that never concludes is worse than one that concludes with its evidence stated. *Commit `shards.json` for balance without sharding the gate* — it would drift on every added test and warn stale for no benefit.

**Residual, stated plainly.** The N=2/3/4 re-measure UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1718's own caveat comment asked for **was not performed**. A dedicated local-CI session was validating on this machine, and concurrent bands would have poisoned both runs and risked the UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1136 fixture race. The 82s figure is the direct per-shard fixed-cost measurement that same comment proposed as the better instrument, and it is what this verdict rests on. If remote CI is re-enabled and the band grows materially, this is the entry to revisit.

**Companion.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1718 issue `## Decision`; composes with the 2026-07-15 cost-gate entry below and UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1890's band measurements.

## 2026-08-07 — Targetless error pages render through Statamic, with one shared body and three per-code views (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1942)

**Decision.** Give `403`, `410` and `451` the site's own error page carrying the rule's `message`, by raising [`App\Exceptions\SiteHttpException`](../app/Exceptions/SiteHttpException.php) from [`respond_with_status`](../app/Tags/RespondWithStatus.php) instead of a bare `abort()`. It is a Symfony `HttpException` wearing Statamic's own `RendersHttpExceptions` trait — the same treatment `Statamic\Exceptions\NotFoundHttpException` gets — and overrides `contents()` only to publish `response_message` on the cascade alongside the `response_code` the trait already publishes. The views are **three thin per-code files** delegating to one shared [`errors/_targetless_response`](../resources/views/errors/_targetless_response.antlers.html) body, and the 404's search box and popular-links list moved into [`errors/_recovery_aids`](../resources/views/errors/_recovery_aids.antlers.html) so all four error pages share them. `404` is deliberately **excluded** from the new path. The `message` is now escaped on every error page, the 404 included.

**Why.** The status was never the defect — UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1641 has been serving all three correctly. Measured against the running site with `APP_DEBUG=false`, a `410` rule carrying a message returned a 7,785-byte page titled "An Error Occurred: Gone" with the message nowhere in it; `451` the same; and `403` reached Laravel's bundled `errors::403`, which *did* render the message but on an unstyled minimal page with no branding and none of the site's recovery aids. So the ticket's premise was right for two codes and wrong for the third, and the fix has to cover both shapes. Adding an `errors/410.antlers.html` alone does **not** work: Laravel renders an error view through a plain `response()->view()`, which applies **no layout** — probed directly, the view was reached and returned a bare unstyled fragment. Statamic's `View` is the only thing that applies the site layout, and the trait that reaches it is public, so the fix is to raise an exception that carries it rather than to reimplement cascade hydration and layout resolution in a `withExceptions()` callback.

**Why the message rides the cascade rather than being re-resolved.** The `404` page reads its message by re-running `{{ find_redirect_rule }}` (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1934), which works only because that page renders inside a request whose cascade is already warm. Publishing `response_message` from the exception is the direct route, costs no second rule lookup, and generalizes to any `abort($code, $message)` in the app — Statamic's `View`, unlike Laravel's error path, does not hand the exception to the template, which is the only reason a template cannot reach `$exception->getMessage()` itself.

**Rejected.** *One shared `errors/4xx` view*, the ticket's own suggestion, resting on Laravel's `getHttpExceptionView()` fallback from `errors::<code>` to `errors::4xx` — it **cannot reach `403` at all**, because the framework ships its own `403.blade.php` in the `errors` namespace and the per-code lookup wins before the fallback is consulted; it is also unreachable from `RendersHttpExceptions`, which resolves `errors.<code>` with no `4xx` step. Widening it to every 4xx would additionally capture `400`, `405`, `413` and `422`, which no one asked for. *Three fully independent per-code views* — triplicates ~50 lines of recovery aids, which is how three pages drift apart. *A `withExceptions()` render callback in [`bootstrap/app.php`](../bootstrap/app.php)* — catches every `HttpException` the framework raises, not just the ones a redirect rule emits, and has to duplicate the cascade hydration and layout resolution the trait already does. *Route `404` through the same exception* — `errors/404.antlers.html` includes `errors/_redirects`, so rendering it through Statamic's view re-runs the rule that raised the error with nothing to bottom out on; a bare `abort(404)` at least stops at depth two inside Laravel's try/catch, and UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1934 already made that branch inert for the same reason.

**The `message` was being rendered raw, on the page that already shipped.** Antlers is raw-by-default and `message` is CP textarea input, so [`errors/_redirect_message`](../resources/views/errors/_redirect_message.antlers.html) rendered whatever markup an author pasted — verified against the running site, where a stored `<img src=x onerror=alert(1)>` reached the 404 page as live markup. That is a pre-existing stored-XSS surface on the same field this change reuses, so it is fixed here rather than deferred: `entities` then `nl2br` on every error page, which escapes the angle brackets while keeping the textarea's line breaks.

**The harness limit, measured rather than asserted.** The prior entry recorded that "an in-process HTTP test cannot substitute, because a frontend 404 resolves through a path where Antlers binds none of the tag's row values". The mechanism is now known: Statamic binds `Cascade` as a **singleton around the request present when the app booted**, and in a test that is the empty bootstrap request — so `current_uri` is `URL::tidy($request->path())` = `/` for every request a test makes, whatever URI it asks for. Instrumented and measured: the tag ran twice per request and saw `uri: "/"` both times. A rule seeded for the URI under test therefore never matches and every assertion passes for the wrong reason. `tests/Browser` — the harness the ticket proposed — cannot serve it either, because the `browser` job omits the `content/` submodule for cost and every real front-end route 500s there. [`TargetlessErrorPageTest`](../tests/Feature/Redirects/TargetlessErrorPageTest.php) therefore hands the exception to the real `ExceptionHandler`, which is the same call `Kernel::handle()`'s catch makes and covers everything downstream of the throw; the framework glue that reaches it is stated in the file rather than left implied, and every code was cross-checked over real HTTP against the running site in both debug states.

**Every producer of a targetless status raises the exception — adding the views made a bare `abort()` actively worse.** The adversarial review found this and it is the non-obvious half of the decision. [`RedirectWordPressQueryPermalinks`](../app/Http/Middleware/RedirectWordPressQueryPermalinks.php) serves the `?p=` / `?page_id=` rules (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1657) and had its own targetless branch calling `abort((int) $response)`. That was harmless while no `errors::<code>` view existed — Laravel fell through to `convertExceptionToResponse()` and answered with Symfony's complete generic document. The moment this change added `errors/410.antlers.html`, `RegisterErrorViewPaths` put the app's `errors` directory *ahead* of the framework's and Statamic's finder puts `antlers.html` ahead of `blade.php`, so that same `abort()` began resolving the new view through `response()->view()` — the layout-less path this entry's second paragraph already measured. A live front-end route would have gone from a valid generic page to a bare `<article>` fragment with no `<!doctype>`, no `<head>` and no CSS, *and* still dropped the message. So the middleware now raises `SiteHttpException` too, and its `ABORT_RESPONSES` is documented as necessarily the same set as `RespondWithStatus::SITE_RENDERED_CODES`. The general rule this establishes: **once an app-level `errors/<code>` view exists, every `abort($code)` in the app is a latent layout-less response** — the view is not additive, it is a takeover of that code's rendering.

**The breadcrumb trail had to widen with it.** [`_breadcrumbs`](../resources/views/layout/breadcrumbs/_breadcrumbs.antlers.html) gated its non-link terminal crumb on `response_code == 404`. The three new pages are equally routeless, so they fell to the `else` arm, whose `nav:breadcrumbs` yields nothing and leaves the trail ending on an *ancestor* — asserting the visitor stands on a page they do not, and emitting a `BreadcrumbList` that terminates there. The gate now covers all four codes and the leaf label is driven by `response_code`.

**Residual, named rather than silent.** No test drives a stored rule end to end over HTTP, for the reason above; the chain is covered in two halves that meet at the exception. The four surviving mutants on `RespondWithStatus` are equivalent — two `in_array()` strict-flag flips over int arrays, and an increment/decrement of the `0` fallback for a non-numeric code — and are documented in the test file rather than chased. The breadcrumb widening is pinned against the **view source** rather than a render, and that is a genuine gap rather than a preference: in-process the `Cascade` singleton resolves `current_uri` to `/`, so `is_homepage` is true and `$breadcrumbs_is_high_level_homepage` suppresses the entire breadcrumb nav — a rendered assertion would pass on markup that never appears. Confirmed by probe: the rendered targetless page contains no `<nav aria-label="breadcrumb">` at all, and its canonical is the site root.

**Companion.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1942 issue `## Decision`; [`SiteHttpException`](../app/Exceptions/SiteHttpException.php) carries the per-behavior rationale; predecessor UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1933 / UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1934 above; UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1641 introduced the `410 Gone` path.

## 2026-08-11 — Linked worktrees do NOT share the `content` submodule HEAD (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1959)

**Decision.** Measured on the pinned git (2.32.0.windows.2): every linked worktree has its **own** `content` gitdir at `.git/worktrees/<name>/modules/content`, and only the primary uses `.git/modules/content`. [`.claude/rules/worktrees.md`](../.claude/rules/worktrees.md) asserted the opposite and is corrected; the [`local-ci`](../.claude/skills/local-ci/SKILL.md) skill had it right and now cites the rule instead of restating it. Two things follow. `.githooks/pre-commit` and `scripts/local-ci.mjs` **keep** `--ignore-submodules=all`, but on a different stated reason — a merge that moves the `content` gitlink leaves *your own* worktree's submodule at the old revision until `git submodule update` runs, and on the rarer occasion that such a merge also conflicts (the only kind this gate sees, since a clean merge commits without running `pre-commit`) its "stage them" advice would commit the stale revision back. And the [`update-dependencies-ticket`](../.claude/skills/update-dependencies-ticket/SKILL.md) sweep moves from an ephemeral worktree to a **slot**, since the "solo → primary checkout" default it claimed an exception to was removed by UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1947.

**Why.** The claim was load-bearing in three files and decided whether two sessions could hold different `content` revisions at all. The confirming observation was live rather than contrived: four trees on four branches carrying two different `content` revisions, each tree's HEAD matching its *own* superproject gitlink — the CI slot validating a branch at one content commit while the primary served the site from another. That state cannot exist on a shared HEAD. A second claim in the same bullet — that `git submodule status` from a linked worktree reports the *primary's* revision — did not reproduce either; every tree reported its own, including the CI slot on its divergent commit.

**Rejected.** *Contrive the experiment by moving a slot's `content` and watching the primary* — the natural experiment above was already running, at zero risk to the served site. *Delete the version-dependence note from the `local-ci` skill now that the pinned git is measured* — that note is about git versions this repo has not measured, and the conservative procedure it justifies costs nothing here; retiring it would trade a real safety margin for tidiness, on no evidence. *Drop `--ignore-submodules=all` along with its false premise* — the flag is still needed; removing it would fire the merge gate on `content` in the case where its advice is actively harmful. *Keep the deps sweep on an ephemeral worktree (what UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1959 itself proposed)* — a slot is the rule's ordinary path for branch work and arrives pre-bootstrapped, so the ephemeral form charges a full `composer install` + `npm ci` + four submodule checkouts to isolate something a slot already isolates.

**Not settled, and named rather than assumed.** The object store is *not* shared either — no gitdir carries an `objects/info/alternates`, and three measured here held 4.1M, 2.5M and 1.2M independently. So why `content` and `app/private/assets` initialize without a network clone while `users` and `public/assets` fetch remains **unexplained**. An earlier draft of this correction asserted a shared object store as the explanation and was wrong, which is worth recording next to the verdict: the failure mode this entry exists to fix is precisely writing down an unmeasured mechanism.

**Companion.** [#1959](https://github.com/UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive/issues/1959); the measurement and its procedure live in [`.claude/rules/worktrees.md`](../.claude/rules/worktrees.md).

## 2026-08-12 — The `uams-2020` theme data API is a clean drop; the `show_in_rest` half was never the theme's (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1644, UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1650)

**Decision.** **Drop** the theme's `quicklinks` REST endpoint — no native reimplementation, no shim, no redirect. It has been switched off upstream since **2024-05-06** (`UAMS-Web/uams-2020@8726534c`, "[FD20-4032] Comment out instances of add_action for quick links") and returns `404` in production today, so nothing can be depending on it. Separately, the "theme-level `show_in_rest`" half of UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1644 is **scoped out**: the theme registers no route, post type, taxonomy, REST field or meta at all. `functions.php:945` only attaches a `filter` **query parameter** to core's existing `wp/v2` per-post-type routes, and its consumer is the UAMS News Syndication **plugin**, so disposing of the theme does not dispose of it.

**Why.** The risk this catalog exists to catch is an *uncontrolled external* consumer — the UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1043 precedent, where a shipped native app pinned the Find-a-Doc payload shape. Here there is no endpoint to consume, which is a stronger answer than an access-log trace and needs no logs: a `404` is a `404` for every caller. Verified rather than inferred — `GET /wp-json/` lists every registered namespace, and a sweep of **64 distinct production hosts** returned **63 reachable, none exposing a `menus/*` namespace**; `/wp-json/menus/v2/quicklinks` returns `404` on the hosts probed directly. The theme's own consumer is dead too: `uamswp_request_quicklinks()` still `wp_remote_get()`s three hard-coded URLs, but its only caller's hook is commented out (`lib/quicklinks.php:15`), so profile pages fall through to a hard-coded default link list. The single unreachable host, `inside.uams.edu`, resolves to a private RFC1918 address and is intranet-only, so it cannot carry an external consumer by construction.

**Rejected.** *Reimplement `quicklinks` natively in Statamic* — rebuilds a feature that has been off for over two years and whose output is already a static list in the template. *Preserve it as a contract* — nothing holds the other end. *Trace consumers from server access logs*, which the UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1650 spike prescribed — unnecessary once the route is shown not to be registered, and it would have been the expensive path to a weaker answer. *Keep `show_in_rest` inside UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1644's scope* — it is core plus a plugin, so a theme-disposition ticket cannot decide it.

**Companion.** Issues UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1644 (`## Decision`, the catalog) and UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1650 (the consumer evidence). Sibling MVP source: the 2026-07-06 Find-a-Doc entry below (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1043). **Residual:** `uams-analytics/v1` is a custom UAMS REST namespace live on every host swept; it is a plugin rather than the theme, and nothing tracks its post-migration disposition.

## 2026-08-12 — Retire our field-conditions evaluator once upstream fixes `eval()`; ask Statamic to slim the bundle (#1931, UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1979)

**Decision.** When a `statamic/cms` release removes the `eval()` from the public field-conditions path, **go back to the vendor bundle** and delete the evaluator UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1902 built. #1931 previously defaulted to keeping ours permanently; that default is reversed. Separately, ask upstream on [`statamic/ideas`](https://github.com/statamic/ideas/issues) to stop shipping the `$passkeys` WebAuthn client to anonymous visitors (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1979) — feature requests route there, while the `eval()` defect (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1929) and its fix (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1930) belong on `statamic/cms`, which accepts only bug reports and pull requests.

**Why.** Three arguments held up "keep ours" and only one survived scrutiny. **Payload survives**: the bundle is 98,735 bytes raw, 30,404 gzipped, browser-cached. **Test coverage does not** — [`validator.test.js`](../resources/js/conditions/validator.test.js)'s sharp cases pin *our replication of upstream's quirks* ("never matches `===` against a number", "the operand-coercion trap"), so that coverage exists **because** we forked; retiring removes the need for it rather than losing something earned. **Vocabulary tracking argues the other way**: the Control Panel still uses the vendor evaluator, so our front-end vocabulary must match theirs or an editor sets a condition in the CP, sees it work in preview, and gets different behavior on the public page — and UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1902's constants-parity guard pins `KEYS`, `OPERATORS` and `ALIASES`, catching a **vocabulary** change but **not a semantic** one. If upstream changes how `contains` coerces, the constants still match, the guard stays green, and behavior diverges silently. The trade is one cached asset against 389 lines of owned evaluator (996 with docblocks), 537 lines of tests serving only it, and permanent exposure to that silent divergence. On the bundle: `resources/js/frontend/helpers.js` has three commits in its whole history — created for field conditions in 2022 (`4632bbf5`), reformatted in 2025, then `8cd4799c` ("[6.x] Frontend Passkeys") in April 2026 added **+2 lines** pulling in a 251-line WebAuthn client and `@simplewebauthn/browser`. Front-end passkey login is opt-in and rare; field conditions are ordinary, so the default charges the common case for the rare one.

**Rejected.** *Keep ours permanently and close #1931 as not planned* — #1931's original default. Buys back one cached asset by accepting the divergence risk indefinitely, resting on a coverage argument that is circular. *Close #1931 now with no recorded direction* — leaves a ticket whose trigger cannot fire (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1929 and UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1930 have not been started, `WATCHES` is empty, and `statamic/cms` is pinned exactly, so a fixed release arrives only on a deliberate bump) and no guidance for whoever picks it up. *Fold the bundle-size ask into UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1929's defect report* — two asks in one upstream thread dilute both, and they belong in different repos anyway. *File the bundle ask on `statamic/cms`* — that repo sets `blank_issues_enabled: false` and redirects feature requests to `statamic/ideas`; it would have been closed.

**Companion.** Issues #1931 (`## Decision`, the retirement) and UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1979 (the upstream ask). Builds on the 2026-08-05 entry below (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1902), which owns why we forked. Nothing waits on either: #667's `blocked_by` edges are both closed.

## 2026-08-13 — Tia's cache is pinned per checkout, so isolation stops depending on an upstream accident (#1992, UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1924)

**Decision.** Configure `pest()->tia()->directory('tests/.pest/tia')` in [`tests/Pest.php`](../tests/Pest.php) and bump `pestphp/pest` to `^5.1.1` for the setting. A project-relative path makes `Storage::tempDir()` return `$projectRoot.'/'.$directory` and never consult `projectKey()`, so every checkout and worktree gets its own graph by construction. Upstream's answer about the default keying becomes informational; [`pestphp/pest#1820`](https://github.com/pestphp/pest/pull/1820) is watched against #1992 for its terminal signal only.

**Why.** The default key is `<folder-slug>-<origin-hash>`, and `slug()` lowercases and collapses runs of non-alphanumerics — so `uams-statamic-a` and `uams_statamic_a` produce one key. Edges are global to that key (`Graph::replaceEdges()` takes no branch), so a shared key lets one checkout's edge set drive another's affected-set computation: a green run that executed nothing, which is the failure UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1717 adopted Tia to avoid and is silent. Linked worktrees are separated **today** only because `Storage::rawOriginUrl()` does `is_file()` on a `gitdir:` pointer and falls back to a path hash. That is not a guarantee we can hold upstream to: [`pestphp/docs/tia.md`](https://github.com/pestphp/docs/blob/master/tia.md) documents the opposite intent — *"multiple worktrees of the same repository share one cache"* — and UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1820 is an open PR to make it so. Measured on `v5.1.1` and on UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1820's head: two clones sharing a folder name **already** share a cache today, and four roots resolve to four distinct paths once the directory is configured.

**This refines a UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1717 rejection rather than reversing it, and the distinction is the whole point.** That entry rejected *"enable Tia globally via `pest()->tia()` in `tests/Pest.php`"* because it "puts Tia-aware code in the gate's own bootstrap." `directory()` does **not** enable the engine — Tia stays off unless `--tia` or `PEST_TIA` is passed, and the call only sets a static path. The residual coupling is real but bounded: one line in the bootstrap that would fatal if upstream removed the API, which the `^5.1.1` constraint bounds. Pest exposes no other configuration surface for this, so the alternative was not adopting it at all. **This was shipped before the rejection was noticed** — the decision-log consult that this file's own header mandates did not happen, and the refinement was reasoned out afterwards rather than before.

**Rejected.** *Wait for upstream's answer, then act* — the original plan; rejected because no answer can make the current behavior safe to rely on, since even "intended" would describe behavior their docs contradict and their open PR changes. *Defend by keeping folder names distinct* — a discipline, not a mechanism, and it fails silently on the slug collision above. *Rely on the slot layout* — same objection, and it leaves `EnterWorktree`'s ephemeral trees and any second checkout unaccounted for. *Store the cache outside the repo at a per-checkout absolute path* — equivalent isolation, but an absolute path is machine-specific and cannot be committed.

**Residual.** `--baselined` is unused here (no invocation in `composer.json`, `scripts/`, or CI), so moving the cache does not touch that pipeline; the only reference is a `ci.yml` comment explaining why Tia is not in the gate. Guarding the config line itself is UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1991. The separate question of whether a linked worktree silently reuses a **stale** graph across `composer.lock` changes is invalidation rather than storage, and is UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1996.

**Companion.** #1992 issue `## Decision`; UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1924 for the upstream question and the measured key pairs; [`tests/Pest.php`](../tests/Pest.php).

## 2026-08-13 — `main` keeps `enforce_admins: false` in both repos; the flag is a measured no-op against the current rule set (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1843)

**Superseded** by the 2026-10-05 entry for UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2016: classic branch protection on `main` was replaced by two organization rulesets with no bypass list, so `enforce_admins` no longer exists to set. This entry is kept as the record of what was measured.

**Decision.** `enforce_admins` stays **`false`** on `main` in both `uams-statamic` and `wordpress-importer`, and the two repos stay **matched**. No configuration change ships. The value is revisited at exactly one trigger — the first time a rule an administrator could otherwise bypass (`required_status_checks`, `required_pull_request_reviews`, `required_linear_history`, `required_conversation_resolution`) is added to either repo — tracked as a follow-up rather than a date.

**Why.** The ticket was filed on the premise that an admin token can force-push to `main` today and that `enforce_admins: true` is what would stop it. **Measured, that premise is false.** Every arm was run twice on a throwaway protected branch, flag off then on, with `main` never touched:

| Arm | `false` | `true` |
| --- | --- | --- |
| REST `PUT .../pulls/{n}/merge`, `gh pr merge --squash`, `gh pr merge --squash --admin` | merged | merged |
| Direct fast-forward write to the protected ref | ok | ok |
| Force-push — REST `PATCH /git/refs … force=true` **and** git transport | blocked 422 / GH006 | blocked 422 / GH006 |
| Branch deletion | blocked 422 | (no weaker than `false`) |

The delta is **zero on every arm**. `allow_force_pushes: false` and `allow_deletions: false` already bind repository administrators, on both transports, with `enforce_admins: false` — so the threat the ticket set out to close is already closed. `enforce_admins` governs only the rules an admin could otherwise bypass, and **none of those are configured**; it has nothing to act on. Both transports were tested deliberately, because the REST refs API and a git push could plausibly have enforced differently and the two readings led to opposite decisions.

That leaves the choice as a bet on a *future* failure, not a security decision. Setting `true` today buys nothing measurable and accepts a concrete latent cost: `RUN_REMOTE_CI` is off, every job reports `skipped`, and a required status check added later would sit pending forever — with `enforce_admins: true` that hard-deadlocks every merge in a repo where four administrators perform all of them, recoverable only by lifting protection and re-applying it. The competing risk, a required check that admins silently bypass and that therefore only *looks* like a gate, is real but arrives with its own deliberate decision point: adding such a rule is itself the moment to set this flag. The near, current risk loses to the far, self-announcing one.

**On Q3, for the record.** `false` was **not** an unconsidered default. It was written as a literal `false` in the `PUT` payload and displayed to the maintainer in an approval preview they selected at `2026-08-01T01:53:23Z`, ten minutes before `wordpress-importer#515` merged. What is genuinely absent is a stated *rationale* — and no option in that session ever offered `true`, so the value was approved as part of a package rather than weighed as a fork. This entry supplies the rationale that was missing.

**The deny list is not a mitigation and must not be cited as one.** `.claude/settings.json`'s deny entries match only the flag-*first* spellings; flag-last `-X DELETE`, `gh api graphql` mutations, an allow-listed `-X PUT` overwriting a protection object, and `git push origin :branch` all reach the same end state. The merged policy is also wider than the repo file — `~/.claude/settings.json` on the primary development machine carries `Bash(gh api *)`, `Bash(gh pr *)` and an **empty** deny list. Branch protection is the boundary; the deny list is a guardrail against a reflexive mistake.

**Rejected.** *Set `enforce_admins: true` now* ("it is free today, so pre-arm it") — a no-op that changes behavior later is not free, it is a deferred cost with no owner, and the behavior it would change first is the deadlock above. *Differ per repo* — the two configs are field-for-field identical, run the same autonomous validate-and-merge loop, and nothing about a package versus the host app bears on administrator bypass; divergence would be drift, not a decision. *Revisit at Launch / 1.0* — a calendar trigger fires when nothing has changed and stays silent when something has; the event trigger is the one that matters. *File a follow-up per repo to apply the decided value* (the ticket's original acceptance criteria) — both would close immediately as no-ops, so they collapse into the single revisit-trigger ticket.

**Residual.** Organization-level rulesets could not be read (the token lacks `admin:org`), so a rule invisible to this measurement could exist. Deletion under `true` was inferred rather than observed — it is blocked at `false`, so it cannot be weaker. The measurement describes today's rule set only.

**Companion.** [`docs/runbooks/branch-protection.md`](runbooks/branch-protection.md) — the settings of record, and the recovery procedure for the force-push and deletion that protection blocks *today*, administrators included. Also UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1843 (the spike, carrying the full arm-by-arm log); `wordpress-importer#515` (which applied the **importer's** protection, the policy this repo mirrors); UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1841 (which applied this repo's, by hand from a local admin session).

## 2026-08-13 — Ontology removal rules suppress Areas of Expertise only, on the profile only, matching ANY region (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1687)

**Decision.** Port WordPress' `$hide_medical_ontology` as a `hide_medical_ontology` computed on `people` and `clinical_providers`, with three deliberate divergences. **(1) Only Areas of Expertise is suppressed**, not all three ontology sections. **(2) Only the profile render honors it** — a suppressed provider still appears in Find-a-Doc listings, in the card's facet attributes, and on the Areas of Expertise pages themselves. **(3) A region criterion matches when ANY of the provider's regions is the named one**, not only when it is their sole region. Row semantics are ported faithfully: every non-empty criterion in a row must match, an all-empty row matches nothing, the first matching row wins.

**Why.** Each divergence answers a question WordPress cannot. *Sections:* `clinical_expertise` is the only routable ontology collection — `clinical_conditions` and `clinical_services` define no `route`, so those sections already render unlinked text and suppression would remove no link. *Surfaces:* WordPress computes the flag on the provider profile and nowhere else, so profile-only is what the editors who wrote the rule have observed for years; it is also the only option whose blast radius is auditable, since reaching further would change which providers a Find-a-Doc block *returns* (`OntologyOverlapExpertise`) and would mutate other entries on save (`MirrorSpotlightFeaturedProviderRelationships`). *Regions:* WordPress compares `in_array(implode('', $provider_region), $remove_region)`, concatenating a provider's region ids into one string, so a provider in regions `[65, 66]` becomes `"6566"` and can never match `["65"]` — only a sole-region provider is reachable at all. That is a string-concatenation accident rather than a rule, and our `regions` is an aggregate across a provider's locations, so reproducing it would leave the feature nearly inert. On a stale 2024 sample the two readings differ by roughly an order of magnitude: 68 of 112 providers carry the configured region somewhere, 7 hold it alone.

**Rejected.** *Suppress all three sections* (WordPress parity) — the "Remove Medical Ontology" label promises it and an editor cannot express a subset, but two of the three have no links to remove, so it would hide plain clinical text for no stated benefit. *Per-section criteria* — needs new blueprint fields, which UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1687 puts out of scope. *All presentation surfaces*, and *a full provider-level property* — each more coherent if the motive is SEO, but both lose to the auditable port; the excluded surfaces are named in a follow-up rather than dropped silently. *Bug-for-bug region parity* — indefensible to explain and nearly inert on this data model.

**Known cost, accepted.** Suppressing a section changes what renders between the two appointment CTAs, so `ProviderProfileBodyResolver` now skips a suppressed `expertise` — without that, a provider whose expertise was their only body content gets UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1695's back-to-back CTA back. The existing drift guard could not see this: it counts *opening* partial calls, and an `{{ if }}` wrapper leaves the count unchanged. A second guard now pins the set of conditions gating a section partial. The residual, named rather than silent: the reverse direction survives, so the Areas of Expertise page still lists a suppressed provider and person JSON-LD still emits `knowsAbout` derived from `expertise`.

**Companion.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1687, and the follow-up tracking the excluded surfaces.

## 2026-08-13 — Fix null-bearing `Value` handling at the Antlers choke point, as a tracked vendor patch (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1673)

**Decision.** Carry a `statamic/cms` Composer patch that moves the `Statamic\Fields\Value` unwrap **above** the null guard in `ModifierManager::evaluate()`, so a `Value` wrapping `null` short-circuits the modifier chain exactly as a bare `null` already does. Tracked through the four-ticket chain in the [`upstream-patch-lifecycle`](../.claude/skills/upstream-patch-lifecycle/SKILL.md) skill — write the patch, file upstream, offer the PR, retire on a fixed release — with a `WATCHES` row on the upstream signal.

**Why.** The bug is not two modifiers, it is the choke point. Measured on Statamic 6.26.0: of the **240** registered core modifier handles — 235 after excluding the five debug handles a sweep cannot safely invoke — **57** emit an `E_DEPRECATED` when handed a null, because Statamic's core modifiers never had the PHP 8.1 null-safety sweep. Augmented replicator rows reach them constantly — `performAugmentation()` materializes every blueprint handle, so an absent optional field arrives as `Value(null)` — and this repo escapes every editor-controlled sink (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1500, UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1672), 213 `| sanitize` and 102 `| strip_tags` occurrences across 78 view files. One reorder at the single point every modifier invocation passes through closes all 57, and closes the next one nobody has written yet. Verified by applying the reorder and re-measuring: eight sampled modifiers spanning eight distinct PHP string functions go from one deprecation each to zero, with `tests/Unit/Modifiers` green (621 tests), and a hand negative control turning it red again for the right reason.

**Rejected.** *Guard at each sink* (`{{ ( x ?? '' ) | sanitize }}`) — verified to work, but it is 315 edits across 78 files, covers only the sinks anyone remembers, and leaves sink 316 exposed. *A project modifier in `app/Modifiers/`* — the issue proposed overriding `sanitize` there, which **cannot work**: `ExtensionServiceProvider` registers app extensions first and then merges core over the top, so a `App\Modifiers\Sanitize` is silently shadowed by `CoreModifiers@sanitize` (probed directly). A boot-time `register()` *does* win, but it fixes 2 of 57 and makes `{{ x | sanitize }}` mean something a template reader cannot see. *Patching `CoreModifiers::sanitize()`/`stripTags()` instead* — narrower semantics but the wrong file: 3,405 lines against `ModifierManager`'s 164, so far more patch-drift risk under `composer-exit-on-patch-failure: true`, and still only two of the affected modifiers. *Accept and document* — the deprecation is inert here (the `deprecations` log channel routes to `null`), but the underlying inconsistency is not, and the escaping pattern is still spreading.

**Known cost, accepted.** Every modifier that coerced a *missing* value into a *present* one stops doing so. Enumerated by rendering each registered handle's bare-null return: **44** handles change — 36 core (`format_number`, `to_json`, `md5`, `length`, `sum`, `round`, `word_count`, `type_of`, `json`, … ) and 8 app (`safe_count`, `format_bytes`, `format_duration`, `indefinite_article`, `is_null`, `read_time_uams`, `word_count_uams`, `format_bitrate`). Each now returns what it **already** returned for a bare `null`, so this removes a divergence rather than creating 44. Only **two** call sites in the repo render differently, both needing blueprint-invalid data to reach: `( simple_list | safe_count ) >= 1` in `_uamsweb_block_image_side_by_side.antlers.html` (an absent `list` counted as one item, so a block with no list passed its own validity gate — it now suppresses, which is what the gate says), and `"embedUrl": {{ url | to_json }}` in `_video_segment_full.antlers.html` (emitted the literal `null`, now emits nothing — a JSON syntax error, hardened here by making the key conditional). The first draft of this entry claimed the list was three handles; that was a sample of the deprecation sweep mistaken for an enumeration of output changes, and the adversarial review caught it.

**Companion.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1673, and the patch section in [`patches/README.md`](../patches/README.md).

**Retired (2026-10-02, UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2003, UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2774).** `statamic/cms` 6.35.0 carries the same reorder upstream (`statamic/cms#15540`), so the patch was removed before the package moved. Re-measured on the unpatched 6.35.0: rendering `{{ wrapped | <handle> }}` with `wrapped` a `Value(null)` raises no `E_DEPRECATED` for any of the 214 core modifier handles named by `CoreModifiers`' public methods (the six debug handles excluded), against 53 handles that deprecate when handed a bare null through `Modify`, which bypasses the guard. That count is of method names, not the 240 registered handles above, which include aliases.

## 2026-08-13 — The test env reads content through a disposable mirror of `content/`, not the submodule (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1916)

**Decision.** While `APP_ENV=testing`, every content-backed Stache store **and** the Eloquent asset-metadata dual-write root at `storage/framework/testing/content-mirror`, a copy of `content/` that `test:seed-stache` builds before it warms. Both roots resolve through one function (`App\Support\ContentRoot::path()`) so they cannot move apart; the asset-metadata path is a config key (`asset_metadata.yaml_directory`) read at call time, which is what lets a test point it elsewhere without `App::setBasePath()`. `App\Support\ContentMirror` owns the build, with two deliberately different entry points — `rebuildIfStale()` for `test:seed-stache` between bands, and `ensureExists()` at process boot, which builds only when the mirror is **absent** and never when it has merely drifted. `App\Support\StacheSeedStamp` records which root each snapshot indexes, and a process whose stores read a different one refuses to seed from it.

**Why.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1873 closed the collection-tree quarter of this and deliberately left the rest, because the obvious general fix is destructive: `ExistsAsFile::writeFile()` recomputes `buildPath()` and `File::delete()`s the `initialPath` when they differ, so re-rooting a store while its cached index still resolves real paths **moves committed files**.
