That is not a hypothesis — `content/trees/collections/default/pages.yaml` (166 lines) was deleted twice during this ticket, once by the spike and once by an ordinary branch switch, and on both occasions the tests were passing. Warming the snapshot through the same config is what makes the two halves agree, which is why the mirror build belongs inside `test:seed-stache` rather than beside it. The mirror is also the only fix that covers tests **nobody has written yet**: a per-test trait protects the tests that remember it, which is exactly how UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1919's opt-out flag let three suites corrupt `content/models/assets/1.yml`.

**Why the asset-metadata surface needed its own seam.** It is outside the Stache entirely — the write hangs off Eloquent's `saved` event and resolved `base_path('content/models/assets/{id}.yml')` — so no store re-root reaches it, and a mid-run kill left exactly `M models/assets/1.yml` behind. Under test the metadata database is `:memory:`, so its autoincrement restarts at `1` in every process and the first asset any test creates lands on a real image's committed alt text, caption and photographer credit. Making it configuration rather than a literal also retired `App::setBasePath()` from three suites, which had been redirecting this one directory by moving a process-wide root every other `base_path()` caller reads.

**Cost.** Measured against `origin/main` at `cd574e47d` in the same worktree, byte-identical pest flags, route cache on, fresh seed per arm: band **1880s → 1893s (+0.7%)**, inside the ±1.2% this suite varies by, and ~23s of the serial arm's +44s is the 11 new tests themselves. The one attributable cost is the **seed step, 24s → 50s** — the mirror build plus warming through a freshly-copied tree — paid once per band. Test counts matched exactly on both arms (6594→6605 parallel, 471→482 serial), which is what licensed the comparison.

**Rejected.** *A `StacheIsolatedTestCase` per file* — safe and consistent, but these tests exist to read real content; most would need fixtures built for content they currently read, changing what they assert. *Copy `content/` once per process* — reaches the same safe state, but ~12 paratest workers each copying 3,233 files is the concurrent-I/O saturation UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1537 documents; one shared mirror built once by the seed costs a single copy per band. *Per-test store sets* — whether a store can be isolated depends on whether that test reads real items of it, a per-test fact that does not generalize. *Rebuild the mirror on drift at process boot* — symmetrical with the seed and wrong: mid-band that drift is a concurrent worker's live fixtures, so "restoring" it deletes files another worker is reading. *An unconditional rebuild in the seed* — correct but wasteful; a two-sided stat fingerprint makes a redundant seed ~1s instead of ~4s. *Trusting `copyDirectory()`'s return value* — UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1740 established here that it is not proof, and a short mirror copy is a checkout that quietly lost content. *Relying on UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1919's `$skipYamlSync` default alone* — it stops the write from happening and does nothing when a test legitimately lifts it; the two are complementary.

**Residual.** The serial arm resolves **1** `pages` entry where the checkout holds 82 — reproduced on `origin/main` with none of this code present, so it predates the mirror and is tracked in **UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1994**. `ContentMirrorIsolationTest` therefore counts files on disk and only *reports* the Stache count; restoring that assertion is an acceptance criterion on UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1994.

**Companion.** Issue UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1916 (`## Decision`), [`docs/assets-metadata.md`](assets-metadata.md) (*Where the YAML lives*), and the headers of `config/statamic/stache.php`, `App\Support\ContentMirror` and `App\Support\StacheSeedStamp`. Predecessors: UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1873 (the tree half and the two-safe-states rule), UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1919 (the asset-metadata flag), UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1845 (how a stray file becomes boot-visible input), UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1740 / UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1978 (why a copy is verified rather than assumed).

## 2026-08-18 — Both HTMLPurifier serializer-cache races are dismissed; the recovery path is hardened instead (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1869)

**Decision.** **Dismiss both races UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1869 filed, and fix a third defect the adversarial review found in the recovery they rely on.** The type-subdirectory `mkdir()` race does not exist in the HTMLPurifier we ship, and the `Cache.SerializerPermissions => null` fix UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1853 withdrew is a *measured no-op* rather than deferred debt. The non-atomic `.ser` write is real, but every way it can fail lands in `TableCellSanitizer::sanitizeAfterFailure()` and produces byte-identical sanitized output. What the review found is that this recovery **logs before it returns anything safe**, and a broken logger throws out of `sanitize()` — so `logWithoutThrowing()` now guards both log calls. That is the only code change; neither race is fixed.

**Why.** The issue's premise did not survive being checked against `vendor/`. It quotes a `_prepareDir()` built on `umask(0000); mkdir($directory, $chmod); umask($old);`; root `composer.json` pins `^4.19`, and **v4.19.0** uses `!@mkdir(…) && !is_dir(…)` in *both* branches — 2 guarded occurrences, 0 unguarded, no `umask`. Measured with 40 children released together onto a cold cache (peak overlap 40, computed from per-child timestamps), 3 rounds each: stock at default `0755` **0 failures / 120**, stock at `null` **0 failures / 120**, and a positive control with the vendor reverted to the unguarded form **2 failures / 120** (`mkdir(): File exists`) — the control is what makes the zeros evidence rather than a weak probe. A second, independent block holds even if a future version regressed the idiom: every `mkdir()` in `_prepareDir()` is `@`-suppressed, and `HandleExceptions::handleError` promotes only when `error_reporting() & $level`, which PHP 8's `@` masks out. UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1853's real failures came from our own *unsuppressed* `ensureDirectoryExists()`. On the second race: the definition is **two** non-atomic writes totalling 19,855 bytes (`HTML` 19,339, `URI` 516 — the issue estimates "hundreds of KB"), and a cold compile-and-write costs 45.9 ms. Truncation at 50/90/99% always throws rather than yielding a partial definition — a prefix cannot satisfy the property count in `O:…:N:{…}` — and a **zero-byte** read is silent (`unserialize('')` returns `false` with no diagnostic at all, so nothing is promoted and nothing is logged). Driven through the real `TableCellSanitizer` over seven cells including `<script>alert(document.cookie)</script>`, `javascript:` links and `<img onerror>`, the truncated regime does not throw and its output is byte-identical to both the warm and no-cache regimes. A window with no observable consequence is not worth new code in a security control's read path — but a recovery that can itself throw is.

**Rejected.** *Set `Cache.SerializerPermissions => null`* — the issue's own proposal and the thing UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1853 withdrew; measured as a no-op, and it would additionally cost two unconditional `@mkdir()` calls **per cache miss** (`_prepareDir()` is never reached on a warm hit — `get()` does not call it, and `Config::getDefinition()` returns before `add()`). *An atomic write via a registered custom `HTMLPurifier_DefinitionCache`* (`DefinitionCacheFactory::register()` + `Cache.DefinitionImpl`, ~40 lines, no vendor patch) — genuinely available and the cleanest hardening, but it changes no output and closes a window rather than an outcome. *Compile the definition at deploy time* — closes the window at its source, but needs a human to edit the Ploi/Forge hosting panel, and a deploy step that cannot be exercised from the repo decays silently. *Add a regression test pinning the cache recovery* — UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1853 already ships three (`does not throw when the definition cache is corrupt`, `never leaks the raw cell through the Antlers modifier when the sanitizer fails`, `still sanitizes when the cache directory cannot be created`). *Unlink the damaged `.ser` inside `sanitizeAfterFailure()`* — cannot fire on the silent zero-byte case, which never enters the handler, and is unnecessary for the truncated one; worse, it would guarantee a cold cache for every concurrent process at exactly the moment several are running. *Patch `vendor/` directly* — never on the table for a defect with no observable effect.

**Corrections this supersedes, recorded because each was stated confidently and was wrong.** (1) The deploy's `statamic:static:warm --queue` was cited as proof of concurrent first-writers, "silently falling back to an inline pool at `warm_concurrency: 10`". Three errors: the fallback **prints** `The queue connection is set to "sync". Queueing will be disabled.`; `warm_concurrency: 10` is scoped to the `full` strategy only, so `half` takes the vendor default of 25; and `STATAMIC_STATIC_CACHING_STRATEGY=null` in the shipped `.env.example`, so `StaticWarm` exits 1 at "Static caching is not enabled" before any of it runs. The conclusion survives by a different and stronger route — with no static cache, *every* production request is dynamic, so concurrent PHP-FPM workers are the first-writers. (2) The `.ser` race was described as having a single reachable outcome, a `Log::warning`; the zero-byte read is a second, completely silent one, and it is untested — UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1853's helper truncates to 40 bytes, not 0. (3) The logger defect was first escalated here as a stored-XSS fail-open, by analogy to UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1853. Running it end to end refuted that: the same broken logger breaks Statamic's own handler, so the render **500s** rather than publishing the cell. It is availability, not XSS.

**Residual.** A **third** concurrency trigger exists on this path and was not in the issue: `Serializer::cleanup()` — invoked by the default-registered `Cleanup` decorator whenever `get()` misses — does an **unsuppressed** `file_exists()`-then-`unlink()`, so two processes pruning the same stale `.ser` after a version bump race, and unlike the `mkdir()`s that warning is *not* `@`-suppressed. Its outcome is the same proven-benign recovery, so it is recorded rather than fixed. A `.ser` left short by a **crashed** writer is never repaired: `add()` bails on `file_exists`, and `cleanup()` skips it because `isOld()` is false for the current key — so it must be cleared by hand, and its cost repeats per process until the key rotates. That is a killed writer, not the concurrent-writer race, which converges because both writers emit identical bytes. Reachability of a torn read on the **production platform is unmeasured**: on Windows, four readers busy-spinning ~105,000 iterations each across a 20 MB / 42 ms write saw **zero** partial states, but that is a Windows-filesystem visibility property of `file_put_contents` and does not generalize to Linux, where a create-and-truncate open publishes a zero-byte file immediately. Installing a Linux environment was not warranted, because the outcome is benign either way. A HTMLPurifier **5.x** major could reintroduce an unguarded `_prepareDir()`; `^4.19` bounds that, and a major bump should re-run the probe.

**Companion.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1869 (findings comment + `## Decision`); UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1853 (the shipped half, and the `SerializerPermissions` withdrawal this closes out); `app/Services/Tables/TableCellSanitizer.php`; `tests/Feature/Tables/TableCellSanitizerCacheIsolationTest.php`.

## 2026-08-18 — Guard `composer test:tia` against Tia's blind fingerprint rather than pinning or patching Pest (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1996)

**Decision.** Refuse `--tia` from [`scripts/check-tia-safe.php`](../scripts/check-tia-safe.php) when Pest reports a present, git-tracked fingerprint input as absent, **measuring** `Fingerprint::compute()` rather than inferring the worktree shape. Track [`pestphp/pest#1813`](https://github.com/pestphp/pest/pull/1813) via `WATCHES` against #2041, which retires the guard once a release carries the fix.

**Why.** Tia validates a cached impact graph from a structural fingerprint over `composer.lock`, `phpunit.xml` and the JS lock files, each gated on a Symfony Finder query with `ignoreVCSIgnored(true)`. Where that query returns nothing for tracked files, every input is `null`, `structuralMatches()` compares null to null and reports a match, and a dependency change invalidates **nothing** — the silent green run UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1717 adopted Tia to avoid. Measured: editing `composer.lock` moved the fingerprint in the primary and in a sibling slot (invalidates), and left it null on both sides under `.claude/worktrees/` (replays a stale graph). Exposure is bounded — `--tia` is excluded from both gate paths, so only the opt-in `composer test:tia` is affected — which is what makes a guard proportionate.

**Rejected.** *Pin `pestphp/pest` past a release carrying UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1813* — there is no such release; UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1813 is an open PR, so this is a wait, not a remedy. *Carry a local patch* — the repo already carries four, each a standing maintenance cost, and patching a dependency to fix an **opt-in local accelerator** is out of proportion to the exposure. *Accept and document the limit* — leaves a live false green in the one tool whose failure mode is indistinguishable from success, which is the whole objection UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1717 recorded. *Encode the trigger conditions as a rule instead of measuring* — cheaper, but it would keep refusing in a tree upstream had fixed, and a guard that can never go green is one people learn to skip (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1952); measuring retires the refusal by itself.

**The trigger, characterized.** A linked worktree, an ancestor holding a real `.git` **directory** whose ignore rules match the worktree's path, and a **realpath** root. Upstream names only the first two. The third is an observation artifact rather than part of the bug: Symfony's `VcsIgnoredFilterIterator` compares a resolved `getRealPath()` against a `baseDir` it never resolves, so a symlinked root disengages the ignore filter — the same tree fingerprints resolved through `/var/folders/…` and null through `/private/var/folders/…`. An earlier revision of this change recorded "a synthetic fixture did not reproduce" and concluded the mechanism was open; that fixture had merely been built under `sys_get_temp_dir()`.

**Residual.** The sibling slots are safe only because no ancestor is a repository — relocating them under an ignored path would expose them, which [`.claude/rules/worktrees.md`](../.claude/rules/worktrees.md) now records. Retirement is #2041.

**Companion.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1996 issue; UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1717 (the Tia adoption); #1992 / UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1924 (the cache-*key* question, a different axis).

## 2026-08-18 — Cloud sessions get Pint as a lock-pinned phar over git, not a Composer fix (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1880)

**Decision.** `scripts/fetch-pint.mjs` fetches Laravel Pint's prebuilt phar at the exact reference `composer.lock` pins, over the git transport a cloud session already has, into the git-ignored `.tools/`. `php .tools/pint/builds/pint --test --config pint.json` is then the formatting check. Nothing about Composer, `require-dev`, or `phpstan/phpstan` changes.

**Why.** The ticket framed this as a dependency-resolution problem, which made every answer awkward. It is not one: Pint declares **no package dependencies** (only `php ^8.2` and four standard extensions) and already ships as a self-contained ~22 MB phar — `vendor/bin/pint` is merely a Composer proxy to it. Composer is how we happen to deliver Pint, not something Pint requires, so the `--prefer-source` constraint that blocks the other tools simply does not apply. The cost lands only on the sessions that need it. Verified locally: the phar runs with no autoloader outside the project, honors `pint.json`, and exits 1 on a file that needs formatting and 0 on a clean one.

**Rejected.** *Commit the phar* — 22.5 MB in every clone forever, permanent in git history, hand-updated and silently drifting from the lock, to serve a minority path. *Give `phpstan/phpstan` a source path* — the most ambitious option, and it would fix Pest and Larastan too, but it makes every developer inherit a non-obvious Composer indirection for a cloud-only problem; phpstan is deliberately dist-only upstream (a considered fix to a 7 GB repo), so this needs a fork we own, and the other tools are explicitly out of the ticket's scope. *Accept and document* — legitimate, and it remains the fallback if the git fetch turns out to be blocked, but it was the status quo arrived at by default rather than chosen.

**Residual.** Reachability of `github.com/laravel/pint` over git transport from a **cloud session** is inferred, not measured — it follows from `preferred-install=source` working there for 231 packages, but was verified only locally. The ticket's "demonstrate on a mis-formatted file" criterion has to be discharged inside a cloud session. If the fetch fails there, this collapses into the rejected *accept and document* option.

**Companion.** [`docs/runbooks/claude-code-cloud-sessions.md`](runbooks/claude-code-cloud-sessions.md) — "Running the formatting check anyway"; UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1880.

## 2026-08-18 — `statamic:site:clear` is refused outright via a container binding, with no escape hatch (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2022)

**Decision.** Bind `Statamic\Console\Commands\SiteClear` to `App\Console\Commands\RefusedSiteClear`, whose `handle()` explains and returns `FAILURE`. Absolute refusal, no env flag and no confirmation phrase. No separate guard for the `config/filesystems.php` rewrite.

**Why.** The command is written for a scratch site; here it clears the tracked `content/` and `users/` submodules, `resources/{views,blueprints,fieldsets}`, `resources/forms`, `storage/forms`, and replaces `config/statamic` with Statamic's defaults. Its "skip the default disks" filter protects nothing: all **eleven** asset containers use bespoke disk names, every one is `driver: local`, and every root sits inside a tracked submodule — nine under `public/assets/`, two under `app/private/assets/`. For each it also regex-strips that disk out of `config/filesystems.php`. The confirmation prompt is not a safety net but the reverse — `shouldAbort()` returns `false` (*proceed*) as soon as `--no-interaction` is present, and [`CLAUDE.md`](../CLAUDE.md) instructs every agent to pass that flag to all Artisan commands.

**Two registration paths, and the binding is the second layer rather than the only one.** The class answers to two names: `php artisan statamic:site:clear` and `php please site:clear` (Statamic's `please` application strips the `statamic:` prefix). **Both are covered without the binding**, by Laravel's `ApplicationBuilder::withCommands()` auto-discovery of `app/Console/Commands`: the subclass inherits the signature, and its bootstrapper is registered *after* Statamic's `ConsoleServiceProvider`, so Symfony's later-registration-wins rule overrides Statamic's under both names — including under `please`, whose kernel overrides `shouldDiscoverCommands()` to return true for itself. Measured by removing the binding at source: `app(SiteClear::class)` returned Statamic's real class while `please find('site:clear')` still returned the refusing one. The binding is therefore defense in depth — it covers the paths that resolve the *class* rather than the name (`app()`, `Artisan::call(SiteClear::class)`), and it keeps the guard alive if the subclass is ever moved out of the auto-discovered directory. The subclass must also re-declare `use RunsInPlease` even though the parent has it — `Please\Application::resolve()` gates on `class_uses()`, which ignores inherited traits; without it the command is deferred, `site:clear` vanishes from `please`, and Symfony's namespace abbreviation silently resolves it to `sitemapamic:clear` instead.

**Rejected.** *A `CommandStarting` listener* — matches on the command name, so it needs both strings enumerated, and a rename makes it stop matching **silently** while still passing invocations through. *Removing the command from the Artisan registry* — fail-closed, but indistinguishable from a broken install. *An env-flag escape hatch* — `--no-interaction` would satisfy it, and slot provisioning copies `.env` between worktrees, so enabling it once propagates it everywhere. *A typed-confirmation escape hatch* — safe, and the fallback had absolute refusal been judged too strict; not adopted because no current workflow needs the command. *A separate guard on the config rewrite* — `removeAssetContainerDisk()` has exactly one caller and is the only writer of `config_path('filesystems.php')` in `vendor/statamic/cms/src/` or `app/`, so refusing the command makes it unreachable.

**Residual.** This does not explain the 2026-08-13 destruction of the importer slot's submodules, whose signature (staged deletions) does not match this command's plain filesystem deletes. The guard protects this application only.

**Companion.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2022 issue `## Decision`; [`app/Providers/SiteClearGuardServiceProvider.php`](../app/Providers/SiteClearGuardServiceProvider.php); UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2023 (detection) and UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2024 (the worktrees rule) are the other two legs.

## 2026-08-28 — `organizations`: store the Workday tree, separate its identity from the public label (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2125)

**Decision.** Record UAMS's Workday supervisory hierarchy in the `organizations` collection and keep an entry's Workday identity separate from what it displays. Twelve parts. *Fields:* `workday_supervisory_organization_name`, `workday_supervisory_organization_id`, an `inherit_public_label_query` toggle, `public_label`, and `alternate_name` from the reusable fieldsets. *Labels:* `public_label` is required only when `inherit_public_label_query` is `false`, using the `if:` + `validate: [required, sometimes]` pairing the `provenance` field already uses; when the toggle is `true` the field is hidden and **no value is stored** (`always_save` left at `false`). Inheritance resolves to the nearest ancestor whose toggle is `false`, and affects **display only** — never identity for `related_organizations`, `department_organization` or schema. The toggle defaults to `false`. *Naming:* the existing schema-grade `name` stays separate and optional, with public output resolving to `name` where set and falling back to `public_label`. *Title:* computed at read time as `Workday name (ID) — public label`, dropping the trailing segment when the label is absent or identical to the Workday name — conditional on the search index reading the non-computed inputs, reverting to written-on-save if it cannot. *Scope:* store all **2,963** nodes; external organizations move to their own blueprint; bulk import populates `public_label` with the Workday name where none exists.

**Why.** Workday's tree is a *supervisory* structure and its labels are internal — `ICE | CORE PA Clinic Access B`, `COM | Department Chairs`, `ICE | Perioperative Services`. Measured on the fully expanded subtree (`SOC300000002`): 2,963 organizations, 12 levels, **74%** below level 3, **66%** leaves, **447** carrying a `CW` marker, **31** non-unique names, **11** with no Workday id at all. Against 181 existing entries, 145 at tiers `entries_by_organization_role_department_unit` accepts. Storing those labels and displaying them verbatim would put internal strings on the public site; storing them and displaying nothing discards the hierarchy — hence the split. Storing the whole tree rather than the publicly meaningful subset is what makes bulk import and reconciliation against a Workday extract possible instead of hand-checking 181 entries. It is safe for the six scoped entry pickers because `EntriesByOrganizationRole::apply()` does `whereIn('organization_role', $roles)`, so entries carrying no role match nothing and never appear. `name` and `public_label` stay separate specifically so inheritance cannot reach schema identity, which is what makes the display-only rule enforceable rather than aspirational. `inherit_public_label_query` follows this repo's `*_query` convention, whose polarity is **inverted** from a plain "display my own" flag: `true` means *take this from elsewhere*, per `uamsweb_block_form.yaml`'s `block_description_query`, where the own-value field appears on `if: … 'equals false'`.

**Rejected.** *Store only the publicly meaningful tiers* — cheaper and leaves pickers untouched, but reconciliation then covers a subset and the deeper tree lives only as an external file. *An authored title* — keeps the backend independent of Workday, but leaves the 31 duplicate names indistinguishable across a 2,963-row listing. *Written-on-save title* — searchable by construction, but unnecessary once the index reads the non-computed inputs instead of the computed output. *Inherit `name` and drop `public_label`* — one fewer field, but inheritance would then change a child's schema identity, contradicting the display-only rule. *Reuse `provenance` to mark external organizations* — it is conditional on `organization_role: equals brand`, so **176 of 181** entries cannot express it. *Require `public_label` unconditionally* — ceremony on ~2,800 entries that will never display one. *Set `inherit_public_label_query: true` at import instead of populating the label* — superseded; populating from the Workday name leaves entries saveable at the default and composes with the title rule, which drops the segment when the strings match.

**Companion.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2125 (`## Decision`), UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2124 (closed, superseded), #2126 (the launch-phase label cleanup this deliberately creates), `UAMS-Web/wordpress-importer#768` (the Departmental News Hub designation, the most exposed consumer). Files: `resources/blueprints/collections/organizations/organization.yaml`, `config/statamic/search.php`. The captured tree — 2,967 rows with explicit `parent_org_id`, manager names and headcounts stripped — is a secret gist linked from UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2125. **Residue accepted deliberately:** after import ~2,800 entries hold an internal Workday string in `public_label`, so the "no internal label reaches a public page" guarantee rests on those entries never rendering — which follows from their carrying no `organization_role` — rather than on the field being empty. That is weaker than an absent value, and is why #2126 exists and why the guarantee is to be tested against a designated hub rather than assumed.

## 2026-09-02 — Endowed and named chairs are recognitions, recorded in `awards`, not in the title grammar (people titles)

**Decision.** A named or endowed chair — *Steven K. Wilson Endowed Chair in Prosthetic Urology*, *Martha Wood Bentley Chair in Ophthalmology* — is a **recognition a person holds**, not a job title. It belongs in the `awards` replicator on the people blueprint (Award Title / Date Awarded / Award Notes, translated from the WordPress `physician_awards` repeater under UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#802). The `role_title` grammar deliberately does **not** model it, and no configuration, slot or vocabulary should be added for it.

**Why.** The source content puts these in prose rather than in title fields. `Endowed Chair in` appears across 40 export files in `UAMS-Web/wordpress-importer-exports`, concentrated in news and biography — `news-uams-edu` 187 and 125 occurrences in its two parts, the `inside-uams-edu` news archive 101, `medicine-uams-edu` 120 — and the non-news instances are biography sentences rather than structured titles. One of them draws the distinction itself: *"Professor and the Chairman of the Department of Urology, **as well as** the Steven K. Wilson Endowed Chair in Prosthetic Urology."* The writer separates the job titles from the endowed chair, because they are different kinds of thing: the chair is held *alongside* a faculty appointment, is named for a donor rather than drawn from a controlled vocabulary, and carries no reporting line. Modeling it as a title would need an open-ended proper-noun slot, would duplicate `role: chair` with an unrelated sense of the word, and would still not say what the person's job is.

**Rejected.** *A fifth `role_title` configuration for endowed appointments* — proposed on the strength of the raw counts (`endowed chair in` 1,291 occurrences across 41 exports case-insensitively, against 1,150 across 40 for the capitalized `Endowed Chair in` counted above) before the distribution was examined; the counts are prose mentions, not titles. *A new set in the `job_title` replicator alongside the leadership sets* — same defect, and it would place a recognition in the field that answers "what does this person do". *`recognition_lists`* — that collection was re-modeled on 2026-07-15 for membership in published lists (Best Doctors and similar), which a donor-named chair is not.

**Recorded because it was not.** This had been settled previously and was written down nowhere — not in `docs/`, not in `.claude/`, and not in either tracker; a search for "endowed" across both repositories returns no issues, and the `physician_awards` references (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#802, `UAMS-Web/wordpress-importer#19`) state the field's existence without stating the disposition. It was re-opened during the 2026-09-02 title-grammar work and reached the point of a proposed ticket before someone recalled the original ruling. That is the failure this log exists to prevent. It is no longer only here: the disposition is published at <https://communications.uams.edu/web/kb/job-titles/> under *Endowed and Named Chairs Are Not Job Titles*, and recorded in `specs/foundations/role-titles.md` in `UAMS-Web/uams-brand-system`.

## 2026-09-02 — Clinical leadership titles decompose the unit out of the role, but not the discipline (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2143)

**Decision.** The clinical leadership configuration's four compound role terms — `service line administrator`, `service line director`, `service line medical director`, `service line nursing director` — split into an **`object` of `service line` plus a bare role** (`administrator`, `director`, `medical director`, `nursing director`), reusing the existing `director` key rather than adding a fifth spelling of it. `medical` and `nursing` stay **fused** to `director` rather than becoming a discipline facet of their own.

**Why.** The `co-` slot decided the same day composes after the object and before the positional qualifier, so a unit fused into the role leaves the prefix nothing to follow and yields `co-service line director` — **zero** attestations in `UAMS-Web/wordpress-importer-exports` against an attested `service line co-director`. That matches the object-outside-the-prefix pattern the placement rests on: `program co-director` 12 distinct exports against `co-program director` 5, `fellowship co-director` 3 against 0, `section co-director` 1 against 0. The discipline goes the other way and the counts are emphatic: **`co-medical director` 16 exports against `medical co-director` 6**, so `medical director` is one position name with the prefix in front of it, and splitting it would produce the minority form. Decomposition also gives clinical the same object-plus-role shape every other configuration already has.

**Rejected.** *Split `medical` / `nursing` into a discipline facet ahead of the role*, proposed so `co-` would attach directly to `director` — refuted by the 16-against-6 count above. *Keep the four compound role terms* — leaves `co-` unable to compose a correct shared clinical title at all, and keeps a third and fourth spelling of `director` in the vocabulary. *Scope the schema rule by configuration rather than by object* — the mapping already carries `object` conditions on 8 of its 45 rules, so an object condition is the shape the file already uses; the clinical rule is the only single-field rule in it and is what the retired keys match on.

**Companion.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2143 carries the acceptance criteria and the schema-mapping detail; UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2139 records the `co-` placement this follows from, and UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2136 the missing clinical composer that must read the new slots.

## 2026-09-02 — `co-` is its own title slot, after the unit and before the positional qualifier (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2139)

**Decision.** The shared-role prefix `co-` leaves the `title_options` checkbox set and becomes a slot of its own, composed **immediately after the object and immediately before the positional qualifier**, gluing only to the fragment that follows it. With a positional qualifier present a title reads **`co-vice chair`**. The global `str_replace('- ', '-')` over the finished line goes away with it.

**Why.** `co-` is a bound prefix, single-valued, and constrained to sit adjacent to the position name — none of which membership in a multi-select set can express, and the old string pass bound it to whatever fragment happened to follow, producing `co-department director` and editing hyphens out of editor-typed scope names (`Jones Eye - Little Rock` → `Jones Eye -Little Rock`). Placement was settled against the exported production content in `UAMS-Web/wordpress-importer-exports`, counted by distinct site exports so one repeated page cannot carry a form: `co-vice chair` 8 exports against `vice co-chair` 1; `co-medical director` 16 against `medical co-director` 6; `program co-director` 12 against `co-program director` 5; `associate <unit> …` 38 against `<unit> associate …` 12. Those constraints form a **cycle** — hierarchical modifier before the unit, unit before `co-`, `co-` before the hierarchical modifier — so no fixed position satisfies every attested form, because real titles bind `co-` to whatever begins the position *name* and which words count varies by title. The cycle is broken at its weakest edge, costing `co-associate director` (6 exports), which composes `associate co-director` instead — itself attested at 3 exports, so the minority reading rather than an invented one.

**Rejected.** *`co-` immediately before the hierarchical modifier* — recorded first, superseded once the unit-order counts were measured; saves 6 exports and loses 16 + 12 + 3 + 1. *Keeping `co-` in `title_options` with a rule that it composes last within that group* — that group composes ahead of every slot in the position name, so no ordering rule inside it can reach `co-vice chair`; the editorial decision forces the structural one. *`vice co-chair`* — 4 occurrences, all inside a single site export.

**Companion.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2139 carries the evidence table, the reproduction and the acceptance criteria; UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2138 covers the honorific in the same options set, and UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2136 the clinical leadership configuration that composes nothing today.

## 2026-09-04 — Commit the coordination tooling's three-field repository binding and declare a coordinator identity (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2166)

**Superseded.** The binding and the instructions around it were retired in UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2531. This entry is kept for the reasoning, restated without the tooling's identifiers.

**Decision.** Reduce the repository's binding for the out-of-band coordination tooling to the three-field shape used by every repository that tooling served: a workspace id, the name of the environment variable that holds the credential, and the identifier of the one app whose directives count as the coordinator's. A fourth field, the workspace's display name, is **removed**. Declaring the coordinator identifier means a directive from that app is shown as verified to a session working here. It grants nothing about what such a directive may ask for.

**Why.** The tooling verifies a directive by comparing the sender identifier the messaging service assigns, which no sender can set, against the identifier this binding declares. With none declared, the comparison never ran: a genuine coordinator directive and a deliberate forgery from an ordinary session displayed identically, although the evidence that tells them apart was present on every message. Declaring the field turns that declined comparison into a real one. The display name was removed because no code path read it once the workspace id was declared, and because one shape across every repository is auditable at a glance where a per-repository exception is not.

**What verification does not buy.** It is per message, not per sender, and it applies only to messages that declare themselves directives, so an untyped message is never checked. A verified directive is still an input, never a warrant: declaring the field makes a forged directive detectable and closes no authorization gap.

**Rejected.** *Keep the four-field shape* — it retained a field nothing read. *Declare the identifier before the comparison had been measured against real messages* — a verified badge over an unverified comparison invites trust the code had not earned. *Provision the binding per machine, untracked* — it removed both the guard against posting into the wrong workspace and directive verification. *Treat the identifier as a credential* — it authenticates nothing without the token, so keeping it out of the repository bought nothing.

**Companion.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2166 (`## Designation — decided`), and UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2531, which retired the binding.

## 2026-09-05 — The check mark is retired from PR verification lists; the emoji ban takes no carve-out (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2199)

**Decision.** `U+2713` gets no exception. [`writing-pull-requests`](../.claude/skills/writing-pull-requests/SKILL.md) stops prescribing a literal check mark on a `## Verification` item; a checked item takes a `[x]` task-list box or the word `verified`. The second `Status: undecided` section in [`no-emoji-in-durable-records`](../.claude/rules/no-emoji-in-durable-records.md) records the verdict, and UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2185 drops the `## Out of scope` line that pointed at the fork.

**Why.** The skill prescribed the character and the rule forbade it, in the same file once UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2178 added the rule's pointer there. `U+2713` is absent from `cp1252`, so it fails exactly as the warning glyph did: a generator emitting it dies on a Windows stdout sized to the console code page. It reads as exempt because it is a text symbol rather than a colored emoji, and that is the reason it was prescribed rather than a reason it is safe.

**What this does not decide.** *Already-published PR bodies keep their check marks* — 33 merged ones carry `U+2713` and no open or closed one does; rewriting published records is a separate question nobody has answered. *UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2185's comparison-table carve-out stands* — it covers a different glyph marking which option was chosen in a width-constrained cell, and a decision about `U+2713` does not reach a carve-out that was never about `U+2713`.

**Rejected.** *Carve `U+2713` out of the ban and let the skill keep prescribing it.* It is the arm that preserves every existing body, so its cost is genuinely lower — and it fails for the reason the release callout failed. An exception written into the rule for the one character people actually reach for is an exception that swallows the rule, and a reader meeting it learns that the ban is negotiable per glyph.

**Companion.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2203 (the build); UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2199 (where the verdict was recorded while it waited); UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2190 (the release-path fork, decided the same day and on the same reasoning); UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2164 (`## The second conflict`, where this fork was first written down).

## 2026-09-05 — The breaking-change callout is words; the release path takes no emoji exception (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2190)

**Decision.** Remove `U+26A0 U+FE0F` from the release path. [`writing-release-notes`](../.claude/skills/writing-release-notes/SKILL.md) mandates `**Breaking change** — …` and [`gen_release_notes.py`](../.claude/skills/writing-release-notes/gen_release_notes.py) emits that form, so the callout is words and [`no-emoji-in-durable-records`](../.claude/rules/no-emoji-in-durable-records.md) carries no exception. New releases read differently from published ones, which is accepted rather than mitigated.

**Why.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2164 added the rule and deliberately left this fork open rather than resolving it in passing, on the grounds that a generator edit changing published-artifact formatting is not a formatting change and a rule with an unstated exception is one people learn to skip. Both halves argue the same way once the decision is actually made. The rule's rationale is that the glyph is not encodable in `cp1252` and kills tooling that touches it — which applies to a generator writing into a redirected stdout at least as strongly as to an author typing prose, so exempting the one place the emission is automatic inverts the reason for the rule. And the exception could not have stayed unstated: the release vocabulary appears in four further files, each of which would have had to repeat it.

**What this does not decide, stated because each was live and none is closed by it.** *Published release notes keep their glyph* — this governs what is written from here, and rewriting published artifacts is a separate act. *The `U+2713` check mark* in a PR verification list remains recorded as undecided in the same rule, untouched: retiring it would invalidate a marker existing PR bodies already carry, which is a decision about those records rather than about this one. *The generator's stream guarantee* (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2175) is independent in both directions — removing the hard-coded glyph closes only the reproduced path, while `--breaking` text and live PR titles are contributor-supplied and can carry any character outside `cp1252`, so the crash class survives this change and its fix remains correct after it.

**Rejected.** *Carve the release callout out of the rule permanently.* It costs nothing to implement — the generator keeps the glyph and no file needs touching — and it fails on its own terms for the two reasons above. *Rewrite the published releases to match.* Out of scope by the same argument that makes the forward decision safe: the records say what they said. *Leave it undecided and let the rule ship with a standing conflict.* That was UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2164's deliberate interim position and it was correct as an interim one; carrying it indefinitely leaves the corpus with one documented place where its own rule does not apply.

**Companion.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2190 (the build); UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2164 (`## The conflict this rule creates`, where the fork was recorded); UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2175 (the stream guarantee, independent); UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2185 (the corpus sweep, whose release-vocabulary group moved to UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2190 rather than being split across two tickets). The 2026-07-08 versioning entry above records the marker as `U+26A0` by code point rather than reproducing it, which is what that rule prescribes where the glyph is the thing being identified.

## 2026-09-05 — Merged branches are retained on the remote, because PR-body links resolve against the head branch (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2177)

**Decision.** `delete_branch_on_merge` stays **off**, merged head branches are kept, and `gh pr merge --squash` is used without `--delete-branch`. The retained branches are a deliberate steady state rather than accumulated drift.

**Why.** The [`writing-pull-requests`](../.claude/skills/writing-pull-requests/SKILL.md) convention requires every file link in a PR body to be an absolute head-branch URL — `.../blob/<head-branch>/path` — including for files that already exist on `main`. Deleting the head branch therefore breaks every such link in that pull request, permanently. Measured on this repository: `delete_branch_on_merge` is `false`, 546 branches are present across six pages, and 25 of 25 sampled merged pull requests still have their head branch. The bodies do depend on it — UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2213 has 1 of 1 blob links pointing at its head, UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2167 2 of 2, UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2153 4 of 4. The failure mode is confirmed against the API rather than assumed: `contents/README.md?ref=zzz-no-such-branch-88104` returns `404 No commit found for the ref`, where the same call at `ref=main` returns the file.

**The reason is preventative, not remedial, and UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2177's own evidence for it was wrong.** That issue cited PR UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1774's head branch `docs-worktree-entry-path` as absent with its URL returning 404. The branch exists (`7a5208a7d`) and `README.md` resolves at it (6,950 bytes). The 404 came from an unauthenticated request to a **private** repository, which returns 404 for every path — the control, `blob/main/README.md` unauthenticated, is also 404. Nothing in this repository has broken, because nothing has been deleted; the cost is what would follow from enabling deletion, and it is entirely forward-looking.

**Rejected.** *Enable `delete_branch_on_merge` for a tidier remote.* The branch count is the visible cost and the file links are the invisible one, which is the wrong way round: a count is cosmetic and recoverable, while every merged pull request's links would 404 at once and could not be repaired without rewriting the bodies. *Keep the convention but switch PR bodies to `blob/main/` links.* It permits deletion and breaks the case the convention exists for — a file added by the pull request does not exist on `main` when the body is written, so the link is dead at review time, which is when it is read most.

**Companion.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2177 (this record); the `Never delete the branch` line in [`worktrees.md`](../.claude/rules/worktrees.md), which covers the local half — a released slot moves off its branch without deleting it — and now cross-references this entry for the remote half.

## 2026-09-05 — The shared-traverser Stache defect is carried as a `composer` patch, on the `upstream-patch-lifecycle` chain (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2037)

**Decision.** `patches/statamic-stache-traverser-shared-filter.patch` makes `Traverser::filter()` set its callback on a **clone**, registered under `extra.patches.statamic/cms`. The defect is reported upstream and the patch retires when a fixed release ships, tracked by the four-ticket chain in [`upstream-patch-lifecycle`](../.claude/skills/upstream-patch-lifecycle/SKILL.md).

**Why.** Core reaches the traverser through a real-time facade (`use Facades\Statamic\Stache\Traverser`), and Laravel caches a facade's resolved object — so every store in a request shares one instance while `filter()` writes to it and `traverse()` reads it back mid-`foreach`. A nested traversal replaces the outer one's filter and the rest of that directory is judged by the wrong store. Measured on `entries::pages` against a cold cache: 82 files traversed, 1 kept, 81 rejected by `CollectionsStore::getItemFilter`, which accepts only `.yaml`. The truthy one-item result is cached as authoritative and never self-heals.

**Rejected: a container bind over `Statamic\Stache\Traverser`.** This was recorded here as the *chosen* path earlier the same day and is now reversed. It works — the regression test was red-green verified against it with a negative control. What it does not do is start any clock. A patch is visible in `patches/`, documented in `patches/README.md`, registered in `composer.json`, watched by `watch-upstream.yml`, and carries a written retirement condition; a bind is app code that nobody has a reason to revisit, and the only thing that would ever retire it was a prose note on a ticket. The first entry's objections — patch maintenance cost, and a newly added patch not being applied by a plain `composer install` — were both real and both already solved: this repository carries **four** other `statamic/cms` patches, requires `cweagans/composer-patches ^2.0`, sets `composer-exit-on-patch-failure: true`, and the skill documents the reused-`vendor/` re-patch trap explicitly. The original entry rejected the house pattern as though it were novel, having consulted this log but not the skills corpus or `patches/`.

**Rejected: relying on UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1994's cache mitigation.** It removed the only trigger in the serial band and left the defect untouched; anything that empties the cache still gets a starved rebuild, which is why UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2037 existed separately.

**Rejected: waiting for an upstream fix with no local carry.** No fix would exist here until a release shipped and was adopted, and the starvation is silent — a starved store reports green, because tests needing *any* entry pass and tests needing a *specific* one skip through the UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1738 discriminator.

**Companion.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2037 (the patch and the measurement); UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2241 (report it upstream — ticket 2 of the chain); UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2242 (whether `TaxonomyTermsStore`, the third call site, can starve); UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1994 and UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1747 (the starvation this produced).

**Retired (2026-10-02, UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2260, UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2774).** `statamic/cms` 6.35.0 carries the fix (`statamic/cms#15551`), and the patch was removed before the package moved.

## 2026-09-06 — Mutation criterion is "no unexplained survivors", not a score threshold (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2265)

**Decision.** Acceptance criteria stop naming a mutation-score percentage. Every surviving mutant gets one of three dispositions — killed, deleted as dead code, or annotated in source with the reason it cannot be killed — and only the third needs writing down. The annotation is `// @pest-mutate-ignore`, per line, with `:MutatorName` to scope it to the mutators that produced the survivor.

**Why.** A percentage aggregates the two survivor classes it most needs to separate. An unexamined survivor and a provably equivalent one are the same number under a threshold, while being opposite states: one is work not done, the other is work that cannot be done. Raising the bar makes it worse rather than better, because the last few percent are usually equivalent mutants — so a high threshold converts "explain this survivor" into "delete a defensive guard to move the number". The shape also has a concrete instance: UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1764 reported 85.02% with 46 survivors, of which two were provably equivalent and six were described by their own author as real and reachable. Under a percentage those eight are interchangeable; under this rule six are outstanding work and two are a one-line annotation each. [`UAMS-Web/wordpress-importer`](https://github.com/UAMS-Web/wordpress-importer) already carried the convention in this shape, so the wording is matched rather than reinvented.

**The instrument was verified before the rule was written, and the obvious candidate is the wrong one.** `Contracts/Configuration.php` exposes `ignore(...$paths)` and `except(...$mutators)`, which silence a whole file or a mutator repository-wide; neither can carry a per-survivor justification, and `except()` would blind a mutator everywhere in order to explain one line. The per-line comment is parsed in `Support/NodeVisitor.php` — bare form skips every mutator on the node, the `:Name,Name` form only those listed — and again per line in `Support/MutationGenerator.php`. The plugin uses it in its own source.

**Rejected.** *Keep a percentage and set it per class (85% data-heavy, 95% logic-dense).* It preserves exactly the defect: the number still cannot distinguish an explained survivor from an unexamined one, and the per-class bands add a second thing to argue about at review. *Record accepted equivalents in the pull-request body instead of in source.* The reason then lives where it is read once and rots away from the code it describes; the annotation keeps it adjacent to the line and travels with any move of that line. *Enforce the criterion with a `min()` score in CI.* **Decided against** — see the 2026-08-03 entry on the `mutate` job (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1731), which gates the tooling rather than a score after the UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1728 false green. That reasoning is unchanged here: a threshold as the gate is the thing this entry replaces, not a possible extension of it.

**Companion.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2265 (this record); the mutation bullet in [`adversarial-review.md`](../.claude/rules/adversarial-review.md), which now carries the criterion and the instrument; UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1764, the first ticket the shape applies to.

## 2026-09-08 — No attribution footer on pull-request or issue bodies, so the no-emoji rule takes no carve-out for it (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2321)

**Decision.** Pull-request and issue bodies carry no attribution footer. Nothing emits the "Generated with Claude Code" line, with or without its `U+1F916` glyph. [`no-emoji-in-durable-records`](../.claude/rules/no-emoji-in-durable-records.md) therefore stays as written, with no exception for it.

**Why.** The rule forbids emoji in pull-request bodies, and the harness's footer carried `U+1F916` on every body it wrote, so a compliant author broke a merged rule by default. UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2168 was clean apart from that one line. Not emitting the footer removes one side of the collision rather than reconciling the two. There is nothing to carve out, nothing to strip at intake, and nothing to remember. UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2234's check needs no exception logic, and a pull request passes intake without a manual edit to its last line. The decision covers issue bodies too, a surface the ticket did not audit, which would have kept producing the same line after a pull-request-only fix.

**Rejected.** *Carve the footer out of the rule.* One sentence, but the rule stops being true as written, and UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2234's check then needs a permanent exception. *Drop the glyph and keep the attribution.* It leaves the harness instruction half-satisfied, and keeps a line on every body that nobody writes deliberately. *Strip it at intake.* It puts a manual step on the validating lane for every pull request, indefinitely.

**Companion.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2321 (the ticket; decided in its 2026-09-08 comment, recorded here 2026-09-28); UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2168 (where the collision was found at intake); UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2234 (the CI check, which needs no exception); UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2185 (the corpus sweep, out of scope).

## 2026-09-09 — A deleted entry's redirect is captured automatically and disabled, for an editor to complete (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#301)

**Decision.** Deleting an entry with a public URL writes a `redirect` row into the `redirects` global immediately, carrying the URI it occupied and **no destination**, with `enabled: false`. An editor supplies the target and turns the row on as one deliberate act. The row is written on `EntryDeleted`, so a cancelled delete records nothing.

**Why.** The two failure modes are not symmetric. A capture that is never taken leaves a 404 — the status quo, and recoverable, because the row records the URL that went dark and the editor can complete it whenever they notice. A capture that serves immediately sends a visitor to an empty destination, which is worse than the 404 it replaced and is invisible to the person who caused it. Writing the row at the moment of deletion is also the only point at which the URI is reliably knowable: nothing afterwards remembers what the entry's URL was.

**`enabled` is pre-existing and was unreachable, which is what made this cheap.** `RedirectRuleMatcher` has always skipped a row whose `enabled` is false — `($redirect['enabled'] ?? true)`, at both of its call sites — and `RedirectStatus`'s docblock names it "the existing per-row `enabled` flag" that gates serving beside approval status. What was missing was a Control Panel field to set it, so the blueprint gains an `enabled` toggle defaulting to on. No new mechanism was introduced; a legacy row without the key still grandfathers to enabled.

**A capture deliberately carries no `status`, so it never enters the marketing-redirect approval queue.** `MarketingRedirectStore::pending()` selects rows whose effective status is `pending`, and `RedirectStatus::fromRow()` grandfathers a row with no `status` key to `Approved`. A capture is therefore approved-but-disabled: invisible to the UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1357 approver queue and unable to serve. That is the correct separation — the approval queue gates *whether a requested redirect should exist*, while a capture is a redirect that plainly should exist and is merely missing its target. Routing captures through the approver queue would let an approver promote a row with an empty destination.

**Rejected.** *Prompt the editor for a destination at delete time.* It blocks a delete on a decision the editor often cannot make yet — the replacement page may not exist — and it is unreachable for a delete that is not interactive: a bulk action, a queued job, or the CLI. *Capture nothing and let the 404 stand.* It discards the only moment the URI is knowable, so the work of finding out what the URL had been falls on whoever notices the 404, if anyone does. *Write the row enabled, targeting the collection index or the parent.* It guesses, and a wrong redirect is harder to detect than a 404 because it returns 200.

**A defect reported during review turned out not to exist, and the test it produced was kept.** `EntryDeleted` fires after the entry has left its structure, so a URI re-derived at that point would be the parentless `/cardiology` in place of the live `/services/cardiology`. Measured on Statamic 6 (macOS), that does not happen: a truly cold process with `uri()` never read before the delete still captures the full path, because Statamic resolves and caches the entry's URI during the delete itself. The original evidence for the defect was an artifact — a probe that flushed the URI cache from inside the listener, which nothing does in production — and a two-phase `EntryDeleting`/`EntryDeleted` capture built on it was reverted when two independent negative controls both stayed green. The page-tree test remains, pinning behavior the capture *depends on* rather than behavior it implements: `Entry::delete()`'s caching is an implementation detail, not a documented guarantee, and if it changes this is what turns a silently wrong redirect into a red suite.

**Companion.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#301 (this record and the build); the `enabled` filter in [`RedirectRuleMatcher`](../app/Support/Redirects/RedirectRuleMatcher.php); UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1357 (the approval queue this deliberately stays out of); UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1110 (the read-time merge that serves the global on every site).

## 2026-09-11 — Identifier uniqueness is guarded in two places, compared case-insensitively, and scanned naively (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2221)

**Decision.** Three answers, for the Workday, NPI, SER and Epic DEP identifiers UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2152 validates. **(1)** Both a save-time rule *and* an audit command are built, with the audit as the load-bearing half rather than a deferred extra. **(2)** Two values are the same identifier when they match after folding case and trimming surrounding whitespace. **(3)** The check finds other entries by scanning them directly; UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2152's existing criterion that the approach be measured on the largest affected collection is the trigger to revisit.

**Why.** The first answer turns on a mechanic that was verified rather than assumed, and it inverts the reading the ticket started from. Blueprint validation runs only in the Control Panel — `Http/Controllers/CP/Collections/EntriesController.php` calls `->validator()` at its update and store actions — and `Statamic\Entries\Entry::save()` contains no validation reference at all. The importer writes entries through a direct `$entryData->save()` (`BaseEntryCreator.php`, in its save-the-entry path), so **a save-time rule never sees imported content**. The two halves therefore cover disjoint populations: the rule guards the hand-edited entries, and the audit is the only mechanism that will ever see the import — which is where every duplicate is going to come from. Deferring the audit leaves the import path guarded by nothing.

The second is settled from the identifier specifications rather than from a sample, because there is no sample: measured on `main`, all four Workday fields are empty across every one of the 43 `people` entries, and the only two identifier values in the repository are numeric and caseless. A case-sensitive comparison of a 32-character GUID misses a real collision, which is the failure the rule exists to prevent; the opposite error needs two genuinely distinct identifiers differing only by case, which a GUID space does not produce.

The third rests on the premise being false today. `clinical_providers` and `clinical_locations` hold zero entries and `people` holds 43, of which one carries an `npi`, so a direct scan is not a trade-off. The number that decides this is the one taken after the first import, and it does not exist yet.

**The measurement was taken twice, because the first instrument was blind.** A non-recursive glob over `content/collections/people/*.md` reported zero for every field — including a `title` control that must be 43, since entries are nested below that path. The same shape had already produced a false `0 collisions` from `0 files` while UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2221 was being written. The figures above are from the recursive read whose control passes.

**Rejected.** *Build the save-time rule only, and file the audit for later* — the ticket's own starting position, which the mechanic above defeats: the population it defers guarding is the one that will contain the duplicates. *Build the audit only* — it leaves an editor free to create a collision in the Control Panel with nothing to stop them. *Compare case-sensitively* — correct only if one of these identifier spaces is genuinely case-distinguishing, and wrong in the direction that lets a collision through. *Maintain a separate lookup index* — highest build cost, removal costs a migration, and its failure mode is a stale index silently accepting a duplicate, which is precisely the outcome being prevented. *Query through the Stache index instead of scanning* — no cheaper at this scale and it makes correctness depend on index freshness.

**Companion.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2221 (this record and the fork); UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2152 (the specification and the acceptance criteria these release); UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2395 (the audit command, spun off by the first decision); #803 and the people-as-single-source entry above, which are why the uniqueness set is per collection rather than pooled.

## 2026-09-11 — A dual-rendition podcast episode is one entry carrying an optional video rendition (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1807)

**Decision.** An episode that has both an audio and a video rendition stays a **single entry** in `posts` (and its `clinical_resources` mirror), and the video is carried as an additional set inside the episode's existing `segments` replicator. The **whole `new_video_segment_group` is registered** — `youtube`, `vimeo` and `box` — rather than YouTube alone, so the rendition is not tied to one host. The episode keeps its `audio_series` term and its `subtype: podcast`; nothing moves to `video_series`. No new entry, no relationship field, and no canonical-for-schema rule is needed, because there is only ever one entry to be canonical.

**Why.** The ten video-bearing episodes are different edits of the same episode, not different episodes — the video runs longer in every observed pair, and the feed declares only the audio. Two entries would therefore duplicate one episode in every listing, search result and sitemap, while asserting through the content model that UAMS published two things when it published one. Presenting only the video would discard a rendition and, for those ten, the podcast identity the feed still carries.

**The blueprint asymmetry argues for this rather than against it, which inverts how the ticket framed it.** `audio.yaml` and `video.yaml` already use the **same field handle** — `segments`, a required replicator — and differ only in which sets are registered: `local_file` on audio, `youtube`/`vimeo`/`box` on video. The six episode fields (`episode_number`, `season_number`, `show_notes`, `episode_transcript`, `episode_duration`, `podcast_credits`) exist on `audio.yaml` and nowhere in `video.yaml`. So a second entry must either duplicate those six fields or accept video entries with no episode metadata, while this option duplicates nothing: the sets are shared fieldset imports already registered by two other blueprints, and the episode fields never move. The constraint the ticket called "the main constraint" on the fork turns out to bear on the three options that were rejected.

**Not YouTube-only, and the reason is not hypothetical.** Both renditions already live on `@UAMSHealth`, so YouTube is the presumptive player today; but the vendor's masters are not embeddable for four independent reasons, any of which could change — the contract, the ~15.6 Mbps production files with no rendition ladder, captions timed to the audio edit, and the absence of CORS on either vendor host. Registering the full set group means a change in any of those is a per-episode editorial choice rather than a schema migration.

**Companion.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1807 (this record); #1808, the build and the vodcast backfill, which this unblocks; UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1803, whose decision 1 selects the collection; the *Renditions: audio and video* section of [`docs/specs/uams-health-talk-podcast.md`](specs/uams-health-talk-podcast.md).

**Rejected.** *Separate `audio` and `video` entries.* Shows every dual-rendition episode twice in listings, search and the sitemap; splits its series identity across `audio_series` and `video_series`; and forces either six duplicated fields or video entries with no episode metadata. *Separate entries, cross-linked by a relationship.* All of the above, plus a canonical-for-schema rule to write and maintain, in exchange for recording a relationship the single-entry model expresses by construction. *Video-only where a video exists.* Discards the audio rendition, all six episode fields, the audio player and the `PodcastEpisode` identity for the ten newest episodes, while the feed continues to declare audio only — the model would contradict the feed it is derived from. *Hybrid limited to a YouTube set.* Correct today and brittle: it hard-codes the current hosting accident into the content model, so any change to the four blockers above becomes a blueprint migration.

**Residual, recorded rather than resolved.** Whether episode `083` behaves like `109`–`118` is unverified. Episodes `116`–`118` had masters but no upload as of 2026-07-31, so the backfill will find gaps. If the vendor later publishes video as a `<podcast:alternateEnclosure>` on the same `<item>`, this model absorbs it directly — one item, two renditions — but the ingestion path in UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1803 changes.

## 2026-09-14 — The users repository stays on the file driver, and the git exposure is closed separately (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2444)

**Decision.** `statamic.users.repository` stays `file`. Self-service registration (#2113) ships on it with no delay. The exposure that made this look urgent — registrant records reaching git history — is closed independently by an allowlist `.gitignore` in the `users` submodule (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2454), which leaves the two seed accounts tracked and makes every new record impossible to commit. One setting across all environments; the tracked records and the submodule are unchanged.

**Why.** The ticket framed one question where there were two. Registrant records reaching git is a property of `users/` being a git-tracked path with no ignore rule, not a property of the driver — so it is closable in three lines without touching authentication, and closing it that way keeps the driver choice reversible and decidable later on its own merits.

The separability is measured rather than assumed. `statamic.git.enabled` is `true` and `base_path('users')` is in `statamic.git.paths`, but `statamic.git.automatic` is `false` — `Statamic\Git\Subscriber` line 53 short-circuits on every save event while it is — and `statamic.git.push` is `false`. Nothing commits a record on its own, which is confirmed on disk rather than inferred: a third user record sits in `users/` untracked, having never been staged. The allowlist was verified against the live submodule with `git -c core.excludesFile=... check-ignore` before the decision, not after: a new record is ignored and both seed accounts are not, and an ignore rule does not untrack what is already tracked.

**The cost that decided it against moving is one nobody had counted.** `statamic/eloquent-driver` ships 18 migrations and none for auth — the string `user` does not appear in its `ServiceProvider` at all — and `statamic/cms` ships zero migration files. `Statamic\Auth\Eloquent\RoleModel` expects a `roles` table (`permissions` json, `preferences` json) and `UserGroupModel` a `groups` table (`roles` json, `data` json), while the live `users` table has 8 columns with no `super` and no `preferences` and an autoincrement integer `id` where `HasUuids` requires a string. So "flip the config and run the importer" is three hand-written migrations with no upstream schema to copy, owned here across every Statamic upgrade.

**Rejected.** *Move to eloquent before #2113 ships.* Its case is real and was the strongest argument on the table: the primary-key change is free right now, because the `users` table holds **0 rows** and one nullable foreign key, and it gets more expensive with every registrant. It loses on what it buys that with — the three migrations above; a one-way door, since `statamic:eloquent:import-users` ships in core and no export command exists; and roles leaving git review, because `Statamic\Providers\AuthServiceProvider` lines 77 and 81 resolve the role and group repositories through the selected user repository, taking the 5 authored roles in `resources/users/roles.yaml` with them (`groups.yaml` defines none). Every one of those is permanent; the risk it closes is closable reversibly for three lines. *Stay on file and change nothing.* Leaves the one irreversible harm open, which is the reason the ticket existed.

**Companion.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2444 (this record and the fork); UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2454, which closes the git half and blocks #2113; UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2455, the at-rest and backup residual this decision deliberately does not answer; UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2096, where the growth and write-path consequences of a file-backed user store are recorded; #2113 and #2115, which put real registrant records on disk.

**Residual, recorded rather than resolved.** Blocking a record from being committed does not make it protected: the records remain plain-text YAML on the deployed server and in whatever backs it up, to a standard nobody has stated. That is UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2455, and it can reopen this decision rather than merely follow it. `STATAMIC_GIT_AUTOMATIC` also remains one flip from sweeping `users/` wholesale, which UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2454 makes harmless for user records specifically and is worth confirming once it lands.

## 2026-09-14 — A leadership scope links its organizational unit, keeps free text for disciplines, and derives the article from the link (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2146)

**Decision.** `role_title`'s `scope.scope_name` becomes **a link or free text**: an entries link into `organizations` for a scope that names an organizational unit, and the existing text field retained for a scope that names a discipline. Unit-ness is then **derived from the link** rather than declared by the author, collapsing the stored joiner from five combined values to three prepositions plus a derived article — **sequenced after UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2407 merges**, not instead of it.

**Two of the ticket's four questions were not decided, because they were not open.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2407 adds a `names_a_unit` flag to each joiner option and partitions exactly on the article: `for_without_the` and `of_without_the` are `false`, while `for_with_the`, `of_with_the` and `in_the` are `true`. That partition already draws the unit-versus-discipline line the ticket was reaching for. A scope with no unit behind it — `professor of pediatrics`, 101 exports — uses `of_without_the`, so the discipline path exists already and already stays free text; that is also why the field becomes *link or text* rather than a link alone. And the object slot's fate is not a separate choice: `scopeNamesAUnit()` already suppresses the object segment when true, so if a unit scope always links, the object is always suppressed for those titles.

**Why the article derives, and why it waits.** The evidence for deriving it is not close: `in the` introduces only units and has no article-less form in the corpus at all, 8,687 against 26, while every unit type keeps its article — `of the College of Medicine` 1,806 against 273, `of the Winthrop P. Rockefeller Cancer Institute` 952 against 32. What makes deriving it *possible* is the link. UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2407 put `names_a_unit` on the joiner rather than deriving it for a stated reason — that `scope_name` is "free text an author may capitalize any way at all" — and this decision removes exactly that premise, because a link is a typed reference rather than free text. So the collapse supersedes reasoning in a pull request that has not landed. Sequencing it after UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2407 keeps that as one reviewable change rather than two entangled ones.

**What the build already has.** [`organization.yaml`](../resources/blueprints/collections/organizations/organization.yaml) carries `public_label` and `inherit_public_label_query` from UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2125, so there is a label to resolve at compose time. `composeScopeSuffix()` interpolates `{name}` into a translation template keyed by joiner; that mechanism is unchanged and only the source of `{name}` moves.

**Rejected.** *A link only, with no text branch.* It gives the strongest guarantee — a row could never name a unit it does not link — and was rejected because 101 `professor of pediatrics` exports name a discipline, which has no `organizations` row to link. *A link plus a cached display name refreshed on save.* It removes the compose-time lookup, and reintroduces precisely the staleness this ticket exists to remove: a renamed unit leaves every unsaved row wrong until someone re-saves it. *Keeping unit-ness author-declared on the joiner.* It is already built and already tested by UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2407, and it permits a row to link a unit while choosing an article-less joiner with nothing reporting the contradiction.

**Bound, and it is load-bearing.** Everything above about `names_a_unit` and `scopeNamesAUnit()` is read from **UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2407, which is open and unmerged**. If it changes in review, the reasoning that dissolved two of the four questions moves with it.

**Companion.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2146 (this record); UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2145 and its UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2407 (the vocabulary and the flag this rests on); UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2125 (the `organizations` labels read at compose time); UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2138, open on the same files with its ordering against this unexamined.

## 2026-09-14 — Edge redirects carry a static-safe subset, with the PHP path as the fallback, and the generator waits for real inputs (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1935)

**Decision.** The deploy-time generator emits **only the rules that are statically safe** — enabled, approved, carrying no validity window, and using exact or wildcard matching rather than regex. Everything else keeps being served by the existing PHP path. The generator itself is **deferred** until the store holds real rules and a deploy surface exists; what happens now is a measurement of the export corpus.

**The output-target sub-fork the ticket posed is not answered, because it stopped being a fork.** `UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1935` asked for Cloudflare rulesets against a `_redirects` file, and its own AC3 forecloses the choice: a `_redirects` file cannot express a regular expression or a date comparison at all. Under full parity that eliminates it on capability rather than on preference. Under the subset decided here neither capability is needed, so **both formats remain viable** and the target can be chosen when there is something to deploy to. That is the point of the subset.

**Why a subset rather than parity.** Two of the engine's four filters are evaluated against mutable state at request time, and no deploy-time artifact can carry either. `RedirectRuleMatcher` tests a validity window with `ValidityWindow::fromRow($redirect)->contains(CarbonImmutable::now())` (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1355), so a rule's applicability depends on the clock; and it withholds rows whose approval status is `pending` or `rejected` (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1357), which an editor changes in the Control Panel with no deploy. Reproducing those at an edge means either encoding time logic there or re-emitting on every change, and both buy completeness by adding a second implementation of semantics that are unusually easy to get wrong: `RedirectPattern` folds case **byte-wise** and does offset arithmetic against the un-folded URI, noting that "only a byte-length-stable fold keeps those offsets aligned; multibyte folding does not."

The subset degrades honestly instead. A rule the artifact cannot represent is not approximated — it falls through to the path that serves it correctly today. The original motivation, not booting a CMS for a hash lookup, still holds for the common case.
