**Why the build waits.** Measured rather than assumed: `content/globals/redirects.yaml` holds **255 site keys, every one `null`**, with zero `source:`/`target:` keys against a positive control. And `.github/workflows/` contains `ci.yml` and `watch-upstream.yml` and **no deploy workflow**. A generator built now would read nothing and have nowhere to run, and its feature mix would be guessed. The one thing worth doing immediately is counting how many incoming rules the subset would actually exclude — `RedirectPattern`'s docblock already cites 508 capture-carrying rules in the export corpus, so the corpus can answer it. That measurement is UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2449.

**Rejected.** *Full parity at the edge.* It forces the ruleset format, puts a credential in the pipeline, gives up a reviewable diff, and requires a second implementation of the byte-wise folding and offset arithmetic above — the likeliest place AC3 fails silently. *Full parity, re-emitted whenever the store changes.* It avoids edge-side time logic but converts every approval flip and every window boundary into a deploy event, and specifies a re-emit pipeline and a lag window that nobody has scoped. *Building the generator now against a fixture global set.* It is what AC5 describes and it was rejected on sequencing rather than on merit: with an empty store and no deploy target, the rule shape it is tuned for is a guess.

**One thing the acceptance criteria get wrong, recorded so the build does not inherit it.** AC3 requires parity on "wildcards, captures, and case-insensitivity". The engine also has a regex mode, a validity window, an approval filter, an `order` key, and entry-typed targets resolved through Statamic. Parity with *the engine* is a larger claim than AC3 states — and it is a different claim from parity with WordPress, since this port deliberately diverges from SRM by substituting captures unconditionally where SRM skips them for absolute-URL targets, a divergence that fixes 76 of those 508 corpus rules.

**Companion.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1935 (this record); UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2449 (the corpus measurement); `UAMS-Web/wordpress-importer#133` (the parent spike whose hosting decision this serves); UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1355 and UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1357 (the two request-time filters that make full parity structural rather than fiddly).

## 2026-09-14 — The shared-traverser Stache defect is reported upstream as an issue and a pull request, deferred (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2241)

**Decision.** The `Statamic\Stache\Traverser` shared-filter defect is reported to `statamic/cms` as an upstream issue **and** an accompanying pull request carrying the fix, rather than an issue alone or neither. **The filing is deliberately deferred** — no upstream action is taken now, and UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2241 stays open holding it with UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2259 and UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2260 blocked behind.

**Why.** The fix currently ships as `patches/statamic-stache-traverser-shared-filter.patch`, registered in `composer.json` with a description naming it temporary. `composer-exit-on-patch-failure: true` means the day an upstream release edits those lines, `composer install` hard-fails for every checkout, including a fresh CI one. That file is actively maintained upstream — last touched 2025-11-10 in a Stache performance change — so the exposure is live rather than theoretical, and only an upstream fix retires it. The pull request is included because the fix is three lines, already written, and already exercised by this repository's regression test, so its marginal cost over an issue alone is the body text; it is also the only option that ends the debt rather than handing it to someone else's triage queue.

**Rejected.** *An issue without a pull request.* It leaves the design to the maintainers, which is defensible, but the patch and its `composer install` exposure persist for however long triage takes, and the saving is a few paragraphs. *Neither — carry the patch indefinitely and close the ticket `not_planned`.* Zero cost now, and it makes "temporary" permanent with nobody upstream aware the defect exists; the install failure then becomes a question of when rather than whether. *Reporting immediately.* Not rejected on the merits — the filing is outward-facing on behalf of the organization and is scheduled rather than skipped.

**The measurement that nearly closed the question the wrong way.** Reading the vendored `statamic/cms` shows `Traverser::filter()` returning a clone, which is exactly the fix being proposed and reads as "already fixed upstream, nothing to report". That is wrong: `vendor/` is the patched copy. Read from `statamic/cms` at the pinned tag and `v6.26.0` still carries `$this->filter = $filter; return $this;`. A version string does not identify code, and a vendored file under a patch set is the last place to establish what upstream does.

**Companion.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2241 (the filing, installment 2); UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2037 and UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2244 (the patch, installment 1); UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2259 (the upstream pull request); UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2260 (adopt the release and remove the patch); the [`upstream-patch-lifecycle`](../.claude/skills/upstream-patch-lifecycle/SKILL.md) skill, whose four-ticket chain this follows; `patches/README.md`, which carries the per-patch detail and the removal checklist.

**Retired (2026-10-02, UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2260, UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2774).** The upstream pull request merged as `statamic/cms#15551` and shipped in 6.35.0, which retires the patch this entry describes.

## 2026-09-14 — The classifieds board keeps self-service posting behind accounts, and imports the full history (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2096)

**Decision.** The Statamic classifieds board replacing WP Adverts on Inside UAMS keeps **front-end self-service posting**, gated behind **user accounts** rather than the anonymous emailed-hash-link model WordPress uses today. The import brings across **all 229 listings**, not only the 39 live ones. All **23 `advert_category` terms** are imported as declared, with no consolidation. Auto-expiry is rebuilt, on the scheduled-job pattern already in the repository.

**What made the identity question answerable differently than the ticket framed it.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2096 offered anonymous-with-hash or UAMS SSO. Both predate #2113, which landed `account_login`, `account_register`, `account_profile`, `account_forgot_password` and `account/reset_password` on 2026-09-11, driven by a Statamic **core** tag (`vendor/statamic/cms/src/Tags/Member.php`) rather than an addon. Accounts are a third position the ticket could not have known about: no SSO integration, and the login half already built.

**Auto-expiry is rebuilt because the data makes it structural rather than optional.** `_expiration_date` is present on 229 of 229 listings, and expiry there is the board's normal lifecycle rather than decay, so removing it would change what the board is. The mechanism has a precedent from UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1772 in [`routes/console.php`](../routes/console.php): `Schedule::job(new ScanLinkHealth)->cron((string) config('link_health.schedule.cron'))`, dispatching rather than holding the scheduler process.

**What the decisions activate, stated because neither is free.** Self-service with accounts makes every poster a user record, and [`config/statamic/users.php`](../config/statamic/users.php) sets `'repository' => 'file'`, so each registrant writes a tracked YAML file into the `users` **git submodule**, which holds two files today. An `eloquent` repository is **already declared in that same file** and simply not selected, so the alternative is one key rather than new configuration — but it is **not** decided here and carries its own ticket. Note that [`config/statamic/eloquent-driver.php`](../config/statamic/eloquent-driver.php) is *not* the surface for this: it declares nineteen repositories and `users` is not among them. Separately, nothing under `app/Http` creates an entry today — no `Entry::make()` anywhere in it — so the posting path is new code rather than a template change.

The full-history import interacts with the account decision, and the interaction needs modelling rather than discovering later: `_adverts_account` is **0 of 229**, so no imported listing has an account to attach to. Imported listings are account-less while new ones are account-bound, and the collection has to carry both.

**Rejected.** *Control-Panel-authored listings.* The cheapest option, and the only one with no public write path, no spam surface and no user-storage question; rejected in favor of preserving self-service on a board in continuous weekly use — 160 posts over 14 months, a median of 12 a month. *Anonymous self-service with an emailed hash link.* It matches today's behavior exactly, on all 229 listings; rejected because accounts are now nearly as cheap to build and are meaningfully harder to abuse. *Importing only the 39 live listings.* Rejected in favor of the full history. The privacy cost is real and is accepted knowingly rather than overlooked: 189 of the 229 are expired, and each carries a poster name and email (229/229) and often a phone number (128/229). *Consolidating the 23 categories at import time.* `General` alone carries 108 of 345 assignments and 7 of the 22 used terms carry three or fewer, so consolidation is tempting; rejected because it bakes an editorial judgment nobody has made into the imported data, and a faithful import stays reversible where a consolidated one does not.

**Companion.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2096 (this record and the build); `UAMS-Web/wordpress-importer#23` (the importer-side disposition this destination serves); `UAMS-Web/wordpress-importer#774`, which UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2096 blocks and which this decision alone does **not** release — build work stands between the two.

## 2026-09-14 — The registration chain is gated in full until the at-rest standard for registrant records is known (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2455)

**Decision.** Self-service registration waits for an at-rest standard. #2113 (the front-end register, login and profile pages) is gated, and #2115 (creating a user from a form submission) is gated through it. UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2454, the allowlist `.gitignore` in the `users` submodule, went ahead because it only reduces exposure, and it has merged. The gate is enforced by native `blocked_by` edges on #2113, not by a note.

**Why.** #2113 builds pages and creates no records, so gating it costs work that carries no exposure. It is gated anyway because the alternative depends on someone remembering, later and under delivery pressure, that a finished front end must not be wired to a write path. A first public registrant record on disk before anyone knows which standard governs it is exactly what the gate prevents.

**Rejected.** *Gate #2115 only.* The narrower gate, and the one the decision packet recommended: the front end progresses while the write path is held. It lost because that separation holds only as long as nobody connects the two, and the gate would have been notional rather than recorded. *Let all three proceed and treat at-rest handling as a follow-up.* Fastest, and it accepts registrant records on disk ahead of the standard, which is the default UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2455 was filed to interrupt.

**The questions the gate now rests on.** This entry settles only the engineering half. The institutional half was declined here as not an engineering call, as it was on #821 and UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2444. When UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2455 was split on 2026-09-28, #2113 was given `blocked_by` edges to both of the tickets that now hold it, before UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2455 closed, so the gate carried over:

- **#2627:** the at-rest standard for registrant records on the deployed server. It covers whether plain-text YAML is permitted and whether UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2444's file-driver decision stands, and it carries the two staff seed records with `password_hash` in the private users repository.
- **#2628:** registrant records in the deployed site's backups, meaning the destination, the retention, and whether they are acceptable.

UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2629, which confirms that UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2454's allowlist holds if `STATAMIC_GIT_AUTOMATIC` is turned on, is measurable and gates nothing, because #2113 creates no records.

**Companion.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2455 (this record, closed after the split); UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2444 (the driver decision that named this residual); UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2454 (the allowlist); #2113 and #2115 (the chain this gates); #2627, #2628 and UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2629 (split from UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2455).

## 2026-09-15 — Telemedicine hours render for a reader and stay out of the JSON-LD (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2405)

**Decision.** A location's `telemedicine`-mode `regular_hours` rules render on the profile as **their own labeled group inside the existing hours section**, beside the in-person group, rather than in a separate section elsewhere on the page. The `openingHoursSpecification` JSON-LD **continues to skip them**, unchanged. The mode labels appear **only when telemedicine hours are present**, so a location with in-person hours alone renders byte-identical output to what it rendered before.

**Why the two surfaces stay divergent, which is the part worth recording.** `openingHoursSpecification` describes when the **physical premises** are open. A remote visit is not a premises opening, so emitting an 8 p.m. telemedicine window there would tell a search engine the building is open at 8 p.m. — a claim a person can act on by driving to a locked door. The skip UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2390 inherited was therefore right for the parser and wrong only in that nothing else read the value; the fix is a second reader, not a change to the first. Asserted rather than assumed: `LocationRegularHoursTest` renders one fixture through both tags and pins that the reader-facing surface names the evening window while the JSON-LD names no `20:00` at all.

**Why one section rather than two.** The blueprint's own instruction is to "add a telemedicine-mode rule only when telemedicine hours differ from the in-person hours", so a telemedicine rule exists *as a contrast* to the in-person hours and is close to meaningless away from them. Putting the two in one section is what lets a reader make that comparison in one glance. It also leaves the template's hours branch untouched, so the UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1328 modified-hours notice still follows the whole hours block rather than splitting it.

**Why the labels are conditional.** Labeling an in-person-only location "In-Person" introduces a distinction the stored data does not make, on the overwhelming majority of locations, to no reader's benefit. Making the labels conditional on a telemedicine group existing keeps the common case unchanged — which is also what makes this change auditable, since every pre-existing assertion about the premises output still holds unedited.

**An absent or unrecognized `service_mode` is in-person**, matching the blueprint default, and matching the reading `SchemaLocationHours` and `SystemLocationClosures` already apply when they skip only an explicit `telemedicine`. The alternative — recognizing `in_person` and dropping everything else — silently discards a rule an editor entered.

**Bound, stated so it is not mistaken for a gap.** A location answering yes to the variable-hours question renders prose and no rules. That is not a missed case: the blueprint gates `regular_hours` behind `if: hours_variable_query: 'equals false'`, so such a location cannot store rules in either mode, and the template branch mirrors the blueprint condition exactly.

**Rejected.** *A separate telemedicine section near other virtual-care copy.* Defensible, and it loses the comparison the blueprint asks the editor to set up; it also needs the template's variable-hours branch reworked to place a second section, for no reader gain. *Emitting telemedicine rules into `openingHoursSpecification`.* Rejected on correctness — see above; it trades a silent omission for an active falsehood. *A third structured-data property for remote hours.* Not rejected on merit and deliberately not attempted here: schema.org models virtual care through `availableService` / `VirtualLocation` rather than through premises hours, which is its own modeling question and its own ticket. *Always labeling both modes.* Rejected because it changes every location's rendered hours to solve a problem only some locations have.

**Companion.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2405 (this record and the build); UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2390 (rendered the in-person hours and established the skip this inverts); UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1328 (the modified-hours notice, whose `special_hours` rules carry the same `service_mode` and whose consumer does **not** yet distinguish it — tracked separately).

## 2026-09-15 — Identifier uniqueness is two purpose-built rules, wired per blueprint field (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2152)

**Decision.** Identifier uniqueness is enforced by two new rules — `UniqueIdentifier` for single-value fields and `UniqueIdentifierList` for the two `clinical_locations` replicator lists — rather than by Statamic's `UniqueEntryValue`. Both are attached **per blueprint field**, never in the shared fieldsets. The uniqueness pool is a rule parameter, so the four Workday fields name each other and form one namespace.

**Why not core's rule, measured against it rather than assumed.** `UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2152`'s own *Current state* says Statamic "ships no `unique` validator for entry fields", and that is wrong — `Statamic\Rules\UniqueEntryValue` exists and is used as `unique_entry_value:{collection},{id},{site}`. Its **conclusion** survives the correction, because the rule satisfies none of the requirements here: it queries `where($attribute, $value)`, so it can only ever compare a field against the **same-named** field and cannot express the pooled Workday namespace; it compares case-sensitively and without trimming, against a decision requiring both; it treats a blank as a value, so the second entry leaving an identifier empty would collide with the first; and it fails with a message naming neither the value nor the conflicting entry, where the criteria require both. Four independent misses, any one of which is disqualifying.

**Why the wiring sits on the blueprint field and not in the fieldset — this is the load-bearing choice.** `uamsweb_common_fad.epic_dep_ids` backs **two** fields on a location: the declared list in General Details, and the MyChart scheduling widget's `dep`. The widget is validated `dep_in_declared_list:epic_dep_ids`, which *requires* it to repeat the declared IDs. A uniqueness rule in the fieldset would therefore reach the widget and reject correct data on every location that uses one. Per-field wiring is what keeps them apart, and because nothing in the fieldset records that, a test asserts the fieldset stays free of the rule rather than leaving the next editor to rediscover it.

**Two rules rather than one.** A single-value field and a replicator list differ in what they read, in what "within this entry" means (a sibling field against a sibling row), and in their failure messages. One class taking an optional row key would branch on that key in every method and would make the pooled case and the list case read as variants of each other, which they are not.

**An absent `{id}` on a create arrives as the literal string `NULL`.** `Statamic\Fields\Validator`'s string-rule path substitutes `Arr::get($replacements, 'id', 'NULL')`, and a create has no `id` in its replacements. It is read as *exclude nothing*, which is what it means; reading it as an id would be harmless today only because no entry carries that string.

**The cost is recorded, since the naive scan was chosen on the condition that it would be.** Measured in an isolated Stache against 43 entries — `people` as it stands on disk, and the largest affected collection, with `clinical_locations` and `clinical_providers` at zero. A save validating all six wired fields, each running its own scan, with a clean payload so no rule exits early:

```
before   ~0.1-1.1 ms      after   ~8.1-9.6 ms      added   ~7.5-8.5 ms
```

`UniqueIdentifierCostTest` keeps the measurement in the suite and prints both arms, so re-measuring is a side effect of running the tests rather than an errand someone has to remember. Its bound is deliberately loose: it guards against a change in complexity, not against a machine being busy.

**The first version of this measurement was wrong, and the way it was wrong is worth keeping.** Without a warm-up call the first `Validator::make` of the process pays one-off setup, and that lands in whichever arm runs first. Run alone, the before-arm read 5.0 ms and the delta looked like **3.3 ms**; run after another test file, the same before-arm read 0.1 ms and the delta was **8.4 ms**. The figures disagreed by a factor of two-and-a-half with the code unchanged, and the smaller one — the flattering one — was warm-up subtracted from itself. The test now warms the validator before timing either arm, which is why the figures above hold in both contexts.

**The pool is named by DOT PATH, and getting that wrong is a silent no-op.** `person.yaml` puts all four Workday identifiers inside a `uams_workday_id` field of `type: group`, so both the stored data and the validated attribute are nested — a real entry reads `uams_workday_id: {workday_universal_id: '400026157', …}`, and a probe against the blueprint returns the rule key `uams_workday_id.workday_universal_id`. A rule reading the bare handle finds `null` on **every** entry, so the check cannot fire and nothing about a passing save says so. The first version of this build did exactly that, and its tests passed because they were written with flat fixtures — the author choosing the fixture shape and the reader both being wrong in the same direction. The fixtures now carry the nested shape, a regression test asserts the value is genuinely absent at the top level before asserting the collision, and reverting the path read turns three tests red. `npi` and `epic_ser_id` are top-level on `people`, and neither `clinical_locations` list field sits in a group; all three were checked rather than assumed by symmetry.

**A neighbor already knew the path, which is the part worth noticing.** `App\Services\People\PeopleDuplicateDetector` has read `uams_workday_id.workday_universal_id` since UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#839, and it was the thing that surfaced the defect. It also normalizes identifiers *differently* — `preg_replace('/\s+/', '', …)` strips whitespace everywhere, where this rule trims only the ends per the UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2221 decision. That divergence is deliberate and is left standing: the detector's job is to over-match and hand candidates to a human, where folding harder is correct; a rule that blocks a save must not reject two genuinely different identifiers because one was typed with an interior space.

**Rejected.** *Reusing `UniqueEntryValue` and layering the pooled case on top.* It would leave two comparison semantics in one blueprint — core's case-sensitive one on `npi`, ours on the Workday group — which is the kind of difference nobody discovers until data disagrees. *Wiring the rule in the shared fieldset.* Fewer lines, and it breaks the scheduling widget, which is the single most likely way this change causes an outage. *Indexing identifiers for lookup.* Rejected on UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2221 and not reopened; at 43 entries the scan adds ~3.3 ms and the criterion to re-measure is the trigger to revisit. *Pooling `npi` across `people` and `clinical_locations`.* NPPES issues individual and organizational NPIs from one globally unique space, so a collision cannot occur in valid data and pooling would add nothing.

**The comparison semantics could NOT be stated in the Workday field instructions, and that is a cross-repository constraint rather than a preference.** The criterion asked for them in the field instructions. For `npi` and `epic_ser_id` that is what happens. The four Workday descriptions are **mirrors of upstream WordPress field descriptions**, pinned character for character by `WorkdayIdentifierFieldsTest` against `UAMSWP-People-Showcase#56` and `UAMSWP-Find-a-Doc#819`; that test's own comment says a reword upstream is what should drive a change here, so appending from this side is precisely what it exists to stop. It caught the attempt, correctly, and the first version of this branch was wrong.

So the Workday half is carried by the **validation message** instead — which an editor meets at the moment the save is refused, rather than while reading a form. All four messages state it, asserted rather than assumed. Making the instructions say it as well needs an upstream reword first, which is UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2469.

**One accepted gap alongside it.** The sentence that *does* go in the fieldset reaches `clinical_providers` too, which is deliberately unwired — so those two fields describe a rule not applied there. Accepted because the collection holds no entries, is unrouted and is being retired, and because the alternative is copying instruction text into two blueprints where it would drift out of step with the fieldset.

**Companion.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2152 (this record and the build); UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2221 (the three forks this implements, decided 2026-09-11); UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2395 (the audit command covering imported content, which blueprint validation never sees); #803 (retiring `clinical_providers`, which is why it stays unwired); `UAMS-Web/wordpress-importer#842`, which must not write `''` for an absent identifier.

## 2026-09-15 — Organization pronunciation is a separate optional pair field, not a conversion of `name` (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2363)

**Decision.** Organizations get a new optional `name_pronunciation` field of the existing `text_phonetic_pair` type, alongside the existing `name`. The `organization-type-base-organization` schema template emits `PronounceableText` when that pair carries phonetic text and the plain official name otherwise. `name` itself is **not** converted to a pair.

**Why not convert `name`, which is the obvious reading of the criterion.** `App\Fieldtypes\TextPhoneticPair::normalizeStored()` returns `emptyPair()` for any value that is not an array, and `process()` routes through it. Every one of the **181** organization entries stores `name` as a plain string today, so converting the field would discard the stored name on the next save of each entry — silently, and with the Control Panel showing an empty box rather than an error. A separate optional field satisfies the criterion's actual requirement (the input uses `text_phonetic_pair`) at no such cost.

**The spoken form is deliberately its own text, not a copy of the name.** `organization.yaml` instructs editors to enter the full official name and *not* to follow it with an acronym, while UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#554's own worked example emits `textValue: "UAMS"` — an acronym, and exactly the case where pronunciation markup earns its keep, since a speech engine reads it as a word rather than four letters. So the pair holds the form a person would *say* plus its IPA, and the official name stays untouched in `name`.

**The template branches rather than calling the tag unconditionally, and that branch is the load-bearing half.** `name_pronunciation` is optional and is empty on every organization today, so an unconditional call would emit nothing at all for them — dropping the name rather than degrading to `Text`. The tag degrades correctly for an *empty phonetic* on a populated pair; it has nothing to emit for an entirely empty pair. Three tests cover populated, empty-pair and field-absent, and reverting the template turns the first red.

**Scope, decided rather than assumed.** The ticket asked which organization types carry the input. `organization-type-base-organization` renders `{{ name }}` from an `organizations` entry and is the entry-driven organization path; templates whose `name` is `mode: inherit` follow it for free. Two neighbors are deliberately excluded: `medicalorganization-type-base-clinical-location` renders `{{ title }}` from a `clinical_locations` entry, a different collection and a different input surface, and `arkansas-childrens-medicalorganization` names an external body this repository does not own the pronunciation of. `alternateName` is not wired because the `organizations` blueprint has no such field — checked rather than skipped.

**Rejected.** *Converting `name` to a pair.* The reading the criterion most invites, and it is a silent data-loss path across 181 entries. *A plain text IPA field beside `name`, with the tag called as `text_value` plus `phonetic_text`.* It works and it is smaller, and it was rejected because the criterion names `text_phonetic_pair` and because a bare IPA box gives an editor nowhere to put the spoken form when it differs from the official name. *Wiring every organization-typed template.* Most do not render from an `organizations` entry at all, so the field would have no input to read.

**Companion.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2363 (this record and the build); the `uams-statamic-content` companion carrying the template change, which merges **first**; UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#554 (the parent and the source of the `MedicalOrganization` example); UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2362 (the SSML derivation, which is why no tag change was needed).

## 2026-09-15 — A special-hours notice is scoped to the service mode it describes (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2466)

**Decision.** `LocationModifiedHoursNotice` reads `service_mode`. A notice row carries the mode it describes; a telemedicine row is labeled and a premises row is not. `allRulesClosed()` judges a closure from the rules describing that mode only, and an entry counts as remote only when **every** rule it carries is a telemedicine rule.

**Why it could not stay as it was, and the two directions are not equally bad.** The tag walked `special_hours` rules reading `date`, `through` and `status` and never `service_mode`, so `array_all` over every rule was wrong both ways. An open telemedicine rule beside closed premises rules made a real closure read "Modified hours" — an **understated** closure, where a reader arrives at a shut building. A closed telemedicine-only entry made premises that were open all week read "Closed" — an **overstated** closure, which sends someone away from a clinic that would have seen them. The second is the one that decided the priority: a reader who is turned away never discovers the notice was wrong.

**`ruleMode()` moved into `ResolvesSpecialHoursValues` rather than being written twice.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2405 put it on `LocationRegularHours`; this is the second consumer, and two copies of *what counts as telemedicine* is exactly the drift that produces an hours section and a notice disagreeing about one entry. The label map moved with it, behind a `serviceModeLabel()` accessor — without that, the two classes that read modes and never display one inherit an unused constant, which `phpstan` flags and which is a fair complaint rather than noise.

**An entry is remote only if ALL its rules are.** One premises rule makes the whole entry a premises exception, because that is the part a reader standing outside the building needs. A rule-less entry stays a premises exception too: nothing in the data says "remote", so nothing should assert it.

**An absent or unrecognized `service_mode` is in-person**, matching UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2405 and the other two consumers. The same reading in all four places is the point.

**Rejected.** *Suppressing telemedicine-only notices entirely.* It removes the false "Closed" and loses a real exception the editor entered — the UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2405 defect in a new place. *Labeling both modes.* Rejected for the reason UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2405 rejected it: an in-person row is the overwhelming majority and labeling it invents a distinction the data does not make; leaving it unlabeled also keeps the existing output byte-identical, which is what makes this change auditable — all 15 pre-existing tests pass unedited. *Folding the mode into the status text* (`"Closed (telemedicine)"`). Cheaper, and it buries the mode inside a sentence where no stylesheet can reach it and nothing parsing the markup can read it.

**Verified by mutation rather than by reading.** Un-scoping `allRulesClosed()` turns the understated-closure test red; removing the mode label turns three red; restoring returns 23 passed.

**Companion.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2466 (this record and the build); UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2405 (the hours-section precedent and the source of `ruleMode()`); UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1328 (the notice); UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1327 (the org-wide closure broadcast, whose merge service already gated on an in-person rule).

## 2026-09-15 — `boost:update` is run only as `--ignore-skills`, and `infer-conventions` is declined (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2319)

**Decision.** `php artisan boost:update` is run only as `boost:update --ignore-skills`, as its own reviewed change, never as a refresh reached for mid-ticket; and the Boost core skill `infer-conventions` is declined.

**Why.** Bare, the command rewrites the harness skill trees as a side effect of a guideline refresh — `laravel-best-practices` in both `.claude/skills/` and `.cursor/skills/`, `pest-testing` and `tailwindcss-development` replaced with `.ai/skills/` **symlinks**, and an `infer-conventions` added that nobody asked for — all landing silently in whatever diff is open (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2168 reverted it twice). Characterized from a run on current `main` 2026-09-15: the symlinking is confirmed (it is the UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2170 regression a default Windows clone turns into a vanished skill, gated by `tests/Feature/Config/SkillMirrorsTest.php`), while `--ignore-skills` on the same tree touched only `CLAUDE.md` and `AGENTS.md`. The one documented flag closes every skill-writing hazard while leaving guideline updates flowing. `infer-conventions` records findings into `.ai/rules` through an MCP tool, opening a second authoring route beside this repo's hand-written `.claude/rules/` + this log, where each rule carries its own measured evidence.

**Rejected.** *`boost.skills.exclude` in `config/boost.php`* — undocumented (absent from Boost's own config stub) and its `reject()` runs before the user-skills merge, so it structurally cannot suppress anything under `.ai/skills/`, and it does not survive UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2320. *Stop running `boost:update` entirely* — forfeits upstream guideline improvements permanently and lets drift accumulate unreported. *Accept the churn and review it each time* — relies on every contributor reading `git status` carefully, the exact failure UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2168 hit twice while doing so. *Adopt `infer-conventions` and try it once* — its own ground rules are sound, but it is a second corpus to keep in step with different provenance standards; reversible if a concrete need appears.

**Companion.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2319 (this record, the characterizing run, the guidance rule [`.claude/rules/running-boost-update.md`](../.claude/rules/running-boost-update.md), and the `SkillMirrorsTest` assertion); UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2320 (the `laravel-best-practices` move that makes `--ignore-skills` the only remaining control); UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2168 (the churn); UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2169 / UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2170 (the symlink regression).

## 2026-09-15 — The identifier audit derives its surface from the blueprints rather than restating it (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2395)

**Decision.** `identifiers:audit-duplicates` reads the wired `unique_identifier` and `unique_identifier_list` rules out of the blueprints and audits exactly what they enforce, rather than carrying its own list of collections and fields. Comparison comes from the same trait the rule uses. The command reports and never repairs, and always prints what it examined.

**Why derive rather than restate.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2395's criterion is that the audit and the save-time rule "cannot disagree about what the same identifier means". Shared normalization gives that for the *comparison*; a second hand-maintained field list would give it away again for the *coverage*. Deriving makes two more of the ticket's requirements hold by construction rather than by a skip-list nobody remembers to update: the MyChart scheduling widget's `dep` field is unscanned because it carries `dep_in_declared_list` and no uniqueness rule, and `clinical_providers` is unscanned because nothing there is wired.

**The cost is real and is accepted.** Removing a rule's wiring silently removes that field from the audit too. That is the intended direction — the audit follows the policy rather than asserting one of its own — but it means a blueprint edit is the only place the surface can change, and a test asserts the derived set so the change is at least visible.

**A clean report prints its denominator, and an empty surface is an ERROR rather than a pass.** "No duplicates found" and "found nothing because it looked nowhere" are otherwise byte-identical, and the second is what a broken scan produces. So the command prints the group count, the entry count, the collection count, the read count and the value count *before* any finding, and names each audited group; and if no rule is wired at all it exits `FAILURE` saying so rather than reporting a clean scan over nothing. Both are mutation-verified: removing the denominator reds two tests, collapsing the pooled Workday namespace reds three.

**Reads and entries are printed separately, because one number would mislead.** The collection is read once per group, so a `people` scan of 43 entries performs 129 reads. A single figure would be taken for the entry count by any reader.

**A correction to the ticket, since it states a measurement that is false.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2395's *Why* says the repository holds "43 `people` entries, one `npi`, one `epic_ser_id`, **zero Workday identifiers of any kind**". There are two, both on one entry: `uams_workday_id.workday_universal_id` and `uams_workday_id.workday_employee_id` on `c-lowry-barnes`. The conclusion is untouched — the import is still what changes the population — but the command finding exactly those two was an unplanned positive control on reading the nested group path that UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2405 had to correct.

**Rejected.** *A hand-maintained map of collections and fields in the command.* Simpler to read, and it is a second copy of the coverage policy that drifts the first time a rule moves. *Reading the rules from the validator registry rather than the blueprints.* The registry knows the rule names, not which fields carry them. *Exiting non-zero on findings.* A duplicate is a finding to act on, not a command failure, and a non-zero exit would make the command unusable in any chain that treats that as an error.

**Companion.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2395 (this record and the build); UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2152 (the save-time rule whose coverage this derives from); UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2221 (the decision that this is built rather than deferred, and the comparison semantics); UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2405 and UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2466 (where the shared normalization and `ruleMode()` came from).

## 2026-09-16 — The serial tail's single-process constraint is inherent where a reason is recorded, and its cost is three files (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2291)

**Decision.** The `serial` group stays single-process. Six files gated on two mechanisms that are closed on both platforms return to the parallel band: five Antlers render files gated by convention, in a feature pull request with no observed failure behind the gate, on "the shared, paratest-non-isolated compiled-view cache" (per worker under Laravel's `TestViews` since parallel testing existed, `storage/framework/views/test_<token>`, the directory UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2442 patched for concurrent bands) and `PeopleShowcaseSortOrderTest`, gated on a worker asserting before the shared Stache was warmed (closed by UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1590, which seeds every worker's own cache from one snapshot). Four more render files, gated on the same attribution after an *observed* flake (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1447, UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1457, UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1571), stay: the attribution was already false when the flake was observed, so the cause is unexplained and `tests/README.md`'s ten-run bar for an observed flake applies (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2503). The remaining 73 stay: 26 on a mechanism that is real and shared or on a gate kept by intent, 12 on a Windows file-lock race in isolated-Stache fixtures, 33 with no recorded reason that sit on the same bases in the same era as the file-lock siblings, and 2 with no recorded reason on plain `TestCase`, kept as candidates. `tests/README.md` carries the per-file table -- mechanism, verdict, origin pull request, basis -- and is the record a new gate joins.

**Why the cost question is answered by a different fact than the ticket assumed.** The ticket read the tail as 84 files with cost spread across them. Measured from the gate's own `junit-serial.xml` (macOS, 2026-09-16, `phpunit`-job parity): 502 tests sum to 386.4s of test time against a ~508s wall, and three files are 54% of it. `SitemapTaxonomyTermsTest` was 122.2s in one test -- 3.78s in `Term::save()` and 116.85s in the teardown, and the mechanism is the one `TaxonomyTermArchiveTest` already records for the same term (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1634): `TermSaved` and `TermDeleted` dispatch `Statamic\Search\UpdateItemIndexes`, a `ShouldQueue` listener that runs inline under the suite's sync queue across every local search index. With `saveQuietly()` and `deleteQuietly()` the same test, same sweep, runs in 0.92s and the file in 2.27s; the first cut of this change had the observation right and the mechanism wrong (a query on a dirtied index), and the adversarial review caught it through the sibling. `AssetsMinimumDimensionsEnforcementTest` (53.0s) and `AssetsByFileTypeScopeTest` (31.6s) pay `Stache::clear()` in every `afterEach`, about 8s a test; that is UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2500. Measured on the same machine, the same day, with the `phpunit` job's flags, one run each side (before from the CI worktree's gate run, after from a work slot): the band went 157.8s to 155–162s over three runs while absorbing the 131 returned tests, and the tail went 387.7s (502 tests) to 256.1s (371) — 110s of it the sitemap teardown, the rest the returned files and the ~0.24s per-test boot the single process pays. The files are returned because their reason is stale, not for the seconds.

**Why runs refute and never establish, and what three runs are worth.** The ticket's first pass measured the constraint: the same `--parallel` command on the same tree produced 8 then 6 failures, 4 then 5 distinct files, five of seven appearing in exactly one run. So a per-file verdict of "safe" cannot come from observation at low N; it comes from the code, and a run can only falsify it. Ten candidate files -- the six returned and the four re-gated -- were run in three consecutive parity-matched parallel bands with the gate's flags and produced zero failures; that bounds variance and refutes nothing, and is recorded as such rather than as proof. It is also why the README's bar now distinguishes a gate added by convention, which comes off on the code read, from a gate added on an observed flake, which needs the ten-run bar the README has stated since UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1700.

**Why the 33 unrecorded gates on the isolation bases stay.** Their files use `StacheIsolatedTestCase` or `CollectionTreeIsolatedTestCase`, whose per-process throwaway roots close the hazard the bases exist for (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1957, UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1960) -- which is why the ticket's second pass named them the strongest candidates. But every file on those bases that *does* record a reason names a Windows file-lock race in the same fixtures, the reason `TitleDisambiguatorTest` has carried since the suite was parallelized, and a macOS run cannot refute a Windows-only race. Returning them on macOS evidence would ship a Windows flake. The refutation they need is a Windows arm, tracked as UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2501.

**Rejected.** *Return every isolation-base file on the code read alone.* The read is sound for the throwaway-root hazard and silent on the file-lock one, and the silent half is the one recorded. *Run the parallel band N times until the failure union plateaus and classify from that.* The first pass showed the union growing with N and nothing bounding it; the cost of enough runs to plateau on two platforms exceeds the ~45s a run of the returned files saves. *Move the self-provisioning per-`it` gates (`ReverseEntryReferenceResolverTest` and its neighbors) onto an isolated base.* Correct in kind and out of this ticket's scope: it is a fix to each test, not a decision about the gate, and none of them is a measurable share of the tail.

**Companion.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2291 (this record, the audit table in `tests/README.md`, the `SitemapTaxonomyTermsTest` teardown); UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1537 and UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1890 (the measurements the ticket cites); UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1149 (the isolation guard whose Rule A keeps the `Storage::fake` files gated regardless); UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2442 (the compiled-view patch that shows the per-worker directory is real); UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1590 (the snapshot seeding that closed the warm-up race); UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2500 (the asset files' `Stache::clear()` cost); UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2501 (the Windows-arm refutation for the kept files).

## 2026-09-16 — A mutation run that cannot launch a mutant is patched to abort, not to score (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2322)

**Decision.** `pest-plugin-mutate` is patched in two places rather than worked around by invocation guidance: `MutationTestRunner::setOriginalArguments()` normalizes the separator in `argv[0]`, and `run()` probes that command once and **aborts with exit 1** if a child still cannot be launched. A `scripts/check-mutation-instrument.php` negative control, a guard test over the vendored source, and a CLI-contract test cover it, and the criterion for trusting a score is recorded in [`pcov-setup`](../.claude/skills/pcov-setup/SKILL.md) as **disagreement rather than speed**.

**Why.** Each mutant runs in a child process built from `$_SERVER['argv']` with argv[0] reused verbatim as the executable, and `MutationTest::hasFinished()` reads any non-zero exit as *the test failed* — so a child that could not be **launched** is recorded as a kill. PHP sets argv[0] to the script path as typed, so a forward-slashed relative path reaches the child unchanged and `cmd.exe` cannot start it. Measured on Windows 11 Pro 10.0.26200 / PHP 8.4.20 / v5.0.2, one class in one tree with the cache cleared between arms: `vendor/bin/pest` and `php vendor/bin/pest` both reported `20 tested, 0 untested, 100.00%`, while two separator-correct forms reported `14 tested, 6 untested, 70.00%`. Adding `php` in front changes nothing, which is why the form the skill taught looked safe.

**The invalidation scope is a bound, not a list, and that is the finding.** Measured across this repository: **210 recorded `Mutation:` lines across 209 test files, none naming the machine**, and **163 of them reporting exactly `100%`** — simultaneously the house standard and the fabrication signature. So which pre-patch scores need re-running is not derivable from the artifacts, only from which were produced on Windows. Pre-patch scores are therefore treated as **unverified rather than wrong**, re-run when load-bearing, and the platform is recorded alongside the score from here.

**Rejected.** *Guidance alone — "always use `composer mutate`".* It is the form Composer happens to backslash, so it works for a reason no reader could predict, and a remedy that works for an unstated reason confirms a wrong model instead of exposing it; it also leaves every other invocation silently fabricating. *Normalizing without the abort.* It fixes the one form this repository met and leaves any other command that cannot start scoring silently, which is the property that made the defect expensive rather than the specific separator. *Aborting without normalizing.* Correct but hostile: every documented invocation would start failing, for a defect the patch can simply fix. *A wrapper script owning the invocation.* Helps only callers who use the wrapper, and the rule's own examples are typed by hand. *Treating all 209 recorded scores as invalid.* Not supported by the evidence — the macOS figures are corroborated by UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2317 having merged after acting on their survivors — and a wholesale invalidation nobody can act on is indistinguishable from none.

**Companion.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2322 (this record, the measurement, and the build); [`pcov-setup`](../.claude/skills/pcov-setup/SKILL.md) (the negative control and the provenance requirement); [`adversarial-review`](../.claude/rules/adversarial-review.md) (the mutation step this defeated); UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2298 and UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2301 (the measurements it invalidated, already withdrawn); UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2327 (the sibling patch, and the `composer install` re-patch trap both share).

## 2026-09-16 — Rewording is the only remedy for a closing keyword; no Markdown construct disarms one (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2484)

**Decision.** Step 8 of [`pre-merge-check`](../.claude/rules/pre-merge-check.md) reads a pull request's `closingIssuesReferences` **and** re-scans the joined text before merging, because the two answer different questions. [`writing-pull-requests`](../.claude/skills/writing-pull-requests/SKILL.md)'s claim that a code span around the keyword disarms it is withdrawn. The only remedy measured to hold is **rewording** — breaking the adjacency between keyword and reference.

**Why.** Two code paths decide what closes and they disagree by construction: the pull-request linkage parses the **body as Markdown**, honoring spans and fences; the push-time scan reads the **squash commit message as plain text**, where neither exists. This repository squash-merges, so the body becomes that message and every Markdown defense evaporates at the moment it is needed. Three specimens, each re-measured here rather than cited:

```
wordpress-importer#1036   keyword inside a CODE SPAN, one keyword-adjacent pair in the body,
                          span carried verbatim into the squash message
                          -> field linked nothing; its target closed 1s after the merge, same commit
UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2482                     two directives inside a FENCE naming an unintended ticket
                          -> field linked its intended ticket and was correct about it;
                             merging closed the unlisted ticket anyway. Chain green on 13 jobs.
UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2446                     cross-line, unfenced, formed by table alignment
                          -> field empty; closed a ticket the body declined to close
```

**The populated field is the more dangerous specimen, which inverts the intuition.** An empty `closingIssuesReferences` invites a second look; one that is populated and accurate about what it lists closes the question while saying nothing about what it omits.

**Rejected.** *Reading `closingIssuesReferences` alone*, which the ticket proposed — it is necessary and demonstrably not sufficient, since it was correct and still failed to predict `UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2482`'s merge. *Widening [`scripts/check-closing-keyword-across-lines.mjs`](../scripts/check-closing-keyword-across-lines.mjs) to flag every same-line occurrence* — it would fire on any body discussing a past closure, and the cost of a check nobody trusts is higher than the defect. *Keeping the code-span guidance and noting an exception* — its only load-bearing row was a counter-example, so there was no rule left to except. *Automated enforcement in the chain* — the body is not an input to it, and `UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2484` puts that out of scope.

**Companion.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2484 (this record and the step); UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2464 and UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2482 (the cross-line instrument and the pull request that exposed the gap); UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2446 (the empty-field specimen); `UAMS-Web/wordpress-importer#1036` (the code-span counter-example).

## 2026-09-16 — Both gates check out the `users` submodule, so the registrant-record allowlist guard runs there (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2477)

**Decision.** `.github/workflows/ci.yml`'s pest job and `scripts/local-ci.mjs` initialize `users` alongside `content` (`git submodule update --init --depth 1 content users`), and the CI slot's expected submodules in `scripts/worktree-slots.mjs` are `content` and `users`. `tests/Feature/Users/RegistrantRecordsAreGitIgnoredTest.php` therefore runs in both gates rather than skipping, and `tests/skip-baseline.json` pins the parallel band at 3 skips instead of 6 so a gate that stops checking `users` out surfaces as three unexpected skips. `tests/Feature/GatesInitializeUsersSubmoduleTest.php` pins the two invocations themselves.

**Why.** The guard UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2454 added asserts that registrant records cannot reach git, and its acceptance criterion read as gate coverage while neither gate ran it: a reverted allowlist got a green chain. The reason the gates skipped `users` — `ci.yml`'s "hold user data and binary assets" — stopped being true of `users` at UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2454, which left only the mechanical cost. Measured on the primary checkout on macOS: the submodule is three tracked files (`.gitignore` and the two seed accounts), a 2.05 KiB pack, 20 KB on disk; on macOS a slot initializes it from local objects with no network fetch, and in Actions it is one more clone through the `COMPOSER_AUTH_TOKEN` `insteadOf` rewrite already scoped for the same organization. The privacy objection is gone and the cost is a rounding error against the `content` clone the same step already makes.

**What checking it out changes, and what it does not.** Accounts that Feature tests create (`FrontEndAccountFlowTest`, `TriggerActivateRouteTest` and their neighbors — not the browser suite, whose users store `BrowserWriteIsolation` re-roots at a throwaway directory) are written by the file driver into `users/` whichever way the gate is configured: into a plain empty directory before, into the submodule's working tree now, where the allowlist ignores them by design. So a killed run can leave ignored `*.yaml` litter there that `git status` never shows, exactly as it could leave the same files in the plain directory before; this ticket neither creates nor removes that exposure. What it does create is a tracked working tree a test can damage — a seed account deleted or rewritten stays that way in the reused CI worktree, since `submodule update --init` on an unchanged gitlink restores nothing — so the UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1873 litter probe now covers `users/` as well as `content/`, and the init step clears a plain, uninitialized `users/` directory that stranded app output would otherwise make un-cloneable (the UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1999 trap). The two seed accounts are now present during the suite in the gate, as they already were in every developer checkout and worktree slot, which is where the suite has been run with them all along.

**Rejected.** *Neither gate, documented.* Zero cost and it leaves the one test written to catch a silent revert in the one place a silent revert is caught, which is the defect the ticket names; the only argument for it was the privacy cost, and that cost no longer exists. *`scripts/local-ci.mjs` only.* The two files are a mirrored pair the pre-commit hook holds together, remote CI is budget-gated rather than removed, and the Actions cost is one 2 KiB clone with a token that already reaches the organization — diverging them deliberately needs a reason and there is none. *`public/assets` and `app/private/assets` too.* The reasoning here does not transfer: those genuinely hold binary assets and user data, `public/assets` is 170 MB, and `StoredSvgHazardScanTest` keeps naming its own gap for that reason (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1739).

**Companion.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2477 (this record and the build); UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2454 (the allowlist and the guard); UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2455 (protection at rest and in backups, deliberately not touched); UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1744 (the skip baseline this lowers); UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1952 (the CI slot's own expectations in `worktree-slots.mjs`).

## 2026-09-16 — The union driver is bounded to merges where the attribute is already in effect (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2471)

**Decision.** `docs/DECISIONS.md merge=union` stays, and the comment above it in [`.gitattributes`](../.gitattributes) stops promising conflict-free merging unconditionally. The driver resolves the tail-append conflict where the attribute is already in effect on both sides; syncing a branch that predates the line can still conflict, and that conflict is not evidence of a missing or broken driver. What is unconditional is the other half: the driver never orders the result, so [`scripts/check-decision-log-order.mjs`](../scripts/check-decision-log-order.mjs) is the gate and the merge exit code is not a signal.

**The measurement.** Two Windows binaries on one machine, same OS build, same filesystem, fresh repository per cell (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2471, carried from UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2452):

```
incoming commit                              2.32.0.windows.2   2.53.0.windows.4
appends AND adds `log.md merge=union`        rc=0, 0 markers    rc=1, 1 marker
appends, adds .gitattributes with no union   rc=1, 1 marker     rc=1, 1 marker
appends, adds no .gitattributes              rc=1, 1 marker     rc=1, 1 marker
```

Reproduced first-hand on **macOS 26.6.2, git 2.39.5 (Apple Git-154)**, which agrees with the right-hand column on all three rows. A fourth arm was added there and is the one that makes the table readable:

```
POSITIVE CONTROL: union line already in the base, both sides   rc=0, 0 markers
```

**That control is why "the driver stopped working" is the wrong reading.** Without it the three rows are all `rc=1` on a newer git and support exactly that conclusion — which would argue for removing the attribute. With it, the driver demonstrably still resolves the append conflict on the same binary, and the finding narrows to its real shape: **an attribute arriving in a merge does not govern that merge.** Rows two and three are the further controls, separating the union line from the mere existence of a `.gitattributes` in the incoming commit.

**What is NOT established, stated because the claim it replaces was wrong four times.** The measurement holds the machine constant and so excludes the operating system, but it does not separate *version* from *build* from *compiled-in defaults*. The binary differs; which property of it is responsible is unknown and is out of scope — naming the version is provenance, not cause. The macOS `2.54.0` arm is cited from UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2471 and was not re-run here.

**Rejected.** *Remove `merge=union`.* It earns its place wherever the attribute is already in effect, which the control demonstrates on a current git; UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2452 settled this and the finding does not reopen it. *Replace the unconditional promise with an unconditional denial* — "the driver does not apply on modern git" is the same error inverted, and the control refutes it directly. *Leave the comment and document the bound only here.* The comment is what an author reads at the moment they hit the conflict, which is the moment the old wording misleads them.

**Companion.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2471 (this record and the comment); UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2452 and PR UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2453 (the driver, the attribute, and the original comment); UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2445 (the conflict it was added for); UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2463 (the dropped blank-line separator, the other half of what the driver does silently); UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2236 (the order guard).

## 2026-09-25 — Classified expiry unpublishes on the board's boundary and tells no one yet (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2520)

**Decision.** A scheduled job unpublishes every published `classifieds` listing whose `expiration_date` has passed, deciding each through `App\Support\Classifieds\ClassifiedExpiry` — the same definition `App\Scopes\LiveClassifieds` and a listing's page read — so the stored state cannot disagree with what the board shows. It runs on the UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1772 `Schedule::job()` pattern, with its cron read in `statamic.system.display_timezone`. **It sends the poster no notice.** Notifying account holders is UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2523, blocked by UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2519.

**Why.** No current listing has a verified address to notify. All 229 imported listings have an empty `account`, and `contact_email` is free text copied from WP Adverts. 189 were already expired, so a notice in this job would have mailed about 189 unverified addresses about long-expired ads on its first run, which cannot be recalled. UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2519 binds new listings to user accounts, whose addresses are verified; a notice becomes worth sending once that exists.

**Rejected.** *Email `contact_email` on expiry* — mails unverified strangers on day one, and is not reversible. *Notify account holders in this ticket* — sends nothing today, since no imported listing has an account, and depends on UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2519's account model, which is still being built. *Restate the boundary as a query in the job* — a second definition of expiry is a second thing to keep in step with the board, and the two would drift.

**Companion.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2520 (the job and this decision); UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2523 (the account-holder notice); UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2519 (accounts); UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2096 (the board and `ClassifiedExpiry`); UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1772 (the scheduled-job pattern).

**Superseded in part (2026-10-05, UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2523).** The job now tells an expired listing's account holder, and only the account holder; see the UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2523 entry below. Listings with no account, which includes every imported one, still send nothing.

## 2026-09-25 — A scope's unit link lives inside `role_title`, and the joiner collapse is its own change (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2146)

**Decision.** The link the 2026-09-14 record calls for is a **new `scope.scope_unit` entries link inside the `role_title` payload**, into `organizations`, rather than the row's existing `department` / `service_line` link. The composer reads the linked unit's display name at compose time, a link supersedes and clears the typed `scope_name` on save, and a row that also links its own unit through `scope_unit_row_link` must link the same entry. Decision (2)'s collapse of the stored joiner to three prepositions plus a derived article is **split out** to UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2546, because it rewrites stored joiner values across the content repository and none of UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2146's acceptance criteria depend on it.

**Why the link lives in the payload.** The composer reads only the `role_title` payload, in PHP, from `augment()` and `preProcess()`; a sibling link would have to be threaded through every caller and into the CP. The row links also carry `query_scopes` that can exclude the unit a title names, and none of the 27 scoped rows in the local corpus sets its row link at all, so deriving from it would have made every existing scope unresolvable. The row link still constrains the scope where both are set, which is what keeps a row from linking one unit and naming another.

**A linked name is public or absent.** The name is the unit's resolved public label; an unlabeled unit without a Workday name falls back to its authored title, as its generated title already does. An unlabeled unit that carries a Workday name has **no** public name, because that name is internal, so validation refuses to link it rather than compose it.

**Existing rows are linked only where nothing changes.** `role-title:link-scope-units` links a typed name only when it equals the unit's display name exactly, so the composed title is unchanged by construction; every other case is reported. Measured on the local corpus: 15 unit-naming scopes, 7 exact matches, 8 naming no unit — among them `UAMS College of Medicine`, whose entry is titled `College of Medicine` with no public label.

**Rejected.** *Derive the scope from the row's `department` / `service_line` link.* It makes the no-disagreement criterion true by construction, and fails on the data: no existing scoped row sets that link, and its query scopes limit which units a row may link. *Fall back to the typed name when a link does not resolve.* It keeps a title readable after its unit is deleted, and reintroduces a title naming a unit it does not link. *Match typed names case-insensitively or with a `UAMS ` prefix stripped.* It links more rows, and every extra row it links changes what the title says.

**Companion.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2146 (this record and the build); UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2546 (the joiner collapse); UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2407 (the `names_a_unit` flag this reads); UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2125 (the public label resolved at compose time).

## 2026-09-25 — A Windows mutant child that ran nothing is patched, and upstream is not told yet (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2524)

**Decision.** `pest-plugin-mutate` is patched in two more places. `MutationTest::start()` passes the child's `--filter` without literal quotes, and `StreamWrapper::stream_lock()` honors the requested lock operation instead of always taking `LOCK_SH`, answering PHP's `0` support probe with `true`. A guard test exercises the wrapper's locking directly. No upstream issue is filed for now, which was the operator's decision on UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2524.

**Why.** Each defect alone makes a Windows mutant child fail without testing anything, and `hasFinished()` scores any failure as a kill.
- **The filter.** Through the `vendor\bin\pest` batch launcher that the UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2322 patch selects, `cmd.exe` reads the unquoted `|` in a filter naming two or more tests as a pipe, and the child exits 255.
- **The lock.** A child that does run fails its first `LOCK_EX` write, because a Windows shared lock denies writes through its own handle.

Measured on Windows 11 Pro 10.0.26200 / PHP 8.4.20 / v5.0.2:

| Class | Group | Unpatched | Patched |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `App\Jobs\UnpublishExpiredClassifieds` | `serial` | `11 tested, 100.00%` in 0.53s | `4 untested, 1 timeout, 6 tested` |
| `App\Modifiers\SanitizeUrl` | not `serial` | `34 tested, 100.00%` | `3 untested, 31 tested` |

The three `SanitizeUrl` survivors are the equivalents its test file already records, and a hand control confirmed one of them. macOS never produced these false kills, because its `flock()` is advisory and its shell is not `cmd.exe`. The patch still changes macOS slightly: exclusive locks now exclude, and test selection becomes case-insensitive, because PHPUnit had read the old quoted value as a `"`-delimited regex. The patched plugin has not been re-measured there.

**The UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2322 record's "executed" arms are now unverified too.** They were taken through the batch launcher before this patch, so any of their kills may be false: the filter defect reaches multi-test mutants, and the lock defect reaches any child that writes a locked file. As with UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2322, pre-patch Windows scores are treated as unverified rather than wrong, and are re-run when load-bearing.

**Rejected.**
- *Refuse the run in `scripts/check-mutation-instrument.php` instead of patching.* It would stop the false kills, and it would also leave every Windows mutation run unusable, for defects the patch simply fixes. The check probes whether a child *launches*, and both defects hit children that launch.
- *Launch the child through `php` rather than the batch launcher.* That avoids `cmd.exe` for the filter, but it leaves the lock defect, which is independent of how the child starts.
- *Pass every lock request through unchanged.* PHP's first call is `0`, which `flock()` rejects with a `ValueError`, so every child would fail differently.

**Companion.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2524 (this record, the measurement, and the build); UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2322 (the launch patch this sits beside); UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2478 (whose probe concluded the filter candidate did not fire; its filter and launcher are not recorded); [`pcov-setup`](../.claude/skills/pcov-setup/SKILL.md) and [`adversarial-review`](../.claude/rules/adversarial-review.md) (both corrected).

## 2026-09-25 — A scope joiner stores the preposition alone, a linked unit always takes the article, and a typed scope keeps its own article choice (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2546)

**Decision.** `role_title`'s `scope.scope_joiner` stores **the preposition alone** — `for`, `of`, `in` — in place of the five combined values. A **linked** unit always composes with `the`; a **typed** scope carries its own `scope_article` (`the` or none), stored separately from the typed name. A linked unit composes with the unit's new optional **`formal_name`**, the full name as it reads in running text, falling back to its public name. `names_a_unit` is retired: a scope names a unit when it links one. A typed `in` scope must choose `the`, since the corpus has no article-less `in`.

**This amends the 2026-09-14 record rather than completing it as written.** That record derived the article from the link for every scope, typed ones included. Measured against the stored content it would have changed 8 of 15 article-bearing titles: `dean of the UAMS College of Medicine` would have read `of UAMS College of Medicine` typed, or `of the College of Medicine` linked, because the college's own name carries no `UAMS`; and `vice chancellor for the Northwest Arkansas region`, which names a region rather than a unit, has nothing to link. The wording each of those titles already has is the requirement, so the article stays separate for typed text and the unit gains a name for running text.

**Why a `formal_name` rather than the public label.** The public label is the unit's public name everywhere: it drives the organization's computed `title`, and descendants that inherit a label display it. Setting it to `UAMS College of Medicine` to fix one title would rename the college site-wide. A separate field changes only where it is read, and is named for the general case — running text — rather than for titles.

**Migration, and what it proved.** `role-title:collapse-scope-joiners` maps each legacy joiner to its preposition and moves the article into `scope_article` for a typed scope; `role-title:link-scope-units` then links a typed name that equals a unit's composing name exactly, so linking cannot change a title. On the local corpus, with `formal_name` set on five colleges and two institute rows added: 41 joiners collapsed, 14 scopes linked, 1 left typed with its article (`the Northwest Arkansas region`). All 41 composed titles are byte-identical before and after, compared against titles composed by `main`'s code; removing one `formal_name` changes exactly one of them, so the comparison can detect a change.

**Rejected.** *Fold the article into the typed name* (`scope_name: the Northwest Arkansas region`). It needs no new field, and makes a name carry grammar, so a name can no longer be compared with a unit's name or reused on its own. *Set the colleges' public label to `UAMS College of …`.* It needs no new field, and renames each college everywhere it is shown. *List the eight changed titles for later review*, as the ticket permitted. It ships titles that read worse and leaves the list as the finished state rather than a review step.

**Companion.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2546 (this record and the build); UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2146 and the 2026-09-14 and 2026-09-25 records above (the link and the collapse this amends); UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2407 (the `names_a_unit` flag retired here); UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2125 (the public label a `formal_name` falls back to).

## 2026-09-25 — A worker's cache path ends in its process id, inside the existing TEST_TOKEN conditional (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1985)

**Decision.** `config/cache.php` and `config/tables.php` key a paratest worker's directory on `<TEST_TOKEN>-<pid>` rather than on the bare token. Both keep the existing `env('TEST_TOKEN')` conditional, so production and serial runs still resolve the shared default. Each worker deletes its own two directories when it exits, guarded by `Tests\Support\WorkerCacheDirectory` so that a serial run's shared default is never touched. `test:seed-stache` still clears the shared `data/` parent. The seed snapshot is unaffected, because workers copy it from `framework/stache-seed`.

**Why.** `TEST_TOKEN` is the worker index, 1..N, so two concurrent bands in one checkout both resolved `data/1`, and one band's cache reset deleted the other's Stache index mid-test. The collision was reproduced before building, as the ticket's decision required. The measurement was taken on Windows 11 Pro 10.0.26200, at `084282e76`, over five pairs of concurrent `--parallel --exclude-group=serial` bands over `tests/Feature/Scopes`, with UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1984's compiled-view patch in place:

| arm | bands failing | the signature |
| --- | --- | --- |
| before | 9 of 10, in all 5 pairs | `UnexpectedValueException` from `FilesystemIterator` on `cache/data/<n>\stache\...` |
| after | 0 of 10 | none; every band `46 passed (68 assertions)`, matching a lone band |

- **The pid rather than paratest's `UNIQUE_TEST_TOKEN`.** This follows `ThrowawayRoot` (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1960): no two live processes share a pid.
- **The exit cleanup.** A warm worker cache is 4,242 files and 21 MB, so without it every band would leave about 250 MB behind until the next seed. Its negative control left one directory with the cleanup disabled and none with it enabled.

**Rejected.**
- *Leave both files on the bare token (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1960's choice).* It was correct on the evidence then, and the measurement above supersedes it.
- *Fix unconditionally without a red run.* The ticket's decision rejected it.
- *Rely on the next seed to clear the per-process directories.* Hand-run bands do not seed, so they would accumulate.

**Companion.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1985 (this record and the build); UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1960 (`ThrowawayRoot`, and the decision this revises); UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1970 (the layered races); UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1984 (the compiled-view layer, patched first).

## 2026-09-25 — The link checker may fetch configured intranet hosts in private-network space; nothing else may (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1768)

**Decision.** `OutboundUrlGuard::fetch()` takes an optional list of **exact host names** that may resolve to private-network space, and **only `ExternalProber` passes one**, read from the new `link_health.intranet_hosts` key (`LINK_HEALTH_INTRANET_HOSTS`, default `inside.uams.edu`). A listed host is admitted in RFC1918 IPv4 or IPv6 ULA (`fc00::/7`) and in nothing else non-public, on its scheme's default port only; it is still pinned to the vetted address, and every redirect hop is judged by its own host. A listed host that never answers is the new `ProbeOutcome::Unverifiable`, never broken or newly broken, and the guard's refusal or the transport error is stored beside the outcome in a new `link_health_occurrences.reason` column. Once a listed host has answered, a later failure is judged like any other link: a failing status is `Broken`, and a redirect that is then refused or dies keeps that outcome.

**Why.** The ticket's premise was that these hosts are unreachable. They are not: `inside.uams.edu` resolves to a private RFC1918 address that is reachable from the campus network, and production runs the scheduled scan on that network. The false "broken" results came from the guard refusing a private address, which is the guard working as designed for every editor-supplied fetch. So the fix is a reachability exception scoped to the one caller whose URLs come from published content and whose job is to reach them, bounded so that a poisoned or misconfigured listed name cannot reach loopback, link-local (cloud metadata), CGNAT or reserved space.

Reachability stays out of `config/first_party.php`. That file records **ownership** — `inside.uams.edu` is first-party through `uams.edu` either way — and merging the two properties would corrupt the classification `FirstPartyUrl` exists to provide.

**Bounds, each tested.** Exact names only: no wildcard, suffix, subdomain or parent match, case-insensitive and ignoring one trailing root dot. A host that is an IP literal never matches, including the obfuscated IPv4 forms (`0x0a.0.0.1`, `167772161`) the guard already normalizes. The scheme's default port only, so content cannot use the link checker to probe other ports on the intranet address. `isAllowedUrl()` takes no list, so no caller's pre-flight is widened, and the guard is `final`. `OutboundUrlGuardCallersTest` parses every file under `app/`, `bootstrap/`, `config/`, `database/` and `routes/` that names the guard, and fails if a second caller passes a list — positionally, as the named argument, or through a spread — or calls the guard in a shape it cannot see into; its classifier is proven on synthetic code carrying each evasion. The config key is read by `ExternalProber` alone.
