**Residue.** A public link that redirects to a listed host is judged by the public host, so off-network its failure reads `unreachable`; production scans on-network, where the hop is reached. `--skip-external` still records every external link, listed ones included, as `refused`, as it did before.

**Rejected.** *Keep the guard strict and mark these hosts permanently unverifiable* — it checks nothing, when production can check them. *Discover the set from guard refusals* — it would absorb genuine outages as "unverifiable", silently. *Provision a separate campus runner* — unnecessary, since production already has the access. *Admit any non-public address for a listed host* — a listed name whose DNS is poisoned toward `169.254.169.254` would then reach cloud metadata; the exception names the address class the intranet actually uses.

**Companion.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1768 (the `## Decision` section and the build); UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1766 (the prober and the `Refused` outcome this extends); #1762 (the epic); the 2026-07-30 *Link health* record above.

## 2026-09-25 — The slowest-class guard judges a share of the band, with an absolute backstop (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2530)

**Decision.** [`scripts/check-slowest-class.php`](../scripts/check-slowest-class.php) fails a band when its costliest class exceeds a **share of the band's summed class time** — **7.5%** for the parallel band, **40%** for the serial band — or an absolute **1500s backstop**. The 700s ceiling UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1890 committed is retired as the primary check. The docblock no longer claims summed class `time` is independent of machine load: a JUnit `time` is wall-clock per test, so it inflates under CPU starvation exactly as band wall time does.

**Why.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2525 red the `pest` job with identical test and assertion counts: its costliest class read 784.9s against a 700s ceiling on a host at a 15-minute load average of 28.9 on 12 cores. A share is dimensionless, so inflation that hits every class alike cancels. Measured before adopting it, on macOS (10 cores) at local-ci parity, four rounds of both bands with one round run beside a second checkout's full parallel band: the parallel share read **5.11 / 5.02 / 5.31 / 5.09%** while the contended round doubled every raw number (summed class time 2834s → 6042s). The 12-core gate host reads 5.82% (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1890) and 5.90% (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2525); no quiet gate-host report with its summed time survives, so its share rests on those two points. 7.5% sits 27% above the worst of those and below 8.33%, the point at which a class binds twelve workers. The serial share is less stable — **19.7 / 17.6 / 24.3 / 19.7%**, because a single process puts a load spike on whichever class is running — so it carries a wider ceiling of its own. The backstop catches the growth a share cannot see, a cost that raises every class together as UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1348's route-count tax did; at 1500s it is 3.6x the worst quiet reading on the gate host (412.2s) and 1.9x the worst contended one.

**Known limit.** A share cancels inflation spread across the band, not a load spike confined to the window in which the costliest class runs. The serial band runs one class in that window, so a spike slowing it about 2.7x passes 40%. In the parallel band the spike also slows the classes beside it, so passing 7.5% from a 5.11% share takes about 2.9x on ten workers and 5.7x on twelve.

**Rejected.** *Raise the ceiling* — it blinds the guard to the straggler growth it exists to catch, and the ceiling is recorded as a deliberate, reviewable number. *Skip the check when the host is oversubscribed* — it goes blind exactly when the host is busy and needs a portable definition of oversubscribed; it was the fallback had the share not held, and the parallel share held. *One share ceiling for both bands* — the serial band's worst reading (24.3%) is four times the parallel band's, so a shared number is either useless for one or flaky for the other. *Guard `slowest ÷ ideal N-way`* (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1890's rejected alternative) — it needs the worker count at check time; a share against a fixed ceiling needs nothing but the report.

**Companion.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2530 (this record and the build); UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1890 and its 2026-08-04 record above (the 700s ceiling this replaces); UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2525 (the band that surfaced it).

## 2026-09-25 — Ontology suppression is editorial and stays on the provider profile; every other surface is deliberately excluded (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2015)

**Decision.** The **Remove Medical Ontology** rules exist for editorial correctness on the provider profile, not for SEO or duplicate-content control, so `hide_medical_ontology` keeps the reach UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1687 gave it: the Areas of Expertise section of `/provider/{slug}` and nothing else. The nine other surfaces that read a provider's `expertise`, `conditions` or `treatments` are **deliberately excluded**, and none changes behavior. With `remove_ontology_criteria` empty, every surface behaves as it did before, because none of them reads the rules.

**Why the purpose decides everything.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2015 asked what the rules are for before anything else, and the other two questions follow from the answer. Under an editorial reading, the profile-only scope UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1687 shipped is coherent: it matches WordPress, which computes `$hide_medical_ontology` on the profile and nowhere else. Under an SEO reading it would be self-defeating, because a provider hidden from one visible list would still be emitted as `knowsAbout` in the page's structured data. The reading is editorial, so the structured data stays complete and accurate.

**Each excluded surface, and why.**

| Surface | Kind | Why it is excluded |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Find-a-Doc card facets (`page_builder/components/_fad_provider_card.antlers.html`) | presentation | A filter facet on a directory card, not the profile's curated expertise list. The editorial rules do not govern it. |
| Area of Expertise routed subpage (`clinical_expertise/section.antlers.html`) | presentation | Lists the providers under an expertise. Hiding one would be a membership rule, not an editorial one about the profile. |
| Area of Expertise overview listing (`clinical_expertise/_related.antlers.html`) | presentation | Renders the stored reverse relation. The same reasoning as the subpage applies. |
| Person JSON-LD `knowsAbout` (`ImpliedOntologyComputedValues`) | structured data | Under an editorial reading, the structured description of a provider's expertise stays accurate and complete. |
| Find-a-Doc block membership (`OntologyOverlapExpertise`) | query membership | Changing it would decide which providers a block returns at all. That is a filtering feature wearing suppression's name. |
| Spotlight mirror (`MirrorSpotlightFeaturedProviderRelationships`) | save-time behavior | Copies relations onto a resources entry when it is saved. Suppression is a render-time decision, and changing stored data is not in its remit. |
| Reverse sync (`StillatRelationshipsServiceProvider`) | stored data | Maps `people.expertise` to `clinical_expertise.clinical_providers` in both directions. It must stay a faithful mirror of the stored relation. |
| `asp_filter` computed (`ProviderPeopleListingComputedValues::computeAspFilter()`) | editor-visible computed | Editors see the provider's full relations. The rules suppress a public rendering, not the record. |
| `knows_about_entries` computed (`PersonKnowsAboutComputer`) | editor-visible computed | The same as `asp_filter`, and it feeds the structured data kept complete above. |

**Rejected.** *Extend suppression to the three presentation surfaces and `knowsAbout`*, with the structural surfaces split into their own ticket. That is the coherent package if the motive is SEO, and the motive is not SEO. *Hold the decision until the editors who configured the rules are asked.* The feature is inert in production until `UAMS-Web/wordpress-importer#601` imports the rules, but the question does not depend on that wait.

**Companion.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2015 (this record); UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1687 and its 2026-08-13 entry (the shipped profile-only implementation); `UAMS-Web/wordpress-importer#601` (imports the rules).

## 2026-09-25 — Test runs keep Comb search indexes in a per-process directory, set by the test bootstrap (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2536)

**Decision.** `tests/CreatesApplication.php` points the `local` search driver at `storage/framework/testing/search/<ThrowawayRoot segment>` for every test process, serial or parallel, and deletes that directory when the process exits. `config/statamic/search.php` is unchanged, so production and the served site keep `storage/statamic/search`. `tests/Feature/Search/SearchIndexIsolationTest.php` saves an entry through Statamic's real `UpdateItemIndexes` subscriber and fails if the shared directory changes.

**Why.** Every entry, term, user and asset save runs that subscriber inline (`QUEUE_CONNECTION=sync`). It reads a Comb index file, merges the item in and writes it back, or rebuilds the whole index when the file is missing. On the default path, every paratest worker, every `--mutate` child and every serial run in a checkout did that to the same files, which are also the checkout's own site index. A `--mutate --parallel` run left `cp.json` as invalid JSON, and a plain `--parallel` band was observed rewriting `cp.json` mid-run. The segment ends in the process id, so no two live processes share a directory.

**Rejected.** *Key the path on `TEST_TOKEN` in `config/statamic/search.php`, like `config/cache.php`.* It isolates paratest workers and mutant children, but serial runs keep writing the served index, so no guard could require that a test run never writes it. *Switch every index to Statamic's `null` driver under test.* It removes the writes, and also changes the driver the index-config tests exercise. *Remove the `UpdateItemIndexes` subscriber under test.* It removes the writes by changing what a save does, which is the behavior tests exist to observe.

**Companion.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2536 (this record and the build); UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1960 and `Tests\Support\ThrowawayRoot` (the per-process segment); UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1985 (the same shape for the file cache).

## 2026-09-25 — The `decision-fork` label is kept after the `hitl` to `afk` flip (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2499)

**Decision.** `decision-fork` stays on an issue when its fork is decided and the issue flips `hitl` → `afk`. It records provenance, meaning the ticket was decided through a fork. The lifecycle is written once, in the [`design-decision-forks`](../.claude/rules/design-decision-forks.md) rule, and the [`writing-issues`](../.claude/skills/writing-issues/SKILL.md) skill points there.

**Why.** Nothing in this repository routes on the label. Measured 2026-09-25 over tracked `*.mjs`, `*.py`, `*.php`, `*.yml` and `*.yaml` files, the label appears in none. The same `grep` over tracked `*.md` files finds it, so the pattern can match and the zero is an absence. `hitl` already marks a decision still waiting on a human. Practice was already to keep the label: UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2444, UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2321 and UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2241 all carry `afk` with `decision-fork`. The work was to write that down.

**Rejected.** *Remove the label on the flip.* It would only repeat what the `hitl` → `afk` change already says, and needs about 30 issues relabeled for no routing benefit. *Match `wordpress-importer`'s exception, which removes the label from a ticket that carried build work alongside its fork.* That repository keeps the label by default too (`UAMS-Web/wordpress-importer#1141`); the exception exists because work there is assigned by reading the label. No script or workflow there reads it. Here a fork ticket carries no build work, so the exception would have nothing to act on. (Corrected 2026-09-30, UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2717: this entry first said that repository removes the label by default because a script there reads it. Neither was true.)

**Companion.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2499 (the ticket and its `## Decision` section); the 2026-07-06 entry above that introduced the `decision-fork` / `action-flavor` split.

## 2026-09-26 — A mutant child drops the run's own `--filter`, by a local patch (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2570)

**Decision.** `pest-plugin-mutate` is patched a fourth time. `MutationTest::start()` now removes `--filter=<pattern>` and `--filter <pattern>` from the arguments each mutant child inherits, and keeps the child's own covering-test `--filter`. A guard test reads the vendored source. No upstream issue is filed yet; that is left to the operator, as it was on UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2524.

**Why.** With the run's `--filter` still present, every child carried two, and PHPUnit exited 1 on `Option --filter cannot be used more than once` even when every test passed. The plugin scores any non-zero exit as a kill, so any `--mutate` run given `--filter` could not report a survivor. Measured on macOS against `App\Widgets\IncompletePublishedEntries`, `DecrementInteger` only, `--parallel`: `15 tested, 100.00%` with `--filter`, against `4 untested, 11 tested` both by file path and with `--filter` once patched. The four survivors include an equivalent mutant no test can kill. This, not parallelism, was the false-kill source UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2570 was filed to find; UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2536's search-index fix stands on its own.

**Rejected.** *Document "never pass `--filter` with `--mutate`".* It costs nothing, and leaves a trap that fails silently with a perfect score, which is exactly how UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2536's measurement was misread. *Merge the two filters into one pattern.* The covering tests were collected from the filtered run, so they already sit inside its selection; merging adds a regex to get wrong and selects nothing different.

**Companion.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2570 (this record and the patch); UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2536 (where the 100% score was first doubted); UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2544 (the survivor list that supplied the control); UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2524 and UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2322 (the plugin's other false-kill patches); [`patches/README.md`](../patches/README.md).

## 2026-09-26 — UAMS Health Talk episodes are synced daily into `clinical_resources/audio`, the feed owns only its own fields, and an unmapped speaker defers the episode (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1803)

**Decision.** A scheduled job, `podcasts:sync-health-talk`, reconciles `clinical_resources` `audio` entries with DoctorPodcasting's public feed **daily**, keyed on the item's `<customid>` (`source_item_id`). It creates new episodes, rewrites changed ones in place, unpublishes withdrawn ones (never deletes them), and re-publishes returned ones. It writes **only the fields the feed supplies**: title, date, show notes, duration, episode number, transcript, the `guest` credit role, the speaker provenance, and one `remote_audio` segment row. Every other field, credit role and segment row belongs to editors and survives every run. Speakers resolve through a **global set with a replicator** (`podcast_speaker_mappings`): a row maps a raw speaker string to a `people` entry or to "no entry expected". A speaker with no row resolves by the item page's `uamshealth.com/provider/<slug>/` link when a `people` entry has that slug. An episode with any unresolved speaker is **not imported at all**, a panel included; the sync adds a row for each unresolved string, and a dashboard widget reports the backlog. An imported entry stores its speaker names and slugs, and every run re-resolves its guests from them, so a completed or changed mapping reaches it without refetching the page; while any of its speakers is unresolved it keeps its previous guests and is never re-published. A missing mapping set aborts the run. Audio is **not re-hosted**: the enclosure URL is stored in a new `remote_audio` segment set and played with a native `<audio>` element.

**Why.** The series is active and DoctorPodcasting expresses a withdrawn clinician only by dropping the item, so a one-shot backfill goes stale in both directions. Daily is enough, since a withdrawal is the only time-sensitive event and the series publishes monthly. Owning only the supplied fields lets editors add what the feed cannot, such as a host or a video rendition, without the next run removing it. Deferring an unmapped episode instead of holding a draft keeps an unpublished entry meaning one thing only, "withdrawn from the feed", so no code path can republish an episode with unresolved credits. A global set needs no custom Control Panel code, and editors maintain it without a deploy. A URL segment set is the only home for vendor audio that does not copy files into an asset container; the enclosure is accepted only under the vendor's own `https://support.doctorpodcasting.com/` prefix, since it is rendered as a player source.

**Coverage, measured 2026-09-25 on the 116-item feed.** 45 episodes have every speaker linked to a provider page and import once those 40 slugs have `people` entries. The other 71 wait on mapping rows for 70 distinct speaker strings: 70 episodes carry no link, and one panel links only one of its two speakers. Against the local 43-entry partial `people` set, 2 episodes import on a first run.

**Rejected.** *`posts/audio`, or a rule routing between the two collections* — the provider lens (#1685) is the main consumer, and a routing rule is a second definition of where an episode lives. *Hourly or weekly cadences* — hourly buys nothing for a monthly series; weekly leaves a departed clinician's episode up for days. *A full mirror* — it erases editor additions on every run. *Seed-then-diverge* — it stops a feed correction reaching an existing episode. *A dedicated Control Panel mapping screen* — more code for what a replicator and a widget already give. *Name matching against `people`* — the speaker strings are dirty (`Tyler Rives, M.D`), and a guessed credit is worse than a deferred one. *Importing with empty credits, or as drafts* — ships incomplete episodes silently, or gives an unpublished entry a second meaning. *Downloading the audio into an asset container* — the ticket puts re-hosting out of scope, and a local file cannot follow the vendor's withdrawal.

**Residual.** An item page is fetched again only when its feed item changes, so a transcript published later does not reach an existing entry; how pages are revisited is open in #2574.

**Companion.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1803 (this record and the build); [`docs/specs/uams-health-talk-podcast.md`](specs/uams-health-talk-podcast.md) (the source, mapping and reconciliation table); #1685 (the provider lens this unblocks); UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1611 (the `audio_series` `subtype` gate); UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1551 and UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1406 (why a mapping save triggers no listener).

## 2026-09-28 — `clinical_specializations`, `geographic_locations` and `patient_types` each live on `default` only, picked across sites (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2605, UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2597, UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2598)

**Decision.** Each of the three taxonomies being converted to structured collections becomes a collection on `default` only, one tree with one entry per code, place or patient type. Every picker that references one sets `select_across_sites: true`, and the `health` Find-a-Doc filters read `patient_types` from `default`. Decided per ticket, all three the same way.

**Why.** Each is a shared vocabulary: a NUCC code, a place or a patient type means the same thing on every site, and every consumer assumes one record per value — the Find-a-Doc provider API's `specialties` (an external contract), the JSON-LD `MedicalCode` and `AdministrativeArea`, `ClinicalTitleResolver`, `Dateline`, the filters' slugs, and the match-only imports (`UAMS-Web/wordpress-importer#336`, `#335`). At `statamic/cms` v6.26.0, `Fieldtypes/Entries.php` lists every site the editor can view when `select_across_sites` is set, and `augment()` resolves a stored id with no site filter, so a `default` entry renders on any site. The flag is already used in `people/person.yaml` and `clinical_locations/location.yaml`.

**Rejected.** *Site-scoped copies, as UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2532 to UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2535 did for tags* — one value would carry a different id per site, and roughly 870 NUCC codes would be copied per site with nothing to vary. *An origin with a localization per site* — one identity, but a localization with nothing to change on every site.

**Residual.** `SitePolicy::view` gates the cross-site listing on `access default site`, which every role in `resources/users/roles.yaml` grants today.

**Companion.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2605 (split from UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2596, which builds it), UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2597, UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2598; UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2604 (the entries code-paste picker).

## 2026-09-28 — The four converted tag collections are not made localizable; each site defines its own list (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2599)

**Decision.** `post_tags`, `logos_item_labels`, `knowledge_base_tags` and `faq_categories` stay as UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2532 to UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2535 built them: site-scoped, `propagate: false`, each site's entries independent origins, `title` and `slug` not localizable. UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2599 closes as not planned.

**Why.** Each site defines its own tag list, which is what those collections already implement. Localization would add only a link from one site's tag to another's, and nothing reads such a link: `PostsInPostTagBySlug`, `PostsInPostTag`, `LogoItemsWithLabels`, `SitemapInclusionComputedsRegistrar`, the layout's tag title and search all resolve a tag within its own site. A localizable `post_tags` slug is not needed for per-site URLs either: `resources/sites.yaml` gives every site its own prefix.

**Rejected.** *Optional localization with `title` and `slug` localizable*, and *with only `title` localizable* — a cross-site link no reader follows, plus an editorial question of which site's tag is the origin. *`propagate: true`* — copies every tag into up to 32 sites.

**Residual.** Reopen with the consumer named if one ever needs a tag across sites.

**Companion.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2599; UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2532 to UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2535; `UAMS-Web/wordpress-importer#1220`, which is unaffected.

## 2026-09-28 — The `sprintf()`-over-interpolation convention is dropped to guidance (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2567)

**Decision.** Double-quoted interpolation is allowed again. Section 5 of the `php-coding-standards` skill and `.cursor/rules/php-string-interpolation.mdc` become guidance: `sprintf()` for format specifiers, casts or several placeholders; interpolation for a simple variable; a heredoc for generated source. Nothing is swept.

**Why.** Its only enforcement is gone: `rector/rector` 2.6.1 leaves `EncapsedStringsToSprintfRector` out of `CodingStyleLevel::RULES` and throws if it is registered, calling it a readability preference. Nothing else enforces it, and at `2aeaa596c` `app/` already held 76 interpolated lines in 31 files beside 322 `sprintf(` calls, with new interpolation still landing.

**Rejected.** *Keep it unenforced* — the measured drift. *Enforce it with an in-repo PHPStan or Rector rule* — this repository's first custom analysis rule, with upkeep, to hold a style its own authors retired.

**Companion.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2567; UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2607 (the document change); UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1031 and UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2540.

## 2026-09-28 — `pages` becomes undated and schedules with `publish_at` / `unpublish_at`, as `people` does (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2564)

**Decision.** `pages.yaml` drops `date: true` and `date_behavior`, so saving a page no longer renames its file. Both pages blueprints replace the sidebar `date` with optional `publish_at` and `unpublish_at` and drop the Scheduled status, and UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2552's `ApplyScheduledPeoplePublishing` job is generalized to publish and unpublish pages too.

**Why.** It gains a scheduled unpublish, which a dated collection cannot express, and gives pages and people one scheduling mechanism. Nothing else reads a page's date: pages order by their tree, the route is `{parent_uri}/{slug}`, and the sitemap reads `updated_at`. JSON-LD `datePublished` falls back to `updated_at`, as accepted for people.

**Rejected.** *Keep `pages` dated and prefix the 82 files* — the smaller change, keeping the WordPress publish date, but with no scheduled unpublish and two scheduling mechanisms.

**Residual.** No importer follow-up: at `UAMS-Web/wordpress-importer` `main` `a74c55b60`, a `future` post schedules through `publish_at` whenever the blueprint has that field (#1233).

**Companion.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2564; UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2552 and PR UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2566.

## 2026-09-28 — The `location` address override splits into street, facility, unit and map, to match Find-a-Doc (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2516)

**Decision.** A master toggle plus four sub-toggles, each governed field resolved per group as parking already is. Decided now against `UAMS-Web/UAMSWP-Find-a-Doc#833`'s shape on `dev` and `staging`; the build is blocked by that ticket so it re-reads the released groups first.

**Why.** One toggle today governs seven fields together, so a child on another floor of the same building can only be stored by copying the parent's street, which drifts. Find-a-Doc's editors already work at this granularity, so one model and a lossless import. Facility is its own group because a parent that is the building answers `facility_query` "No".

**Rejected.** *Keep the single toggle and copy parent values on import* — stores data the source deliberately does not, and drifts. *Wait for the Find-a-Doc release to decide* — the blocked-by edge covers the same risk.

**Companion.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2516; UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2608 (the build); `UAMS-Web/wordpress-importer#1178` (the import).

## 2026-09-28 — CSP-mode field conditions support nested replicators (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2133)

**Decision.** The `AlpineCsp` driver binds a subfield's `x-model` to its innermost bracket group at any replicator depth, and a browser test covers a conditional subfield in a second-level set. Single-level behavior is unchanged.

**Why.** A form author who nests a replicator should get working conditions rather than a silent fall-through, and the importer maps three WordPress repeater shapes to `replicator`.

**Rejected.** *Document the bound instead* — the smaller change; measured demand today is nil (no form blueprint uses a replicator, and no Gravity Forms nested form in the corpus nests again).

**Companion.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2133; UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2059 and UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2056.

## 2026-09-28 — A front-end account gates only per-person content (#2114)

**Decision.** Nothing is behind login at launch outside the LMS; whether the AGEC course is gated here follows #2111. Pages WordPress gated with WP-Members migrate through the mechanisms that already exist: `published`, `private` or `password_protected`, chosen per page by its owner.

**Why.** Password and editor-only visibility already exist without an account (`ProtectEntry`, UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#223, with the importer mapping post passwords). Measured over the exported corpus, published: 61 password-protected items on 35 sites, already covered; 6 WP-Members-gated pages, the only login gating outside the LMS; UserPro `restrict_roles` empty in all 406 rows. Front-end staff accounts without single sign-on for six pages would cost more than the pages need.

**Rejected.** *Carry WP-Members gating onto the `logged_in` scheme.* *Wait for #2111* — only the AGEC course depends on it, and a blocked-by edge carries that.

**Residual.** The importer does not read the WP-Members block setting, so those pages would import public: `UAMS-Web/wordpress-importer#1240` imports them as `private` until their owners decide in #2610.

**Companion.** #2114; #2112 (the accounts epic); #2111.

## 2026-09-28 — Google Business Profile categories: key by GCID, seed the curated list, and report the diff on a schedule (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2091)

**Decision.** A read-only `gcid` field, Google's category id, keys each term. The curated 257-category list editors already use is seeded once. A report-only command diffs a category list against the taxonomy by GCID, never writing terms or `collections`; it ships with a pasted-file source and an overdue flag, and runs monthly against the Google Business Information API once access is obtained.

**Why.** UAMS uses about 257 of Google's 4,044 categories. A full sync measured a cold Stache build about 10 times slower, each term save about 4 times slower and 4,039 more content files, and sits against the Business Profile APIs storage policy. Google renamed at least four curated names in two years, so the key is the GCID. The check runs on a schedule so no one has to remember it, and the API is the sanctioned source.

**Rejected.** *A full sync.* *The report without the seed* — the match-only imports keep flagging. *A scheduled scrape of pleper.com, the third-party mirror the spike evaluated* — its terms forbid commercial exploitation, and its page has no documented format. *A pasted file with a reminder as the permanent process.*

**Residual.** The policy readings are not legal advice, and the API's output has not been seen.

**Companion.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2091 and its spike comment; UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2613, UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2614, UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2615, UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2616, UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2617; `UAMS-Web/wordpress-importer#348` and `#349`.

## 2026-09-28 — The provider lens shows no subtitle when a provider has no clinical specialization, superseding UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1680's fallback (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2600)

**Decision.** On the clinical provider lens, an empty `clinical_title` renders no `<h1>` subtitle. The lens passes `subtitle_only="true"` to the shared identity header, which then skips its `job_title` fallback. This supersedes UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1680's criterion that "a provider with no resolvable `clinical_specializations` falls back to `job_title` rather than rendering an empty subtitle", for the provider lens only. The academic, institutional, researcher and general lenses keep the `job_title` subtitle. The resident override ("Resident Physician") is unchanged.

**Why.** WordPress parity. `single-physicians.php` always printed `<span class="subtitle"><?php echo $provider_occupation_title; ?></span>`, which is empty with no specialization, so WordPress never showed the employment title on a provider page. Under UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1680 a provider such as `/health/provider/c-lowry-barnes/` showed Workday role titles that WordPress did not, on a patient-facing page whose subtitle is meant to be the clinical register.

**Rejected.** *Keep UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1680's fallback.* It shows employment titles WordPress did not show. *Remove the fallback from the shared header.* The other lenses rely on it, since `job_title` is their subtitle. *Resolve a clinical title from something else.* No other field carries the clinical register, and the ticket scopes the change to the template.

**Companion.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2600 (this record and the build); UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1680 (the fallback this supersedes for the provider lens).

## 2026-09-28 — Laravel's parallel compiled-view callbacks are made race-tolerant by a framework patch, not routed around with a run-unique base (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1984)

**Decision.** Both callbacks `Illuminate\Testing\Concerns\TestViews` registers are made race-tolerant in framework code: the setup callback creates the worker's compiled-view directory with a forced `makeDirectory($path, 0755, true, true)` and re-stats it, and the teardown callback catches the `UnexpectedValueException` a directory that vanished under it raises, in the shape UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1853 settled here. The change is carried as `patches/laravel-framework-parallel-compiled-view-race.patch` through `cweagans/composer-patches` (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2442) and offered upstream. The operator decided it on 2026-09-14; this entry was deferred then because the log was contended, and is recorded now.

**Why.** `TestViews` names each worker's directory after `ParallelTesting::token()`, which is the worker index, 1..N, identical across runs. So two concurrent bands in one worktree share `storage/framework/views/test_1`, and both callbacks check then act. Measured on macOS (Darwin 27.0.0), `laravel/framework` v13.24.0, five pairs of `--parallel --exclude-group=serial` bands per arm: unpatched, 5 of 5 pairs aborted with `mkdir(): File exists` before a test ran; patched, 0 of 5 aborted and 10 of 10 bands exited 0. Patching fixes the defect for every consumer of the path and gives upstream a tested fix, where the alternative only moves this repository's runs away from it. `0755` is `ensureDirectoryExists()`'s own default, so no permission changes.

**Rejected.** *A run-unique `view.compiled` base under `APP_ENV=testing`.* It routes around the check-then-act rather than removing it, ships nothing upstream, and gives up cross-run reuse of compiled Blade at a cost that was never measured (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1537 is the open epic on band cost). It is rejected as the resolution, not as a technique: if the upstream fix needs a release cycle, it stays available as a local measure, and would then need that cost measured before it ships.

**Companion.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1984 (this record and the patch's specification); UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2442 (the patch and its guard test, `tests/Feature/LaravelParallelCompiledViewPatchTest.php`); UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2440 (file the defect upstream) and UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2439 (open the upstream pull request), both a human's to perform; UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2441 (update the package and remove the patch); UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1970 (the three layered races, of which this is the second); UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1960 (the same worker-index defect, fixed for fixture roots); [`patches/README.md`](../patches/README.md), which carries the removal checklist.

## 2026-09-29 — The Stache traverser skips a file that vanishes between listing and stat, by a local patch (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2639)

**Decision.** `Traverser::traverse()` skips a file whose mtime read fails **only when a fresh listing of its directory shows the file is gone**, and re-raises every other stat failure, including a failed listing. It ships as its own `statamic/cms` patch, `patches/statamic-stache-traverser-vanished-file.patch`, beside the UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2037 shared-filter patch on the same file. A worker traversing a collection whose entries another worker is removing from the shared `storage/framework/testing/content-mirror` no longer aborts in `traverse()`. That narrows the race rather than closing it: a delete landing after the stat, before `Store::paths()` reads the file, still raises, and so does a whole subdirectory removed before the stat. Both are UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2643.

**Why.** Paratest workers share the content mirror, un-tokenized. A suite that creates and removes entries, such as `ClassifiedsPostingTest` in `classifieds/inside_classifieds`, can delete a file another worker's traverser has just listed. `getMTime()` then raises and the index build fails as a 500 in whatever suite triggered it, with nothing naming the cause; it reached UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2633's validation as a red in `FrontEndAccountFlowTest`. Fixing the failing side covers every victim at once, including suites written later, without relying on any author to remember a rule, and it is also correct in production, where a Control Panel delete can land during an index build. `statamic/cms` has not fixed this: `traverse()` calls `getMTime()` unguarded at `v6.26.0`.

The skip keys on a fresh listing, not on `file_exists()`, because a stat can fail on a file that is still there: if a directory loses search permission after it is listed, every stat inside it fails, and `file_exists()` then reports `false` as well, while a listing of the directory still includes the file (measured on macOS as a non-root user). A `file_exists()` check would have skipped that file silently. The listing tells the two apart, and a listing that itself fails re-raises, so a skip happens only on positive evidence that the file is gone.

It is a separate patch rather than an addition to the shared-filter one because the two change different methods, and each should be retired independently when upstream fixes it.

**Rejected.** *A mirror per worker.* It gives full isolation, but it reworks the path, seed and snapshot machinery every content-backed suite roots at, and copies the 14 MB, 3,234-file `content/` tree to every worker on every run, to close one race. *Confine entry-mutating suites to the `serial` group.* At least 8 parallel-band files save entries into the shared mirror, it lengthens the serial tail, and it depends on every future author remembering. *Move those suites onto `StacheIsolatedTestCase`.* No serial cost, but each suite is rewritten onto fixtures instead of real content, with the same dependence on future authors. *Skip whenever `file_exists()` is false.* Simpler, and it silently drops a present file whose stat fails for any other reason.

**Companion.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2639 (the ticket and its `## Decision` section); UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2643 (the two windows this leaves open); UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2633 (the validation that surfaced it); UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2037 and UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2244 (the shared-filter patch on the same file); UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2241 (the upstream report both patches ride with); [`patches/README.md`](../patches/README.md) (the per-patch detail and removal checklist).

## 2026-09-29 — Pest's exact PHPUnit pin is accepted as intentional, and no relaxation is requested upstream (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1819)

**Decision.** `pestphp/pest` pins `phpunit/phpunit` to one version by requiring and conflicting on the same number (`^13.3.4` and `>13.3.4` at `v5.2.1` and on `5.x` at `8b6607e34`). This repository accepts that as intended: PHPUnit moves when Pest releases, and the dependency sweep takes the newest Pest rather than investigating PHPUnit. No comment is posted on [`pestphp/pest#1679`](https://github.com/pestphp/pest/issues/1679), and no watch row is added for it. The rule is written into the [`update-dependencies-ticket`](../.claude/skills/update-dependencies-ticket/SKILL.md) skill.

**Why.** The maintainer has already answered the question UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1819 planned to ask. Closing [`#1631`](https://github.com/pestphp/pest/pull/1631) on 2026-02-17, he wrote "We depend too much on the internals of phpunit to not locking it", and [`#1750`](https://github.com/pestphp/pest/pull/1750), which removed the conflict, was also closed unmerged. [`#1876`](https://github.com/pestphp/pest/pull/1876), which widened it to `>=13.4.0`, was withdrawn by its author. When a PHPUnit patch has mattered, Pest shipped a release that allows it ([`#1617`](https://github.com/pestphp/pest/issues/1617), fixed in `v4.3.2`). A third request would ask a settled question, and restating the downstream cost adds nothing the maintainer has not already weighed. PHPUnit is a dev dependency, so the exposure is bounded.

**Rejected.** *A comment on UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1679* asking for a narrower conflict or for the reason behind it: both are already answered. *A watch row on UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1679 with a `composer` key for `pestphp/pest`*: Pest releases about twice a week, so it would notify on nearly every release, which is the reason the `1992-pestphp-pest-1820.json` row carries no such key. *A row without the key*: the question is settled, and the row would keep UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1819 open only to report a close that changes nothing here. *A fork, a VCS repository entry or an inline alias*: rejected in UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1819 itself as worse than the pin.

**Companion.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1819 (the ticket, closed with this outcome); [`pestphp/pest#1679`](https://github.com/pestphp/pest/issues/1679) (the upstream report, left open and untouched).

## 2026-09-29 — Each upstream watch row is its own file, not an entry in one JSON string (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2673)

**Decision.** `watch-upstream.yml` builds its `WATCHES` list from `.github/upstream-watches/*.json`, one file per row named `<tracker>-<owner>-<repo>-<number>.json`, through [`scripts/load-upstream-watches.mjs`](../scripts/load-upstream-watches.mjs). Each file holds the object a row held before, and each row's reasoning moves from comments in the workflow to that directory's `README.md`. The loader fails the run when there are no row files or any file is malformed, has an unknown key, or does not match its own name, and when the last row retires the workflow is disabled rather than left to run with nothing.

**Why.** Every row lived on one line, and each upstream series adds its row in its own pull request from `main`, so any two of those pull requests conflicted by construction. After UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2666 merged, UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2668 and UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2669 each needed a hand-back, a sync and a full local CI run, and UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2672 was next. A resolution that keeps one side drops the other side's rows, and a dropped row fails quietly because nothing ever fires for an upstream nobody is watching. With one file per row, two branches that each add a row merge cleanly. This reverses the upstream-patch-lifecycle skill's earlier "an empty `WATCHES` is a valid resting state": an empty directory is indistinguishable from a wrong path, so it now fails loudly.

**Rejected.** *A multi-line YAML or JSON list in the workflow.* Adjacent additions to a list still conflict at its end, and a list needs a union merge driver to compose, which this file never had. *Batching a wave's rows into one pull request.* It removes the collision only within a wave, and ties each row to whatever else the batch waits on.

**Companion.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2673 (the ticket); UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2666, UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2668, UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2669 and UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2672 (the pull requests that edited the one-line value); [`.github/upstream-watches/README.md`](../.github/upstream-watches/README.md) (how to add or retire a row).

## 2026-09-29 — Redirects for domains that are not Statamic sites are authored in one host-carrying entries collection (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2594)

**Decision.** Redirects for domains that are not Statamic sites are authored in one shared, searchable "redirect domains" entries collection, and each entry carries a host. The 40 domain-level redirect domains of `UAMS-Web/wordpress-importer#778` and `dining.uams.edu` (#1248) are whole-domain entries: a host, a target, and a "whole domain" toggle with no source path, which the generator emits as a single `/*` rule. A target can be an entry as well as a URL. The vanity domains of #1936 and #1937 are ordinary entries, with a host, a source, a target and the rule options the `redirects` global set already has. `redirects:edge-manifest` reads the collection as a second input and emits one manifest per host, without merging the main site's rules beneath it. The main site's own redirects stay in the `redirects` global set, unchanged, and `RedirectRuleMatcher` and the `find_redirect_rule` serving path are untouched, because these domains are served at the edge and never reach Statamic.

**Why.** The generator reads rules one Statamic site at a time and none of these domains is a Statamic site, so nothing stored their rules where it could read them. Every destination of the 40 is migrating, so a redirect nobody can edit in the Control Panel goes stale as soon as its target moves, and an entry target lets a domain follow its destination instead. The vanity domains hold 1,110 published rules between them, 654 of them on `uams.health`, and about 1,150 rules in a replicator list have no search, no filter and no paging, whether split per site or shared. A collection lists, searches and filters them by host, which makes one surface workable for all 45 domains, and a whole-domain entry keeps the 40 from needing a source path nobody should edit. Each of the 40 already fits UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1935's static-safe subset as one `/*` rule with a fixed absolute target, so the generator can express them; only where they live was missing. Whether the generator resolves an entry target to its current URL is unverified and belongs to the build.

**Rejected.** *A shared site whose global-set rules carry a host* (this ticket's first decision, earlier the same day): it works for the 40 alone, but puts the vanity domains' 1,110 rules into one unsearchable list, and building it beside the collection would be two mechanisms doing one job. *A Statamic site per domain*: five vanity sites and 41 one-rule sites, which fits the per-site generator but still leaves `uams.health`'s 654 rules in one unsearchable list; UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#133's reason against per-domain sites was per-request boot cost, which the edge removes, so this is rejected on editing, not cost. *A versioned data file*: the cheapest build, but pointing a domain somewhere new becomes a developer's code change and entry targets are impossible, which leaves the staleness the ticket was filed about. *Authoring at the edge provider*: as in UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#133, no editorial UI and a new importer target.

**Companion.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2594 (the ticket and its `## Decision`, including the per-domain rule counts); UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2678 (the collection and the per-host manifest build) and #2679 (moving the 40 onto the edge), both blocked by UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2594; #1248 (`dining.uams.edu`, folded in as a whole-domain entry); `UAMS-Web/wordpress-importer#778` (the 40-row inventory and the hosting decision); UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#133 and UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1935 (the edge-generator model and its static-safe subset).

## 2026-09-29 — Development moves to a fresh public repository cut from a clean `main`, and this repository becomes the private archive (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2651)

**Decision.** Before going public, a fresh public repository is started from a clean `main` and development moves there. This repository keeps its full history unchanged and becomes the private archive. The move waits until every open `security` issue is completed; then the open issues move to the new repository, and closed issues stay here beside the commits and pull requests they cite. Every moved issue's references are rewritten as part of the move, because a transferred issue gets a new number and a bare `#N` would resolve against the new repository's numbering.

**Why.** Commit `d7c054401` and later export commits put WordPress exports in history: that one commit's files carry 766 distinct `wp:author_email` values and 15,645 items with a `wp:status` of draft, pending or private. They are not on `main`, but a public repository publishes its history. The new repository never contains those commits, so the data is removed by construction and nothing is needed from GitHub. Keeping this repository unchanged means every SHA cited in `.claude/` and `docs/`, about 83 of them, still resolves somewhere real, and the switch is reversible.

**Rejected.** *Rewriting history in place*: every SHA changes and the cited ones stop resolving, and it is not sufficient alone, since GitHub keeps a read-only reference to every pull request's commits (942 here) until GitHub Support purges them. *A public mirror of a clean `main`, with this repository staying the working one*: ruled out by the operator. *Accepting the exposure*: it publishes 766 distinct author emails and 15,645 draft, pending or private items.

**Companion.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2651 (the ticket and its `## Decision`); UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2658 (the cut), #2659 (the move), #2660 (the reference rewrite) and #2661 (the transfer); UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2652 (the review of the open issues that move).

## 2026-09-29 — All four submodules stay private, and a public clone gets their pointers only (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2654)

**Decision.** `content`, `users`, `public/assets` and `app/private/assets` all stay private after the switch. A public clone gets the four pointers and none of the contents.

**Why.** A clone without `content/` already runs the suite: `tests/Support/ContentSubmodule.php` skips a content-dependent test when `content/` is absent (the bare-worktree path UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#856 and UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#900 built), and 87 of the 901 test files reference it, so those cases skip rather than fail. `users` holds the login accounts and `app/private/assets` is private by name, so neither could simply be published with the parent, and nothing more is needed for a usable public clone. Revisit this if outside contributors appear after the switch.

**Rejected.** *Also publishing `content` and `public/assets`*: outsiders could run every test, but it exposes `content`'s 8 unpublished entries and both histories, including anything ever removed, and each module would need its own secret scan, tracker review, license and approval. *Replacing `content` with a sample tree*: outsiders could run every test, but it adds a second content tree to build and keep in step with the blueprints, for readers who may not arrive.

**Companion.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2654 (the ticket and its `## Decision`); UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2653 (the review of what the repository publishes, which carries the public `README.md` note that content-dependent tests skip without the private `content` submodule).

## 2026-09-30 — A Stache file deleted after its stat is skipped at the read too; a vanished subdirectory is accepted as residual (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2643)

**Decision.** Six more local `statamic/cms` patches extend UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2639's rule past the stat, to every Stache read of an already-recorded path. `Store::paths()`, `Store::getItemFromModifiedPath()`, `TaxonomyTermsStore::paths()`, `TaxonomyTermsStore::getItem()` and `BasicStore::getItem()` each treat a file that reads as null as gone when a fresh listing of its directory succeeds and no longer contains it: the index builds skip it, and the `getItem()` reads return null, as `BasicStore` already does for a key with no path. A sixth makes `TaxonomyTermsStore::getItemFromModifiedPath()` null-safe, since it delegates to the patched parent. `CollectionEntriesStore`'s unique-path probe needs no patch: it reads each candidate path and takes the first that reads as null, so a file deleted before that read is genuinely free and is reused, which a test pins. A whole subdirectory removed before the stat stays re-raised, exactly as UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2639 leaves it, and is accepted as residual. Decided by the operator on 2026-09-30.

**Why.** The read after the stat does not raise, which the ticket assumed. `Statamic\Facades\File::get()` returns null for a missing file, and `makeItemFromFile()` then parses that to an empty array, generates an id and, for an entry, writes the file back. Probed in an isolated Stache on macOS: a saved entry's file deleted before the read came back at the same path under a new id, with no title. So the window resurrects a deleted entry as an empty one, in the shared content mirror and, in the same race, in production, when a Control Panel delete lands during an index build. The skip keys on a listing, not on `file_exists()`, for UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2639's reason: `file_exists()` reports false for a present file whose directory lost search permission. Neither window had been seen in a gate run: searched GitHub bodies and comments for both error signatures since UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2644 merged, across 21 merged pull requests, with the bounds recorded on UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2643.

**Rejected.** *Accepting the read-after-stat window as residual.* It is the wider of the two, it is the same race that reached UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2633, and it corrupts content rather than failing loudly. *Keying the skip on `file_exists()`.* It would skip a present file the directory's permissions hide. *Tolerating a vanished subdirectory on evidence that an ancestor is gone.* Unobserved, and it loosens UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2639's rule that only a successful listing licenses a skip.

**Residual.** The gap inside `File::get()` itself: a delete landing between its `isFile()` check and its `file_get_contents()` raises an uncaught `ErrorException`, not a null. `RevisionsStore::makeItemFromFile()` ignores the contents it is passed and reads the file again. Both were outside the operator's scope decision. The operator decided on 2026-09-30 to include the four sites first listed here as residual.

**Companion.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2643 (the ticket and its `## Decision` section); UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2639 and this log's 2026-09-29 entry (the traverser patch whose rule this follows); UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2241 (the upstream report the Stache patches ride with); [`patches/README.md`](../patches/README.md) (the per-patch detail and removal checklist).

## 2026-09-30 — A scheduled publish or unpublish carries to an entry's localizations, whatever status an editor gave them (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2621)

**Decision.** `ApplyScheduledPublishing` applies an origin's due schedule to its localizations. A due unpublish takes down every live localization, and a due publish puts every localization that is not yet live live. A localization an editor set differently from its origin is not treated as an exception: it follows the origin. Only a localization's own `published` flag is written, because `status_and_visibility` is shared with the origin.

**Why.** `publish_at` and `unpublish_at` are `localizable: false`, so a schedule belongs to the entry as a whole, and before UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2552 and UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2564 made these collections undated a localization inherited its origin's `date` and went live and came down with it. The job cannot tell a localization an editor deliberately held back from one that simply had not gone live yet, since both read as a draft, so any exception would rest on intent the data does not record. The publish half already held on `main`: saving the origin re-saves its direct descendants, and `SyncEntryPublishedStatus` maps the shared status onto each one's `published` flag. The unpublish half did not, because that listener keeps a live entry live when it reads a `draft` it takes for a Control Panel default, so the job now takes each live localization down itself.

**Rejected.** *Cascading only an unpublish.* A scheduled launch would leave every localized copy offline until an editor published each one. *Moving only localizations whose status matched the origin's before the transition.* It needs more code and gives the same result as the chosen rule in every case the job can observe.

**Companion.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2621 (the ticket and its `## Decision`); UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2552 and UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2564 (the job and the two collections it schedules).

## 2026-09-30 — The Boost 2.10.0 guideline lines naming uninstalled skills are suppressed, and the strict test rule is kept, through `.ai/guidelines/` overrides (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2708)

**Decision.** Two project overrides in `.ai/guidelines/` replace two of `laravel/boost` 2.10.0's guideline templates. `deployments/core.blade.php` keeps the vendor's Laravel Cloud line and drops the line telling agents to activate the `deploying-to-cloud` skill. `enforce-tests.blade.php` restores the 2.5.0 Test Enforcement wording, "every change must be programmatically tested", which also drops the line telling agents to read the `testing-best-practices` skill. The generated block in `AGENTS.md` is otherwise 2.10.0's, and the resolution ships in the same pull request as the regeneration (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2709), so no session loads the unresolved block.

**Why.** At 2.10.0 the generated block points agents at two skills that ship inside the package but are not installed here: `boost.json` lists neither, and `--ignore-skills` (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2483) writes no skill directories. An instruction to read a skill that cannot be loaded is worse than no instruction. `boost.json` sets `"cloud": false`, so the deployment line also describes a platform this repository does not deploy to. The relaxed test wording would exempt copy, styling and layout-only changes from testing in guidance every session loads, and the operator kept the stricter rule. `.ai/guidelines/` is the sanctioned override surface. A file there with the same path as a vendor template replaces that template whole, so each override carries over the vendor lines it keeps; a file with a new name would add a section beside the vendor one instead (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2255).

**Rejected.** *Installing the two skills*: it adds a Laravel Cloud procedure for a platform not in use, and a second testing skill beside `pest-testing`, to keep in step for guidance nobody asked for. *Accepting the block as generated*: it leaves both dangling references and the relaxed test rule in place. *Editing the generated block by hand*: the next `boost:update` overwrites it, and UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2670 keeps `.ai/guidelines/` as the place to change it. *Resolving it in a later pull request*: UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2709 would merge the unresolved block first, and every session would load it in the meantime.

**Companion.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2708 (the ticket and its `## Decision`); UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2670 and UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2709 (the regeneration at 2.10.0); UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2483 (`--ignore-skills`); UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2539 (guidelines written to `AGENTS.md`); UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2255 (how a same-named override replaces a vendor template).

## 2026-10-01 — Staff email addresses leave form configuration and test fixtures before the repository goes public (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2740)

**Decision.** Form notification recipients move from each form's `to:` in `resources/forms/` to environment configuration: a form names a configuration key, and each environment sets the value. How the values are set and changed must be documented clearly, ideally in the control panel where an editor sees the form, not only in `docs/`. Tests read the seed-account names from the private `users` submodule's `.gitignore` instead of writing the addresses out. If that proves unworkable, the reason is recorded on UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2740 and the names stay as they are.

**Why.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2651 starts a public repository from a clean `main`, and six form configs and several test fixtures name individual staff members' `@uams.edu` addresses. A form recipient written into YAML also makes a role change into a code change. Statamic already resolves `config:` references in a form email's configuration (`Statamic\Forms\Email::parseConfig()`), so environment configuration needs no custom mechanism. Reading the seed-account names from `users/.gitignore` keeps the tests' strength without publishing the names or renaming anyone's account.

**Rejected.** *Leaving the recipients in the form YAML*: it publishes individual staff addresses and keeps role changes as code changes. *Routing to role mailboxes*, rejected for now: it depends on each office creating or naming a mailbox outside this repository, and environment configuration does not rule it out later. *Renaming the seed accounts*: it changes the private submodule, the tests and provisioning together, and changes those people's sign-in names. *Keeping the seed-account names in the tests* is the fallback, not the first choice, since it publishes two addresses as file names.

**Companion.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2740 (the ticket and its `## Decision`, decided by the operator 2026-10-01); UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2745 (form recipients), UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2746 (seed-account names) and UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2747 (a media-contacts test that wrote a real person's details), each blocked by UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2740; UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2653 and UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2651 (the go-public review and the public repository).

## 2026-10-01 — A side-by-side paragraph with a button gets its own layout and limit, not a lower shared limit (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2759)

**Decision.** The Side-by-Side Image and Text block's call-to-action button text is limited to one line, 42 characters. Paragraph only keeps its 449-character paragraph and no longer shows the button; a new Paragraph and button layout (`paragraph_and_button`) carries its own paragraph field, `body_with_button`, limited to 359 characters, with the button. Existing Paragraph only blocks that carry a button move to the new layout. Limits come from UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2757's line-budget method: the heading at 2 lines, the button at 1, and the paragraph the whole lines left, measured at every width from 992 to 1452 px at a viewport height of 488, each limit the smallest of the ten Blind Text Generator sample texts. Paragraph only measures 10 lines (451 characters, agreeing with its existing 449); with a button the paragraph gets 8 lines (359).

**Why.** At its existing limits, Paragraph only with a 42-character button overflows its text box by up to 28 px at 179 of the 461 widths. A character limit cannot depend on whether the button is filled in, and `body_body_only` is shared by Paragraph only and Paragraph and simple list, so one lower limit would take two lines from every block without a button. A separate layout keeps both shapes inside the box, which is how UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2757 handled the blockquote and its button.

**Rejected.** *Lowering `body_body_only` to 359*: it fits, but costs every block without a button two lines and changes Paragraph and simple list too. *Keeping 449 and recording the overflow*: it leaves a block that editors can push out of its box with in-limit values.

**Companion.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2759 (the ticket and its `## Decision`, decided by the operator 2026-10-01); `UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-content#68` (moves the six existing blocks with a button); UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2757 (the line-budget method); UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2766 (the link-list layouts overflow at their own limits) and UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2767 (the simple-list layouts have no list limits), the two residuals the measurement surfaced; UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2761 (Control Panel visibility of the block's layout conditions).

## 2026-10-01 — A ticket whose work is posting to a third-party tracker is `afk`, and a human approves the post (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2772)

**Decision.** A ticket whose work is posting something on a third-party tracker (an upstream issue, a pull request, a comment, an upvote or a feature request) is `afk`, not `hitl` with `action-flavor`. An agent prepares the item, and a human approves the post. Decided by the operator on 2026-10-01. Posting to a third-party tracker is not, on its own, a reason for `hitl`; a real-world action such as a deploy or access still is.

**Why.** Labelling these tickets `hitl` treats the whole ticket as human work, when only the last step is. Researching the upstream tracker, checking for an existing report, drafting the body and preparing the branch need no human, but under `hitl` that preparation waited for a person along with the post, so the ticket was never picked up as buildable work. Posting under a maintainer-facing identity still needs a human's approval, so that boundary stays, as one step inside an `afk` ticket. What counts as approval to post stays a human decision; this changes only the label.

**Rejected.** *Keeping these tickets `hitl` with `action-flavor`*, the classification this replaces, for the reason above. No other alternative was recorded.

**Companion.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2772 (the ticket and its `## Decision`); UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2777 (the change to the `upstream-patch-lifecycle` and `writing-issues` skills, and the `action-flavor` label's description); UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2778 (this entry). The open tickets relabelled the same day are listed on UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2772.

## 2026-10-02 — `subject_matter` becomes a structured collection on `default`, keeping one parent per subject (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2822)

**Decision.** The `subject_matter` taxonomy becomes a structured collection on `default` only, one tree with one entry per subject, and every picker sets `select_across_sites: true`, as for `geographic_locations` and `clinical_specializations`. The term `parent` field allowed more than one value; the tree keeps one, so the two subjects with two stored parents keep the first: `health-sciences` under `applied-science`, `urogynecology` under `urology`. Decided by the operator.

**Why.** A subject means the same thing on every site, and nothing reads `parent`: no PHP under `app/` and no content template, so the second values carried no behavior. A tree is how every other converted vocabulary holds its hierarchy.

**Rejected.** *A separate "related broader term" field for the second parent*: worth having only if the second value were used. *No tree, with `parent` kept as a many-entries field*: unlike every other converted vocabulary. *Per-site copies* (a subject would carry a different id on each site) and *an origin with a localization per site* (nothing to vary), as in the 2026-09-28 entry.

**Companion.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2822 (its `## Decision`); UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2597, UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2596, UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2605 (the conversions and scoping it follows); UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2808 (writes the approved `credentials` values as entry references once this lands).

## 2026-10-02 — Form configurations live in a private submodule at `resources/forms` (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2818)

**Decision.** The form configurations in `resources/forms/` move into the private `UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-forms` repository, checked out as a submodule at that path, so an editor can set a real notification address on the form in the Control Panel. This supersedes the recipients half of the 2026-10-01 decision (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2740), which UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2745 built: the rule that form YAML must not name an individual address is withdrawn. The existing `config:forms.recipients.*` keys keep working until an editor replaces each with an address; removing them is a follow-up. The field definitions in `resources/blueprints/forms/` stay in this repository.

**Why.** Form configurations are content an editor maintains, like the entries in the private `content` submodule: recipients, subjects and templates change with staff and offices, not with the code. Holding them privately keeps addresses out of the public repository while letting a staff change be a Control Panel edit committed to the private repository, instead of a server-settings change in each environment. Statamic's Git Automation already tracks `resource_path('forms')` and commits each tracked path to the repository it is in.

**Rejected.** *Environment configuration* (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2740, UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2745): it keeps addresses out of the public repository, but an editor cannot change a recipient, and every change is a server-settings change in each environment. *A recipients global in the `content` submodule*: it avoids a new repository, but depends on form email settings reading a global value, which was unverified, and puts each form's recipients on a separate screen from the form.

**Companion.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2818 (the ticket and its `## Decision`, decided 2026-10-02); [`docs/forms/notification-recipients.md`](forms/notification-recipients.md); UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2819 (the repository and its access); #2815 (when production commits and pushes).

## 2026-10-02 — The application answers what the redirect edge does not, and the production web server is the edge (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2811)

**Decision.** Two decisions, both for the hosts whose rules live in the `redirect_domains` collection. **First:** on the vanity hosts (#1936, #1937), every request the edge manifest sends to the origin is answered by the application, from the same `redirect_domains` entries and through the existing engine. That covers regex rules, every rule listed after the first regex, windowed rules, entry targets that could not be resolved, and unmatched paths. It is UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1935's fallback model applied to the hosts UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1935 left out, so no rule needs rewriting and every WordPress install can retire as soon as its host moves. It reverses UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2594's "these domains never reach Statamic" for those requests only. Built in UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2813. **Second:** the production web server is the edge, and its own redirect configuration is the format. The manifest is rendered into the configuration of whichever web server #2794 chooses, keeping the manifest's first-match order and passing everything else to the application on the same server. Built in #2814, blocked by #2794. This answers the output-target choice UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1935 deferred until there was something to deploy to.

**Why.** Every manifest assumes an origin: a request that matches nothing, and any rule the edge cannot reproduce, goes to it. Once the WordPress installs are retired, the vanity hosts would have none. The 40 domain-level hosts are unaffected, because a whole-domain entry is one `/*` rule that matches every path. With the application as the origin, the edge needs a server that can pass a request on, which rules out a static host. Between the web server and a managed edge, only the web server keeps the manifest's first-match order by construction, without a new account or moving 44 domains' nameservers.

**Rejected.** For the first decision: *no origin, with the edge answering everything*, which needs every vanity domain's regex rules rewritten or dropped before its host moves, against counts nobody has yet; and *keeping each WordPress install as the origin until its rules are rewritten*, which defers the same work and delays the installs' retirement. For the second: *Cloudflare Bulk Redirects*, whose most-specific-wins matching contradicts the manifest's first-match contract and which would need each apex domain's nameservers moved to Cloudflare; and *a static `_redirects` host*, which cannot pass a request on to the application.

**Revisit** the second decision if #2793 chooses a host that puts its own CDN in front of the application.

**Companion.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2811 (the ticket, its `## Decision` and both comparison tables); UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2813 and #2814 (the builds); #1936, #1937 and #2679 (the hosts, updated to name the edge and format); #2793 and #2794 (application hosting and its web server); UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1935 and UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2594 (the static-safe subset and the collection these rules live in).

## 2026-10-04 — Keep this repository's own local CI runner (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2835)

**Decision.** Keep [`scripts/local-ci.mjs`](../scripts/local-ci.mjs) as the gate. Do not move to the shared runner shipped by `UAMS-Web/uams-claude-skills#11` (`local-ci.json` + synced `.claude/skills/local-ci/`). The sync manifest continues to exclude `skills/local-ci` for this repository.

**Why.** The shared runner runs in place from a per-repository job list and posts a pull-request comment. This repository's runner still needs worktree-slot checkout, runner handoff, a single-flight lock, private submodule init, parallel Pest with a serial tail, the mutation-tooling smoke, optional commit status, and the Mode A watcher / merge pipeline. Absorbing those into the shared runner for every private target would enlarge that tool beyond the UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#11 brief; dropping them here would lose the gate this repository actually runs.

**Rejected.** *Moving to the shared runner now*: it lacks the worktree and Mode A capabilities above. *Running both side by side*: two job lists and two skills for one gate, with no gain while the repository-specific capabilities remain required.

**Companion.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2835 (the ticket and its `## Decision`); `UAMS-Web/uams-claude-skills#11` and `shared/merge-notes/local-ci.md` in that repository.

## 2026-10-05 — An expired classified listing's account holder gets one notice, matching the WordPress site (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2523)

**Decision.** When the UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2520 job unpublishes an expired `classifieds` listing that has an `account`, it sends that account's email address one plain-text notice, `App\Mail\Classifieds\ClassifiedListingExpired`. The notice gives the listing's title and says the listing has expired and is no longer shown on the site. It is sent on the day the listing is unpublished. There is no advance reminder, and the notice carries no link to the listing, no renewal path and no link to the posting form. A listing with no `account` sends nothing, and `contact_email` is never a recipient. Each notified listing records the expiration date it was notified for in `expiry_notice_sent_for`, in the same save that unpublishes it, so a listing republished with the same date is not notified twice. A listing given a new expiration date is notified again when that date passes.

**Why.** It matches what the WordPress classifieds site sends today. That site has WP Adverts' Emails module enabled, with its "Your Ad has expired." template turned on (checked 2026-10-02). In WP Adverts 2.3.4 that template fires once, on the move from published to expired. The plugin sends no reminder and offers no renewal. The recipient deliberately differs from the plugin, which writes to the free-text contact address: the account's address is the one its holder signed up with (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2519), not text typed into each listing. Registration does not confirm that address, so it is the holder's own claim rather than a proven one; whether accounts should require confirmation is a question for UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2519's account model, not for this notice.

**Rejected.** *An advance reminder before expiry, and a renewal path:* the current site has neither. *Mailing `contact_email`:* unverified free text, and on the first run it would reach about 189 strangers about long-expired imported listings, as UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2520 measured. *Resending after a failed send:* the notice is at most once per date, so a failed send is logged with the listing's id, and the run moves on to the next listing.

**Companion.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2523 (this decision); UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2520 (the expiry job and the decision not to notify); UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2519 (accounts); UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2096 (the board).

## 2026-10-05: The organization rulesets on `main` have no bypass list (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2016)

**Decision.** `main` is governed by two organization rulesets, `main: no deletion or force push` (rules `deletion` and `non_fast_forward`) and `main: pull request required` (rule `pull_request`), and **neither has a bypass list**. Every change to `main` arrives through a pull request, administrators included, and a direct push is refused. The rulesets target `uams-claude-skills`, `uams-statamic`, `uamswp-migration-api` and `wordpress-importer`, so the decision covers all four. They replaced classic branch protection on 2026-10-05; no ruleset change was needed, since the setting in place is the one chosen.

**Why.** An empty bypass list is the only setting under which the pull-request rule binds the accounts that perform merges. The rule requires a pull request, not a status check or an approval, so skipped remote CI cannot deadlock merges, and the risk that led UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1843 to leave `enforce_admins` off does not arise.

**Rejected.** *Organization administrators bypass always*: the rule would bind everyone except the accounts that perform every merge, a gate in appearance only. *Administrators bypass for pull requests only*: with no required approval or status check there is nothing in a pull request for the bypass to skip, so it adds an exception with no use. *A bypass entry for an app or process that pushes to `main`*: none is known, and none was found in this repository's tracked scripts, workflows or skills (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2854); a process found pushing directly moves to a pull request, and a bypass for one is a new decision.

**Supersedes** the 2026-08-13 entry for UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1843, which measured classic protection.

**Companion.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2016 (the decision, with both bypass lists read empty in the organization's settings), [`docs/runbooks/branch-protection.md`](runbooks/branch-protection.md) (what each ruleset enforces, how to read them back, and recovery), UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2854 (this record).

## 2026-10-06 — news.uams.edu duplicate posts: the canonical URL drops the collision suffix, and the text comes from the fullest copy (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1639, #798)

**Decision.** A duplicate set keeps one post. Its URL is the set's hyphenated slug with any `-N` suffix removed, but only when the slug without the suffix is a member of the set or no live post holds it.
