Its text comes from the most complete member: word counts within 2% are treated as equal, then the clean-slug member wins, then the earliest. Its publish date comes from the same member as the text. When the fullest member is a `-2`, that post is kept and given the clean slug, and the post that held the clean slug is dropped. Every dropped URL gets a 301 to the kept post. The rule covers #798's 248 automatic sets and the 45 sets reviewed in UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1639. In UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1639, 35 are duplicates, two of them split by year, and 10 are distinct stories kept whole: yearly SeniorNet class notices, separate grant rounds, and different physicians' profiles.

**Why.** WordPress adds `-2`, `-3` to a slug only to avoid a collision, so the suffix is an accident and not part of the story's address. Taking the URL and the text from different members keeps the clean address and still keeps the best copy, which was often a `-2`. The evidence condition on stripping the suffix protects slugs that really end in a number, such as `covid-19`. Text similarity alone does not decide a set: a re-post can be 80% alike and still be the same story, and yearly notices can be near-identical and still be distinct. So the 45 sets with differing content were confirmed one at a time.

**Rejected.** *The earlier largest-member rule*: the fullest post became canonical with its own slug, so a `-2` could become the permanent URL. *Stripping any trailing `-N`*: it would rename slugs whose number is part of the title. *Keeping the clean-slug post's own text even when it is shorter or empty*: it publishes the weaker copy, and in some sets an empty one. *Deciding the 45 differing sets by a similarity threshold*: no threshold separates re-posts from yearly notices.

**Companion.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1639 (the per-set `## Decision`, decided by the operator 2026-10-06); #798 (`## The canonical rule`, and the cleaner that drops the 248 automatic sets); #2810 (applies UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1639's decisions); `UAMS-Web/wordpress-importer#1305` (the same disposition on the migration-API path); UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1641 (the redirect generator).

## 2026-10-06 — The Workday identifier instructions keep the shared text and append this repository's uniqueness sentence (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2469)

**Decision.** The four Workday identifier instructions in `resources/fieldsets/uamsweb_common_identifiers.yaml` keep the text agreed with `UAMSWP-People-Showcase#56` and `UAMSWP-Find-a-Doc#819` unchanged, and this repository appends one sentence of its own after it, identical on all four: "A Workday ID must not be used by any other entry in the same collection, or in another of this person's Workday ID fields. Values are compared ignoring capitalization and surrounding spaces." The agreed part is shared by the three repositories character for character, and each may append what is true only there. This replaces the UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2152 entry's routing of the Workday semantics to the validation message alone; the messages still state them too.

**Why.** The uniqueness rule is enforced only here (`app/Rules/UniqueIdentifier.php`, pooled across the four Workday fields), and no equivalent check was found in Find-a-Doc, so the sentence is true only in this repository. Appending it keeps the agreed part comparable with the upstream copies. `WorkdayIdentifierFieldsTest` still compares the whole instruction exactly, the agreed text followed by the sentence, because that comparison is also how it proves the fieldset import resolved.

**Rejected.** *The same sentence in all three repositories*: it would state a rule the WordPress plugins do not enforce, and would need a change through each plugin's promotion flow. *Leaving the instructions as they were*: editors would keep learning the rule only when a save is refused.

**Companion.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2469 (the ticket and its `## Decision`, decided 2026-10-06 by the author of both upstream tickets); UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2152 (the wiring, and the earlier routing to the validation message); UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2221 (the comparison semantics).

## 2026-10-06 — A manual-run-only job proves the submodule token, outside the gate chain (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2497)

**Decision.** `.github/workflows/ci.yml` carries a `submodule-access` job that runs only on `workflow_dispatch`, is not gated on `RUN_REMOTE_CI`, needs no job and is needed by none. It runs the pest job's submodule checkout step unchanged, so a manual run on `main` shows whether `secrets.COMPOSER_AUTH_TOKEN` can clone `content`, `users` and `resources/forms` while remote CI stays off. It has no counterpart in `scripts/local-ci.mjs`, because the secret exists only on GitHub. It is the first job outside the strict sequential chain recorded on 2026-07-15, and the chain parity test names it in an explicit `REMOTE_ONLY` list rather than relaxing that rule.

**Why.** A manual run otherwise does nothing while `RUN_REMOTE_CI` is `false`: every job is gated on it, and the pest job also needs phpstan. A job that runs only the checkout is cheap and can be re-run whenever a private submodule is added. The token is fine-grained, so each new repository has to be granted on its settings page before the first run that needs it.

**Rejected.** *Letting pest and phpstan also run on manual runs*: it proves the exact step, but every run costs phpstan plus the full suite. *A repository-level `RUN_REMOTE_CI=true` for one run*: no code change, but it runs the whole workflow, and any pull request pushed meanwhile runs it too. *Waiting for remote CI to come back*: it leaves the criterion here, and the matching one on UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2818, open with no date.

**Companion.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2497 (its `## Decision` and the confirmed token repository list); UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2818; UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2865 (the parity-hook defect hit while adding the job).

## 2026-10-06: Public forms keep a plain `x-model` binding, and prefill reaches them through `x-data` (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2800)

**Decision.** Public forms bind fields with a plain `x-model`, not the `x-model.fill` that `statamic/cms` emits since 6.31.0 (`statamic/cms#14706`). `App\Forms\JsDrivers\AlpineCsp` renames the binding, and `ReplicatorSubfieldConditionsTest` asserts that no `x-model.fill` reaches the markup. Any value a form starts with, from a failed validation, a URL parameter or a computed default, is resolved into the form's `x-data` or written to its scope, never seeded from markup.

**Why.** Each time Alpine creates the binding, `.fill` copies the input's rendered value into an empty model, and always does so for a checkbox group or a multi-select. A field that a condition removes and restores is created again, so a checkbox group's choice is replaced by the server render (measured on 6.35.0 by `ConditionalRebuildStateSweepTest` during UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2774). It adds no value these forms lack: every public form submits as a full-page POST, and its `x-data` is built from the same blueprint values the markup is, including `old()` after a failed validation (`AbstractJsDriver::getInitialFormData()`). The Gravity Forms and Formidable prefill this replaces (query-string population, dynamic defaults such as `{date_mdy}`) is computed on the server too, so its Statamic form is a value in `x-data` or one the calculation engine writes to the scope, which works with the plain binding.

**Rejected.** *Adopting `.fill` as Statamic ships it*: it reintroduces the measured loss of a rebuilt checkbox group's or multi-select's choice. *`.fill` on first render only*: it needs a custom CSP-safe directive to guard behavior Alpine runs on every binding init, and still depends on the same server-side values, which `x-data` already carries.

**Companion.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2800 (the packet and its `## Decision`); UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2774 and UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2806 (the upgrade that adds the rename); UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2884 (a `[get_param:…]` dynamic default never fills on a public form), UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2885 (a date field's `default: now` reaches `x-data` as the string "now") and UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2886 (the choice fieldtypes' dynamic default config has no public-form consumer), each filed from this decision's review of the WordPress form features.

## 2026-10-06: The iteration skills are retired (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2821)

**Decision.** `.claude/skills/iteration-end/`, `.claude/skills/iteration-pack/`, `.claude/skills/iteration-report/` and `.claude/scripts/pack-iterations.py` are removed. The project no longer works in iterations: project 1 keeps issue membership and `Status`, and `Iteration`, `Priority`, `Size`, `Estimate` and `Phase` are no longer set or read. Releases are cut once a business day at 10:00 Central when `main` carries unreleased work, with no immediate release on a breaking change, which ships at the next daily cut and is called out in its notes. Dependency updates come from Dependabot (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2852).

**Why.** The three skills planned, packed and closed iterations, and that work no longer exists. Kept, they would still match "close out the iteration" style requests and act on board fields nobody reads.

**Rejected.** None was recorded here. The cadence, the board fields and Dependabot were decided in `UAMS-Web/uams-claude-skills#13` (items 7, "Iterations and board fields" and "Dependency updates", and the operator's 2026-10-02 addition dropping the breaking-change release), which carries the reasoning.

**Companion.** `UAMS-Web/uams-claude-skills#13` (the decision, and the shared `cutting-releases`, `writing-issues` and `update-dependencies-ticket` rewrites its sync delivers); UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2852 (Dependabot); UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2821 (this retirement). The project's `Iteration` field itself is not part of this change.

## 2026-10-06: The gate posts a hand-back's verdict and its label in one command (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2845)

**Decision.** `scripts/post-verdict.mjs` posts the gate's verdict comment and, with `--hand-back`, applies `handed-back` in the same run, then reads the labels back and fails unless they agree. The `local-ci` skill uses it in place of the separate comment and label calls, so applying the label is no longer a step of its own. A green verdict with no finding passes no flag and never touches the labels. Removal stays with `.github/workflows/clear-handed-back.yml` on a push (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2667, UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2671).

**Why.** The label was the second of two calls and the one missed: six of eight hand-backs read from their label events carried none, which leaves queue depth counting work that waits on its author. One command removes the call that could be skipped, keeps the label a gate act as the synced `handed-back-label` skill and `pre-merge-check` rule describe it, and needs no workflow.

**Rejected.** *A workflow that labels any comment opening with the hand-back marker*: no discipline needed, but it couples the label to the comment's wording, fires for any comment carrying the marker when every session posts under one account, fails silently when the Actions allowance runs out, needs a second marker for a finding on a green chain, and would make the synced skill's "the gate applies it" wrong.

**Companion.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2845 (its `## Decision`, decided by the operator 2026-10-06); UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2514, UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2515, UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2667 and UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2671 (the label's lifecycle and its removal on push).

## 2026-10-07: The Side-by-Side simple list is capped at six one-line items, and Paragraph and simple list gets its own paragraph (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2767)

**Decision.** The Side-by-Side Image and Text block's `simple_list` becomes a one-column grid of up to six rows, each item limited to 22 characters, because a `list` field carries neither an item cap nor a character limit. Paragraph and simple list gets its own paragraph field, `body_simple_list`, limited to 173 characters; `body_body_only` (449) now serves Paragraph only. Limits come from UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2757's line-budget method: heading 2 lines, button 1, each item 1 line of its column, and the paragraph the 4 whole lines left, the same shape as the link list (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2766).

**Why.** At 449 the paragraph fills the text box, so even one list item overflows it, by up to 70 px with the button. Lowering `body_body_only` would cost Paragraph only six lines, which UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2759 rejected for the same field. The list is shared by both list layouts, so its limits are the ones that fit beside the paragraph; Simple list only then fits with room to spare.

**Rejected.** *Lower `body_body_only` for both layouts*: takes six lines from every Paragraph only block. *Four two-line items with a 3-line paragraph*: also fits, but gives the two list layouts a different shape from the link list for no measured gain. *Per-item validation on the existing `list` field*: enforced where the block's other limits are editor hints, and shows the editor no limit while typing.

**Companion.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2767; UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2757 (the method); UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2759 (the precedent for a layout's own paragraph field); UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2766 (the link list's shape). No content or importer mapping used either list layout when this changed.

## 2026-10-07: The notification gate reads an empty condition group as the public page does (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2895)

**Decision.** An empty condition group (`if: []`, `if: {}`, and the same under every other key) goes through the same ALL/ANY logic and inversion as any other group, with no conditions to test, in `ConditionalLogicEvaluator`. So `if` and `unless_any` pass, `if_any` and `unless` fail, and their `show_when` / `hide_when` synonyms match. The public page is unchanged. A null group, a scalar group, and a list whose rules all name no field still pass.

**Why.** The public page already reads an empty group this way, because `[]` and `{}` are truthy in JavaScript. Matching it means visitors see no change and the email follows what the page shows. It removes the one exception UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2896 left in `evaluateKey()` rather than adding a rule on the browser side. `FieldRequiredHelper` reads the same gate (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2892), so a field the page hides behind an empty group is no longer required.

**Rejected.** The public page treats an empty group as no condition and always shows the field: it changes what visitors see for `if_any` and `unless`, needs a browser build, and does so for a shape the Control Panel builder never saves.

**Companion.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2895 (the decision), UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2906 (the build), UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2889 and UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2896 (the field map of empty lists).

## 2026-10-07: The choice fieldtypes' Dynamic Default Value applies on Select and Radio, and Checkboxes stops offering it (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2886)

**Decision.** A single Select and a Radio field apply their Dynamic Default Value (`calculation_query`, `calculation_formula`) on the public form: their templates emit the `data-calculation-*` attributes `calculation-engine.js` scans for, and the engine's text result selects the matching option. Checkboxes no longer offers the setting in the Control Panel. Wiring Checkboxes is UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2924, which waits on UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2913. A multiple Select keeps the setting in the Control Panel but is not wired either, for the same reason, and is part of UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2924.

**Why.** A rendered public form showed the setting applying on none of the three fieldtypes, while a Text field with the same formula filled in. Adding the three attributes to the Select and Radio markup was enough for the engine to select the option, with no engine change. Checkboxes binds `x-model` to a list, which the engine does not write, and the engine file is being changed for UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2913. No stored blueprint, form or fieldset set `calculation_query` on any field, so removing it from Checkboxes loses nothing.

**Rejected.** *Remove the setting from all three*: the fewest pieces, but it drops a URL-preselected Select or Radio that works with a template change alone. *Wire all three now*: Checkboxes needs an engine change in the file UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2913 is changing, and its behavior was not measured.

**Companion.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2886 (the check, the packet and the build), UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2924 (Checkboxes and the multiple Select), UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2913 (the engine change that follow-up waits on), UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2800 (the binding decision).

## 2026-10-07: The parallel-safety guard allows ContentRootTest's fixed mirror path by name (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2919)

**Decision.** `scripts/check-parallel-safe-tests.php` gets its first `ALLOWLIST` row since UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1957: `tests/Unit/Support/ContentRootTest.php`, which asserts that `ContentRoot::path('testing')` returns `storage_path('framework/testing/content-mirror')`. The row carries its reason and a retirement condition: remove it when `ContentRoot::path()` or the test reads or writes the filesystem, or when the mirror path becomes per-worker.

**Why.** Rule B flags a fixed `framework/testing` path because two parallel runs race on it. This test only compares two strings, so there is nothing to race on, and the fixed path is the contract under test: a per-worker token on the expected side would only break the assertion. No other test pins that exact string. A named row keeps the exception visible in the guard, which is the guard's stated design.

**Rejected.** *Quarantine the whole file in the `serial` group*: no hazard to isolate, and it spends serial-tail time on every run. *Reshape the assertion so Rule B cannot see the literal* (for example `storage_path().'/framework/testing/content-mirror'`): silences the guard with no exception recorded anywhere. *Drop the testing case*: loses the only test of the mirror path.

**Companion.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2919 (coverage batch 3, handed back for this), #2834, UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1149 and UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1957 (the allowlist's history).

## 2026-10-07: Checkboxes and a multiple Select apply their Dynamic Default Value, and Checkboxes offers the setting again (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2924)

**Decision.** `calculation-engine.js` treats an input named `<handle>[]` as a list field: it writes the formula's text result to the field's Alpine key as the comma-separated option values it names, each trimmed, empty ones dropped, so `a,c` checks `a` and `c`. The Checkboxes template emits the `data-calculation-*` attributes on every box, as Radio does on every radio, and the Select template emits them on a multiple Select too. Checkboxes offers `calculation_query` and `calculation_formula` again. This reverses the Checkboxes half of the 2026-10-07 entry for UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2886, which removed the setting only because the public form could not yet apply it.

**Why.** The engine skipped any bracketed name, and both fields post as `<handle>[]`, so it never bound them; their `x-model` binds a list, so a single string checked nothing. Reading `[]` as a list and writing an array makes the existing binding check the boxes and select the options, measured in a browser. Every box carrying the attributes means a click on any of them marks the field as the one the visitor last touched, and the engine leaves it alone while it stays so, the same rule a Radio follows. After writing a list, the engine fires `change`, as a click would, so a required Checkboxes group clears `required` once a box is checked; without it the form could not be submitted.

**Rejected.** *A separator other than a comma*: a comma is what a person types into a URL parameter and what a text formula joins most simply; the cost is that an option value containing a comma cannot be named. *Attributes on the hidden placeholder input only*: one binding per field, but a click on a box would not mark the field as the visitor's choice, so a later change to a referenced field would undo it.

**Companion.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2924 (this build), UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2886 (the decision this partly reverses), UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2913 (the engine change it waited on), UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2800 (the binding decision).

## 2026-10-07: A parallel-safety ALLOWLIST row exempts only the rules it names (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2939)

**Decision.** Each row in `scripts/check-parallel-safe-tests.php`'s `ALLOWLIST` is now `['rules' => [...], 'reason' => '...']`, and it suppresses only the rules it lists. The file is still scanned for every other rule, and Rule C's symbol collection is skipped only for a row that names C. A row may name only A, B, C or D. One that names E, an unknown rule or no rule makes the guard exit 1. The `ContentRootTest` row from UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2919 names Rule B and keeps its reason and retirement condition.

**Why.** A row was keyed by path alone and skipped Rules A to D together, while its docblock said a row exempts "a rule" and UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2919's reason argued Rule B only. UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2939 measured the gap: a `Storage::fake` added to the allowlisted file reported nothing until the row was removed. Scoping makes a reason's reach match its argument. It also means the retirement condition "when the test touches the filesystem" is enforced by the guard instead of trusted, because a later Rule A hit in that file is now reported.

**Rejected.** *Correct the docblock to say a row exempts A to D together*: cheaper, but every future row then needs a four-rule argument for what is usually a one-rule exception. The next reader has to notice that a one-rule reason is doing a four-rule job, which is how UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1149's row outlived its reason.

**Companion.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2939 (this change), UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2919 (the row that prompted it), UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1149 and UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1957 (the allowlist's history).

## 2026-10-08: The migration redirect generator reads the WXR cleaner's duplicate pairs and sends each dropped URL straight to its kept post (#798, UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2943)

**Decision.** The WXR cleaner writes the news.uams.edu duplicate disposition as WordPress-to-WordPress pairs: a JSON list of rows with `source` (a dropped post's permalink, or a kept post's old permalink when its slug changed), `target` (the kept post's permalink), `response` (`301`) and `notes`, as #798 records it. That repository's README does not document the format yet, so the reader follows #798's text and UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2943's example, and changes with the cleaner's output if the two differ. `content:generate-migration-redirects --dedup-redirects=<file>` reads the file, resolves each `target` to the entry whose `original_wordpress_url` it is, and adds one `301` from the source's path straight to that entry's Statamic URL, through the generator's existing dedupe, so a re-run adds nothing and a human-authored rule for the same source wins. A pair is reported and not written when its source has more than one target, when its target is itself a dropped post, when no entry carries its target, or when the kept entry's Statamic URL is a dropped post's path.

**Why.** A dropped post is never imported, so the generator's entry-move pass never sees it, and the cleaner sees only WordPress URLs, so it cannot name the Statamic one. Resolving here gives one hop per dropped URL, and the loop and dropped-target checks run where the Statamic URLs are known.

**Rejected.** *Entering the pairs by hand in the redirects Control Panel*: manual, and not repeatable after a re-import. *Storing the WordPress-to-WordPress pairs as they are*: two `301`s per dropped URL, and no Statamic URLs to run the loop and target checks against.

**Companion.** #798 (the disposition and the decision), UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2943 (this build), #2810 (the 45 content-differs sets, in the same file), UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1641 (the generator), UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1355 (the `redirects` store).

## 2026-10-08: A certification term records its credential kind, and assessment-based certificates and Focused Practice Designations are kinds of it (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2970)

**Decision.** A `certifications` term carries a required single-select `credential_kind`: `certification`, `focused_practice_designation` or `assessment_based_certificate`. `board_certification_query` stays and applies only to `certification`, so a board certification is still a `certification` term with that flag true, and the person row keeps its Board Certification and Other Certification sets. An assessment-based certificate is a `certifications` term of that kind: it shows in the provider "Credentials" block with an issued date only, and its `credentials_term` accepts a CTDL `Certificate` credential. Profiles keep one "Certifications" list, ordered board, Focused Practice Designation, other certification, certificate, with each row's label naming its kind.

**Why.** Kind is a fact about the credential, not about the person holding it, and board status already set that precedent by living on the term. Recording it once per term means no two person rows can classify the same credential differently. A Focused Practice Designation (the American Board of Surgery says it is neither a specialty nor a subspecialty certification) and an assessment-based certificate (a one-time award with no maintenance cycle) each had nowhere to go but an unlabeled Other Certification row, where a certificate reads as a certification it is not.

**Rejected.** *Kind as a person replicator set*: every editor re-classifies the same credential on every row, and rows can disagree. *A set per kind validated against the term*: the largest change, adding a picker step for a fact the term already holds. *Certificates in the professional development taxonomy*: it describes activities rather than awards, and it does not reach the provider profile. *A new `certificates` taxonomy*: it duplicates the issuer, search, picker and template surface for one distinction a field can carry. *A sub-heading per kind*: more headings than a provider's handful of credentials warrants.

**Companion.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2970 (the decision), UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2972 (the term field, pickers, `credentials_term` and backfill), UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2973 (person-row dates, labels and order).

## 2026-10-08: A duplicate pair carrying a WordPress post id is resolved by that id, among the entries of the site named by `--dedup-site` (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2960)

**Decision.** A row of the WXR cleaner's pair file may carry `source_wordpress_post_id` and `target_wordpress_post_id`. When the target's id is present, `content:generate-migration-redirects` resolves the pair to the imported entry whose `wordpress_post_id` it is, and never falls back to the URL when no entry has it. The id is looked up only among the entries of the site named by `--dedup-site=<handle>`, which the command requires whenever any row carries a post id and refuses when it names no site. An id two entries on that site share is still reported and not written. A row without ids is resolved by URL, as in UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2943, and needs no site.

**Why.** An entry's `original_wordpress_url` is the export's `<link>`, on the host the export was taken from and sometimes in the `?p=` form, so it need not equal the public permalink a pair names; the post id is the key both sides share. Post ids repeat across WordPress subsites, and the importer records the post id but no subsite, so an id is meaningful only within the site its posts were imported into, which the operator knows and the file does not.

**Rejected.** *Search every site's entries and refuse an id more than one carries*: no option to pass, but a duplicate set whose kept post shares its id with a post on any other subsite would be refused for a reason unrelated to the pair, and the refusals would grow with every subsite imported. *A site key on each row of the pair file*: the cleaner works from one WordPress export and knows no Statamic site handle, so it would have to be told one, and a per-row key allows a file that mixes sites, which no export produces.

**Companion.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2960 (this build), UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2943 (the pair reader), #798 (the disposition).

## 2026-10-08: A concept the NUCC code set covers lives only in clinical_specializations, and the subject pickers offer both collections (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2823)

**Decision.** Decided 2026-10-02, with four build questions settled 2026-10-08. A concept covered by the NUCC Health Care Provider Taxonomy code set, or by a UAMS extension of it, lives only in `clinical_specializations`; `subject_matter` keeps academic disciplines, recognition and workforce categories. Where a concept is both a discipline and a code-set concept (genetics), the `clinical_specializations` entry is used, research included. Measured against NUCC 26.1, 42 of the 53 subjects move and 11 stay: a subject matching a NUCC grouping becomes a grouping entry with no code (groupings carry none), a subject bundling several classifications under a UAMS label becomes a grouping extension, and where one name has several codes the practitioner code wins over a clinic or facility code. An extension now has a kind, `specialization` or `grouping`, and only a specialization carries the qualification, certification and training fields. `clinical_specializations` gains the shared `keywords` field, so a moved concept keeps the keywords its subject held and knowsAbout reads them as before. The specialty pickers offer `clinical_specializations` only, `other_specialties` offers `subject_matter` only, and research specialties, fields of study, development activities, credentials and the shared subject field offer both, searched through one `subject_concepts` index. A test fails when a subject repeats a clinical specialization by name or code.

**Why.** Two collections modeling one concept let content tag it twice, and each new subject risked another copy. Groupings have no NUCC code, so a code criterion could not hold for them; the existing `allopathic-osteopathic-physicians` entry already models a grouping without one. The blueprint's extension fields describe a specialization proposed for the code set, which a workforce label such as "nursing support" (nurse's aides and home health aides) is not, so a kind keeps those labels from needing invented certification answers.

**Rejected.** *Keep both and link each overlapping subject to its specialization*: the copies remain and the links invite more pairs. *Keep both and guard only new subjects*: the existing overlap stays. *Leave the multi-classification labels in `subject_matter`*: the decision's "or by UAMS's extensions to it" covers them. *Model those labels as grouping entries with no extension flag*: a UAMS label would read as a NUCC name. *Give extensions only the existing fields*: the certification and training fields would stay required and empty.

**Companion.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2823 (this build, and its classification table), UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2822 (the collection conversion), UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1107 and UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2808 (the credential values, translated here), UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2596 (the `clinical_specializations` conversion), `UAMS-Web/wordpress-importer#1347` (matching imported terms by code).

## 2026-10-09: The application refuses addresses it does not serve, with the allowed list read from the redirect domains, and links follow the address it accepted (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2873)

**Decision.** Decided 2026-10-09. The application turns on Laravel's `TrustHosts` guard with a list built on each request: `APP_URL` and its subdomains, the site's other domains such as `uamshealth.com`, and every published domain in the `redirect_domains` collection, read from the same data `ServeRedirectDomainRequests` uses. A request for any other address is refused with a 400 (A1). Full links, including emailed ones such as the Control Panel password reset, use the address the request named, which the guard has already accepted, so a page on `uamshealth.com` or `medicine.uams.edu` keeps its links there (B2). Which address search engines treat as canonical stays with the multi-domain work in #2986, not with link generation.

**Why.** Since UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2866 the `Host` header picks a redirect rule as well as a Statamic site, and editors add redirect domains by publishing an entry. Anything that trusts the requested address (a cache in front of the site, or a link Laravel builds from it) can then be steered by a request naming an address UAMS does not own. Statamic's password-reset link is built with `route()`, so a reset requested under an attacker's address would email an editor a link to that address. Refusing unknown addresses in the application protects the site whichever web server or host is chosen (#2793, #2794), keeps no second list by hand, and can be tested here. Once the guard has checked the address, following it in links is safe and keeps visitors on the address they used.

**Rejected.** *A2, a typed list in config*: a newly published redirect domain would be refused until the next deploy. *A3, only the web server in front*: the protection would live in a configuration not yet written, and a later change to the web server or a new CDN could quietly remove it. *A4, nothing*: leaves cache and link tampering open if the front end ever passes unknown addresses through. *B1, always `APP_URL`*: sends links on `uamshealth.com` and `medicine.uams.edu` to `uams.edu`, against the multi-domain plan. *B3, each site's canonical address*: needed only if the requested address could not be trusted, which A1 rules out.

**Companion.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2873 (the decision and its comparison), UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#3014 (the build, with the residuals: an end-to-end password-reset test, whether `RedirectDomainRules::read()` is cached, exact pattern escaping and `www.` forms, and monitoring addresses), #2986 (where the site's own domains are recorded), UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2866 (the redirect-domain middleware), UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2811 (how the redirect domains are served).

## 2026-10-14: The 29 credentials with no fitting subject get broad non-clinical `subject_matter` subjects (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2824)

**Decision.** Decided in two parts, 2026-10-02 for 16 credentials and 2026-10-14 for the remaining 13. Every one of the 29 gets a `subject_matter` value, and none goes without. The subjects are Science (the one that exists), Humanities, Education, Child development, Art, Theology, Professional practice, Research, Business, Health administration, Public administration, Law and Bioethics. The credential-by-credential tables are on UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2824, UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2830 and UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#3055.

**Why.** The `credentials` blueprint requires `subject_matter` unconditionally, and a credential's subject feeds its holder's `knowsAbout`. The subjects are kept broad, but a distinct field gets its own subject where a broader one would understate it (Child development apart from Education, Health and Public administration apart from Business). The same subjects are the vocabulary the `fields_of_study` terms will be mapped onto in UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2825, which already separates business, health and public administration, legal studies and bioethics. No `clinical_specializations` entry fits any of the 29.

**Rejected.** *No subject for credentials of this kind*: makes the blueprint's `required` rule conditional and leaves those holders' `knowsAbout` without the credential's field. *Two subjects, Business and Law, for the ten business, administration and law credentials*: an MHA or MPA holder would read as knowing Business. *Ethics or Humanities for the two health care ethics doctorates*: broader than both, which are bioethics doctorates. *Research for the Doctor of Professional Practice*: does not describe a professional-practice doctorate.

**Companion.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2824 (the decision and its comparison), UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2830 (the seven subjects and 16 values decided 2026-10-02), UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#3055 (the five subjects and 13 values decided 2026-10-14), UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1107 (the mapping review and the sources for each credential), UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2823 (why a code-set concept is never a new subject).

## 2026-10-14: PersonNameDisplayFormats PHP and JavaScript are checked against PHP-generated JSON fixtures (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#3043)

**Decision.** Decided 2026-10-14. A committed JSON fixture file, generated from `PersonNameDisplayFormats::buildOptions()` over a shared corpus of normalized names, is read by a Vitest spec that asserts `buildDisplayFormatOptions()` returns the same `{key, label, group}` list for each input. A Pest test builds the same output from the PHP in memory and fails when the committed file differs, so a PHP change that is not regenerated cannot pass, and a JavaScript change that no longer matches fails `npm run test:js`.

**Why.** The two files are a hand-maintained mirrored pair that had already drifted twice (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#3019, UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#3044), and each side's tests see only itself. Fixtures keep each CI job on its own toolchain: the `pest` job sets up no Node, the `js` job needs no PHP, and `scripts/local-ci.mjs` resolves Node 22 only for its Node jobs, so the JavaScript runs under the pinned Node 22 rather than whatever `node` is on `PATH`. A change to the PHP output also shows in the pull-request diff as a fixture change, where a reviewer can see it.

**Rejected.** *Executing the JavaScript from a PHP test through a Node subprocess*: fewer moving parts and a direct comparison, but it adds Node to the `pest` job locally and in CI, runs against an unpinned `node`, and no test here spawns `node` today.

**Companion.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#3043 (the decision and its comparison), UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#3053 (the build, including how the corpus maps the JavaScript's `includeNickname` option onto the PHP's `include_nickname` setting), UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#3019 and UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#3044 (the hand-mirrored drift fixes).

## 2026-10-14: The form customizations move into four Statamic add-ons, built A to D, with every key name unchanged (#3071)

**Decision.** Decided 2026-10-14. The app's form customizations move into four add-ons, each in its own repository, built in this order: A, form settings (`statamic-form-settings`: the Control Panel form settings and the notification pipeline that reads them, one add-on with a config flag per feature); B, the CSP JavaScript driver (`statamic-alpine-csp-forms`: the `alpine_csp` driver and the browser-side field-condition evaluator it calls); C, quiz and survey (`statamic-form-scoring`: scoring, their tags and the `quiz` and `likert_scale` fieldtypes, with a grading tab that plugs into A); D, submission data (`statamic-submission-context`: submission metadata, the captured request context and the queued-email warning). Each Composer package takes its repository's name under `uams-web/`. Config keys, form settings keys and fieldtype handles keep their current names. The front-end form views, the `form_data` tag, the form Bard set and page-builder block, the other form-selectable fieldtypes, and the `Checkboxes`, `Radio` and `Select` overrides with their per-choice submission limits stay in the site. A is sequenced after the multisite-forms adoption tickets that edit the same files (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#3064, UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#3067), and D after UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#3068.

**Repositories.** Each add-on repository is created from a template repository, `UAMS-Web/statamic-addon-template`, built from `UAMS-Web/statamic-multisite-forms`, so all four start with the same Composer layout, CI and synced skills. The generic scripts and workflows such a repository carries are later delivered by the shared skills sync instead of by the template: UAMS-Web/uams-claude-skills#155 (`ci.yml`, `dependabot.yml`, issue and pull-request templates), #160 (test-isolation guard), #161 (decision-log template and order check), #162 (git-hooks installer), #163 (upstream-watch workflow), #164 (`.editorconfig`), #165 and #170 (required status checks) and #173 (spell check).

**Why.** Statamic has previewed Forms 2 with no release date and no published extension API, and the app overrides core form behavior in several places: the Control Panel `FormsController` binding, the notification job, the JavaScript driver and the choice fieldtypes. When Forms 2 ships, some of these may become core features, some may need rewriting and some may still be needed. As separate add-ons each can be disabled, replaced or kept on its own while the site keeps working. Key names stay fixed because `wordpress-importer` writes form settings keys and the `quiz` and `likert_scale` handles into imported forms, so a rename would need an importer change.

**Rejected.** *Keep everything in the app*: cheapest now, but Forms 2 would force one large rewrite with no way to switch a single customization off. *One add-on for everything*: one repository and one release, but the driver, scoring and settings could not be retired separately, and they are the parts most likely to reach core at different times. *Wait for Forms 2*: there is no date, and extracting first is what makes the later migration incremental. *One add-on per feature inside A* (separate confirmations, restrictions and routing add-ons): those features share the `FormsController` override and the classes that build the settings blueprint, so several add-ons would compete for one binding.

**Companion.** #3071 (the epic and its comparison), #3072, UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#3073, #3074 and #3075 (the four extractions), UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#3066 (the multisite-forms adoption).

## 2026-10-14: `fields_of_study` terms take the broad discipline above them as `subject_matter` (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2825)

**Decision.** Decided 2026-10-14. Each `fields_of_study` term's `subject_matter` points at the broad discipline above it, not at a subject mirroring the term. A term whose name joins two disciplines (agricultural economics, art therapy, environmental law, music education) takes two subjects; every other term takes one. Fourteen new broad disciplines are added: Engineering, Agriculture, Mathematics, Computing, Languages and literature, Music, Social sciences, Communication, Architecture, Environmental science, Psychology, Area studies, Criminal justice, and Library and information science. Health professions take their NUCC 26.1 `clinical_specializations` entry, per UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2823, including the clinical, counseling and school psychology fields, mortuary science (Funeral Director), veterinary (Veterinarian) and respiratory therapy (both NUCC respiratory therapist codes). Pre-professional tracks take the field they prepare for at the broad level: the pre-health tracks to Health sciences, pre-law to Law, pre-seminary to Theology. The approved row-by-row mapping is on UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2825; it changed from the proposal only in moving emergency management from Criminal justice to Public administration.

**Why.** The term already supplies its own narrow concept: `PersonKnowsAboutComputer` adds the `fields_of_study` term to `knows_about_terms` and its subjects to `knows_about_entries` beside it, so the subject's job is the discipline above it. A small set of broad disciplines also keeps the review to groups that can be approved as a whole.

**Rejected.** *One subject per field*: about 400 new subjects, making `subject_matter` a second copy of `fields_of_study`. *No subject*: drops the discipline from `knowsAbout` and makes the blueprint's `required` rule conditional, which UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2824 declined. *Always one subject*: loses one of the two disciplines a compound field names. *Folding Area studies into Humanities and Psychology into Social sciences*: loses two disciplines large enough to stand alone. *Applied science for mortuary science and veterinary*: NUCC covers both, and UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2823 routes code-set concepts to `clinical_specializations`.

**Companion.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2825 (the decision and its mapping), UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#3062 (writing the values), UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2830 and UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#3055 (subjects it depends on), UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2823 and UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2824 (the routing and the `required` rule).

## 2026-10-14: `specialties_acgme` terms take the most specific matching NUCC code as `subject_matter` (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2826)

**Decision.** Decided 2026-10-14. Each `specialties_acgme` term's `subject_matter` points at the `clinical_specializations` entry for the most specific NUCC 26.1 specialization matching the ACGME specialty or subspecialty, as the three existing values already do. An entry not yet in `clinical_specializations` is created from NUCC 26.1. A subspecialty shared across boards (sports medicine, pain medicine, sleep medicine, hospice and palliative medicine) points at every NUCC entry it sits under, and internal medicine-pediatrics takes both parents because NUCC has no combined code. The transitional year takes General Practice (`208D00000X`). Twelve terms the code set does not name become UAMS extension entries: craniofacial plastic surgery, ten ACGME fellowships each defined from its program requirements, and the term for catheter-based treatment of the brain and spine, named after the ABMS focused practice designation that three boards issue for it (see UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2826). The approved row-by-row mapping, including its judgement calls, is on UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2826.

**Why.** It gives each training specialty its own NUCC identity in `knowsAbout` and matches the values already written. The field allows several values, so a shared subspecialty does not have to be attributed to one board.

**Rejected.** *Parent classification only*: every internal medicine subspecialty would read as Internal Medicine. *One UAMS extension per term*: mirrors the taxonomy into `clinical_specializations` without a NUCC code. *One primary entry for a shared subspecialty*: wrongly describes physicians certified under the other boards. For the transitional year, *an `if:` gate exempting it* (a blueprint change for one term) and *a UAMS extension* (mirrors the taxonomy).

**Companion.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2826 (the decision and its mapping), UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#3061 (writing the values, and deciding whether the reverse `training_specialty` field on `clinical_specializations` is written too or kept consistent by a test).

## 2026-10-14: `specialties_aoa` terms take the most specific matching NUCC code as `subject_matter` (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2827)

**Decision.** Decided 2026-10-14. Each `specialties_aoa` term's `subject_matter` follows the UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2826 rule: the most specific matching NUCC 26.1 entry, created from NUCC where missing, every matching entry for a subspecialty shared across boards, and a UAMS extension where the code set has no concept. A term combining a specialty with osteopathic manipulative treatment takes two values: the specialty's NUCC entry and the NUCC osteopathic manipulative medicine entry (`204D00000X`). Two terms become UAMS extensions. Correctional medicine takes its name and definition from the ACGME Sponsoring Institution-based correctional medicine fellowship, with the AOA conjoint subspecialty's three primaries (Family Medicine, Internal Medicine, Preventive Medicine) as candidate parents; NUCC's only related row, Prison Health (`261QP2400X`), is a facility code. Metabolic and bariatric surgery follows the American Board of Surgery focused practice designation and is parented to Surgery (`208600000X`), which the board describes as a variation of general surgery. The approved row-by-row mapping is on UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2827, unchanged from the proposal.

**Why.** NUCC does not split a field by training route, so AOA- and ACGME-trained physicians in the same field land on the same concept, and the field allows several values, so neither a shared subspecialty nor a combined term has to lose half of what it names.

**Rejected.** *Parent classification only*: subspecialties read as their broad classification. *One UAMS extension per term*: mirrors the taxonomy without a NUCC code. *One primary entry for a shared subspecialty.* For the osteopathic-combined terms, *the specialty only* (drops the OMT training) and *a UAMS extension* (a concept with no NUCC code where NUCC has one).

**Companion.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2827 (the decision and its mapping), UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#3060 (writing the values, with the same open question about `training_specialty` as UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#3061).

## 2026-10-14: `specialties_non_accredited` terms map onto Adult Reconstructive Orthopaedic Surgery and one extension (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2828)

**Decision.** Decided 2026-10-14. With three terms, this is the whole mapping: adult reconstructive orthopaedic surgery and total joint replacement both take the NUCC Adult Reconstructive Orthopaedic Surgery specialization under Orthopaedic Surgery, created from NUCC 26.1; shoulder and elbow surgery takes a new UAMS extension entry of kind `specialization`, parented to Orthopaedic Surgery (`207X00000X`).

**Why.** Total joint replacement is the core of adult reconstructive surgery, so it names no concept NUCC lacks, and an extension would split one field in two in `knowsAbout`. Shoulder and elbow surgery is a distinct subspecialty NUCC does not name. The adult reconstructive term also lands on the concept the same-named `specialties_acgme` term reaches under UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2826.

**Rejected.** *An extension for each uncovered term*: gives total joint replacement its own extension beside the NUCC concept it is part of.

**Companion.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2828 (the decision), UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#3058 (writing the values, checking the exact NUCC code and settling the extension's name, parent and training fields).

## 2026-10-14: Laboratory genetics and genomics takes both NUCC laboratory-genetics codes (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2829)

**Decision.** Decided 2026-10-14. The one `research_specialties_acgme` term without a value, laboratory genetics and genomics, takes two `subject_matter` entries from the NUCC 26.1 Medical Genetics classification: Clinical Molecular Genetics (`207SG0203X`) and Clinical Cytogenetics (`207SC0300X`). Both are created in `clinical_specializations` from NUCC 26.1 before the value is written. Clinical biochemical genetics keeps its existing value (`207SG0202X`).

**Why.** NUCC 26.1 has no laboratory genetics and genomics specialization; its two laboratory-genetics specializations are these two, so the term takes every matching entry, the rule applied on UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2826 and UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2827, and stays inside NUCC.

**Rejected.** *Clinical Molecular Genetics alone*: omits cytogenetics. *The Medical Genetics grouping entry*: marked invalid as a code and too broad. *A UAMS extension*: a parallel vocabulary where NUCC covers the concept.

**Companion.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2829 (the decision), UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#3057 (writing the value).

## 2026-10-14: Forms become per-site through `uams-web/statamic-multisite-forms`, with access by role, recipients by config reference and one recorded site (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#3066)

**Decision.** Decided 2026-10-14. The site requires `uams-web/statamic-multisite-forms` `^0.6`, so a form carries a Sites assignment, per-site title, store and email overrides, a recorded site on each submission, and Control Panel access scoped by site. Three forks the adoption raised are settled: site access is granted through roles; a per-site override row names its recipients with `{{ config:forms.recipients.<key> }}` references, the mechanism UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2745 uses for the base recipients; and the app's `_site_handle`, `_site_name` and `_site_url` submission keys take the add-on's resolved site. The add-on's `not_assigned` setting stays `empty`, because a form reaches a page only as a block in an entry's content, so a form not assigned to the entry's site should drop that block rather than return a 404 for the whole page.

**Why.** The site is a multisite install whose forms belong to particular sites, and core forms are global. Config references keep recipient addresses out of the forms repository on every site, as they are for the base rows. One recorded site means a submission's site keys and the add-on's site filter cannot disagree.

**Rejected.** *A package setting that reads per-site recipients by config key*: a release for what core's email parsing already does; it is kept as the fallback if editors find the grid cells error-prone. *Keeping the app's site keys independent of the add-on's site*: two answers to which site a submission came from. *`not_assigned: 404`*: one mismatched form block would take the rest of its page down.

**Companion.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#3066 (the adoption), UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#3067, UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#3068 and UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#3069 (follow-ups that wait on it), UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2745 (the base recipients' config references).

## 2026-10-15: The email grid's four `required` rules bind, and the stored forms' email rows are filled to pass them (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#3082)

**Decision.** Decided 2026-10-15. All four `required` rules the email grid customizer specifies bind once the form settings customizations run on the Statamic 6.35 blueprint shape. The same change fills the email rows of the forms in `resources/forms` that would otherwise fail them: `name` takes the form's title, `subject` takes `New submission from <form title>` (following the existing `spread_kindness` row), and `message` takes a short placeholder.

**Why.** Six of the seven stored forms carry email rows without `name` or `message` (five hold only `to`, and `spread_kindness` holds `to` and `subject`), so binding the rules without filling them would mean those forms could not be saved in the Control Panel, including for unrelated edits. Filling them keeps every form saveable and keeps the guarantees the rules were written to give. Nothing in the send path reads `message` today; whether the notification email renders it, or the column is retired, is a follow-up under #3072.

**Rejected.** *Bind the rules and leave the forms for editors to fill*: six forms could not be saved until edited. *Drop `required` from `name`, `message` and `subject`*: every form saveable, but the settings lose the guarantees the rules exist for.

**Companion.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#3082 (the decision and the fix), `UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-forms#1` (the filled rows).

## 2026-10-17: An email row's `message` is the notification body (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#3094)

**Decision.** Decided 2026-10-17. Each notification sends its email row's `message`, rendered with the submission's merge tags, as the email body, in an HTML part and a plain-text part. A row whose `message` is empty sends the field list the notification sent before. `{{ all_fields }}` renders every field the field list renders, replicator rows included (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2055), and the placeholder UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#3082 wrote into every stored form's rows becomes `{{ all_fields }}`, so each existing form keeps listing every submitted field. Submitted values reach the body escaped and are never parsed as Antlers.

**Why.** `message` is the editor-written body, and every source the importer reads fills it with that form's own notification wording, with its field-list tag (`{all_fields}`, `[default-message]`, `{fields_table}`) translated to `{{ all_fields }}`. Sending it is what lets an imported form send the notification its WordPress source sent.

**Rejected.** *Show `message` above the submitted fields*: most imported bodies already contain `{{ all_fields }}`, so every field would be listed twice. *Retire the column*: it discards the notification wording migrated from every source, leaves the `all_fields` tag and the merge-tag instructions unused, and needs a matching change in `UAMS-Web/wordpress-importer`, which writes the column.

**Companion.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#3094 (the decision), UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#3149 (the build), `UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-forms#3` (the stored forms' rows), #3072 (the form settings move).

## 2026-10-17: A form confirmation's query string is optional for the redirect and entry types (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#3161)

**Decision.** Decided 2026-10-17. `confirmation_query_string` carries no `required_if`. It keeps `exclude_unless:confirmation_type,redirect,entry`, so a query string is kept for the redirect and entry types, dropped for the others, and may be left empty.

**Why.** Everything that consumes the value treats it as optional: `ApplyFormConfirmation::withQueryString()` has a branch for an empty query string, `docs/forms/confirmation-rendering.md` and UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1101 call it optional, and three tests configure a redirect without one. The builder's own convention is that a type's payload gets `required_if` while an optional extra under the same condition does not: the destination URL and page are already required separately, and `confirmation_redirect_target` carries no rule. `exclude_unless` stays because it stops a stale query string being kept for a type with no destination.

**Rejected.** *Keeping `required_if`*: once the conditional rules resolve (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#3162), every redirect and entry confirmation would have to invent a query string, against every surface that consumes the value.

**Companion.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#3161 (the decision), UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#3101 and UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#3158 (the change), UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#3162 (making the nested rules resolve).

## 2026-10-19: Credentials take the reviewed `subject_matter` values, and those with no fitting term wait for a vocabulary decision (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1107)

**Decision.** Decided 2026-10-02. The `credentials` mapping on UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1107 is approved as proposed: the 83 high-confidence clinical rows and the 15 sourced but uncertain rows. Where the 2026-07-18 draft differs, the proposal stands (`lpe` and `lpe-i` are psychology and neuropsychology, `odt` physical and occupational therapy, `pmhs` advanced practice nursing, `mscp` gynecology). A value naming a concept the NUCC code set covers is written as the matching `clinical_specializations` entry, per UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2823. The 29 credentials with no fitting term get none from this mapping; UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2824 decided their subjects. <!-- cspell:ignore pmhs mscp -->

**Why.** The `credentials` blueprint requires `subject_matter` unconditionally, and 127 of the 129 terms had no value, so they failed the blueprint's own validation in the Control Panel. Each value was reviewed against a source rather than guessed, because a credential's subject feeds its holder's `knowsAbout`.

**Rejected.** *The draft's best guesses* where the proposal corrected them with a source. *`support-infrastructure` for business, administration and law*: its notes describe home and vehicle modification contractors and drivers. *Force-fitting the 29 credentials into an existing term*: they were left for a vocabulary decision instead.

**Companion.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1107 (the mapping, its sources and the rows changed from the draft), UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2808 (writing the 98 values), UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2823 (the translation to `clinical_specializations`), UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2824, UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2830 and UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#3055 (the 29 credentials).

## 2026-10-19: A future-dated practice location confers provider profile eligibility only from its date (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#3176)

**Decision.** Decided 2026-10-19. The `has_published_entry` rule counts a linked `clinical_locations` entry only when its resolved status is `published`, so a location published with a future date is `scheduled` and does not make its providers eligible until that date. `App\Jobs\RefreshProviderEligibilityForDatedLocations` re-indexes the people linked to each location whose date has passed since its last run, on the cadence in `clinical_provider_profile.location_date_refresh.cron`, so a provider becomes eligible without a save.

**Why.** A profile is meant to offer somewhere a patient can be seen. A location that is not yet publicly reachable should not put a provider in the directory, the lens, Find-a-Doc or the API ahead of it. Reading the status makes eligibility depend on time, which a Stache computed-value index does not follow on its own; the scheduled refresh closes the gap the save and delete listener from UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#3123 cannot reach. The refresh is its own job rather than part of `ApplyScheduledPublishing`, because it saves nothing and belongs to the clinical provider layer that moves into `uams-web/statamic-people` (#3181), while scheduled publishing stays in the site.

**Rejected.** *Count a location as soon as it is published, whatever its date*: a provider would appear everywhere eligibility reaches as soon as a location dated next month is created.

**Companion.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#3176 (the decision), UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#3178 (the build), UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#3123 (the rule and the listener).

## 2026-10-19: A program with no accrediting authority and no examination is not a code-set extension (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#3169)

**Decision.** Decided 2026-10-19. A UAMS `clinical_specializations` extension whose authority is `none` must still have a third-party accredited training program, an accreditor that sets program requirements, reviews programs and can sanction them, and a certification examination for graduates; the blueprint's three `accepted` rules stay as they are. The Shoulder and Elbow Surgery extension UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#3058 created fails all three, so it is removed, and the `shoulder-and-elbow-surgery` term in `specialties_non_accredited` keeps its names and points at the Orthopaedic Surgery classification (`207X00000X`). This amends UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2828's mapping for that one term.

**Why.** The three questions are NUCC's own criteria for a new physician code where the area has no ABMS or AOA board certification and no ACGME or AOA-accredited training: Section C.3 of the [NUCC Taxonomy Code Request Form](https://nucc.org/images/stories/word/NUCC_Taxonomy_Code_Request_Form_v7_042024.docx) (version 7, April 2024). UAMS extensions apply those criteria and leave out only Section A, the business justification (a health plan need or a statutory or regulatory requirement). The term's `subject_matter` field accepts only `clinical_specializations` entries, so a `subject_matter` subject cannot stand in, and the classification is the nearest coded concept, as `207XS0114X` is for the adult reconstructive term.

**Rejected.** *Relaxing the rule so the three questions can record a truthful `false`*: it would admit an extension on no third-party evidence at all, and every later extension could do the same.

**Companion.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#3169 (the decision), UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#3179 (removing the extension and pointing the term at the classification), UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#3058 (which created it), UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2828 (the mapping this amends), UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2823 (the code-set boundary the extension fields enforce).

## 2026-10-20: `people` editors are scoped by the entry's organizations, and only administrators publish (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#3128)

**Decision.** A user group names the `organizations` its members may edit, and a member may view, create and change a `people` entry when any organization the entry links to (a role's department or service line, or a top-level organization field) is one the group covers (the next entry says which those are). The entry policy enforces it on view, edit, delete and publish, the Control Panel listing and search show a scoped editor only the entries in scope, and a save that would leave an entry outside the editor's organizations is refused. The `people_editor` role carries no publish permission, so only administrators publish. Administrators are not scoped.

**Why.** The organization is already the relationship that says whose person an entry is, so a new entry is in scope as soon as its organizations are set, with no per-entry step and no list of editors to keep in step with staffing.

**Rejected.** *A per-entry editors field*: every entry needs a step, and the field goes stale as staff change. *Collection-wide roles*: any editor could change any person. *People groups or clinical locations as the attribute*: neither says which unit a person belongs to. *A scoped publisher role*: publishing stays with administrators. *A user record linked by an editor*: it gives a person, not a unit, edit rights.

**Companion.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#3128 (the decision), UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#3182 (the build), UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#3127 and UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#3184 (the review path), #1267 (other collections).

## 2026-10-20: A group covers the organizations it names and every organization below them, never a brand (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#3182)

**Decision.** A user group covers each organization its `organizations` field names and every organization below it in the `organizations` tree, never one above it. The coverage is read from the tree on every request, with no persistent cache, so a move in the tree takes effect on the next request. A top-level organization (a brand) is refused when a group is saved, and a group that names one by another route gains nothing through it. People linked to no organization stay outside every group.

**Why.** The natural editing group is a college, institute or service line, and its people link to the departments and divisions under it. Under exact match a College of Medicine group covers none of the people linked to its departments.

**Rejected.** *Exact match*: a college-level editor covers almost nobody. *Descendants with no limit*: a brand-level group grants nearly every person in one choice. *Descendants with a warning only*: the warning does not stop that choice.

**Companion.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#3182 (the build and the decision), UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#3128 (scoping by organization), UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#3192 (relationship pickers).

## 2026-10-20: A change to a published `people` entry waits as a working copy until an administrator publishes it (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#3127)

**Decision.** Statamic revisions are on for the application, and `people` is the only collection that sets `revisions: true`. A change to a published `people` entry is saved as a working copy and goes live when an administrator publishes it; a draft is edited as before. Working copies are stored under `storage/statamic/revisions`, outside the content repository, and the README says how they survive a deploy and where they are backed up.

**Why.** A working copy keeps the published entry unchanged while a change waits, uses Statamic's own review screen and history, and needs nothing built to hold the pending change.

**Rejected.** *Holding submissions apart in a separate record*: a second store to build, review and merge back by hand. *Draft-only writes*: unpublishing a live entry to change it takes the person off the site while the change waits.

**Companion.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#3127 (the decision), UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#3184 (the build), UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#3128 and UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#3182 (who may make the change).

## 2026-10-20: A per-site override row's email grid offers exactly the base email grid's columns (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#3152)

**Decision.** Decided 2026-10-19. The `email` grid of a `site_overrides` row offers the columns of the form's own email grid as that grid ends up after every customization, in the same order. The app's `EmailGridBlueprintCustomizer` copies the finished columns into the override grid; the add-on's Sites section is already in the contents it receives, so the add-on needs no hook (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#3164).

**Why.** An override row replaces the form's email list on its site, so a row needs every column a base row has. The job already honored the Active toggle, the recipient sources and the conditional logic from YAML; only the editing surface was missing, and since UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#3094 a missing `message` changes the body a site sends. One rule, the override row mirrors the base row, keeps the two grids identical whatever adds a column to the base.

**Rejected.** *The add-on carrying the app's notification columns*: it ties a general multisite add-on to this app's notification features and keeps two copies of the columns in sync. *The app customizing the nested grid as a separate rule*: kept as the implementation route, not the decision, so the grids cannot drift apart again. *Hand-edited YAML with a Control Panel warning*: leaves editors without the base row's columns, and sends the wrong body once `message` is the notification body.

**Companion.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#3152 (the decision), UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#3164 (the build), [`docs/forms/notification-recipients.md`](forms/notification-recipients.md).

## 2026-10-20: A form's default confirmation must carry its message, and the stored forms take theirs from their WordPress source (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#3162)

**Decision.** Decided 2026-10-20. Once the confirmation group's conditional rules resolve against the nested data, the type's own setting is required: a `message` confirmation needs its message, and the default type is `message`. The rule stays strict. Each of the seven stored forms in `resources/forms` is given the confirmation message its Gravity Forms source in `UAMS-Web/wordpress-importer-exports` shows, copied as written; a form with no source text would be listed rather than given invented wording, and none lacked one. The email grid's recipient rules, which had the same path defect, now resolve per row too: a TO address is required when its source is an address, while an empty CC, BCC or Reply-To address stays allowed, and the field and routing sources require their value for every recipient (confirmed 2026-10-20).

**Why.** An empty message makes `ApplyFormConfirmation` flash nothing, so the visitor sees Statamic's generic text instead of the wording the form had on WordPress. Seeding from the source keeps the migrated wording rather than inventing it.

**Rejected.** *Relaxing the rule so an empty default message passes*: it keeps the generic text on every form that never set one. *Writing placeholder wording into the stored forms*: it publishes text nobody chose. *Requiring every recipient's address once the rules resolve*: the address source is the default, so every row without a CC would be rejected.

**Companion.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#3162 (the rules and the decision), `UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-forms` (the seeded messages), UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#3161 (the optional query string).

## 2026-10-20: Workday's identity keys, HR facts, trainee status and legal name stay as Workday sent them, and only gender may differ (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#3130)

**Decision.** Decided 2026-10-20. On a `people` entry the four Workday IDs (`workday_universal_id`, `workday_employee_id`, `workday_contingent_worker_id`, `workday_student_id`), `uams_worker_query`, `uams_worker_subtype`, `uams_employee_subtype` and the primary UAMS email (#3137) are never overridden and are read-only. Trainee status is Workday's value when Workday supplies one, and is not overridable; the intake asks for it only when Workday is silent. The legal parts of `person_name` are Workday's value and read-only, while nickname, display format and pronunciation live in their own fields that the sync never writes. `gender` is overridable by the person and by an editor, starting from Workday's value. No field is overridable by an editor but not by the person. A field Workday turns out not to supply (#3142) becomes an ordinary field.

**Why.** Identity keys and HR facts drive matching, eligibility and editing scope (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#3128), so a different value there is a data error rather than a preference. A name needs no override once the refinements have their own fields. Gender is the one field where a person's own answer can rightly differ from the HR record, and patients filter providers by it.

**Rejected.** *Also overriding the legal name parts*: the refinement fields already carry what a person wants shown. *Overriding nothing*: leaves a person unable to state their gender where it differs from the HR record. *Overridable trainee status*: trainee status feeds provider eligibility, and Workday is its system of record.

**Companion.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#3130 (the decision), UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#3186 (the build), UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#3131 and UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#3132 (storing a gender override and keeping it when Workday changes), #3142 (what Workday supplies).

## 2026-10-21: Workday-managed people fields bind everyone but administrators, lock a trainee field only when the sync lists it, and keep the name refinements in sibling fields (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#3186)

**Decision.** Decided 2026-10-21. The Workday-managed `people` fields are read-only for every user except super users and users who may configure collections: the field is shown read-only, and a save guard puts back the stored value, because Statamic's `read_only` visibility is only a display setting. A trainee status field is locked only when the entry's `workday_supplied` list names it, so every trainee field stays editable until the sync writes that list (#3142). `person_name` keeps only the legal name parts; `nickname`, `name_pronunciation` and `name_display_format` are sibling fields the sync never writes, and one accessor (`PersonNameComposite`) rebuilds the old combined name for every reader. `content:split-person-names` moves the stored entries in the same release, and the Find-a-Doc API output is unchanged. The change covers today's fields; #3137, #3138 and the person-facing form tickets carry the same criterion.

**Why.** Administrators must be able to correct a bad Workday value while no sync exists. A non-empty trainee field cannot say whether Workday or an editor set it, so only the sync's own record can lock one. A lock on part of one field cannot be expressed per sub-field, and a field the sync writes must not hold what a person edits.

**Rejected.** *Binding administrators too*: leaves no way to correct a value before the sync exists. *Display-only locking*: any request can bypass it. *Inferring Workday's trainee value from a non-empty field*: locks an editor's answer as if Workday had sent it. *Locking only some sub-keys of `person_name`*: Statamic cannot express it, and the sync would still write a field a person edits.

**Companion.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#3186 (the build and the decision), UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#3130 (the parent decision), `UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-content` (the split entries and the schema person template), #3137, #3138, #3183 and #3185 (the same criterion for later fields).

## 2026-10-21: Listeners are registered only through `$listen`, and the clinical-expertise validator does not depend on listener order (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#3225)

**Decision.** Decided 2026-10-21. Listener discovery is turned off, and every listener is listed once in `EventServiceProvider::$listen`, including those registered today only by discovery. A test asserts that every class in `app/Listeners` is registered exactly once. `ValidateClinicalExpertiseEntry` decides whether an entry will be published from `status_and_visibility` itself rather than from `published()`.

**Why.** Every listener in `$listen` was also discovered, so 43 (event, listener) pairs ran twice, and some had side effects: `GenerateNewsReleasePdf` rendered each PDF twice. Discovery orders listeners by an unsorted filesystem scan, which on macOS is not alphabetical and is not measured elsewhere. Of the pairs whose order differs between the two lists, one changes behavior: the UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1066 anchor rule held only because discovery's second pass ran `SyncEntryPublishedStatus` before the validator. An explicit list keeps the order in one reviewed file, and the validator change removes that pair's dependence on order.

**Rejected.** *Discovery only*: leaves the anchor rule depending on filesystem order on each host. *`$listen` only, reordered, with the validator unchanged*: holds today but keeps the guard dependent on a hand-kept order. *Discovery only plus the validator change*: fixes this pair, but any later order dependency would again vary by host and be invisible in review.

**Companion.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#3225 (the decision), UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#3240 (the build), UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#3223 (where the double registration was found), UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#563 and UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#587 (the earlier single-listener fix).

## 2026-10-22: A gender override sits beside a read-only Workday value, is kept and flagged when Workday changes, and is shown in place (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#3131, UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#3132, UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#3133)
