**Decision.** Decided 2026-10-20 on UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#3131, UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#3132 and UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#3133, and built in UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#3187. `gender` stays the field every reader uses, and a read-only `workday_gender` holds what the Workday sync last sent. The sync copies its value into `gender` only while `gender` is empty or still equals the previous `workday_gender`; an entry is overridden when the two differ, so no override flag is stored. When Workday sends a new value for an overridden entry, the override is kept and the entry is flagged for review, and a new value equal to `gender` flags nothing; an entry's first sync follows the same rule. Flagged entries are listed on a dashboard widget, where an editor clears a flag by confirming the override or by taking Workday's value, and nobody is emailed. `workday_gender` is shown read-only in place under `gender`, with an "Overridden" marker when the two differ, to editors and to the person alike. `WorkdayGender` holds the rule for the sync (#3142) to call; the review flag is the entry data key `workday_gender_review`, outside the blueprint, so no Control Panel form sets or clears it.

**Why.** Gender is the one Workday field a person's own answer can rightly differ from (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#3130), and keeping every reader on `gender` leaves the Find-a-Doc API, templates, JSON-LD and eligibility unchanged. Keeping Workday's value on the entry lets a later change be detected and explains the override to whoever reads it. A flag keeps a real change in front of an editor without overwriting the person's own statement.

**Rejected.** *A separate override field read through a resolved value*: every reader of `gender` would switch to the resolved value. *A per-field do-not-sync flag*: Workday's current value is lost once overridden. *Keeping an override silently*: a real change would go unseen. *Taking Workday's value*: it overwrites the person's own statement. *Emailing the person*: nothing of theirs is lost, and no email path to people exists for it. *A separate Workday section on the entry*: it repeats every Workday field, which UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#3130 already shows read-only in place. *Hiding Workday's value from the person*: it is their own record and explains the override.

**Companion.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#3131, UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#3132 and UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#3133 (the decisions), UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#3187 (the build), UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#3130 (the parent decision), #3142 (the Workday sync, which calls the rule).

## 2026-10-23: A certification date parses only when the text carries a real date (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#3246)

**Decision.** Decided 2026-10-23 on UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#3246, built in UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#3265. `CertificationActivityValidity::parsePlainDate()` reads a string as a date only when it starts with an ISO 8601 date: a strict `Y-m-d`, or that date followed by a time, a `Z` or `z`, or an offset. A time, a zone letter or an offset counts only when it is attached to that date. `Tuesday 10:30`, `tomorrow 10:30`, `next week 10:30`, `Mondayz`, `z`, `Zulu` and `05/01/2024 Z` all read as no date. <!-- cspell:ignore Mondayz -->

**Why.** A date derived from the clock is a fabricated value presented as data, which is the harm UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#3010 set out to remove. It is worse than an absent date, because `activity_valid_for` and `activity_active_query` are computed from it and nothing downstream can tell it from a real one.

**Rejected.** *Keep the old reading*, where a time or a zone letter anywhere in the text admits it: it keeps producing a clock-derived date presented as data. *Read time-only and zone-only text as no date, but keep a relative word with a time* (`tomorrow 10:30`): the date still comes from the clock rather than from the source field.

**Companion.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#3246 (the decision), UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#3265 (the build), UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#3010 (the `T` test and the `catch` it builds on).

## 2026-10-25: Gate `entries` relations on a bare `| count`, retiring the `| pluck:'id' | count` idiom (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2422)

**Decision.** An Antlers section gated on an `entries` relation counts it as `{{ rel | count }}`. The `| pluck:'id' | count` idiom the 2026-07-25 entry (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1693) set is retired at every site, and the comments that explained it now explain only why the gate is a count rather than bare truthiness: an empty relation augments to a `StatusQueryBuilder` that is truthy, and a loop over it falls through to the parent scope (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#571, UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2026).

**Why.** `statamic/cms#15546`, the maintainer's fix for `statamic/cms#15536` (filed from UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1697), makes `StatusQueryBuilder` apply its published-only filter to `count()`, `exists()`, `pluck()` and its other result methods, not only `get()`. It first shipped in the v6.35 line, and the installed `statamic/cms` v6.35.1 carries it. Measured through a real `entries` field over one published and one draft entry: `| count`, `| pluck:'id' | count` and `| length` all return 1, and with the fix's `RESULT_METHODS` block removed from the installed file, `| count` and `| length` return 2 while the idiom returns 1. `ProviderLensResourcesTest` pins the three counts.

**Rejected.** *Keep the idiom, since it is still correct*: it reads as a workaround for a defect that no longer exists, and every site carries a comment explaining that defect. *Switch to `| length`*: it is now equivalent, but `| count` is what the rest of the templates use.

**Accepted cost.** A downgrade of `statamic/cms` below v6.35 would bring the over-count back at every site, and only `ProviderLensResourcesTest` would say so.

**Companion.** Issue UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2422; the 2026-07-25 entry for UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#1693; upstream `statamic/cms#15546`.

## 2026-10-26: A fellowship designation stays out of a person's display name and is recorded as a membership level (#304)

**Decision.** Decided 2026-10-26 on #304. A fellowship designation such as FAAOS or FACS is not part of a `people` display name: the name carries degrees and the credentials that qualify a person to practice. The eight fellowship terms leave the `credentials` taxonomy (#305): fellowships that are a membership level are deleted, and those earned by examination or training (FRCS, FRCSC, FRACS, FAAOMPT) move to `certifications` as specialty certifications, listed beside board certifications rather than in the name. A fellowship is recorded instead as a level beside the organization in Professional Memberships (`member_of`), as Member, Fellow or Emeritus Fellow (#306), and the provider lens shows memberships, so a Fellow reads "Fellow of the American Academy of Orthopaedic Surgeons" below the name rather than in it (#307). <!-- cspell:ignore FAAOS AAOS FACS FRCS FRCSC FRACS FAAOMPT -->

**Why.** The published standard, [Provider Degrees and Credentials](https://communications.uams.edu/web/kb/provider-degrees-credentials/), already limits the display name to the provider's regulatory designation; a fellowship is not one, so this applies that rule rather than setting a new one. The designation depends on a membership status this site cannot keep current: AAOS restricts FAAOS to Active and Emeritus Fellows and excludes non-members and suspended fellows, so it stops being true when dues lapse, with nothing to say so. The clinical substance it stands on, board certification, is already recorded in `certifications` and shown on the provider lens. AAOS's own ordering of letters after a name places FAAOS with awards and honors, and this log already keeps honors out of the name (2026-09-02, endowed and named chairs).

**Rejected.** *Keep fellowship letters in the display name, as AAOS asks its Fellows to do in physician directories*: an association's guidance on promoting membership does not set this site's naming standard. *Name only degrees and practice credentials*: it would also drop nurse practitioner certifications such as FNP-BC, which are classed as certifications without the practice flag. *Record the organization with no level*: it loses the Fellow level entirely. *Record the fellowship in `awards`*: a membership level is not an award, and it would sit apart from its organization.

**Companion.** #304 (the decision), #305, #306 and #307 (the builds), UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2970 (whose residual named fellowship-conferred titles as undecided).

## 2026-10-27: The public repository starts as one parentless snapshot commit, created private and switched to public once the open issues have moved (#2649, #2659)

**Decision.** The method both sibling repositories were published by. The new repository holds one parentless commit whose tree equals a chosen `main` commit, authored and committed with a GitHub `noreply` address, with a message naming the archive and the source SHA. It is created private and empty, its settings and organization ruleset membership are recreated, the open issues are transferred to it while it is still private (#2661), and it is switched to public within hours (#2657). This repository's last act before the snapshot is a release on its own release line at the chosen commit, and the new repository's single commit is tagged with the same version. The commit is chosen last, immediately before the creation, as decided on 2026-10-13 (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2658, folded into #2659).

**Why.** Only the first commit and the moved issues are published, so only those have to be clean, and this repository keeps every SHA and link its pull requests cite (the 2026-09-29 entry for UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2651). GitHub refuses to transfer an issue from a private repository to a public one: on `UAMS-Web/uamswp-migration-api-archive#469`, whose new repository was created public, `transferIssue` answered "Old issue cannot be transferred from private repository to public repository", while `UAMS-Web/wordpress-importer-archive#1300` created its repository private and transferred 93 open issues in eleven minutes. Releasing at the chosen commit and tagging the snapshot with the same version makes the archive's last release and the new repository's first tag name the same tree, so the pre-snapshot changes appear in release notes once.

**Rejected.** *Create the new repository public*: the open issues then cannot be transferred and have to be recreated by hand, as on `UAMS-Web/uamswp-migration-api-archive#469`. *Copy or rewrite history into it*: it publishes the WordPress export commits the move exists to keep out (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2651). *Release before the freeze*: the archive's last release then leaves out UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#3382, #3383 and the other pre-snapshot changes. *Give the new repository's first tag a different version*: it releases an identical tree with no changes.

**Companion.** #2649 (the epic and its `## Decision`), #2659 (the creation), #2661 (the transfer), #2657 (the switch to public); `UAMS-Web/uamswp-migration-api-archive#465` and `UAMS-Web/wordpress-importer-archive#1244` (the same move for the sibling repositories).

## 2026-10-27: The new repository takes the name `uams-statamic`, and this one is renamed `uams-statamic-archive` and stays private (#2659)

**Decision.** This repository is renamed `uams-statamic-archive` and stays private, and the new public repository is created as `uams-statamic`.

**Why.** Keeping the name keeps the Composer package `uams-web/uams-statamic`, every tool and script that names the repository, the deploy configuration and the working copies pointing at the right place, the same reason `UAMS-Web/uamswp-migration-api-archive#468` and `UAMS-Web/wordpress-importer-archive#1292` kept theirs.

**Accepted cost.** GitHub redirects a renamed repository's old name only until a new repository takes it. From then on every link meant for this repository's history (pull-request file links, full `UAMS-Web/uams-statamic#N` references, and links in `.claude/` and `docs/`) resolves against the new repository, where those commits, branches and numbers do not exist. #3383 re-points this repository's own tracked files before the snapshot, and #3384 re-points everything else written before the creation, afterwards.

**Rejected.** *A new name for the public repository*: every old link keeps resolving here, but every tool, script, package reference, working copy and the deploy configuration changes, and the public name stops matching the package name.

**Companion.** #2659 (its `## Decision`), #3383 and #3384 (the two re-pointing passes).

## 2026-10-27: The move waits only on its own prerequisites, not on the open `security` tickets (#2659, #2661)

**Decision.** The cut and the transfer wait only on the cut prerequisites: the history and tree scans (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2650, and #2659 step 2), the redaction of the issues that move (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2652, with the re-scan #3036 on the day of the transfer), the trim and fixture pass (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#3382), and the license on `main` (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#3038). The open `security` tickets that are product work (caching, CSP, HSTS, campaign mail, e-learning hosting, accounts, payments, registrant records) transfer like any other open issue. This supersedes the 2026-09-29 entry's condition (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2651) that the move waits until every open `security` issue is completed.

**Why.** Those tickets are product work that may take months and moves to the new repository either way, and nothing in them is published by the snapshot that the scans, the redaction and the trim do not already cover. Both sibling repositories moved on the same terms.

**Rejected.** *Keep every open `security` ticket as a blocker*: it holds the move behind months of product work without changing what the snapshot publishes.

**Companion.** #2659 and #2661 (each `## Decision`), #2649 (the epic); the 2026-09-29 entry for UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2651, whose gate this replaces.

## 2026-10-27: A license lands on `main` before the snapshot, and publication has no separate approval or disclosure step (#2649)

**Decision.** A license is still chosen and lands on `main` before the snapshot: the MIT license, added by UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#3038. Publication does not wait on UAMS legal's general decision (#3039), and there is no separate approval or disclosure step: UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2656 and UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#3037 are closed as not planned.

**Why.** The sibling repositories were published on the same terms, and the snapshot's content is already bounded by the scans, the redaction and the trim. A license on `main` before the cut puts it in the new repository's only commit, so the public tree is never unlicensed.

**Rejected.** *Keep approval and disclosure as gates*: they hold the move behind a step the sibling repositories did without, and add no check on what the snapshot publishes that the prerequisites do not already make.

**Companion.** #2649 (its `## Decision`), UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#3038 (the license on `main`), UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2655 (the earlier choice ticket, closed as not planned), #3039 (the general legal decision, not waited on), UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#2656 and UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#3037 (closed as not planned).

## 2026-10-27: Font Awesome Free 7 from npm replaces the Font Awesome Pro 5.15.4 bundle (UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#3395)

**Decision.** Decided 2026-10-27 on UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#3395 and built in UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#3398. The front end gets its icons from the npm package `@fortawesome/fontawesome-free` at the latest 7.x release, loaded by `resources/js/uams_2020/uams-2020-fontawesome-entry.js` as the SVG with JavaScript build (`js/all.js` and `js/v4-shims.js`). The Font Awesome Pro 5.15.4 files under `resources/js/uams_2020/source/fontawesome/` are deleted. Templates keep their Font Awesome 5 names, which Free 7's `all.js` still resolves, and the v4 shims cover `fa-file-text`. Three creator-role icons were Pro-only and are replaced with Free ones (decided 2026-10-27 on UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#3398): Host `fa-megaphone` becomes `fa-bullhorn`, Videographer `fa-camcorder` becomes `fa-video`, and Audio Editor `fa-computer-speaker` becomes `fa-volume-high`. <!-- cspell:ignore fontawesome -->

**Why.** The repository goes public by the snapshot method (#2659), and the Pro files carry a commercial license. Free is self-hosted under licenses a public repository may carry (icons CC BY 4.0, fonts SIL OFL 1.1, code MIT) and needs no account set-up, secret or CDN. The current major (7) rather than 5.15.4, because it ships `fa-bluesky` and `fa-square-x-twitter` and Font Awesome 6 receives only critical fixes. npm rather than the Composer package `fortawesome/font-awesome`, because Vite bundles the icons from `node_modules` and no PHP code reads Font Awesome files.

**Rejected.** *A hosted Pro kit*: a CDN dependency at page load, for icons Free already covers. *Pro installed at build time with a registry token*: a secret in every build path (CI, deployment, each developer machine) for the same icons. *Keeping the Pro files*: it rests on purchase terms nobody has read, and a published file cannot be unpublished.

**Accepted cost.** A Pro-only icon no longer renders, including one an editor types into a knowledge-base topic field.

**Companion.** UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#3395 (the decision), UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#3398 (the build), #2659 (the snapshot), UAMS-Web/uams-statamic-archive#3038 (the `LICENSE` change that listed the Pro directory as not covered).
